# Twitch-Bot — Parity-Audit Python -> Rust (VERIFIZIERT)

> **Stand 2026-06-21.** Read-only Voll-Audit nach dem PY→Rust-Cutover. Default-Annahme: Rust = faithful 1:1-Port; wo Rust *bewusst besser* gebaut ist, ist es kein Bug (siehe Abschnitt „Rust-Verbesserungen"). Gejagt: **fehlende Features + Regressionen**. Jeder Befund unten ist im echten Code **adversarial gegengeprüft** (Default „refuted") — nur unabhängig Bestätigtes steht hier.

**Methodik:** Jede Python-Datei wurde in Zeilen-Fenster (an Funktionsgrenzen) zerlegt; pro Fenster las ein Opus-Agent beide Seiten *Zeile für Zeile* und führte ein Pflicht-Coverage-Manifest pro Konstrukt. Ernste Rohbefunde gingen durch einen zweiten, adversarialen Verifier. **Es wurde nichts geändert** — Fixes folgen separat.

**Lesehilfe zum Fixen:** Pro Befund steht oben die exakte Fundstelle in **Python** (Soll-Verhalten) und **Rust** (Ist-Zustand). „**Befund**" erklärt den Mechanismus (wie Python es macht, was Rust auslässt, welche Folge entsteht). „**Verifikation**" ist die Beweiskette am Code — dort steht, *wo genau* anzusetzen ist.

**Stand-Warnung:** `dashboard` ist eine **Untergrenze** — ein Teil der Verifier starb am Session-Limit; nach dem Nachzieh-Lauf steigen die dashboard-Zahlen. **Welle 3** (engagement / social_media / highlight / storage / runtime) ist noch nicht geprüft.

## Schweregrad-Uebersicht (verifiziert)

| Severity | Bedeutung | Anzahl |
|---|---|---|
| P0 | user-sichtbar kaputt / Daten-, Geld-, Sicherheitsrelevant | 1 |
| P1 | ganzes Feature / Verhalten fehlt | 57 |
| P2 | Verhaltensdrift / Teilverlust | 145 |
| P3 | intern / kosmetisch | 29 |
| **Summe** | | **232** |

## P0 — user-sichtbar kaputt / Daten-, Geld- oder Sicherheitsrelevant  (1)

### P0.1 [dashboard:live] add_streamer admin flow has no native Rust endpoint (live SPA POSTs /twitch/add_streamer to dead Python proxy)

- **Python:** `live.py:1728-1788`
- **Rust:** `ABSENT`
- **Status:** missing

**Befund:** Admins cannot add new Twitch streamers via the dashboard. The only add-streamer capability (_do_add) has zero native Rust route. With Python dashboard dead, the action 502s. This is the same 'Dashboard live tot' P0 class already flagged in memory.

*Python-Soll:* live.py:1728-1788 add_streamer (calls _do_add + _discord_profile); live admin SPA bot/admin_dashboard/src/api/client.ts:1100 submitLegacyAction('/twitch/add_streamer', ...)

*Rust-Ist:* ABSENT (searched: grep -rniE 'add_streamer|_do_add' crates/tb-dashboard-api bin; grep '"/twitch/add_streamer"' in routers — no hit. lib.rs:715 comment: 'add_streamer ... separate follow-up tickets and deliberately NOT included'). Legacy /twitch/* paths fall through to strangler fallback (bin/tb-dashboard/src/main.rs:47-64) -> Python 8765, which per project memory is dead (502).

**Verifikation:** Independently confirmed in current code. Python: bot/dashboard/live/live.py:1728-1788 add_streamer (calls _do_add + _discord_profile). The admin SPA still POSTs to the dashboard host: bot/admin_dashboard/src/api/client.ts:1100 submitLegacyAction('/twitch/add_streamer', normalized). On the Rust dashboard (tb-dashboard, port 8769) the native routers register NO /twitch/add_streamer route: build_admin_legacy_forms_router (lib.rs:690-702) wires only /twitch/admin/manual-plan and .../clear; build_entry_admin_router (lib.rs:718-739) is GET-only SPA/redirects. grep for '/twitch/add_streamer' across *.rs returns zero route matches. lib.rs:714-717 and admin_spa.rs:24-27 explicitly say add_streamer/verify/archive/discord_flag/manual-plan are 'bewusst NICHT enthalten'. Unmatched /twitch/* falls to app.fallback(dashboard_fallback_handler) (bin/tb-dashboard/src/main.rs:47-64) → Python 8765, which per project memory is dead → 502. NUANCE checked and rejected as a refutation: a native add-streamer DOES exist but on a DIFFERENT surface — POST /internal/twitch/v1/streamers (tb-internal-api/src/handlers/streamers.rs:1285 add_handler, base INTERNAL_API_BASE_PATH=/internal/twitch/v1, port 8776, X-Internal-Token auth). That backend-to-backend endpoint is not reachable from, and not called by, the dashboard SPA's /twitch/add_streamer path. So the user-facing admin dashboard flow has no native endpoint and 502s. Severity kept critical: complete loss of a core admin capability with a confirmed dead path (matches the known 'Dashboard live tot' P0 class). Caveat: surface is admin/localhost-gated, not end-user-facing.

**Laufzeit-Check:** On the dashboard host, POST form-encoded login to /twitch/add_streamer (port 8769) and observe HTTP 502 legacy_upstream_unavailable; contrast with POST /internal/twitch/v1/streamers (port 8776, X-Internal-Token) which succeeds.

## P1 — ganzes Feature / Verhalten fehlt  (57)

### P1.1 [chat:moderation] channel_settings-Drop schreibt keine Outbound-Suppression mehr (7d/3d) — Promo/Recruitment/Raid spammen geblockte Kanäle endlos

- **Python:** `moderation.py:1310-1329`
- **Rust:** `rust/crates/tb-chat/src/moderation.rs:793-827`
- **Status:** missing

**Befund:** Die READ-Seite (check_suppression/is_muted, moderation.rs:865-911) ist verdrahtet und wird vom Promo-Pfad genutzt, findet aber nie Einträge aus dem channel_settings-Drop, weil niemand sie schreibt. Folge: Kanäle, die Bot-Nachrichten via channel_settings serverseitig droppen, werden bei jedem Promo-/Recruitment-/Raid-Zyklus erneut angeschrieben statt für 7/3 Tage unterdrückt — wiederholte verworfene Sends, unnötige Helix-Last, mögliche weitere Eskalation Richtung Bot-Ban.

*Python-Soll:* moderation.py:1310-1329 _maybe_blacklist_for_drop_reason ruft bei JEDEM is_sent=false-Drop (Aufruf 1525-1530) _set_outbound_chat_suppression (1000-1066: UPSERT in twitch_outbound_chat_suppressions, suppressed_until=now+TTL). Bei drop_code='channel_settings' und source∈{promo(7d),recruitment(7d),partner_raid(3d)} wird der Kanal für Tage stummgeschaltet; readonly_connection ist autocommit=True (pg.py:1124-1130), der Write committet also real.

*Rust-Ist:* OutboundSuppressionStore::upsert_suppression existiert (rust/crates/tb-chat/src/moderation.rs:793-827) hat aber NULL Produktions-Caller — grep -rn 'upsert_suppression' crates/ liefert nur die Definition + tests/suppression_db.rs. Der Drop-Pfad (SendOutcome::Dropped{code:'channel_settings'}) wird nur von timeout_tracking.rs:49,63 ausgewertet, das channel_settings explizit ignoriert; promos.rs:1310-1314 behandelt alles ≠ Sent als generischen Fehler ohne Suppression-Write.

**Verifikation:** Independently confirmed in current code. Python bot/chat/moderation.py:1310-1329 (_maybe_blacklist_for_drop_reason) unconditionally calls _set_outbound_chat_suppression (1000-1066, UPSERT into twitch_outbound_chat_suppressions with 7d/3d TTL for promo/recruitment/partner_raid on reason_code='channel_settings'); invoked from the is_sent=false drop path at 1525-1530. In Rust, OutboundSuppressionStore::upsert_suppression (rust/crates/tb-chat/src/moderation.rs:793) has ZERO production callers — grep across crates/ (excluding /tests/) returns only the definition; the sole real reference is tests/suppression_db.rs. The only production SendOutcome::Dropped consumer is TimeoutTrackingChatApi::send_message (timeout_tracking.rs:170-209), which reacts only to is_bot_timeout_drop (BOT_TIMEOUT_DROP_CODES) and bot_banned_reason (sender_banned / HTTP-ban) and explicitly excludes channel_settings (doc 43-44, tests 442/522). The READ side (is_muted, used by promos.rs:784,825) is wired but never finds channel_settings rows because nothing writes them. Result: channels that drop bot messages via channel_settings are re-messaged every promo/recruitment/raid cycle instead of being suppressed for 7/3 days — repeated dropped sends, extra Helix load, escalation risk toward bot-ban. Genuine missing-feature regression in the cutover. Severity high retained (bot-ban escalation vector); medium would also be defensible since impact is bounded waste, not data loss/crash.

**Laufzeit-Check:** After a channel_settings-dropped promo/recruitment/raid send, query twitch_outbound_chat_suppressions for that target_login — Rust leaves it empty (Python would have a row with suppressed_until = now+7d/3d); same channel gets re-messaged next cycle.

### P1.2 [chat:connection] Runtime auto-mod-retry on 403 chat-join failure is absent in Rust (no self-healing of lost mod status)

- **Python:** `bot/chat/connection.py:1558-1624`
- **Rust:** `ABSENT`
- **Status:** missing

**Befund:** If the bot loses moderator status in a partner channel at runtime (e.g. de-modded, temporarily banned then unbanned), Python automatically re-mods itself and re-subscribes, optionally retrying every 10 minutes. Rust marks the chat subscription perm_failed and never retries until a full restart, so chat ingestion for that channel silently stays dead. This is a real recovery regression, partially mitigated only if partner-setup guarantees mod status up front.

*Python-Soll:* bot/chat/connection.py:1558-1624 — on a 403 'subscription missing proper authorization', join() calls _ensure_bot_is_mod(channel_id, login), sleeps 1s, re-subscribes, and on success returns joined_after_mod_retry; on failure sets a 10-min _mod_retry_cooldown (1606) and retries later.

*Rust-Ist:* ABSENT (searched: grep -rn 'ensure_bot_is_mod|automatisch als Mod|mod_retry_cooldown|als Mod zu setzen' crates/ → no hits). The only mod-setting is HelixClient::add_channel_moderator at rust/crates/tb-transport-twitch/src/moderation.rs:22, which uses the BROADCASTER token at partner-setup time, not the bot self-modding on a runtime 403. In the chat path, ensure_subscription_with_condition (rust/crates/tb-monitoring/src/subscriptions.rs:785) collapses any 403 into a permanent perm_failed entry (no retry until process restart).

**Verifikation:** Python (bot/chat/connection.py:1558-1624) self-heals a runtime 403 "missing authorization" by calling _ensure_bot_is_mod() to auto-re-mod the bot, sleeping 1s, re-subscribing, returning joined_after_mod_retry on success, and on failure setting a 10-min _mod_retry_cooldown to retry periodically. Rust has no equivalent in the chat/subscription path: ensure_subscription_with_condition (rust/crates/tb-monitoring/src/subscriptions.rs:783-795) collapses any 403 into a permanent perm_failed HashSet entry with the explicit comment "kein weiterer Retry bis Neustart", and the reconcile loop skips perm_failed entries (lines 744-752). perm_failed is constructed empty only at SubscriptionManager::new() (line 248) and is NEVER cleared at runtime (no remove/clear/drain anywhere in the crate). add_channel_moderator (rust/crates/tb-transport-twitch/src/moderation.rs:22) exists only as a partner-SETUP-time call (rust/crates/tb-raid/src/partner_setup.rs:1019-1021) using the broadcaster token, not as a runtime self-mod on a 403. The only runtime response to a de-mod is a user-facing DM (rust/crates/tb-raid/src/token_lifecycle.rs:139, user_dm_bot_banned_text) instructing the streamer to manually /unban + /mod. So if the bot is de-modded mid-runtime, Python auto-recovers without human action while Rust stays perm_failed (chat ingestion dead for that channel) until the streamer manually re-mods or the process restarts. Genuine recovery regression. Severity stays high but is partially mitigated by the explanatory DM (so the failure is not fully silent) and the fact that 429/401/400 transients still retry — only the 403/de-mod case is stuck.

**Laufzeit-Check:** In a partner channel where the bot is moderator, run /unmod on the bot at runtime, then watch logs for the channel's chat subscription to log a 403/perm_failed and observe that chat events stop and never recover (no auto re-mod) until process restart, whereas Python re-mods and resumes within ~1s or retries after 10 min.

### P1.3 [chat:constants] Fake-/Scam-Server-Warnung (SCAM_WARNING_*) komplett fehlt im Rust-Promo-Pfad

- **Python:** `bot/chat/constants.py:231-252`
- **Rust:** `ABSENT`
- **Status:** missing

**Befund:** Zuschauer in Partner-Kanaelen erhalten KEINE Warnung mehr vor den Fake-Servern 'Deadlock Discord Deutschland' / 'Deadlock German Competitiv HUB'. In Python wechselten sich Promo und Scam-Warnung selbsttaetig ab (alle 120 min pro Kanal). Verlorenes Sicherheits-/Anti-Scam-Feature; rein verhaltensrelevant, in Prod sichtbar abwesend.

*Python-Soll:* bot/chat/constants.py:231-252 (SCAM_WARNING_ENABLED=True, COOLDOWN_MIN=120, INITIAL_DELAY_MIN=20) + :237-247 (2 Warntexte); konsumiert in bot/chat/promos.py:1017-1095 (_scam_warning_due/_build_scam_warning_text/_maybe_send_scam_warning) — interleaved in den Promo-Sendepfad bei reason in ('promo','chat_activity'), mit eigenem Cooldown, Seed-Logik (now - (COOLDOWN-INITIAL_DELAY)*60) und orange Announcement (source='scam_warning').

*Rust-Ist:* ABSENT (searched: grep -rni 'scam_warning|SCAM_WARNING|fake.server|nicht unsere Server|German Competitiv|Deadlock Discord Deutschland|Achtung|Vorsicht' rust/crates/tb-chat rust/crates/tb-transport-twitch rust/crates/tb-crypto). Einziger Treffer: ein Kommentar in promos.rs:695 ('...Scam-Warnungen erhalten') ohne Implementierung. build_promo_text/send_promo_message (promos.rs:844-889) haben keinen Scam-Warning-Branch.

**Verifikation:** Independently confirmed. Python bot/chat/constants.py:231-252 defines SCAM_WARNING_ENABLED/COOLDOWN_MIN(120)/INITIAL_DELAY_MIN(20) + 2 warning texts; bot/chat/promos.py:1017-1095 implements _scam_warning_due/_build_scam_warning_text/_maybe_send_scam_warning, interleaving the fake-server warning into the promo path (reason in promo/chat_activity) with its own cooldown, seed-backdating (now - (COOLDOWN-INITIAL_DELAY)*60), persisted timestamp, and an orange announcement source='scam_warning'. The Rust side has NO equivalent: grep across the whole rust/ tree for scam_warning, SCAM_WARNING, 'Deadlock Discord Deutschland', 'Deadlock German Competitiv', 'nicht unsere Server', the warning emoji/text, and source='scam_warning' returns zero hits. The two similarly-named Rust modules are different features — tb-chat/src/scam_pitch.rs is a port of service_pitch_warning.py (detects self-promoters/scammers IN chat), and conversation_scam.rs is the LLM-judge first-writer guard; neither posts a fake-server warning into the promo rotation. The Rust promo loop (promos.rs ~705-845) dispatches lurker-tax -> targeted-promo -> promo-with-stats -> viewer-spike with no scam-warning branch; build_promo_text/send_promo_message have no scam-warning path. The sole 'Scam' token is an unbacked comment at promos.rs:695 (partner-gate note). Genuine missing-feature regression: viewers in partner channels no longer receive the periodic warning about the fake servers.

**Laufzeit-Check:** Keep a partner channel live for >2 promo cycles (~ a couple hours with COOLDOWN 120min) and watch its chat: Python posts the orange "Achtung/Vorsicht ... Deadlock Discord Deutschland ... könnte Scam sein" warning interleaved with promos; the Rust bot never emits it.

### P1.4 [chat:promos] Lurker-Tax bot-token scope fallback dropped: gating now requires streamer's own moderator:read:chatters grant

- **Python:** `bot/chat/promos.py:327-349`
- **Rust:** `rust/crates/tb-chat/src/promos.rs:1250-1266`
- **Status:** changed

**Befund:** A streamer whose own raid-auth never carried moderator:read:chatters — but for whom the central bot token holds the scope — gets Lurker-Tax in Python but NOT in Rust. Python explicitly migrated to bot-centric operation; Rust silently regresses to per-streamer-scope gating, suppressing the feature for the intended bot-centric setup. Fail-closed: feature just never fires, no error.

*Python-Soll:* bot/chat/promos.py:327-349 — has_chatters_scope = (_LURKER_TAX_SCOPE in scopes) OR (token_mgr is not None AND bot_scopes_loaded AND _LURKER_TAX_SCOPE in bot_scopes); comment line 357 'Lurker-Tax wird bot-zentriert ueber den zentralen Bot-Token ermoeglicht.' Consumed as gate at promos.py:1410 (if not has_moderator_read_chatters: return False).

*Rust-Ist:* rust/crates/tb-chat/src/promos.rs:1250-1266 — only queries twitch_raid_auth.scopes for the streamer (LOWER(twitch_login)=$1) and checks for 'moderator:read:chatters'; no read of central bot token scopes / token-manager. (searched: grep 'bot_scopes|token_manager.*scopes|bot_id' in crates/tb-chat -> only moderation.rs/conversation_scam.rs, none in promos.rs lurker path)

**Verifikation:** Verified in current code. Python bot/chat/promos.py:345-349 sets has_chatters_scope = (moderator:read:chatters in streamer's twitch_raid_auth scopes) OR (token_mgr loaded AND scope in central bot-token scopes); the comment at 357 documents the deliberate migration to bot-centric operation. The Rust lurker path in rust/crates/tb-chat/src/promos.rs:1250-1266 queries ONLY twitch_raid_auth.scopes for the streamer (LOWER(twitch_login)=$1) and returns early at line 1265 if moderator:read:chatters is absent. There is no bot-token fallback: the PromoEngine struct (lines 474-487) holds no token_mgr/BotTokenManager field at all, and a grep across tb-chat shows BotTokenManager usage only in moderation.rs/token.rs/secret_sink.rs, never in the promos lurker path. A BotTokenManager exposing scopes/bot_user_id exists in the crate but is not wired into PromoEngine. Net effect: a streamer whose own raid-auth lacks moderator:read:chatters, but for whom the central bot token holds it, gets Lurker-Tax in Python and silently never in Rust (fail-closed, no error). Real regression against the explicitly intended bot-centric design. Severity high; could be argued down to medium if in practice nearly all streamer raid-auths already carry the scope, but the documented bot-centric migration makes the fallback the load-bearing path.

**Laufzeit-Check:** In a DB where a streamer's twitch_raid_auth.scopes lacks moderator:read:chatters but the central bot token carries it, trigger a live session and confirm no Lurker-Tax message fires under Rust while Python would send one.

### P1.5 [chat:promos] Lurker-Tax candidate query drops the known-chat-bot exclusion filter

- **Python:** `bot/chat/promos.py:451-458`
- **Rust:** `rust/crates/tb-chat/src/promos.rs:1336-1368`
- **Status:** missing

**Befund:** A known chat bot that was tracked as a silent lurker (messages=0, seen_via_chatters_api) and meets the >=3 sessions / >=240 min thresholds will be selected and publicly @-mentioned by the lurker-tax reminder ('Lurker Steuer: @bot ...'). Python explicitly suppresses bots; Rust pings them.

*Python-Soll:* bot/chat/promos.py:451-458 builds current_bot_clause/historical_bot_clause via build_known_chat_bot_not_in_clause and injects them at lines 490 (historical) and 523 (live) with params at 528/534, so known chat bots are excluded from both the historical lurk aggregation and the live candidate set.

*Rust-Ist:* rust/crates/tb-chat/src/promos.rs:1336-1368 — get_lurker_tax_candidates SQL contains NO bot-exclusion clause; searched grep 'build_known_chat_bot|known_chat_bot|NOT IN' in promos.rs returned only the Python-side import reference, none in Rust.

**Verifikation:** Confirmed in current code. Python bot/chat/promos.py:_get_lurker_tax_candidates builds known-chat-bot NOT IN clauses (build_known_chat_bot_not_in_clause) and injects them into BOTH the historical_lurks aggregation (line 490) and the final live-candidate SELECT (line 523), with params bound at 528/534 — known chat bots are excluded from the lurker-tax candidate set. Rust rust/crates/tb-chat/src/promos.rs:get_lurker_tax_candidates (1336-1368) contains NO bot-exclusion in either CTE or the final SELECT. KNOWN_CHAT_BOTS/is_known_chat_bot exist in tb-chat (mention_scoring.rs) but are never referenced in the lurker path; the caller (1268-1303) only applies per-session dedup and a max-mentions cap before the candidates flow straight into build_lurker_tax_text ('Lurker Steuer: @...'). A known chat bot tracked with messages=0 AND seen_via_chatters_api=TRUE meeting the session/minute thresholds would be publicly @-mentioned. Real regression vs Python.

**Laufzeit-Check:** In a test/staging DB insert a twitch_session_chatters row for a KNOWN_CHAT_BOTS login (e.g. nightbot) with messages=0, seen_via_chatters_api=TRUE meeting the prior-sessions/minutes thresholds across an active and prior session, then trigger the lurker-tax path and observe the bot appears in the 'Lurker Steuer' mention.

### P1.6 [chat:token_manager] oauth: prefix is never stripped in Rust bot-token manager (validate/refresh/returned token)

- **Python:** `token_manager.py:135`
- **Rust:** `crates/tb-chat/src/token.rs:347-351`
- **Status:** changed

**Befund:** If TWITCH_BOT_TOKEN / TWITCH_BOT_REFRESH_TOKEN ever carries the Twitch IRC-style 'oauth:' prefix (legacy seeds did), Python tolerated it everywhere; Rust passes 'oauth:abc...' straight into the OAuth/Bearer header and refresh form, producing 401 at validate and a failed refresh -> bot cannot authenticate. Mitigated only if all current secrets are stored bare. Behavioral guardrail lost.

*Python-Soll:* token_manager.py:135 get_valid_token returns self.access_token.replace('oauth:','').strip(); :144 validate strips before 'OAuth {token}' header; :226 refresh strips 'oauth:' from refresh_token before POST.

*Rust-Ist:* crates/tb-chat/src/token.rs:347-351 validate() sends `OAuth {access_token}` verbatim; :265-280 access_token() returns s.access_token.clone() verbatim; :365-376 refresh_with() puts refresh_token into the form verbatim. grep -rn 'oauth:' crates/tb-chat returned nothing.

**Verifikation:** Independently confirmed in current code. Python bot/api/token_manager.py strips the IRC-style 'oauth:' prefix at every consumption point: get_valid_token (:135 .replace("oauth:","").strip()), _validate_and_fetch_info before the 'OAuth {token}' header (:144), and _refresh_access_token before the refresh POST (:226). Its env loader _load_tokens (:302-303) only .strip()s, so an 'oauth:'-prefixed seed was tolerated end-to-end. The Rust port rust/crates/tb-chat/src/token.rs has NO equivalent: validate() sends format!("OAuth {access_token}") verbatim (:351), access_token() returns s.access_token.clone() verbatim (:265-280), refresh_with() puts refresh_token into the form verbatim (:365-376), and resolve_seed_tokens only .trim()s (:75-94). grep -rni 'oauth:' over the whole tb-chat crate returns only an unrelated German comment in commands.rs — the prefix-stripping guardrail is genuinely absent. If TWITCH_BOT_TOKEN/TWITCH_BOT_REFRESH_TOKEN carries the legacy 'oauth:' prefix, Rust forwards it raw and validate 401s + refresh fails -> bot cannot auth. The divergence is real in code; whether it currently bites depends on the stored secret value, which I cannot read (Infisical off-limits), so I cannot prove it moot. Kept at high (conditional on a legacy-seeded secret; modern standard-flow tokens are bare, but failure mode is total auth loss).

**Laufzeit-Check:** Boot tb-bot with TWITCH_BOT_TOKEN deliberately set to an 'oauth:'-prefixed value and watch for an immediate validate 401 followed by refresh failure in the logs; Python with the same value boots fine.

### P1.7 [chat:twitch_api] get_followers_total dropped the user_token parameter — app-token-only call cannot return the real follower total

- **Python:** `twitch_api.py:831-834`
- **Rust:** `rust/crates/tb-transport-twitch/src/streams.rs:250-273`
- **Status:** missing

**Befund:** Twitch requires a moderator/broadcaster token (moderator:read:followers) to receive the 'total' field on /channels/followers; with an app token the value is omitted -> Rust always yields None. Any consumer using this ported method directly for the live follower count gets None instead of the real number. Whether prod is actually affected hinges on the composition-root FollowerCountSource impl (outside this unit) re-implementing the user-token call.

*Python-Soll:* twitch_api.py:831-834 get_followers_total(self, user_id, user_token=None) forwards user_token to get_followers_total_result; the user_token branch at 741-778 GETs /channels/followers with 'Authorization: Bearer <broadcaster/mod token>'. Production callers pass it: sessions_mixin.py:938/998/1106 (user_token=bot_token/user_token), runtime_support.py:136.

*Rust-Ist:* rust/crates/tb-transport-twitch/src/streams.rs:250-273 — fn get_followers_total(&self, broadcaster_id: &str) has no user_token param and uses self.get() (app token). On non-success it returns Ok(None); the test at streams.rs:548-558 asserts get_followers_total('42') == None on HTTP 401, codifying the app-token failure as expected. ABSENT user_token path (searched: grep -rn 'get_followers_total|channels/followers' crates/ --include=*.rs).

**Verifikation:** Confirmed in current code. Python's session follower-count flow (bot/monitoring/sessions_mixin.py:925-938) explicitly requires the `moderator:read:followers` scope and passes a moderator-scoped user token (bot_token) into get_followers_total, with a 403-not-moderator self-heal/fallback (line 987) — proving Twitch only returns the `total` field on /channels/followers when called with such a user token. The Rust counterpart has no user_token path: HelixClient::get_followers_total (rust/crates/tb-transport-twitch/src/streams.rs:250-273) only uses self.get() (app token) and returns Ok(None) on any non-success; the unit test at streams.rs:548-557 asserts None on HTTP 401, codifying the app-token failure as expected. The composition-root impl HelixFollowerSource::follower_total (rust/bin/tb-bot/src/wiring.rs:174-186) calls the same App-token-only get_followers_total(user_id) with no token branch, and main.rs:327-332 wires it in. So the production Rust session tracker's FollowerCountSource always yields None for the live follower total, whereas Python obtained the real number via the bot's moderator-scoped token. Real regression; high (silent analytics data loss, best-effort degrade to None, not a crash — hence not critical).

**Laufzeit-Check:** On a live channel, watch the Rust session tracker / followers_total written for a tracked Deadlock session and confirm it is null/None while the Twitch dashboard shows a real follower count (and the App-token /channels/followers call logs a 401/403 without a total field).

### P1.8 [raid:auth] auto-scope resolution drops active-partner check and narrows auth-row existence -> returning streamers can be granted base (narrower) OAuth scopes instead of dashboard_reauth

- **Python:** `bot/raid/auth.py:433-482`
- **Rust:** `bin/tb-bot/src/raid_oauth_impl.rs:160-173`
- **Status:** changed

**Befund:** Only reached when scope_profile is omitted (auth_url defaults to "auto", raid_oauth_impl.rs:660). For a streamer who (a) is an active partner but has no qualifying twitch_raid_auth row, or (b) has a twitch_raid_auth row with raid_enabled=false AND authorized_at NULL (pending/disabled/blacklisted), Python resolves dashboard_reauth (extended scopes incl. channel:read:subscriptions, channel:read:hype_train) while Rust resolves base. The re-auth then requests fewer scopes than before, silently degrading dashboard features that depend on the extended scopes for that streamer. No 500, just a wrong gate.

*Python-Soll:* bot/raid/auth.py:433-482 (_has_existing_streamer_context) returns bool(load_active_partner(conn, twitch_login=normalized_login) OR self._has_existing_auth_row(conn, twitch_login=normalized_login)); _has_existing_auth_row (406-431) is a broad SELECT 1 FROM twitch_raid_auth WHERE (user_id match) OR (login match) with NO raid_enabled/authorized_at filter. There is also a dedicated discord:-prefix branch (439-471).

*Rust-Ist:* bin/tb-bot/src/raid_oauth_impl.rs:160-173: SELECT (raid_enabled IS TRUE OR authorized_at IS NOT NULL) FROM twitch_raid_auth WHERE LOWER(twitch_login)=LOWER($1) LIMIT 1 — no twitch_partners/load_active_partner check, no discord: branch, and the auth-row existence is filtered to raid_enabled/authorized_at (Python counts ANY row).

**Verifikation:** Confirmed in current code. Rust scope auto-resolution (tb-raid/src/oauth_flow.rs:95-113) feeds the raw login into PgStreamerContextResolver::has_existing_streamer_context (tb-bot/src/raid_oauth_impl.rs:160-173), which runs `SELECT (raid_enabled IS TRUE OR authorized_at IS NOT NULL) FROM twitch_raid_auth WHERE LOWER(twitch_login)=LOWER($1)`. Python `_has_existing_streamer_context` (bot/raid/auth.py:433-482) instead ORs load_active_partner (twitch_partners) with `_has_existing_auth_row` (auth.py:406-431), a filter-less `SELECT 1 ... WHERE user_id OR login match` counting ANY row, and has a dedicated discord:-prefix branch that resolves identity first. Three real divergences each yield Python=dashboard_reauth but Rust=base: (1) active partner with no qualifying auth row — Rust has no partner check; (2) auth row exists but raid_enabled=false AND authorized_at NULL (pending/disabled) — Python counts it, Rust's filter drops it; (3) `discord:<id>` login — Rust passes the literal string into the login SQL (never matches), while Python resolves the identity first (the discord: handling in build_state_info lines 188-206 only sets expected_login, AFTER scope is already resolved at line 178). dashboard_reauth grants channel:read:subscriptions/channel:read:hype_train/channel:manage:broadcast (scope_profiles.rs:35-54) that base omits. Path reached when scope_profile is omitted (default "auto", raid_oauth_impl.rs:660). Result: silent scope narrowing on re-auth for returning streamers/partners, degrading dashboard features. No crash.

**Laufzeit-Check:** Call auth_url for a streamer who is an active partner (or has a raid_enabled=false/authorized_at NULL auth row) without passing scope_profile, then check the generated authorize URL's scope param: Python includes channel:read:subscriptions/hype_train, Rust will not.

### P1.9 [raid:partner_scores] Partner raid-score refresh/write path entirely missing in Rust — cache never (re)computed

- **Python:** `bot/raid/partner_scores.py:656-797`
- **Rust:** `ABSENT`
- **Status:** missing

**Befund:** The Rust auto_raid_pipeline (rust/crates/tb-raid/src/auto_raid_pipeline.rs:180-181,394 load_many + final_score-based selection) READS twitch_partner_raid_scores but nothing in Rust ever WRITES/refreshes it. After cutover the score cache goes stale (or empty for new partners): final_score/today_received_raids/is_live/duration_score frozen at whatever Python last wrote (or 0). Target selection degrades to stale/neutral scores — wrong partners get raided, daily-cap and fairness penalties stop updating, new partners never enter the cache. This is the entire purpose of partner_scores.py and it does not run in Rust.

*Python-Soll:* bot/raid/partner_scores.py:656-797 (_build_score), 799-853 (_upsert_scores), 856 (partner_raid_score_service), 859-871 (refresh_partner_raid_score / refresh_all_partner_raid_scores), 898-902 (refresh_all_partner_raid_scores_async, invoked periodically by bot/monitoring/partner_ops.py:19/23). This pipeline aggregates session_rows -> recent_durations/avg_duration_sec, computes time_pattern_score_base (weekday+hour match in BERLIN_TZ), today_received_raids (Berlin date), selects live/cache/offline branch, builds _PreparedScore, and upserts into twitch_partner_raid_scores.

*Rust-Ist:* ABSENT. scoring.rs only ports the pure leaf formulas; score_store.rs::upsert (rust/crates/tb-raid/src/score_store.rs:206-288) is the only INSERT but has ZERO non-test callers. Searched: grep -rn 'ScoringInputs {' crates (none outside scoring.rs tests); grep -rn 'compute_scores(' crates (none outside scoring.rs tests); grep -rn '.upsert(' tb-raid/src (only score_store.rs test fns); grep -rn 'recent_started|recent_durations|time_pattern_score_base.*compute|weekday|num_days_from_monday|existing_cache|refresh_all_partner|refresh_partner_score|fn refresh.*partner' crates (no orchestration). No Rust monitoring job calls any refresh.

**Verifikation:** Verified independently. Python bot/raid/partner_scores.py (_build_score 656-797, _upsert_scores 799-853, refresh_all_partner_raid_scores 867+) aggregates session rows -> recent_durations/time_pattern/today_received_raids, computes scores and UPSERTs into twitch_partner_raid_scores; bot/monitoring/partner_ops.py runs this every 300s. In Rust the pipeline is absent: scoring.rs::compute_scores and the ScoringInputs struct are constructed/called ONLY from scoring.rs unit tests; ScoreStore::upsert and ScoreStore::new have ZERO non-test callers (only score_store.rs test fns). The on_score_refresh hook is invoked from handlers.rs, but the only concrete EventSubHooks/PollHooks impls in production code are NoopEventSubHooks/NoopPollHooks (default no-op) — the sole non-noop impl is in tests/eventsub_dispatch.rs. Meanwhile auto_raid_pipeline.rs (181, 394) READS the cache via scores.load_many for final_score-based target selection. So nothing in Rust ever aggregates/computes/writes the score cache; after cutover final_score/today_received_raids/is_live/fairness freeze at Python's last write (or 0 for new partners), degrading target selection. Severity corrected to high (not critical): it silently degrades raid-target quality and fairness/daily-cap updates rather than crashing the bot or losing data — but it is genuinely missing, not merely built differently.

**Laufzeit-Check:** On a Rust-only deployment, watch twitch_partner_raid_scores.last_computed_at across all rows for >5 min — if it never advances (and new partners never get a row) while the poller ticks, the refresh path is dead.

The Rust port lacks this entirely: classify_partner_raid_arrival (crates/tb-raid/src/arrival_confirmation.rs:124) has no expected_partner parameter and no synthetic-override second pass. The service confirm_pending_raid_arrival exposes classification_override/target_is_partner_override params, but the ONLY caller (bin/tb-bot/src/raid_arrival_wiring.rs:704) passes None, None, None, and target_is_partner is derived solely from the real DB lookup (prefetch_lookups -> is_target_partner, line 357-365). grep across crates/ and bin/ for 'expected_partner' and 'pending_partner_override' returns zero hits. ### P1.14 [raid:raid_blacklist] External-target bot-ban-check due-processing loop (chat-bot part/rejoin probe) not ported — pending rows accumulate forever and are never processed, deleted, or rescheduled

- **Python:** `bot/raid/services/raid_blacklist.py:387-456`
- **Rust:** `external_recruitment_store.rs:253`
- **Status:** missing

**Befund:** Twitch bot schedules an external-target bot-ban-check whenever it recruits/raids an external channel (partner_recruit.rs:201, raid_arrival_wiring.rs:616), inserting rows into twitch_external_bot_ban_check_pending with run_after = now+delay. In Python a background loop later parts+rejoins the channel to confirm the chat bot still has access; if access is gone it reschedules, and once confirmed it deletes the pending row. In Rust that loop does not exist, so: (1) the safety/verification behavior — detecting that the bot got banned from a recruited channel — is silently lost; (2) twitch_external_bot_ban_check_pending grows unbounded (rows are written but never deleted), an operational/DB-bloat leak. This is the classic 'write-side ported, read/process-side dropped' migration gap.

*Python-Soll:* bot/raid/services/raid_blacklist.py:387-456 async process_due_external_target_ban_checks: loads due rows (load_due_external_target_ban_checks, LIMIT 25, builder line 628-638), per row skips if already blacklisted (delete) or partner (delete), else fetches chat_bot; if no chat_bot -> reschedule; optionally part_chat_channels then join_chat_channel probe; on success delete_external_target_ban_check_pending, on failure/exception reschedule (or delete if meanwhile blacklisted). Plus the supporting service methods delete_external_target_ban_check_pending (354-365) and reschedule_external_target_ban_check_pending (367-385).

*Rust-Ist:* Write side IS ported and live: external_recruitment_store.rs:253 schedule_bot_ban_check, called from raid_arrival_wiring.rs:616 and partner_recruit.rs:201. Read/process side ABSENT: external_recruitment_store.rs:323 load_due_bot_ban_checks, :293 delete_bot_ban_check, :302 reschedule_bot_ban_check exist but have ZERO callers (searched: grep -rn 'load_due_bot_ban_checks|delete_bot_ban_check|reschedule_bot_ban_check|process_due_external_target_ban' --include='*.rs' over entire rust/ -> matches only inside external_recruitment_store.rs and its own #[cfg(test)] mod). main.rs spawns periodic 300s tasks for process_due_recruitment_blacklists (main.rs:615-620) and partner score refresh (:631) but NONE for bot-ban-checks.

**Verifikation:** Verified in current code. Python process_due_external_target_ban_checks (bot/raid/services/raid_blacklist.py:387-456) loads due rows from the ban-check pending table, deletes for blacklisted/partner targets, parts+rejoins via join_chat_channel as an access probe, then deletes on success or reschedules on failure — plus delete_/reschedule_external_target_ban_check_pending (354-385). On the Rust side the WRITE path is live: external_recruitment_store.rs:253 schedule_bot_ban_check is called from partner_recruit.rs:201 and raid_arrival_wiring.rs:616, inserting into twitch_external_bot_ban_check_pending. The READ/PROCESS path — load_due_bot_ban_checks (:323), delete_bot_ban_check (:293), reschedule_bot_ban_check (:302) — has ZERO production callers; grep across all of bin/ and crates/ shows matches only inside external_recruitment_store.rs's own #[cfg(test)] module. The 300s loop at main.rs:615-620 calls process_due_recruitment_blacklists, which reads a DIFFERENT table (load_due_blacklist_pending / recruitment_blacklist_pending, raid_arrival_wiring.rs:131) and applies the raid-limit grace blacklist — it never touches the ban-check table and never does the part/rejoin access probe. No alternately-named probe loop exists. So: (1) the bot-ban/access-loss verification behavior is silently dropped, and (2) ban-check pending rows are written but never deleted/rescheduled by any running task. Note: the insert is ON CONFLICT(target_id) DO UPDATE, so growth is bounded by distinct target count rather than strictly unbounded per event, but rows still never get cleaned up — a genuine leak. High severity is appropriate (functional safety regression + DB-bloat leak; not a crash/security issue).

**Laufzeit-Check:** On the live DB, count rows in twitch_external_bot_ban_check_pending where run_after has long passed (run_after << now) and confirm none disappear over time — they persist because no task processes them.

### P1.15 [monitoring:embeds_mixin] Missing UI→template config normalization: customized live-announcement embeds silently revert to defaults (and static_ping_role_ids dropped)

GIBT ES NICHT MEHR ES GIB NUR NOCH EIN DEFAUKLT TEMPOLATE DER REST STEGAL
- **Python:** `embeds_mixin.py:360-362`
- **Rust:** `announce/sink.rs:121-124`
- **Status:** changed

**Befund:** Every streamer with a customized announcement config gets the DEFAULT embed in Rust instead of their saved title/description/color/fields/image/footer/button. Worse, mentions.role_id (the single static ping role configured in the UI) maps to static_ping_role_ids in Python but is never read by Rust → that role is silently never pinged and never added to allowed_role_ids. use_streamer_ping_role likewise comes from mentions.enabled in the UI schema (default_live_announcement_config:17 mentions.enabled) but Rust reads mentions.use_streamer_ping_role → defaults to true regardless of the operator turning pings off.

*Python-Soll:* embeds_mixin.py:360-362 _render_live_announcement_payload calls self._normalize_live_announcement_config(self._load_live_announcement_config(login)) BEFORE rendering; _normalize_live_announcement_config (215-309) maps the stored UI schema to the flat template schema: embed.title→title_template (264), embed.description→description_template (267), embed.color (254), embed.fields[].name/value (236-246), embed.image.use_stream_thumbnail→image_mode (248-250,277), embed.thumbnail.mode→thumbnail_mode (271-275), and mentions.role_id (digit string)→static_ping_role_ids (231-234,305). The dashboard persists exactly this UI schema: live_announcement_mixin.py:443 cfg=_deep_merge(_default_live_announcement_config(), parsed) then _la_save (634-648) writes that cfg verbatim; _default_live_announcement_config uses embed.{title,color,fields,image.use_stream_thumbnail}, mentions.role_id, button.label.

*Rust-Ist:* announce/sink.rs:121-124 AnnounceConfigStore::load: serde_json::from_str(&text) → AnnouncementConfig::from_json(&parsed) with NO normalization step. AnnouncementConfig::from_json (template.rs:118-193) reads the FLAT template keys directly: raw.get("title_template") (156,161), raw.get("content_template") (156), images.get("image_mode") (174), images.get("thumbnail_mode") (171), mentions.get("static_ping_role_ids") (139-153), mentions.get("use_streamer_ping_role") (190). None of these keys exist in the stored UI schema (which has embed.title, embed.image.use_stream_thumbnail, mentions.role_id). The promised mapper is referenced only in a doc comment (dashboard_config.rs:5 'via to_template_config') and is ABSENT (searched: grep -rn 'to_template_config\|normalize_live' crates/ → only the comment).

**Verifikation:** Independently confirmed the UI→template normalization gap. Persistence: dashboard writes the UI schema verbatim — live_announcement_mixin.py:334 _la_save serializes cfg from line 314 (_deep_merge(_default_live_announcement_config(), raw_cfg)) via json.dumps at line 634; that default schema uses embed.{title,color,fields,image.use_stream_thumbnail}, mentions.role_id, mentions.enabled. _to_template_config (line 101) is applied only at validation (line 194) and at render/read time, never before INSERT, so config_json in twitch_live_announcement_configs holds the UI schema. Python render path normalizes: embeds_mixin.py:360 calls _normalize_live_announcement_config before rendering, mapping embed.title→title_template, embed.image.use_stream_thumbnail→image_mode, mentions.role_id(digit)→static_ping_role_ids, mentions.enabled→use_streamer_ping_role. Rust does NOT: sink.rs:121-124 AnnounceConfigStore::load does serde_json::from_str then AnnouncementConfig::from_json with no normalization. template.rs from_json (118-193) reads only FLAT keys — raw.get("title_template") (161), images.get("image_mode") (174), mentions.get("static_ping_role_ids") (139), mentions.get("use_streamer_ping_role") (190) — none of which exist in the stored UI schema. The promised mapper exists only as a doc comment (dashboard_config.rs:5 "via to_template_config"); grep across all tb-* crates found no to_template_config/normalize_live implementation. Worse, dashboard_config.rs (UI-schema default + parse_config_json + deep_merge) is dead code: pub-exported in mod.rs but never called by the load path or anywhere else. The Rust test (announce.rs:220-221) only passes because its fixture inserts already-flat JSON ("title_template", "button.enabled") instead of the real UI schema the dashboard writes — masking the gap. Net effect: customized embeds fall back to defaults; mentions.role_id never reaches static_ping_role_ids so that role is never added to allowed_role_ids nor pinged; mentions.enabled=false is ignored because use_streamer_ping_role defaults true. Severity high not critical: announcements still post (default embed), bot does not crash; impact is wrong/degraded content and a silently-dropped static ping role, not an outage.

**Laufzeit-Check:** In a staging DB, save a customized config via the dashboard (set a custom embed title and a static ping role_id), then trigger a go-live for that streamer and confirm the Discord post shows the default title and omits the configured role ping.

### P1.16 [monitoring:embeds_mixin] sanitize_live_content misses mixed-case @everyone/@here → mention-bypass on go-live posts

- **Python:** `embeds_mixin.py:88-93`
- **Rust:** `announce/template.rs:554-567`
- **Status:** changed

**Befund:** An alert_mention or rendered content containing a mixed-case @everyone/@here (e.g. from operator config or a streamer-supplied template field) is not neutralized in Rust, allowing an unintended @everyone/@here ping on every go-live post. Python blocked all casings.

*Python-Soll:* embeds_mixin.py:88-93 _sanitize_live_content: re.sub(r"@everyone", "@​everyone", sanitized, flags=re.IGNORECASE) and the same for @here. re.IGNORECASE neutralizes ANY casing (@EveryOne, @eVeRyOnE, @Here, @hErE). This runs on the live posting content (monitoring.py:1550 prefix sanitize, embeds_mixin.py:894 content sanitize).

*Rust-Ist:* announce/template.rs:554-567 sanitize_live_content: out.replace over a fixed list of 6 literals only — @everyone,@Everyone,@EVERYONE,@here,@Here,@HERE. Any other casing (e.g. @EveryOne, @hEre) is NOT replaced. Called on the real send path at sink.rs:290 and sink.rs:299.

**Verifikation:** Confirmed in current code. Python bot/monitoring/embeds_mixin.py:91-92 _sanitize_live_content uses re.sub with flags=re.IGNORECASE on @everyone and @here, neutralizing ANY casing. Rust rust/crates/tb-monitoring/src/announce/template.rs:554-567 sanitize_live_content uses out.replace over a fixed list of only 6 literal casings (@everyone,@Everyone,@EVERYONE,@here,@Here,@HERE). Mixed casings such as @EveryOne, @eVeRyOne, @hEre are NOT matched and pass through unneutralized. The Rust fn is invoked on the real send path at sink.rs:290 (alert mention) and sink.rs:299 (rendered content), so an operator/template-supplied mixed-case mention yields an unintended @everyone/@here ping on go-live posts — a behavioral regression vs Python.

**Laufzeit-Check:** Set an alert_mention or template field to a mixed-case value like "@EveryOne" and trigger a go-live announcement; observe the Discord post still produces a live @everyone ping in Rust whereas Python suppresses it.

### P1.17 [monitoring:eventsub_webhook] Revocation callback side-effects dropped: Rust only logs, never untracks subscription or restarts EventSub supervisor

- **Python:** `eventsub_webhook.py:78-84`
- **Rust:** `webhook_receiver.rs:185-196`
- **Status:** missing

**Befund:** When Twitch revokes a core subscription (e.g. token/permission loss), Python self-heals by stopping/restarting the EventSub supervisor and untracking the sub so it gets re-subscribed. The Rust receiver silently acks the revocation; the subscription stays revoked with no re-subscribe trigger, so live events (stream.online/offline/channel.update) stop arriving until something else notices. This is a real on/off behavior loss. Confirm whether the Rust subscription manager has an independent re-subscribe loop that compensates — search outside tb-monitoring before treating as fully unmitigated.

*Python-Soll:* eventsub_webhook.py:78-84 set_revocation_callback + 356-380 _handle_revocation invoke a registered callback; the registered callback (eventsub_mixin.py:1310-1339 _handle_eventsub_webhook_revocation) untracks the revoked sub (_eventsub_untrack_sub) and, for core sub types, sets _eventsub_started=False, _eventsub_retry_reason='webhook_revocation', deactivates dispatch and restarts the supervisor.

*Rust-Ist:* webhook_receiver.rs:185-196 handles MSG_TYPE_REVOCATION by emitting a tracing::warn with the status reason and returning 204 NO_CONTENT — no callback. ABSENT revocation hook (searched: grep -rn 'revocation|revoke|on_revocation|RevocationCallback' rust/crates/tb-monitoring/src/ → only webhook_receiver.rs:41 MSG_TYPE_REVOCATION const).

**Verifikation:** Confirmed in current Rust code. webhook_receiver.rs:185-196 handles MSG_TYPE_REVOCATION by only emitting tracing::warn and returning 204 — no callback, no untrack, no supervisor restart. WebhookReceiver::new (bin/tb-bot/src/main.rs:810) is constructed with only secret+dispatcher; there is no revocation hook anywhere (full src sweep finds revocation handling only at webhook_receiver.rs:41/185). Python's _handle_eventsub_webhook_revocation (eventsub_mixin.py:1318-1344) untracks the sub and, for core types, sets _eventsub_started=False and restarts the supervisor.\n\nWhy this is a real on/off regression and not mitigated by the reconcile loop: subscriptions.rs keeps a long-lived in-memory tracked HashSet (line 221). ensure_subscription_with_condition short-circuits at line 740 (is_tracked → return true) so a tracked sub is never re-created. The 6h subscription_maintenance_loop (main.rs:1257-1339) calls cleanup_stale (deletes only subs for broadcasters NOT in active_ids — a revoked sub for a still-active partner is kept) then ensure_core_subscriptions, which skips via is_tracked. The only thing that would drop a revoked (non-enabled) sub from tracked and allow re-subscribe is rehydrate() (subscriptions.rs:277-299, clears + rebuilds keeping status==\"enabled\" only), but rehydrate runs ONLY at startup (main.rs:348/375, engine.rs:136 before the loop) — never inside any periodic loop. So when Twitch revokes a core sub for an active broadcaster, Rust silently acks it and never re-subscribes until the process restarts; live stream.online/offline/channel.update events for that channel stop arriving. The comment at main.rs:1221 claiming the loop catches revoked subs is inaccurate for this path. Severity high confirmed: real silent event-delivery loss with no runtime self-heal, only partial recovery on restart.

**Laufzeit-Check:** On a test channel, revoke the app's EventSub authorization (or delete the sub server-side) so Twitch sends a revocation, then confirm via tb-bot logs that only the warn fires and stream.online/offline events stop arriving for that channel with no re-subscribe until the process is restarted.

### P1.18 [monitoring:eventsub_webhook] Revocation message does nothing in Rust — no subscription untrack, no supervisor restart

- **Python:** `eventsub_webhook.py:597-606`
- **Rust:** `webhook_receiver.rs:185-196`
- **Status:** missing

**Befund:** When Twitch revokes a core subscription (token expiry, scope loss, channel deauth), Python self-heals by deactivating dispatch and restarting the EventSub supervisor to re-subscribe; Rust silently acks (204) and keeps a stale, dead subscription. EventSub delivery for that channel stops with no automatic recovery until a restart or the poll-loop fallback masks it.

*Python-Soll:* eventsub_webhook.py:597-606 calls `await self._handle_revocation(data, message_id=...)`; the wired callback eventsub_mixin.py:1318-1344 untracks the sub (`_eventsub_untrack_sub`) and, for core sub-types, sets `_eventsub_started=False`, deactivates notification dispatch, and calls `_ensure_eventsub_supervisor_running('webhook_revocation')` to rebuild subscriptions.

*Rust-Ist:* webhook_receiver.rs:185-196 — the MSG_TYPE_REVOCATION arm only logs `eventsub_receiver: Subscription widerrufen` and returns 204. No untrack/restart effect anywhere: ABSENT (searched: `grep -rni 'revocation|revoke|widerruf|untrack' rust/crates/tb-monitoring/src rust/crates` — only the webhook_receiver constant + log lines).

**Verifikation:** Confirmed in current code. Rust webhook_receiver.rs:185-196 handles MSG_TYPE_REVOCATION by logging only ("eventsub_receiver: Subscription widerrufen") and returning 204 — no untrack, no supervisor restart. grep across all tb-* crates finds NO revocation callback wiring (no set_revocation_callback / on_revoke / equivalent of Python _handle_eventsub_webhook_revocation); the only revocation references are the webhook_receiver constant+arm and unrelated scam_guard "revoked" strings. Python (eventsub_mixin.py:1318-1344) on revocation calls _eventsub_untrack_sub, sets _eventsub_started=False, deactivates dispatch, and _ensure_eventsub_supervisor_running('webhook_revocation') to rebuild. The Rust gap is actively self-perpetuating: SubscriptionManager keeps an in-memory `tracked` set (subscriptions.rs:262-274); ensure_subscription_with_condition (line 740-743) short-circuits with "bereits subscribed, überspringe" whenever is_tracked is true. On revocation Twitch drops the sub server-side but Rust never removes it from `tracked`, so even the periodic reconcile (ensure_core/ensure_chat_subscriptions) skips re-subscribing. The only path that clears the cache is rehydrate() (subscriptions.rs:277), which runs once at poller start (poller/engine.rs:136) — i.e. recovery only on process restart, exactly as claimed. Severity high (not critical): stream online/offline has a poll-loop fallback that masks state, but chat/notification-only subs for an affected channel silently stop with no auto-recovery.

**Laufzeit-Check:** In a staging EventSub, revoke a core subscription via the Twitch API (or deauth the channel), then confirm the bot logs only the "widerrufen" warning, never re-subscribes on the next reconcile cycle, and only recovers after a process restart.

### P1.19 [monitoring:sessions_mixin] Session follower fetch only uses App-Token in Rust; bot-token + streamer-token + self-heal paths dropped -> followers_end/follower_delta effectively always NULL in prod

- **Python:** `bot/monitoring/sessions_mixin.py:881-1179`
- **Rust:** `rust/bin/tb-bot/src/wiring.rs:173-187`
- **Status:** changed

**Befund:** In production the session-finalize follower total resolves to None on nearly every stream end (App-Token cannot read the `total` field). Consequently followers_end and follower_delta in twitch_stream_sessions are persistently NULL, so per-session follower-growth analytics (post-stream reports, partner views) are dead. The Python bot reliably populated these via the bot/streamer token. This is a real on/off behavior loss, not a cosmetic one.

*Python-Soll:* bot/monitoring/sessions_mixin.py:881-1179 _fetch_followers_total_safe — preferred path uses the central bot token with moderator:read:followers scope (907-948), on HTTP 403 not-moderator runs _attempt_bot_moderator_self_heal and retries (986-1046), then falls back to the per-streamer OAuth user token via _raid_bot.auth_manager.get_valid_token_for_login (1048-1144). Twitch's GET /channels/followers `total` field requires a moderator/broadcaster user token.

*Rust-Ist:* rust/bin/tb-bot/src/wiring.rs:173-187 HelixFollowerSource.follower_total calls only self.helix.get_followers_total(user_id) which (rust/crates/tb-transport-twitch/src/streams.rs:250-273) issues GET /channels/followers with the APP token and explicitly returns None on the resulting 401/403 (doc lines 247-249). No bot-token path, no streamer-token fallback, no self-heal anywhere (grep -rn 'moderator:read:followers|self_heal|attempt_bot_moderator|get_valid_token_for_login' crates/ rust/bin -> none in this flow).

**Verifikation:** Verified in current code. Python bot/monitoring/sessions_mixin.py:881-1179 fetches the follower total with a 3-tier strategy: central bot token with moderator:read:followers (907-948), on HTTP 403 not-moderator it runs _attempt_bot_moderator_self_heal and retries (986-1046), then falls back to the per-streamer OAuth user token via _raid_bot.auth_manager.get_valid_token_for_login (1048-1144). Rust HelixFollowerSource.follower_total (wiring.rs:173-187) calls only helix.get_followers_total(user_id) (streams.rs:250-273), which issues GET /channels/followers via HelixClient.get() — and get() (client.rs:107-114) injects the APP token (access_token()), then returns Ok(None) on any non-success status (401/403). The client HAS get_with_user_token helpers (client.rs:160) used by raid/moderation, but the follower path never uses them; there is no bot-token branch, no streamer-token fallback, and no self-heal. The only moderator:read:followers references in Rust are EventSub subscription scopes (subscriptions.rs:156), not the total fetch. Twitch's /channels/followers `total` requires a moderator/broadcaster user token, so the App-token request gets 401/403 and follower_total returns None. At session finalize (tracker.rs:330-335) this drives followers_end=None and follower_delta=None, persisting as NULL in twitch_stream_sessions. Genuine on/off regression. Severity high (not critical): it degrades per-session follower-growth analytics, not a crash or data-loss/security issue.

**Laufzeit-Check:** End a real stream and query twitch_stream_sessions for the latest finalized row; followers_end and follower_delta will be NULL, and tb-bot debug logs show "followers-total nicht verfügbar (App-Token ohne Moderator-Scope?)".

### P1.20 [monitoring:eventsub_mixin] Webhook-Revocation eines Core-Subs löst in Rust keinen Resubscribe/Supervisor-Restart aus (stiller Event-Verlust)

- **Python:** `bot/monitoring/eventsub_mixin.py:1318-1344`
- **Rust:** `tb-monitoring/src/webhook_receiver.rs:185-196`
- **Status:** changed

**Befund:** Wird eine Core-Subscription (stream.online/offline/channel.update) von Twitch widerrufen (z. B. Auth-/Secret-Rotation, Twitch-seitige Drops), bleibt das In-Memory-Tracking (subscriptions.rs:740 is_tracked) auf 'subscribed'. Der 6h-Reconcile-Loop (main.rs:1300 ensure_core_subscriptions) überspringt den Kanal wegen is_tracked und legt die Subscription NIE neu an — rehydrate() läuft nur beim Boot (main.rs:375), nicht im Loop. Folge: für den betroffenen Kanal gehen Go-Live/Offline/Title-Events bis zum Prozess-Neustart verloren. In Python triggerte derselbe Fall sofort einen Supervisor-Restart + Resubscribe.

*Python-Soll:* bot/monitoring/eventsub_mixin.py:1318-1344 _handle_eventsub_webhook_revocation: bei Revocation wird (a) self._eventsub_untrack_sub(revoked_type, broadcaster_id) gerufen, (b) für _EVENTSUB_WEBHOOK_CORE_SUB_TYPES (stream.online, stream.offline, channel.update) zusätzlich _eventsub_started=False gesetzt, _set_eventsub_webhook_notification_dispatch(active=False) und _ensure_eventsub_supervisor_running('webhook_revocation') zum Neuaufbau ausgelöst.

*Rust-Ist:* tb-monitoring/src/webhook_receiver.rs:185-196 MSG_TYPE_REVOCATION-Arm loggt nur tracing::warn!(...'Subscription widerrufen') und gibt StatusCode::NO_CONTENT zurück. Kein untrack, keine Core-Typ-Prüfung, kein set_dispatch_active(false), kein Supervisor-Restart. cleanup_stale (main.rs:1294) entfernt nur inaktive Broadcaster, nicht revoked Subs aktiver Broadcaster.

**Verifikation:** Confirmed in current code. Python _handle_eventsub_webhook_revocation (eventsub_mixin.py:1318-1344) untracks the sub and, for core types (stream.online/offline/channel.update), sets _eventsub_started=False, deactivates dispatch and calls _ensure_eventsub_supervisor_running('webhook_revocation') to resubscribe. The Rust REVOCATION arm (tb-monitoring/src/webhook_receiver.rs:185-196) only logs tracing::warn! and returns 204 — no untrack, no core-type check, no dispatch deactivation, no supervisor/resubscribe. Structurally it cannot do otherwise: WebhookReceiver (webhook_receiver.rs:49-52) holds only secret + dispatcher and has no SubscriptionManager reference. The leak is real: ensure_subscription_with_condition skips when is_tracked is true (subscriptions.rs:740); `tracked` is only cleared/rebuilt by rehydrate(), which runs solely at boot (bin/tb-bot/src/main.rs:348,375), never in the 6h reconcile loop (main.rs:1262-1300); cleanup_stale (subscriptions.rs:829-867) explicitly `continue`s for active broadcasters (lines 843-846), so it never removes a revoked sub of an active channel from `tracked`. Net: a revoked core sub for an active channel stays marked subscribed until process restart, ensure_core_subscriptions never re-creates it, and go-live/offline/title events are silently lost. Genuine regression vs Python. Severity corrected critical→high: real silent event loss, but trigger is a Twitch-side revocation (auth/secret rotation, Twitch drops) not normal flow, and a process restart re-runs rehydrate and self-heals.</reason>
<parameter name="verify_hint">On a live channel, revoke a core EventSub (e.g. rotate the webhook secret or revoke the app authorization) so Twitch sends a revocation, then go live and watch for missing stream.online/offline/channel.update dispatch in tb-bot logs while no resubscribe occurs until restart.

The candidate poller crates/tb-monitoring/src/irc_lurker.rs writes only twitch_session_chatters (line 101 INSERT_CHATTER), never presence_ticks, and is not even wired into any binary (grep shows only `pub mod irc_lurker;` at lib.rs:30; every IrcLurkerTracker::new call is in tests). Rust DOES read this table productively (viewer_timeline.rs:174/396, post_stream.rs:1035/1059 aggregations) and the table exists in the live DB (baseline migration), so reads don't error — but no live component produces new tick rows. The repo's own audit (rust/docs/audit/_work/findings-2026-06-15.json:9-15) independently documents the identical gap. Net effect: presence/watchtime timelines stop accumulating fresh data after cutover — a genuine analytics regression.

**Laufzeit-Check:** Auf der Live-DB den Max-tick_at-Zeitstempel in twitch_viewer_presence_ticks abfragen und gegen einen aktuell laufenden Stream prüfen: bleibt der Wert vor dem Rust-Cutover stehen, wird nicht mehr geschrieben.

### P1.24 [analytics:mixin] compute_raid_retention hourly loop has no Rust port — twitch_raid_retention never populated

- **Python:** `bot/analytics/mixin.py:2254-2475`
- **Rust:** `ABSENT`

**Befund:** Raid retention analytics are never computed in the Rust runtime. The dashboard's raid_retention_handler reads twitch_raid_retention but the table is only fed by the (now-unrun) Python hourly loop. Over time the table goes stale/empty → raid retention metrics (5m/15m/30m chatter retention, known-from-raider overlap, new-viewer/new-chatter counts, raid match quality) silently show zero/old data. Pure feature loss with no error surfaced.

*Python-Soll:* bot/analytics/mixin.py:2254-2475 (@tasks.loop(hours=1) compute_raid_retention: loads 7-day raids, computes chatters_at_plus5m/15m/30m via _count_chatters, known_from_raider, new_to_target, new_chatters, and INSERTs into twitch_raid_retention ON CONFLICT(raid_id,executed_at) DO NOTHING). Started at mixin.py:82 (self._retention_task = self.compute_raid_retention.start()) and registered as LoopSpec('compute_raid_retention') in runtime_bootstrap.py:557.

*Rust-Ist:* ABSENT (searched: grep -rn 'INSERT INTO twitch_raid_retention' --include=*.rs → 0 hits; grep -rni 'compute_raid_retention|twitch_raid_retention|chatters_at_plus5m|known_from_raider|new_to_target' across rust/crates → only READS in tb-dashboard-api/src/handlers/raid_analytics.rs:204-484; no LoopSpec/cron writer in tb-monitoring/tb-analytics). No drop note in rust/docs or code comments.

**Verifikation:** Independently confirmed in current code. Python is the sole producer of twitch_raid_retention: bot/analytics/mixin.py:2443-2467 INSERTs into the table from the @tasks.loop(hours=1) compute_raid_retention (read at :2254-2475). In the Rust tree there is NO writer: grep for INSERT/UPDATE INTO twitch_raid_retention returns zero hits across all rust/crates (exit=1); no LoopSpec/background task in tb-monitoring or tb-analytics computes it; tb-raid has no retention writer. The only Rust references are READ-ONLY consumers in tb-dashboard-api/src/handlers/raid_analytics.rs: raid_retention_handler (:204-) gates on base_rows from twitch_raid_retention (empty -> dataAvailable:false) and raid_analytics_handler (:382-) seeds per-source aggregation + follow-attribution joins from the same table. Both call recalculate_raid_chat_metrics live, BUT only for raids that ALREADY have a row in twitch_raid_retention — the recalc never inserts, so it cannot compensate for an empty table. rust/docs/02-db-contract.md:68 even lists producer 'analytics-core' / consumer 'analytics-api', yet only the consumer was ported; no drop note exists. With the Twitch-Bot on the Rust runtime (Python decommissioned per cutover state), the table is never populated -> raid-retention dashboard (5m/15m/30m retention, known-from-raider overlap, new-viewer/new-chatter, per-source quality) silently shows no data, and follow-attribution degrades to 'organic'. Severity corrected to high (not critical): it is a permanently dead analytics pipeline scoped to one dashboard view, non-crashing, no runtime error surfaced.

**Laufzeit-Check:** On the live (Rust) bot, after raids occur and the hourly window passes, query the row count of twitch_raid_retention over time — it stays flat/empty while twitch_raid_history keeps growing, and the dashboard raid-retention panel returns dataAvailable:false.

### P1.25 [analytics:mixin] EventSub telemetry inserts bind DateTime<Utc> (timestamptz) into text timestamp columns — type mismatch vs migration schema

- **Python:** `mixin.py:1851,1862-`
- **Rust:** `dispatch.rs:593`
- **Status:** changed

**Befund:** IF the live DB columns are text (as the checked-in migration says), EVERY monetization/telemetry insert in this range fails silently and all bits/sub/ad-break/ban/shoutout/channel-points/channel-update telemetry is lost. telemetry.rs:7 asserts prod columns are timestamptz/boolean (verified 2026-06-09), which would make these binds correct against PROD — but the migration SQL then lies and any fresh DB built from migrations breaks. Either way this is an unresolved schema-fidelity hazard (matches the known 'Fixtures lügen gg. Baseline' migration-bug class). Rust unit tests use ad-hoc CREATE TABLE (e.g. is_automatic INTEGER in monetization.rs:454) so they do not catch it.

*Python-Soll:* Python writes ISO STRINGS into these columns: received_at/started_at/recorded_at = datetime.now(UTC).isoformat(timespec='seconds') (mixin.py:1851,1862-ish,1951,2005,2044,2205,2244); redeemed_at kept as raw event string or now-iso string (mixin.py:2060-2062); ends_at raw string (mixin.py:2183); hype started_at/ended_at raw strings (mixin.py:2100-2101).

*Rust-Ist:* rust/crates/tb-monitoring/src/telemetry.rs binds now: DateTime<Utc> for received_at/started_at/recorded_at (lines 102,130,160,199-ish via redeemed_at,306,349,470) and parse_dt_utc→DateTime<Utc> for redeemed_at(:182-184), ends_at(:290), hype started_at/ended_at(:213-215). Migration declares these columns as text: rust/migrations/20260601000000_baseline_schema.sql:518 (redeemed_at text NOT NULL), :534/recorded_at text, :381+ ad_break started_at text, bits/subscription/ban received_at text, ban ends_at text, hype started_at/ended_at text (schema dump lines confirmed). Binding a timestamptz value to a text column raises Postgres 42804 (no implicit bind cast) → INSERT fails → error swallowed in dispatch.rs:593.

**Verifikation:** Confirmed in current code. Rust telemetry.rs binds native DateTime<Utc> (Postgres timestamptz, binary format) into received_at/started_at/recorded_at/redeemed_at/ends_at/ended_at across store_subscription_event (l102), store_ad_break_event (l130), store_bits_event (l160), store_channel_points_event (redeemed_at l199 via parse_dt_utc l182-184 + now), store_hype_train_event (started_at/ended_at l213-215, bound l265-266/240/245), store_ban_event (ends_at l305 via parse_dt_utc l290, received_at l306), store_shoutout_event (l349), store_channel_update (recorded_at l470). The checked-in baseline migration 20260601000000_baseline_schema.sql declares every one of these columns as text: ban ends_at/received_at (l448-449), subscription received_at (l486), bits received_at (l428/1428-class), channel_points redeemed_at (l518), channel_updates recorded_at (l537), ad_break started_at (l387), hype started_at/ended_at (l956-957). The only type-fixing migration, 20260619010000_runtime_type_contract.sql, converts ONLY two boolean flags (subscription.is_gift, ad_break.is_automatic) and twitch_clips_social_media.created_at to timestamptz — it does NOT touch any of these telemetry timestamp columns. Postgres performs no implicit assignment cast for binary-format bind parameters between timestamptz and text, so against any fresh-migration DB these INSERTs raise 42804 and fail. dispatch.rs:593-595 only logs the error via tracing::error! and returns true (swallowed), so failures are silent and all bits/sub/ad-break/ban/shoutout/channel-points/channel-update/follow/first-message telemetry would be lost. The green unit tests cannot catch this: tests/support/mod.rs (l193,197,201,206,210-211,216,221,225,230,234) build ad-hoc tables with these columns as TIMESTAMPTZ, matching the binds, not the migration. Python evidence verified: bot/analytics/mixin.py writes ISO strings via isoformat() into these columns (l1851,1951,2005,2044,2205,2244 + raw event strings l2060,2100-2101,2183), which is why the baseline schema is text. This is exactly the known 'Fixtures lügen gg. Baseline' migration-fidelity class. The telemetry.rs:7 / fixture comment claims PROD was manually verified timestamptz on 2026-06-09; if true, the live binds work but the migrations are unfaithful (fresh DBs break); if PROD is still text, live telemetry is silently lost. Either way it is an unresolved, unmitigated schema-fidelity hazard — confirmed regression.

**Laufzeit-Check:** On the live Postgres, run \d+ on twitch_ban_events / twitch_bits_events / twitch_subscription_events / twitch_channel_points_events / twitch_channel_updates / twitch_hype_train_events and check whether received_at/started_at/recorded_at/redeemed_at/ends_at/ended_at are timestamptz or text; or build a DB from migrations only and fire one cheer/sub event, then watch for a 42804 'Telemetrie-Insert fehlgeschlagen' error log and a missing row.

### P1.26 [analytics:api_overview] Affiliate-portal HTML page route serves wrong layer (JSON API present, HTML page absent)

- **Python:** `api_overview.py:153`
- **Rust:** `ABSENT`
- **Status:** missing

**Befund:** Visiting /twitch/affiliate/portal (the affiliate's portal page) has no native handler and relies on the strangler-proxy -> dead Python. The JSON API the React app fetches exists, but the page shell that loads it does not, so the portal page would 502 in prod if Python is down.

*Python-Soll:* api_overview.py:153 /twitch/affiliate/portal -> _serve_affiliate_portal (773-783) returns FileResponse(AFFILIATE_PORTAL_INDEX_PATH) — the affiliate portal HTML page from the website build

*Rust-Ist:* ABSENT for the HTML page. handlers/affiliate_portal.rs:23 portal_handler is wired at lib.rs:259 to /twitch/api/v2/affiliate/portal (the JSON DATA API), not the HTML page at /twitch/affiliate/portal. grep '"/twitch/affiliate/portal"' route -> none.

**Verifikation:** Independently confirmed. Python api_overview.py:153 wires GET /twitch/affiliate/portal -> _serve_affiliate_portal (773-783), which returns FileResponse(AFFILIATE_PORTAL_INDEX_PATH = website/dist/affiliate-portal/index.html) — the HTML page shell. In Rust, lib.rs:259-260 wires only /twitch/api/v2/affiliate/portal -> affiliate_portal::portal_handler, which is the JSON DATA API (the handler queries DB tables, schema t_affiliate_portal). grep across all tb-* crates finds NO Rust route serving the HTML page /twitch/affiliate/portal: the sole match in partner_gate.rs:40 is merely an auth-gate allowlist constant, not a serving handler. Rust has no /streamer or website-dist static serving at all (only spa->/analyse = dashboard_v2 and admin_spa->/twitch/admin). Thus the page only resolves via the strangler fallback proxy in bin/tb-dashboard/src/main.rs:50-56, which forwards to Python and is active only when TB_DASHBOARD_LEGACY_FALLBACK_URL is set; otherwise unknown paths return 404. So the portal page has no native handler and depends on live Python (502/404 if down), while the JSON API the React app fetches does exist natively. The auditor's core claim — JSON API present, HTML page route absent — is accurate.

**Laufzeit-Check:** With the Rust tb-dashboard running and the Python fallback disabled/down, GET http://127.0.0.1:8767/twitch/affiliate/portal and observe 404 (no native handler), while GET /twitch/api/v2/affiliate/portal returns JSON.

### P1.32 [analytics:api_v2] Changelog POST same-origin (Origin/Referer) CSRF replaced by X-CSRF-Token header check — browser admin writes get 403 in prod

- **Python:** `api_v2.py:2083-2094`
- **Rust:** `lib.rs:489`
- **Status:** changed

**Befund:** Known migration bug class (c). Per MEMORY (dashboard_v2 csrfToken:null), the v2 dashboard frontend receives csrfToken:null, so a real admin creating a changelog entry from the browser dashboard sends no X-CSRF-Token and is rejected with 403 csrf_failed — even though Python accepted the same browser request via same-origin Origin/Referer. The Localhost changelog-mirror tool (CLAUDE.md) still works via the localhost bypass, masking the regression in tests. Net: browser-based admin changelog creation is broken; only loopback tooling can write.

*Python-Soll:* api_v2.py:2083-2094 — _api_v2_internal_home_changelog_create gates only with _has_dashboard_bound_session + _is_same_origin_session_request (Origin else Referer must equal request origin), returning 403 csrf_origin_invalid only on mismatch. A browser POST automatically sends a same-origin Origin/Referer, so the dashboard UI passes with no token. Helpers _normalize_origin_value (1652-1663), _request_origin (1665-1686), _is_same_origin_session_request (1695-1708).

*Rust-Ist:* lib.rs:489 wraps the internal-home router (routes at lib.rs:152-158) with axum::middleware::from_fn(csrf_protect); csrf.rs:59-105 csrf_protect requires a valid X-CSRF-Token header validated against the session for any non-localhost POST, else 403 csrf_failed. internal_home.rs:1991-2092 changelog_handler performs NO origin/referer check. No _is_same_origin_session_request equivalent exists (searched: grep -rn 'same_origin|Origin|Referer|_request_origin' rust/crates/tb-dashboard-api/src — only csrf token + CORS).

**Verifikation:** Confirmed in current code. Python (bot/analytics/api_v2.py:2083-2094) gates the changelog POST only with _has_dashboard_bound_session + _is_same_origin_session_request (Origin else Referer must match request origin), so a browser POST with auto same-origin Origin/Referer passes with NO token. Rust replaces this with a header-token gate: the changelog route (lib.rs:151-158) is wrapped by csrf_protect (lib.rs:489), and csrf_protect (auth/csrf.rs:59-105) requires a valid X-CSRF-Token header for any non-localhost POST, else 403 csrf_failed. internal_home.rs changelog_handler does no origin/referer check, and a hardened grep across tb-dashboard-api/src found NO same-origin/Referer validation anywhere (only CORS/public-origin/embed uses). Crucially, auth_status.rs:153-275 hardcodes csrfToken:null / csrf_token:null in every response branch, and the v2 frontend createInternalHomeChangelogEntry (bot/dashboard_v2/src/api/home.ts:557-588) POSTs with only Accept + Content-Type and same-origin cookie credentials — no X-CSRF-Token header. So a real browser admin has no token to send and receives 403 csrf_failed, whereas Python accepted the identical browser request. The localhost changelog-mirror tool bypasses via is_local_request, masking the regression. This is a genuine behavioral divergence in current code.</reason>
<parameter name="verify_hint">From the live browser admin dashboard (non-loopback origin), submit a new internal-home changelog entry and observe HTTP 403 with body error=csrf_failed, while the localhost curl mirror to /twitch/api/v2/internal-home/changelog succeeds.

### P1.33 [analytics:raid_metrics] Bot-filter clause drops NULL-login chatters: anonymous chatters miscounted in all 3 raid metric queries

- **Python:** `raid_metrics.py:52-59`
- **Rust:** `raid_analytics.rs:90,130,135,170,175`
- **Status:** changed

**Befund:** Anonymous chatters (rows with chatter_id but NULL chatter_login) are excluded from raid retention (plus5m/15m/30m) and new_chatters counts in Rust, but counted in Python. The new_chatters query is most affected: its entire purpose includes `sc.chatter_login IS NULL` rows, which the != ALL JOIN filter removes before the WHERE clause sees them, systematically undercounting new chatters on the raid-analytics and raid-retention dashboard endpoints. Empty-string ('') logins remain handled correctly since '' != ALL(bots) is TRUE; the defect is specifically NULL logins.

*Python-Soll:* raid_metrics.py:52-59 + chat_bots.py:35-62 build the clause `((col) IS NULL OR (col) = '' OR LOWER(col) NOT IN (...))` which explicitly KEEPS rows where chatter_login is NULL or '' (docstring chat_bots.py:43-44: 'keeps rows where login is missing so anonymous chatter_id rows are not incorrectly filtered out'). Applied to sc.chatter_login in all 3 queries (lines 114, 157, 202) and cr.chatter_login (lines 162, 207). Query 1 (LEFT JOIN, line 96/107) and Query 3 (WHERE sc.chatter_login IS NULL OR ='' , lines 195/208-209) count anonymous chatters via COALESCE(NULLIF(chatter_login,''), chatter_id).

*Rust-Ist:* raid_analytics.rs:90,130,135,170,175 use bare `LOWER(sc.chatter_login) != ALL($2)` / `LOWER(cr.chatter_login) != ALL($2)` with NO `IS NULL OR = ''` guard. In SQL, LOWER(NULL) != ALL(array) evaluates to NULL (not TRUE), so NULL-login rows are filtered out — opposite of Python's keep. The faithful port exists in the SIBLING handler watch_time.rs:125,180,193 which keeps `(chatter_login IS NULL OR chatter_login = '' OR LOWER(chatter_login) <> ALL($2))`, proving this is an oversight in raid_analytics.rs.

**Verifikation:** Independently confirmed in current code. Python build_known_chat_bot_not_in_clause (chat_bots.py:58-61) emits `((col) IS NULL OR (col) = '' OR LOWER(col) NOT IN (...))`, with the docstring (43-44) explicitly stating it KEEPS missing-login rows so anonymous chatter_id rows are not filtered. raid_metrics.py applies it to sc/cr.chatter_login in all 3 queries (lines 114, 157, 162, 202, 207), and the new_chatters query's WHERE (208-210) is built around `sc.chatter_login IS NULL`. The Rust port raid_analytics.rs uses BARE `LOWER(sc.chatter_login) != ALL($2)` / `LOWER(cr.chatter_login) != ALL($2)` at lines 90, 130, 135, 170, 175 with NO `IS NULL OR = ''` guard. SQL semantics: LOWER(NULL) != ALL(array) -> NULL (not TRUE), so NULL-login rows fail the JOIN/WHERE predicate and are dropped — the opposite of Python's keep. For the new_chatters query (165-176) the JOIN at line 170 removes NULL-login rows before the WHERE at 176 (`sc.chatter_login IS NULL OR ...`) can keep them, so the query's core purpose (counting anonymous new chatters) is defeated. Decisive corroboration: a repo-wide grep shows EVERY other analytics module (watch_time.rs:125/180/193, chat_analytics.rs, chat_content_analysis.rs, chat_social_graph.rs, chat_deep_minimax.rs, chat_hype_timeline.rs, overview.rs) uses the faithful `(chatter_login IS NULL OR chatter_login = '' OR LOWER(chatter_login) <> ALL($N))` guard — raid_analytics.rs is the lone outlier, proving an oversight not a deliberate change. Empty-string logins remain correct ('' != ALL(bots) is TRUE); the defect is specifically NULL logins. Affects plus5m/15m/30m retention, known_from_raider, and new_chatters on the extended-plan raid-retention and raid-analytics endpoints.

**Laufzeit-Check:** Insert a twitch_session_chatters row with a non-null chatter_id but NULL chatter_login in a target raid session, then call GET /twitch/api/v2/raid-retention and confirm newChatters/chattersAt30m is one lower than the Python equivalent (or lower than a row with chatter_login='').

### P1.34 [analytics:admin_streamer_queries] Detail sessions array uses different JSON keys than the frontend expects (sessionId/title/category/averageViewers/watchTimeHours all break)

- **Python:** `admin_streamer_queries.py:430-438`
- **Rust:** `rust/crates/tb-dashboard-api/src/handlers/admin_streamers.rs:122-134`
