Mapping the Rust keys against client.ts fallback lists: sessionId reads only sessionId/session_id (Rust=id → undefined); title reads only title (Rust=streamTitle → undefined); category reads category/game_name (Rust=gameName camelCase → undefined, fallback is snake_case so still misses); averageViewers reads averageViewers/avg_viewers (Rust=avgViewers → undefined); watchTimeHours reads watchTimeHours/watch_time_hours/watch_hours (Rust=durationSeconds → undefined). startedAt, endedAt, peakViewers, followerDelta match and still work — exactly as the claim states. StreamerDetail.tsx:177-204,671 consume these undefined values: rows render 'Session #—', 'Kategorie unbekannt', 0 avg viewers, broken row key/sort. The Rust struct comment itself notes Python rounds hours but emits raw durationSeconds anyway. Genuine cutover key-naming regression.

### P1.35 [analytics:api_performance] Heatmap/stats/tag SQL binds timestamptz parameter against TEXT timestamp columns (started_at / ts_utc) — Python bound ISO strings

- **Python:** `api_performance.py:65,82`
- **Rust:** `performance.rs:78,105`
- **Status:** changed

**Befund:** Postgres has no implicit text>=timestamptz operator; binding a timestamptz parameter against a TEXT column raises `operator does not exist: text >= timestamp with time zone` at query time -> every affected endpoint (monthly-stats, weekly-stats, hourly-heatmap, calendar-heatmap, tag-analysis-extended incl. its peer-benchmark sub-queries) returns 500 in production while unit tests pass (the Rust tests CREATE their own tables with `started_at TIMESTAMPTZ`, e.g. tag_analysis.rs:309, masking the prod schema). This is the exact schema-fidelity / int4-bool class regression flagged in MEMORY (Twitch Schema-Fidelity). Confidence <1.0 because the live DB may have been migrated to timestamptz out-of-band; if so, the EXTRACT/DATE calls (identical in both Python and Rust) would work and only the bind type still differs — needs verification against the actual production column type. RESOLUTION PATH: bind `since` as a String (`.to_rfc3339()`/ISO) to mirror Python, OR confirm+convert the columns to timestamptz.

*Python-Soll:* api_performance.py:65,82 (and 97,112; 126,150; 170,191; 251; 293; 474,495): since_date computed via `(datetime.now(UTC)-timedelta(...)).isoformat()` and passed as a STRING parameter into `WHERE s.started_at >= %s` / `WHERE ts_utc >= %s`. Schema columns are TEXT (rust/migrations/20260601000000_baseline_schema.sql:1465 started_at text, 1466 ended_at text, 1442 ts_utc text, 1452 ts_utc text). So Python compares text>=text (ISO-lexicographic, valid).

*Rust-Ist:* performance.rs:78,105 / :154,178 / :221,241 / :278,297 bind `since: DateTime<Utc>` (timestamptz) into `WHERE s.started_at >= $1`; tag_analysis.rs:188,201 same; tag_analysis.rs:117 / :139 / :168 bind `since` timestamptz into `ts_utc >= $1` / `started_at >= $2`. Columns remain TEXT (no ALTER to timestamptz in runtime_type_contract migration 20260619010000 — it only converts is_gift/is_automatic/created_at).

**Verifikation:** Verified independently. Rust binds `since: DateTime<Utc>` (sqlx sends it with an explicit timestamptz type OID) directly against TEXT columns: performance.rs binds `since` into `WHERE s.started_at >= $1` for monthly-stats (L98/105), weekly-stats (L171/178), hourly-heatmap (L235/241), calendar-heatmap (L290/297), and `WHERE ts_utc >= $1` for viewer-timeline (L352/356); tag_analysis.rs binds `since` into `started_at >= $1/$2` (L198/201, L136/139, L164/168) and `ts_utc >= $1` (L115/117). Python instead binds `(datetime.now(UTC)-timedelta(...)).isoformat()` STRINGS (api_performance.py:65,82 / 97,112 / 126 / 170,191; same pattern throughout), so Python evaluates text>=text (ISO-lexicographic, valid). Schema columns are TEXT, confirmed in baseline migration: twitch_stream_sessions.started_at (L1465) + ended_at (L1466) text; twitch_stats_tracked.ts_utc (L1452) text; twitch_stats_category.ts_utc (L1442) text. The only type-converting migration (20260619010000_runtime_type_contract.sql) ALTERs ONLY is_gift, is_automatic, and twitch_clips_social_media.created_at — it never touches started_at/ended_at/ts_utc. A repo-wide grep for `ALTER COLUMN (started_at|ts_utc|ended_at) ... timestamptz` returns nothing. Postgres has no implicit text>=timestamptz operator and the explicit-OID bind prevents text coercion, so each query fails with `operator does not exist: text >= timestamp with time zone` → 500. Unit tests mask it: tag_analysis.rs test schema creates started_at/ended_at/ts_utc as TIMESTAMPTZ (L309, L314), so tests stay green against a schema that does not match production. Note the EXTRACT/DATE calls are identical in Python and Rust (same SQL string), so they are NOT the divergence point — the divergence is exclusively the WHERE-clause bind type. Residual uncertainty (already flagged by auditor): if the live DB was migrated to timestamptz out-of-band (outside the committed migrations), only the bind type would differ and queries could still succeed; but per the committed schema this is a real regression. Severity high stands: every listed analytics endpoint 500s in prod.

**Laufzeit-Check:** On the live DB run \d+ twitch_stream_sessions and \d+ twitch_stats_category and check started_at/ts_utc type; if TEXT, curl the monthly-stats/hourly-heatmap/calendar-heatmap/weekly-stats/tag-analysis-extended endpoints (authenticated) and observe 500 with a Postgres operator-does-not-exist error in the tb-dashboard logs.

### P1.36 [analytics:api_viewers] Segments cross-channel exclusivity counts the home streamer's own channel (Rust) but Python excludes it -> wrong exclusiveViewersPct / avgOtherChannels

- **Python:** `api_viewers.py:758-786`
- **Rust:** `viewers.rs:1063-1083`
- **Status:** changed

**Befund:** exclusiveViewersPct is understated and avgOtherChannels overstated in viewer-segments crossChannelStats for every streamer whose viewers also chat in one other channel. The directory handler correctly does COUNT(DISTINCT ...)-1 (viewers.rs:434) so the bug is specific to the segments path.

*Python-Soll:* api_viewers.py:758-786 — cross_channel_streamer_clause = _build_viewer_identity_not_in_clause(column_expr="sc.streamer_login", excluded_logins=excluded_logins); excluded_logins includes _normalize_login(streamer) (line 33). So the home streamer is excluded from COUNT(DISTINCT streamer_login) => a home-only viewer has ch_count=0, a home+1-other viewer has ch_count=1 (treated exclusive). Then 783-786: if channel_count<=1 exclusive_count+=1; other_channel_sum+=max(0,channel_count-1).

*Rust-Ist:* viewers.rs:1063-1083 — SQL binds only `bots` (KNOWN_CHAT_BOTS, no streamer) on both sc.chatter_login and sc.streamer_login (lines 1070-1071). ch_count therefore INCLUDES the home streamer channel. A home+1-other viewer gets ch_count=2 -> NOT exclusive and other_sum+=1, whereas Python treats them as exclusive with other_sum+=0.

**Verifikation:** Verified in current code. Python (api_viewers.py:758-786): cross_channel_streamer_clause built via _build_viewer_identity_not_in_clause(column_expr="sc.streamer_login", excluded_logins=excluded_logins); excluded_logins is seeded with _normalize_login(streamer) (line 33 in _collect_viewer_exclusion_logins). Thus COUNT(DISTINCT streamer_login) EXCLUDES the home channel -> a home+1-other viewer has ch_count=1 -> counted exclusive, other_sum += 0. Rust segments handler (viewers.rs:1011) builds bots = KNOWN_CHAT_BOTS only (home streamer NOT added). The cross-channel query (1063-1075) applies LOWER(sc.streamer_login) != ALL($3) with $3=bots, so the home channel is INCLUDED in COUNT(DISTINCT streamer_login) -> same viewer has ch_count=2 -> NOT exclusive, other_sum += 1. This understates exclusiveViewersPct and overstates avgOtherChannels for every streamer whose viewers also chat in exactly one other channel. Confirmed segments-specific: the directory handler subtracts the home channel differently (line 434), and even the Rust "shared channels" query right below (1095-1100) does exclude the home streamer via sc2.streamer_login != $3 bound to streamer — proving the omission at line 1071 is an unintentional regression, not a deliberate redesign.

**Laufzeit-Check:** For a test streamer with viewers who chat only in that one channel, call the viewer-segments endpoint and confirm exclusiveViewersPct is below 100% / avgOtherChannels above 0 (Rust) vs the Python value of 100% / 0.

### P1.37 [dashboard:abbo_billing_routes] abbo_cancel: Rust executes cancellation on GET (Python method!=POST guard dropped)

- **Python:** `abbo_billing_routes.py:186-187`
- **Rust:** `lib.rs:786-789`
- **Status:** changed

**Befund:** A plain GET to /twitch/abbo/kündigen now triggers a real Stripe cancel_at_period_end (or portal redirect). GET requests are not CSRF-protected and can be triggered by a prefetch/link/image, enabling unintended or cross-site-induced cancellations. Python deliberately blocked this with a POST-only guard.

*Python-Soll:* abbo_billing_routes.py:186-187 — `if request.method != "POST": raise web.HTTPFound("/twitch/pricing?cancel=post_required")`. routes_billing.py:25-26 registers BOTH GET and POST -> Python only actually cancels on POST.

*Rust-Ist:* lib.rs:786-789 registers `/twitch/abbo/kündigen` with `.get(cancel_handler).post(cancel_handler)`; billing_page.rs:268-336 cancel_handler has no method check and proceeds to portal/cancel on every request.

**Verifikation:** Verified in current code. Python abbo_billing_routes.py:186-187 guards abbo_cancel with `if request.method != "POST": raise web.HTTPFound("/twitch/pricing?cancel=post_required")`, and routes_billing.py:25-26 registers BOTH GET and POST — so a GET only redirects, never cancels. Rust lib.rs:786-789 registers /twitch/abbo/kündigen with `.get(cancel_handler).post(cancel_handler)`, but billing_page.rs:268-336 cancel_handler takes no Method extractor and has zero method check: it goes straight from the auth gate to active_customer_record(), then to create_billing_portal_session / cancel_subscription_at_period_end on every request, GET included. grep confirms the only Method/method references in billing_page.rs are wiremock matchers in the test module. The POST-only guard was genuinely dropped, so a plain (CSRF-unprotected, prefetch/link/image-triggerable) GET by/at a logged-in partner now performs a real Stripe cancellation or portal redirect. Severity kept at high (not critical): the action requires the victim's authenticated session and the cancel is cancel_at_period_end (reversible), not data loss/takeover.

**Laufzeit-Check:** Against a running tb-dashboard, send an authenticated GET (cookie only, no form/CSRF) to /twitch/abbo/kündigen for a partner with a Stripe subscription and observe a 302 to the Stripe portal or a cancel=scheduled redirect instead of cancel=post_required.

### P1.38 [dashboard:abbo_billing_routes] abbo_cancel: CSRF check missing in Rust cancel_handler

- **Python:** `abbo_billing_routes.py:188-191`
- **Rust:** `ABSENT`
- **Status:** changed

**Befund:** The subscription-cancellation endpoint performs a state-changing Stripe action with no CSRF protection. Combined with the dropped POST-only guard, a forged/cross-site request can cancel a logged-in partner's subscription. Python required a valid csrf_token.

*Python-Soll:* abbo_billing_routes.py:188-191 — reads `csrf_token` from POST body and `if not handler._csrf_verify_token(request, csrf_token): raise web.HTTPFound("/twitch/pricing?cancel=csrf_invalid")`.

*Rust-Ist:* ABSENT (searched: `grep -n csrf billing_page.rs` shows no match inside cancel_handler; cancel_handler billing_page.rs:268-336 has no CSRF verification; the cancel route is NOT behind a header-CSRF layer in lib.rs:786-789).

**Verifikation:** Verified in current code. Python abbo_cancel (bot/dashboard/abbo_billing_routes.py:186-191) enforces POST-only AND body csrf_token validation (_csrf_verify_token) before the state-changing Stripe cancel. The Rust port cancel_handler (rust/crates/tb-dashboard-api/src/handlers/billing_page.rs:268-336) takes only auth/config/pool — no Form/header extractor, no CSRF check anywhere in the handler body, and goes straight to create_billing_portal_session / cancel_subscription_at_period_end. The route (lib.rs:786-789) is wired get(cancel_handler).post(cancel_handler) with no csrf_protect layer on build_billing_page_router (contrast lib.rs:489/553/637 where admin routers DO layer csrf_protect). This is a real regression, not an equivalent re-design: the SIBLING billing form route on the same router, rechnungsdaten -> billing_profile::profile_save_handler, explicitly validates body CSRF in-handler via verify_form_csrf (billing_profile.rs:56-58, 384-413), proving the team knew to port body-CSRF and simply omitted it on cancel. The handler also accepts GET, so a plain cross-site link/img can cancel a logged-in partner's subscription. Severity stays high (forged destructive action requires an authenticated victim; cancellation is recoverable and gives the attacker no funds, so not critical).

**Laufzeit-Check:** As a logged-in partner, send a cross-origin GET (or no-CSRF-token POST) to /twitch/abbo/kündigen and confirm it redirects to the Stripe portal / schedules cancel=scheduled instead of returning cancel=csrf_invalid.

### P1.39 [dashboard:server_v2] forward_auth drops admin-session IP-binding, passive/JS fingerprint and fp_pending checks (security weakened on live Caddy forward_auth path)

- **Python:** `server_v2.py:435-472`
- **Rust:** `crates/tb-dashboard-api/src/handlers/forward_auth.rs:50-75`
- **Status:** missing

**Befund:** A stolen/replayed master_dash_session or admin cookie is accepted from any IP / any device / any browser fingerprint, and sessions in the fingerprint-pending state are accepted. Python rejected all of these with 401. This is the gate Caddy uses to authorize the entire admin host, so the regression weakens admin-host access control until B3-10 lands. Mitigated only by Caddy host-gating and cookie secrecy.

*Python-Soll:* server_v2.py:435-472 — validate_admin_session enforces stored client_ip binding (435-452, 401+AUDIT on mismatch), passive fingerprint UA|lang|platform sha256[:32] (454-464), fp_pending (466-468) and missing js_fp for non-discord sessions (470-472).

*Rust-Ist:* crates/tb-dashboard-api/src/handlers/forward_auth.rs:50-75 — validate_admin_session only checks auth.is_privileged() and returns 200/401. Checks ABSENT (searched: grep -rn 'client_ip|passive_fp|js_fp|fp_pending|Sec-CH-UA-Platform|trusted_proxy' crates/tb-dashboard-api/src — no hits). forward_auth.rs:21-27 documents the omission as deferred ticket B3-10.

**Verifikation:** Verified in current code. Python validate_admin_session (bot/dashboard/server_v2.py:435-472) enforces four post-login checks: client_ip binding with trusted-proxy/forwarded-host handling (435-452, 401+AUDIT on mismatch), passive fingerprint sha256(UA|Accept-Language|Sec-CH-UA-Platform)[:32] (454-464), fp_pending rejection (466-468), and missing js_fp for non-discord_dashboard sessions (470-472). The Rust counterpart (rust/crates/tb-dashboard-api/src/handlers/forward_auth.rs:50-75) only calls auth.is_privileged(), which is matches!(Localhost | Admin) (auth/level.rs:66-68). Admin resolution (level.rs:236-238) merely confirms the master_dash_session cookie exists in the DB via load_admin_session — no IP, fingerprint, or fp_pending gating. A hardened grep across all crates for client_ip|passive_fp|js_fp|fp_pending|Sec-CH-UA-Platform|trusted_proxy|forwarded_client|canvas returned only the doc-comment in forward_auth.rs (which itself documents the omission as deferred ticket B3-10, lines 21-27). The route is live: build_entry_admin_router (lib.rs:723-726) registers GET /twitch/auth/validate ahead of the strangler proxy, so this is the gate Caddy uses to authorize the admin host. Net effect: a stolen/replayed admin cookie is accepted from any IP/device/browser and fp_pending sessions are accepted, where Python returned 401. Real regression. Severity stays high (not critical) because exploitation still requires obtaining a valid admin cookie and the admin host remains behind Caddy host-gating.

**Laufzeit-Check:** Authenticate to the admin host to get a valid master_dash_session cookie, then replay that cookie from a different IP/browser to GET /twitch/auth/validate — Rust returns 200 where Python returned 401.

### P1.40 [dashboard:abbo_routes] Streamer promo self-service (disable promos + Discord promo message) has no native Rust handler — depends on the dead Python fallback

- **Python:** `abbo_routes.py L538-546, L609-627: has_promo_disable_control = _billing_plan_has_entitleme`
- **Rust:** `ABSENT`
- **Status:** missing

**Befund:** Entitled streamers (Raid Boost / Analyse / Bundle have chat.promos.disable) can no longer disable bot promos or set their own Discord promo message via the dashboard unless the legacy Python proxy is live. If the fallback is unset or Python is down, these POSTs 404/502. The underlying streamer_plans.promo_disabled/promo_message columns are still read by the chat engine and by admin tooling, so the data path exists — only the self-service write was dropped.

*Python-Soll:* abbo_routes.py L538-546, L609-627: has_promo_disable_control = _billing_plan_has_entitlement(current_plan_id,'chat.promos.disable'); _load_abbo_saved_settings returns promo_disabled/promo_message; render_abbo_page(... promo_disabled, promo_message, promo_error, promo_saved). The rendered card POSTs to /twitch/abbo/promo-settings and /twitch/abbo/promo-message (bot/dashboard/core/abbo_html.py L33).

*Rust-Ist:* ABSENT (searched: grep -rn 'promo-settings|promo_settings|abbo/promo' crates bin → only partner_gate.rs L31-32 allow-list entries; grep for partner-side promo_disabled/promo_message writes in handlers → none; build_billing_page_router lib.rs L775-814 registers no promo route). Reaches a handler only via the Strangler fallback proxy to Python 8765 (main.rs L47-64), which is gated on TB_DASHBOARD_LEGACY_FALLBACK_URL and points at the post-cutover dead Python service.

**Verifikation:** Confirmed in current code. Python serves streamer self-service via abbo_routes.py: the abbo page renders the promo card (L538-546, has_promo_disable_control + _load_abbo_saved_settings) and POST handler abbo_promo_settings (L630-644) writes promo_disabled; a sibling handler writes promo_message; the card POSTs to /twitch/abbo/promo-settings and /twitch/abbo/promo-message (abbo_html.py). On the Rust side build_billing_page_router (lib.rs L775-813) registers NO promo-settings/promo-message route; /twitch/abbo itself is a 301 redirect to /twitch/pricing (abbo_redirect_handler), so the whole self-service abbo page is gone natively. partner_gate.rs L31-32 only allow-lists the two paths but no handler backs them. The only promo writes that exist in Rust are admin-side (admin_promo_mode::set_promo_handler = global config; admin_streamers.rs = admin tooling) — not streamer-facing. A handler is reachable only through the Strangler fallback proxy (tb-dashboard/src/main.rs L50-64), gated on TB_DASHBOARD_LEGACY_FALLBACK_URL; when unset the app 404s these paths, and the proxy targets the post-cutover Python (8765). The data path is otherwise intact: tb-chat/src/promos.rs L909-926 load_streamer_promo_message reads streamer_plans.promo_message and promos.rs L694 honors promo_disabled — so the columns are still read by the chat engine; only the streamer self-service WRITE was dropped. This matches the claim exactly: missing native Rust handler, depends on the dead Python fallback.

**Laufzeit-Check:** With TB_DASHBOARD_LEGACY_FALLBACK_URL unset, POST /twitch/abbo/promo-settings (and /promo-message) against the live Rust dashboard returns 404 (no native route), while an entitled streamer's promo_disabled/promo_message cannot be changed from the dashboard.

**Verifikation:** Confirmed in current code. Python abbo_promo_message (bot/dashboard/abbo_routes.py:703-738) exists exactly as cited: CSRF verify, validate_streamer_promo_message guard with ?promo_error= redirect, UPDATE streamer_plans SET promo_message (empty->NULL) WHERE LOWER(twitch_login)=LOWER(...), no-row warning, ?promo_saved=1 redirect. The validation fn validate_streamer_promo_message IS ported (tb-analytics/promo_mode.rs:297) but has ZERO non-test callers — dead for this path, confirming the guard is unwired. partner_gate.rs:31 even whitelists /twitch/abbo/promo-message as a passive-allowed path, proving the route was intended but never backed by a handler. The seemingly-matching set_promo_handler (admin_promo_mode.rs) is a DIFFERENT feature: admin global promo-MODE singleton (POST /twitch/api/admin/config/promo, table twitch_global_promo_modes, validate_global_promo_mode_config), not the per-streamer editor. Net: streamers cannot set/clear their custom promo message via the live Rust dashboard; the placeholder-validation guard, empty->NULL coercion, and no-row warning are all absent. **Laufzeit-Check:** On the live Rust dashboard, POST to /twitch/abbo/promo-message as a logged-in streamer and observe a 404/no-route instead of a redirect to /twitch/abbo?promo_saved=1; the streamer_plans.promo_message column stays unchanged.

### P1.45 [dashboard:routes_billing] trial/start now requires X-CSRF-Token for partner POSTs (Python had no CSRF) — prod-403 risk

- **Python:** `routes_billing.py:596-619`
- **Rust:** `lib.rs:312-315`
- **Status:** changed

**Befund:** A partner (non-localhost browser) POST to /twitch/api/billing/trial/start without the X-CSRF-Token header is rejected with 403 by the Rust csrf_protect layer, whereas Python accepted it on session auth alone. This is a documented grillme 'CSRF auf allen Write-Actions' hardening, but given the known dashboard_v2 csrfToken:null issue (auth-status returns null token), if the partner pricing frontend does not actually attach a valid token, the trial-start button breaks in prod with 403 while unit/localhost tests pass. Needs verification that the partner UI sends X-CSRF-Token.

*Python-Soll:* routes_billing.py:596-619 — api_billing_trial_start performs only session auth; no CSRF token check anywhere in the handler

*Rust-Ist:* lib.rs:312-315 (route on authed router) + lib.rs:489 (.layer(csrf::csrf_protect)) + auth/csrf.rs:60-78 (write methods from non-localhost require X-CSRF-Token else 403)

**Verifikation:** Confirmed at code level. Python bot/dashboard/routes_billing.py:596-619 (api_billing_trial_start) does session-only auth (extract twitch_user_id/login from session, 401 if missing) with NO CSRF check. Rust route /twitch/api/billing/trial/start (lib.rs:312-315) lives on the authed router that applies .layer(csrf::csrf_protect) (lib.rs:489); csrf.rs:59-105 rejects non-localhost POST/PUT/PATCH/DELETE with 403 csrf_failed unless a valid X-CSRF-Token validates against the session. The divergence is real AND the frontend does not compensate: the trial button in bot/dashboard_v2/src/components/pricing/PricingHero.tsx:10 fires fetch('/twitch/api/billing/trial/start', { method:'POST', credentials:'include' }) with NO X-CSRF-Token header and no global fetch interceptor. By contrast sibling write-actions (TitleGenerator.tsx:108, admin.ts:90, title.ts:46, AdminAffiliates) DO read authStatus.csrfToken and attach the header — proving PricingHero is the outlier. The pricing page is the public partner-facing /twitch/pricing route (non-localhost browser), so the csrf_protect localhost-bypass does not apply. Result: a real partner trial-start POST is 403'd in prod while localhost/unit tests pass. Raised severity to high because this fully breaks the paid-conversion entry point (trial start = top-of-funnel revenue action), not a cosmetic divergence; further aggravated by the known dashboard_v2 csrfToken:null issue (auth-status returns null token) which would null even an attempted header.

**Laufzeit-Check:** From a real (non-localhost) browser logged in as a partner, click '30 Tage kostenlos starten' on /twitch/pricing and watch the network tab / server logs for a 403 csrf_failed on POST /twitch/api/billing/trial/start.

### P1.46 [dashboard:live] remove / add_url / add_login / add_any streamer endpoints unported

- **Python:** `live.py:1680-1726`
- **Rust:** `ABSENT`
- **Status:** missing

**Befund:** Removing a streamer and the alternate add-paths have no native Rust handler; these admin actions 502 against the dead Python dashboard.

*Python-Soll:* live.py:1680-1726 (add_any/add_url/add_login), 2031-2048 (remove); live SPA client.ts:1104 submitLegacyAction('/twitch/remove', ...)

*Rust-Ist:* ABSENT (searched: grep -rniE 'add_any|add_url|add_login|fn remove|/twitch/remove' crates/tb-dashboard-api bin — no routes). Fall to strangler proxy -> dead Python.

**Verifikation:** Confirmed in the real code. The served admin SPA is bot/admin_dashboard/dist (Rust admin_spa::admin_index_handler, DEFAULT_ADMIN_DIST_PATH=bot/admin_dashboard/dist) — the same frontend whose client.ts calls submitLegacyAction to form paths: /twitch/remove (1104), /twitch/add_streamer (1100), /twitch/verify (1108), /twitch/archive (1112/1116), /twitch/discord_link (1120), /twitch/discord_flag (1129), /twitch/reload (1155). In tb-dashboard-api, the merged build_router (lib.rs 822-837) registers NO route for any of these legacy form paths; build_admin_legacy_forms_router wires only /twitch/admin/manual-plan[/clear]. A grep for the exact legacy paths returns zero native routes. They therefore fall through .fallback(legacy_fallback_handler) → strangler proxy → dead Python 8765 → 502. Native handlers for the underlying behavior DO exist but under a different, unreached contract: tb-internal-api has DELETE /streamers/:login (remove_handler) and POST /streamers (add_handler) — a separate internal API host/token; tb-dashboard-api build_admin_streamers_router exposes /twitch/api/admin/streamers/{login}/{verify,archive,block,discord-flag} but has NO add and NO remove route, and the SPA does not call this REST surface anyway. The auditor under-credited those REST handlers, but the user-facing gap is real and in fact broader than 'remove': add_streamer, verify, archive, discord_link and reload are equally stranded. The auditor's specific add_any/add_url/add_login/remove handlers have no native equivalent at all.

**Laufzeit-Check:** Log in to the admin dashboard host and click Remove/Add streamer; observe a 502 (or proxy error) and check the dashboard service log for a strangler-fallback proxy attempt to the dead Python 8765 on POST /twitch/remove.

### P1.47 [dashboard:live_announcement_mixin] Entire Go-Live announcement dashboard (5 routes) is unported — page + read/save/test/preview all return no handler in Rust

- **Python:** `live_announcement_mixin.py:207`

**Befund:** The configurable Go-Live builder UI and its backing API are completely missing from the Rust dashboard. Worse: /twitch/live-announcement is still an allowed post-login redirect target (oauth_login.rs:40), so users can be sent to a 404/dead page after login. This is a dropped user-facing endpoint cluster, the exact migration bug-class (e) we hunt for.

*Python-Soll:* live_announcement_mixin.py:207 live_announcement_page (GET /twitch/live-announcement), :248 api_live_announcement_config (GET), :282 api_live_announcement_save_config (POST), :358 api_live_announcement_test_send (POST), :419 api_live_announcement_preview (GET); registered routes_entry.py:33 + routes_mixin.py:638-641

*Rust-Ist:* ABSENT (searched: grep -rln 'live_announcement|LiveAnnouncement|_la_|live-announcement' crates/tb-dashboard-api bin/tb-dashboard -> 0 hits except oauth_login.rs:40 which is only a redirect-whitelist prefix; handlers/mod.rs has no live/announce module; lib.rs has no live-announcement .route)

**Verifikation:** Verified on both sides. Python registers the full Go-Live builder dashboard: routes_entry.py:33 (GET /twitch/live-announcement -> live_announcement_page) plus routes_mixin.py:638-641 (GET/POST /twitch/api/live-announcement/config, POST .../test, GET .../preview), backed by ~2000 lines in bot/dashboard/live/live_announcement_mixin.py (page render, _la_load/_la_save CRUD, _la_resolve_streamer scope/auth, CSRF, _la_ensure_streamer_ping_role, _la_send_test_dm, _la_preview_payload). In Rust, an exhaustive sweep of crates/tb-dashboard-api and bin/tb-dashboard finds NO live-announcement .route(...) and NO matching handler fn (live_announcement_page / api_live_announcement_*); all 'builder' grep hits are Request::builder()/reqwest noise. The only related Rust file, crates/tb-monitoring/src/announce/dashboard_config.rs, by its own doc comment ports only the pure helpers (default_live_announcement_config, deep_merge, parse_config_json); it has no route handlers, no to_template_config, no save/load/preview/test logic, and is referenced by nothing except mod.rs (pub mod dashboard_config) — dead, unwired scaffolding. Additionally /twitch/live-announcement remains in the OAuth post-login redirect whitelist (oauth_login.rs:40), so a logged-in user can be redirected to a path with no handler. The runtime auto-announcement engine (announce/template.rs, sink.rs, internal-api live_active_announcements_handler) IS ported, so live posting itself still works with the configured/default template — which is why I corrected severity to high (partners/admins lose view/edit/test/preview of their go-live config, not a runtime-down core path) rather than critical.

**Laufzeit-Check:** On the Rust dashboard, request GET /twitch/live-announcement and GET /twitch/api/live-announcement/config while authenticated; a 404/no-route response (vs the Python HTML page / JSON config) confirms the cluster is unported.

### P1.48 [dashboard:?] Config save path: CSRF gate, partner-scope guard, editor-role authorization, validation and disallowed-mention sanitize are all absent

- **Python:** `api_live_announcement_save_config L284-330: _la_csrf_verify -> 403 invalid_csrf; partner-s`
- **Rust:** `ABSENT`
- **Status:** missing

**Befund:** No Rust path persists go-live config, so the entire authorization/validation/anti-@everyone-injection layer that protects this write endpoint is gone. If the route is ever re-added in Rust without re-deriving these guards (CSRF 403, partner self-only scope, editor-role gate, content mention-sanitize on save, validation 400), it would reintroduce permission and Discord-mention-injection risks. Note matches known bug-class (c) csrf_protect on POST routes.

*Python-Soll:* api_live_announcement_save_config L284-330: _la_csrf_verify -> 403 invalid_csrf; partner-scope check L302 -> 403 forbidden_streamer_scope; _validate_config_dict L316 -> 400 validation_failed; _la_can_edit L322 -> 403 editor_role_required; content sanitized via _la_sanitize_disallowed_mentions_text L315

*Rust-Ist:* ABSENT (searched: grep -rn 'forbidden_streamer_scope|editor_role_required|sanitize_disallowed|validate_live_announcement_config' crates/tb-dashboard-api crates/tb-monitoring excl tests -> 0 hits; only render-side _sanitize_live_content exists in template.rs:553)

**Verifikation:** Independently confirmed. Python api_live_announcement_save_config (bot/dashboard/live/live_announcement_mixin.py L282-330) layers five guards on the per-streamer go-live config save: CSRF verify -> 403 invalid_csrf (L285), partner self-scope check -> 403 forbidden_streamer_scope (L302), _la_sanitize_disallowed_mentions_text on cfg.content BEFORE persist (L315), _validate_config_dict -> 400 validation_failed (L316), and _la_can_edit editor-role gate -> 403 editor_role_required (L322). The Rust side has only the READ/consumer half of this feature: tb-monitoring/src/announce/dashboard_config.rs is explicitly documented as a port of just "die reinen Helfer" (default config, deep_merge, parse_config_json); announce/sink.rs:107 only SELECTs config_json. There is NO Rust write handler for twitch_live_announcement_configs in production code — the only INSERTs into that table live in #[cfg(test)] fixture/DDL blocks (tb-analytics/telemetry_routes.rs L188-303, tb-internal-api/telemetry_routes.rs). The lone POST /twitch/api/admin/announcements route (lib.rs:610 -> admin_announcements::save_handler) is a different feature: the global promo-mode editor ported from api_admin.py, not the per-streamer go-live config. grep across crates/bin (excl tests) for editor_role/forbidden_streamer_scope/can_edit/sanitize_disallowed/validate found zero guard logic; only the static "allowed_editor_role_ids": [] default and a table column name appear. Render-side sanitize (template.rs) does not cover the save path. So the entire save endpoint plus its CSRF/partner-scope/editor-role/validation/mention-sanitize layer is genuinely absent in Rust — a real missing-feature regression with a latent permission/@everyone-injection risk if re-added without re-deriving these guards.

**Laufzeit-Check:** Authenticated as a non-admin partner, POST the live-announcement save config to the Rust dashboard for another streamer with @everyone in content and no CSRF token; observe there is no Rust route (404) rather than the Python 403/400 guard chain.

**Befund:** If the Rust dashboard is live (Twitch-Bot Rust cutover), streamers/partners can no longer open the Go-Live announcement builder, edit/save their Discord announcement config, see the live preview, or send a Test-DM. Note the runtime announcement SENDING is ported (tb-monitoring/announce template.rs+sink.rs) and reads twitch_live_announcement_configs, so existing saved configs still fire — but there is no Rust way to create/edit them via the dashboard.

*Python-Soll:* live_announcement_mixin.py:1145-2063 _la_render_page serves GET /twitch/live-announcement (title 'Live Announcement Builder' / 'Discord Announcement Designer'); JS at 1977/1996/2018 calls /twitch/api/live-announcement/preview, /config (POST save), /test (POST). Backing helpers _la_preview_payload (1093-1143) and _la_send_test_dm (973-1091).

*Rust-Ist:* ABSENT. grep -rin 'Discord Announcement Designer|Go-Live Builder|render_page|api/live-announcement' --include=*.rs over rust/ -> zero handler hits. '/twitch/live-announcement' appears only at crates/tb-dashboard-api/src/auth/oauth_login.rs:40 as an OAuth post-login redirect-whitelist prefix (no handler). No route registers the builder page or its 3 API endpoints.

**Verifikation:** Verified independently. Real Python source is bot/dashboard/live/live_announcement_mixin.py (2063 lines; the cited bot/dashboard/live_announcement_mixin.py is a 6-line stub). It serves GET /twitch/live-announcement via _la_render_page (line 1145, titles 'Live Announcement Builder'/'Discord Announcement Designer') and JS calls /twitch/api/live-announcement/preview (1977), /config POST (1996), /test POST (2018), backed by _la_preview_payload (1093) and _la_send_test_dm (973). In Rust, the builder page and all 3 endpoints are ABSENT: grep over crates/**/*.rs for the page titles ('Announcement Designer/Builder', 'go-live-announcements') = zero; no router registers /twitch/live-announcement or /api/live-announcement/{preview,config,test}. The only match for '/twitch/live-announcement' is crates/tb-dashboard-api/src/auth/oauth_login.rs:40, an OAuth redirect-whitelist prefix with no handler — so users land on a dead 404 route. admin_announcements.rs is a separate admin-promo feature; tb-analytics/src/telemetry_routes.rs is live-announcement telemetry (active announcements + link-clicks), not the builder. The runtime SEND path IS ported (tb-monitoring/src/announce/{sink.rs,template.rs,dashboard_config.rs}; dashboard_config.rs explicitly says it ports only the pure helpers) and reads twitch_live_announcement_configs, so pre-existing saved configs still fire — but there is no Rust dashboard way to create/edit/preview/test them. Genuine missing-feature regression.

**Laufzeit-Check:** On the live Rust dashboard, open /twitch/live-announcement (e.g. via the OAuth post-login redirect) and observe a 404/dead page; also confirm GET /twitch/api/live-announcement/preview returns 404.

### P1.51 [dashboard:raid_mixin] Dashboard route GET /twitch/raid/auth (raid_auth_start) not natively ported — relies on legacy Python proxy; 404 if fallback unset

Even with the proxy live, the access-control gate (own-login-only vs admin-token override vs public-onboarding) executes in Python, not Rust — so this Python logic was not migrated.

Implements: session-own-login enforcement (l.209-212 _require_token on mismatch), admin-token override, public website-onboarding branch (l.214-223: BASE_SCOPE_PROFILE + source in {'',website_onboarding} -> login=public:website_onboarding, else HTTPFound to PUBLIC_STREAMER_ONBOARDING_URL), missing-login 400 (l.225-226), 503 not-configured (l.232-233), generate_auth_url, final HTTPFound 302 to Twitch (l.256).

*Rust-Ist:* ABSENT (searched: grep -rn 'raid_auth_start' / '"/twitch/raid/auth"' / 'raid/auth' in crates/tb-dashboard-api + bin/tb-dashboard). '/twitch/raid/auth' appears only as whitelist/prefix strings: oauth_login.rs:39, partner_gate.rs:46,69,168, internal_home.rs:331. No get(...) route registered in lib.rs (route list 650-671) or main.rs. The privileged JSON endpoint tb-internal-api/src/handlers/raid_oauth.rs:422 auth_url_handler is a different subsystem (returns Json{auth_url}, requires AuthLevel::is_privileged, no public-onboarding/session/admin gate, no 302).

**Verifikation:** Independently confirmed in current code. No native GET /twitch/raid/auth route is registered in the Rust dashboard router (crates/tb-dashboard-api/src/lib.rs build_public_router/build_authed_router); the string only appears as auth whitelist/prefix entries (oauth_login.rs:39, partner_gate.rs:46/69/168, internal_home.rs:331) and as the twitch_raid_auth table name. bin/tb-dashboard/src/main.rs:50-63 only registers the fallback proxy when TB_DASHBOARD_LEGACY_FALLBACK_URL is set; proxy.rs:128-134 returns 404 not_found when the proxy is None, so the route 404s without the legacy Python backend. The internal-api GET /raid/auth-url (tb-internal-api/src/lib.rs:176-177 -> raid_oauth.rs:422 auth_url_handler) is a different subsystem: it requires auth.is_privileged() (l.427), returns JSON {auth_url} not a 302 HTTPFound, and lacks the Python access-control gate (session-own-login enforcement, admin-token override, public website_onboarding branch with redirect to PUBLIC_STREAMER_ONBOARDING_URL, 400 missing-login, 503 not-configured) present in bot/dashboard/raids/raid_mixin.py:209-256. So the streamer raid-authorize link and public onboarding OAuth start are not natively ported and depend on the legacy proxy; the Python gate logic was not migrated.

**Laufzeit-Check:** Unset TB_DASHBOARD_LEGACY_FALLBACK_URL, start tb-dashboard, and request GET /twitch/raid/auth?login=earlysalty — observe a 404 not_found JSON instead of a 302 redirect to Twitch's authorize URL.

### P1.52 [dashboard:raid_mixin] Dashboard route GET /twitch/raid/go (raid_auth_go short-redirect for Discord buttons) not natively ported

This endpoint exists specifically because Discord button URLs are capped at 512 chars.

*Rust-Ist:* ABSENT (searched: grep -rn 'raid/go' / 'raid_auth_go' in crates/tb-dashboard-api + bin/tb-dashboard). No route '/twitch/raid/go' registered. tb-internal-api has /internal/twitch/v1/raid/go-url (raid_oauth.rs:576 go_url_handler) which returns Json{auth_url} for privileged internal callers and 404 'state not found or expired' (raid_oauth.rs:603-606) — it is NOT a public 302 redirect and not mounted on the dashboard path /twitch/raid/go.

**Verifikation:** Python raid_auth_go (bot/dashboard/raids/raid_mixin.py:258-293, registered routes_mixin.py:622 web.get("/twitch/raid/go", ...)) is a public, token-less GET that 302-redirects (HTTPFound l.256/293) to the full Twitch OAuth URL, with 400 missing-state (l.268), 503 not-initialized (l.276/282), and a 410 German HTML 'Link abgelaufen oder ungültig' page (l.285-291) — built because Discord button URLs cap at 512 chars. The Rust dashboard (bin/tb-dashboard/src/main.rs:56) installs dashboard_fallback_handler which proxies unmatched paths to the legacy Python upstream, so /twitch/raid/go only works while Python is live; the native 302/410 UX is served by Python, never by Rust, and 404/502s if the proxy is gone. Gap matches the claim.

**Laufzeit-Check:** With the Python legacy upstream stopped, curl -i the Rust dashboard at /twitch/raid/go?state=test and observe a 404/502 instead of a 302 redirect to Twitch or the 410 'Link abgelaufen' German page.

### P1.53 [dashboard:partner_auth_mixin] login_handler sets the wrong cookie (twitch_dash_session instead of twitch_dash_session_partner)

- **Python:** `partner_auth_mixin.py:199-201`
- **Rust:** `handlers/partner_login.rs:203-209`
- **Status:** changed

**Befund:** Python issued a durable partner-ACCESS session/cookie (auth_type=partner_token) after one-time login; the auth-level extractor (auth/level.rs:259-274) only consults twitch_dash_session_partner for the access-session path. Rust instead writes the short-lived OAuth-session cookie name. The session is consumed via load_partner_session (twitch_dash_session) instead of load_partner_access_session, so the binding/UA-fingerprint and the intended 'durable durable-after-link' semantics differ; the cookie the one-time-login flow is designed around is never set. High risk the partner stays logged in via the wrong session class.

*Python-Soll:* partner_auth_mixin.py:199-201 — _create_partner_access_session(request) + _set_partner_access_cookie(...); services.py:141-152 set_partner_access_cookie uses partner_access_cookie_name() = twitch_dash_session_partner (the durable partner-ACCESS cookie).

*Rust-Ist:* handlers/partner_login.rs:203-209 build_session_cookie(PARTNER_COOKIE_NAME,...) where session.rs:221 PARTNER_COOKIE_NAME = "twitch_dash_session" — NOT PARTNER_ACCESS_COOKIE_NAME (session.rs:230 = "twitch_dash_session_partner").

**Verifikation:** Independently confirmed in current code. partner_login.rs (the documented Rust counterpart of the Python one-time partner-login flow auth_partner_link/auth_partner_login) at lines 191-209 calls state.create_partner_session(...) and sets build_session_cookie(PARTNER_COOKIE_NAME, ...). session.rs:221 defines PARTNER_COOKIE_NAME = "twitch_dash_session", and create_partner_session (session.rs:308-338) persists session TYPE "twitch" with NO auth_type and NO fingerprint binding. Python instead (partner_auth_mixin.py:199-201 -> services.py:617 PartnerAccessService.create) issues a DURABLE session with auth_type="partner_token", a UA/platform fingerprint binding, and the cookie partner_access_cookie_name() = "twitch_dash_session_partner". The Rust constants PARTNER_ACCESS_COOKIE_NAME ("twitch_dash_session_partner") and PARTNER_ACCESS_SESSION_TYPE ("partner_token") plus load_partner_access_session (session.rs:882, with UA fingerprint check) DO exist and are wired into the auth-level extractor (level.rs:260-269) on a separate path — but the one-time-login handler bypasses them entirely. So the partner does get logged in, but via the wrong session class: short-lived "twitch" cookie loaded by load_partner_session (type twitch, no fingerprint) instead of the durable partner-ACCESS session the flow is designed around. The intended cookie is never set and the anti-cookie-theft UA-fingerprint binding is dropped. Genuine regression, not a built-differently-but-equivalent design.

**Laufzeit-Check:** Drive the one-time partner login (POST /twitch/auth/partner/login with a valid token) and inspect the Set-Cookie header on the 302 — Rust emits twitch_dash_session; Python emits twitch_dash_session_partner; also check the dashboard_sessions row type (twitch vs partner_token).

### P1.54 [dashboard:services] Partner login redesign: Rust never creates the durable partner-access session (twitch_dash_session_partner) nor its device fingerprint binding

- **Python:** `services.py:617-640`
- **Rust:** `handlers/partner_login.rs:178-209`
- **Status:** changed

**Befund:** The durable, device-bound partner-access session is not implemented. Anti-cookie-theft fingerprint binding (PartnerAccessBinding) is never persisted, so that protection is absent. The login takes a different shape (twitch session instead of partner_token), changing cookie name and lifetime. Functionally a partner can still log in (Rust resolves a partner + issues a twitch session), so it is not a total breakage, but the documented B3-9 durable-access design and its security binding are missing on the write side.

*Python-Soll:* services.py:617-640 PartnerAccessService.create writes a session_type 'partner_token' row with **PartnerAccessBinding.capture(request) (user_agent_family/platform) and sets the twitch_dash_session_partner cookie; partner_auth_mixin.py:199-201 _create_partner_access_session + _set_partner_access_cookie is the ONLY thing auth_partner_login does after consuming the token.

*Rust-Ist:* handlers/partner_login.rs:178-209 login_handler instead calls find_partner_for_login + create_partner_session (session.rs:308-338, session_type 'twitch') and sets PARTNER_COOKIE_NAME 'twitch_dash_session' (SameSite Lax, 6h). grep 'save_partner_access_session|PartnerAccessService|create_partner_access' over crates/tb-dashboard-api + bin/tb-dashboard returns no producer; the partner_token row is never written. load_partner_access_session (session.rs:882) + level.rs:259-274 read it, so the fingerprint-bound read path has no writer (effectively dead).

**Verifikation:** Verified in current code. partner_login.rs login_handler (lines 191-209) creates the session via state.create_partner_session(...) and sets PARTNER_COOKIE_NAME = "twitch_dash_session" (SameSite::Lax, SESSION_CREATE_TTL_SECS = 6h). create_partner_session (session.rs:308-338) persists session_type "twitch" (line 334), NOT "partner_token". A repo-wide grep for save_partner_access_session / create_partner_access / a partner_token writer returns NOTHING except doc comments — there is no producer for the durable partner-access session. Yet the READ path exists: PARTNER_ACCESS_COOKIE_NAME="twitch_dash_session_partner" / PARTNER_ACCESS_SESSION_TYPE="partner_token" (session.rs:230/234), load_partner_access_session (session.rs:882) with RequestFingerprint::capture fingerprint binding, and level.rs:254-274 reads that cookie. So the fingerprint-bound read path is real but has no writer = effectively dead, and PartnerAccessBinding.capture (user_agent_family/platform) is never persisted at login. The anti-cookie-theft device binding from Python (services.py:617-640) is absent on the write side, and the cookie name/lifetime/SameSite differ from the documented B3-9 durable-access design. Login still functions (a partner is resolved and a "twitch" session issued), so it is not a total breakage — but the durable device-bound partner-access session and its security binding are genuinely missing. The claim is accurate.

**Laufzeit-Check:** Complete a partner one-time login against the live Rust dashboard, then inspect Set-Cookie and the dashboard_sessions table: you will see only a twitch_dash_session cookie and a session_type='twitch' row, never twitch_dash_session_partner / session_type='partner_token'.

The only Discord flow ported is the PARTNER discord-LINK flow (handlers/discord_link.rs: /twitch/auth/discord/link/complete), a different endpoint. /callback/discord appears only in partner_gate.rs passive/public lists with no handler.

Python (bot/dashboard/auth/auth_mixin.py:229-230, 263-288) implements shared_discord_auth_callback for GET /callback/discord: it reads state/state_id (400 "Fehlender OAuth-State." + no-store if missing), forwards optional error, and 302-redirects to the admin completion route /twitch/auth/discord/complete via _build_discord_admin_route_url with no-store headers. It is registered as a live route in bot/dashboard/routes_mixin.py:629 (web.get("/callback/discord", self.shared_discord_auth_callback)), alongside the admin /twitch/auth/discord/login (632) and /complete (633). Rust side: build_auth_router (lib.rs:646-654) registers ONLY Twitch routes — /twitch/auth/login, /twitch/auth/callback, /callback/twitch, /twitch/auth/logout. The ONLY ported Discord flow is the PARTNER LINK flow (handlers/discord_link.rs:193-194: /twitch/auth/discord/link and /twitch/auth/discord/link/complete) — a different endpoint and purpose. /callback/discord appears in Rust only in auth/partner_gate.rs:53,87 (passive public/no-auth lists) with NO route handler; /twitch/auth/discord/login and /twitch/auth/discord/complete have no Rust handler at all. The shared admin Discord callback therefore falls through the Strangler proxy to the dead Python upstream (8765) -> 502. The project's own audit (rust/docs/audit/_work/findings-2026-06-15.json:987-995) already LIVE-verified this same admin Discord OAuth chain returns 502 and rated it critical ("kein Rust-Pendant in lib.rs", "vollständiger Verlust des Discord-Admin-OAuth-Logins"). Severity raised from medium to high: this is a verified-live missing route in a chain the project itself rated critical; not full critical only because it is one redirect hop and a localhost/token bypass exists for local admins.
