### P1.56 [dashboard:auth_mixin] Dropped side-effect: valid OAuth login no longer reactivates departnered/archived/paused partners

- **Python:** `bot/dashboard/auth/auth_mixin.py:837-849`
- **Rust:** `ABSENT`
- **Status:** missing

**Befund:** A previously-departnered or admin-archived (or opt-out / token_error-paused) partner who logs into the dashboard via valid Twitch OAuth is admitted (the gate find_partner_for_login only excludes technical_pause_reason='blocked', so archived/departnered rows still match) and receives a working session — but their twitch_partners row is NOT reactivated. In Python the same login self-heals the partner back to active and clears opt-out/token_error/raid-disabled flags, so the bot resumes serving them. In Rust the partner stays inactive: the bot will not treat them as an active partner (raids/announcements/etc. stay off) despite a successful, authenticated login. Silent loss of an auto-recovery path that streamers relied on after re-authenticating.

*Python-Soll:* bot/dashboard/auth/auth_mixin.py:837-849 (_reactivate_partner_after_valid_auth) invoked at auth_mixin.py:1381-1390 in the OAuth callback AFTER the partner gate and BEFORE session creation; implementation bot/storage/partner_registry.py:1355-1401 sets departnered/archived partners back to status='active' (via reactivate_partner) and resets manual_partner_opt_out / raid_bot_enabled / token_error* pauses (blocked & bot_banned are skipped as hard-kills).

*Rust-Ist:* ABSENT (searched: `grep -ri reactivate crates/tb-dashboard-api` -> 0 hits). The Rust callback crates/tb-dashboard-api/src/handlers/auth_login.rs:258-316 calls find_partner_for_login() then create_partner_session() directly with no reactivation step; create_partner_session (session.rs:308-338) only persists a session row.

**Verifikation:** Confirmed in real code. Python: dashboard OAuth callback (auth_mixin.py:1381-1390) calls _reactivate_partner_after_valid_auth AFTER the partner gate and BEFORE session creation, which (partner_registry.py:1355-1401) flips departnered/archived rows back to status='active' and clears manual_partner_opt_out / raid_bot_enabled / token_error pauses (blocked & bot_banned skipped). Rust: the OAuth callback in rust/crates/tb-dashboard-api/src/handlers/auth_login.rs:259-316 goes directly from find_partner_for_login (gate) to create_partner_session with NO reactivation step; the one-time partner_login.rs:179-200 has the same pattern. The gate find_partner_for_login (rust/crates/tb-dashboard-api/src/auth/session.rs:613-635) only excludes technical_pause_reason='blocked' and explicitly ORDER BYs archived/departnered rows, so an inactive partner IS admitted and gets a working session — but the twitch_partners row is never reactivated and the opt-out/token_error/raid flags are never cleared. The Rust reactivation code that does exist (sync_partner_state_after_auth / promote_streamer_to_partner in rust/crates/tb-raid/src/partner_setup.rs) is the RAID/streamer-setup OAuth-followup flow (Python partner_setup_service.py:136 / promote_streamer_to_partner at partner_registry.py:782), a DIFFERENT function; it is only called from complete_setup_for_streamer + tests, never from either dashboard login handler, and tb-dashboard-api imports nothing from partner_setup. The self-heal-on-valid-login path is genuinely absent.

**Laufzeit-Check:** Set a test twitch_partners row to status='departnered' (or manual_partner_opt_out=1), complete a dashboard Twitch OAuth login, then query that row's status/flags: in Rust they stay unchanged despite a successful session+cookie; in Python they flip back to active/cleared.

### P1.57 [dashboard:auth_mixin] Discord-Admin-OAuth-Login-Flow komplett ohne Rust-Pendant (kein Weg, eine master_dash_session zu erzeugen)

- **Python:** `auth_mixin.py:1109-1202`
- **Rust:** `ABSENT`
- **Status:** missing

**Befund:** Die gesamte Discord-Admin-Login-Kette (Code-Tausch -> User-Lookup -> Owner/Guild-Admin/Moderator-Rollen-Autorisierung -> Admin-Cookie) ist nicht portiert. In Rust kann kein Nutzer mehr per Discord-OAuth eine Admin-Session (master_dash_session) erhalten; Admin-Zugang nur noch ueber bestehende DB-Sessions oder _TWITCH_ADMIN_LOGINS-Allowlist. Entspricht dem dokumentierten Admin-Modus-Rollback (MEMORY: project_twitch_admin_mode_rollback), ist aber gegenueber Python ein fehlender Flow inkl. der Owner-Override- und Moderator-Rollen-Autorisierungslogik.

*Python-Soll:* auth_mixin.py:1109-1202 (_exchange_discord_admin_code Discord oauth2/token, _fetch_discord_admin_user /users/@me, _check_discord_admin_membership mit owner_override/guild-administrator-perm/moderator_role-Match/leere-Allowlist->deny) + 933-960 (_set/_clear_discord_admin_cookie mit SHARED_ADMIN_COOKIE_DOMAIN)

*Rust-Ist:* ABSENT (searched: grep -rn 'exchange_discord_admin_code|fetch_discord_admin_user|check_discord_admin_membership|administrator|moderator_role|owner_override|oauth2/token|users/@me' crates/tb-dashboard-api bin/tb-dashboard). create_admin_session existiert (session.rs:346) hat aber 0 Caller; master_dash_session wird nur VALIDIERT (level.rs:236, forward_auth.rs), nie via Discord-Login ERZEUGT.

**Verifikation:** Verified. Python auth_mixin.py:1109-1202 implements the full Discord-admin OAuth chain (_exchange_discord_admin_code -> oauth2/token, _fetch_discord_admin_user -> /users/@me, _check_discord_admin_membership with owner_override / guild administrator-perm / moderator_role match / empty-allowlist->deny) plus _set/_clear_discord_admin_cookie (933-960), wired via shared_discord_auth_callback/discord_auth_login to mint the master_dash_session admin cookie. In Rust: create_admin_session (rust/crates/tb-dashboard-api/src/auth/session.rs:346) exists but has ZERO callers in the entire tree (sole grep hit is its own definition; no test even reaches it). auth_login.rs (the Twitch login/callback + shared_callback_handler for /callback/twitch) has no discord/membership/administrator/moderator_role/owner_override/create_admin_session code — it only does Twitch partner login + raid-OAuth delegation. discord_link.rs is the streamer's own Discord-profile-link flow (calls set_discord_profile), not admin authorization. All oauth2/token references are Twitch (id.twitch.tv); there is no Discord token-exchange, no /users/@me, no Discord admin authorize/callback route. master_dash_session is only validated (level.rs:236, forward_auth) and loaded, never created via Discord login. So no Rust path can produce an admin session via Discord OAuth; the owner-override and moderator-role authorization logic is absent. Severity kept high (missing feature, but mitigated by the documented Admin-Modus rollback and by admin access still being reachable via existing DB sessions / allowlist bypass, so not a silent break of an actively-used prod path).

**Laufzeit-Check:** On the admin dashboard host, attempt the Discord-admin login: there is no authorize/callback route that exchanges a Discord code and sets the master_dash_session cookie, so no fresh admin session can be obtained via Discord OAuth.

## P2 — Verhaltensdrift / Teilverlust  (145)

### P2.1 [chat:service_pitch_warning] Alle 25 TWITCH_SERVICE_WARNING_* Tuning-Env-Variablen werden in Rust nicht gelesen — Laufzeit-Konfiguration auf Defaults eingefroren

- **Python:** `service_pitch_warning.py:14-139`
- **Rust:** `ABSENT`
- **Status:** missing

**Befund:** Jede produktive Anpassung dieser Detektor-Tuning-Parameter via Umgebung (z.B. Schwellen lockern/verschaerfen, Cooldowns, Cache-TTLs, Fenstergroessen) wird in Rust still ignoriert. Wenn das Deployment irgendeine dieser Vars gesetzt hat, weicht das Live-Verhalten von der Python-Baseline ab (z.B. anderer MIN_SCORE/PUBLIC_THRESHOLD -> andere Warn/Timeout-Rate). Da die Detektor-Defaults vermutlich produktiv unveraendert liefen, medium statt high; aber die Tuning-Faehigkeit ist verloren und der Threshold-Ordering-Schutz (Z.141-144) ist damit ebenfalls gegenstandslos.

*Python-Soll:* service_pitch_warning.py:14-139 — _env_int() liest 25 Env-Vars (TWITCH_SERVICE_WARNING_ACCOUNT_MAX_DAYS, _MAX_FOLLOWERS, _WINDOW_SEC, _MIN_SCORE, _MIN_MESSAGES, _LIGHT/_PUBLIC/_STRONG_THRESHOLD, _CHANNEL/_USER/_HINT_COOLDOWN_SEC, _ACCOUNT/_FOLLOWER_CACHE_TTL_SEC, _FIRST_CHAT_WINDOW_SEC, _SEQUENCE_WINDOW_SEC, _SEQUENCE_MIN_MSGS, _SHORT_MSG_MAX_CHARS, _OBSERVED_MSG_CACHE_TTL_SEC, _MESSAGE_HISTORY/_ACTIVITY_BUCKET/_TRACKED_USER_STATE/_CHANNEL_STATE_MAXLEN, _STATE_PRUNE_INTERVAL_SEC) mit max(minimum, parsed)-Clamp. Lines 141-144 clampen zusaetzlich die Threshold-Ordnung.

*Rust-Ist:* ABSENT (searched: grep -n 'env::var|std::env|TWITCH_SERVICE_WARNING|env_int|from_env' scam_pitch.rs). Treffer nur Doc-Kommentare Z.37-120 und unrelated MINIMAX/LOG_DIR Env-Vars. Alle 25 Werte sind hartkodierte const (scam_pitch.rs:39-120) ausschliesslich auf den Python-Defaults.

**Verifikation:** Python service_pitch_warning.py:14-144 reads 25 TWITCH_SERVICE_WARNING_* env vars at module load via _env_int (with max(minimum, parsed) clamp) plus threshold-ordering enforcement (Z.141-144). Rust scam_pitch.rs:39-120 hardcodes all 25 as `const` (values equal Python defaults), and the doc comments only NAME the env vars. Grep across the entire tb-chat crate shows the only runtime env reads relevant to this module are TWITCH_SERVICE_WARNING_LOG_DIR (scam_pitch.rs:1382) and MINIMAX_* API keys — none of the 25 tuning vars are read anywhere. So Rust genuinely ignores all runtime tuning, and the threshold-ordering safeguard is moot since values are fixed at compile time. Severity medium is correct: defaults are identical to Python, so live behavior diverges ONLY if the deployment actually sets one of these vars; it is a lost configurability capability rather than a guaranteed behavior change.

**Laufzeit-Check:** Set e.g. TWITCH_SERVICE_WARNING_MIN_SCORE=99 in the bot's environment and confirm the Rust detector's trigger rate is unchanged (the var is ignored), while the Python build would suppress nearly all warnings.

### P2.2 [chat:service_pitch_warning] Escalation timeout chat message is built but never sent (silent timeout)

- **Python:** `service_pitch_warning.py:954-960`
- **Rust:** `scam_pitch.rs:1015-1041`
- **Status:** missing

**Befund:** On a repeat scam-pitch that triggers escalation, the user is timed out 10 minutes but the chat receives no warning/explanation. Python posted a visible mod message naming the timeout and recommending a ban; Rust executes the timeout silently (only an internal Discord alert fires). Moderators/community lose the public signal.

*Python-Soll:* service_pitch_warning.py:954-960 — on STRONG re-trigger during user cooldown, after the 600s timeout it builds escalation_text ("🛡️ @{login} Timeout (10m) wegen wiederholter Service-Pitches/Spam. Empfehlung: User bannen.") and sends it: `await self._send_chat_message(channel, escalation_text, source=...)`.

*Rust-Ist:* scam_pitch.rs:1015-1041 builds escalation_text and returns PitchDecision::StrongTimeout{ text, duration:600s }; pipeline.rs:486 `PitchDecision::StrongTimeout { .. } =>` destructures the text away and only calls api.timeout_user(...) + alerter.send(...). No api.send_message of the escalation text anywhere (grep send_message in pipeline.rs shows none in the StrongTimeout arm).

**Verifikation:** Confirmed in current code. Python (bot/chat/service_pitch_warning.py:944-960) times out 600s and then calls await self._send_chat_message(channel, escalation_text, ...) posting the visible "🛡️ Timeout (10m) ... Empfehlung: User bannen." mod message. Rust scam_pitch.rs:1015-1041 builds the identical escalation_text but in the 'escalate' branch only calls log_warning (internal logging, not a chat send) and returns PitchDecision::StrongTimeout { text, duration: 600s }. Crucially, the normal warn branch sends via self.api.send_message (line 1054), but the escalation branch never does. In pipeline.rs:486-512 the StrongTimeout { .. } arm destructures the text away and calls only api.timeout_user(...) + alerter.send(...) (internal Discord alert). No api.send_message of the escalation text exists in that arm. The text therefore flows from build -> PitchDecision.text -> dropped, and the chat receives nothing. The comment at pipeline.rs:480 ("Detektor sendet Chat-Warnung intern") holds only for the non-escalation warns, not for StrongTimeout. The timeout enforcement and internal alert still fire, so moderation function is intact; only the public chat signal is lost — medium is correct.

**Laufzeit-Check:** On a partner channel, trigger a repeat STRONG service-pitch during the user-cooldown window to force escalation, then observe the user gets a 10-min timeout but no escalation message appears in chat (only a Discord scam_pitch_timeout alert).

### P2.3 [chat:moderation] Outbound manual_partner_opt_out-Guard im Chat-Sendepfad nicht portiert

- **Python:** `moderation.py:1286-1308`
- **Rust:** `ABSENT`
- **Status:** missing

**Befund:** Partner, die manuell aus ausgehenden Bot-Chats raus wollten, könnten im Rust-Sendepfad weiterhin Promo-/Recruitment-Nachrichten erhalten. Impact abhängig davon, ob ein anderes vorgeschaltetes Gate (Partner-Roster/Active-Check) den Opt-out faktisch bereits abfängt — daher medium statt high.

*Python-Soll:* moderation.py:1286-1308 _is_manual_partner_opt_out_for_chat (load_active_partner → manual_partner_opt_out bzw. row[12]) wird im Sendepfad (1430) als Abbruchbedingung genutzt: Partner mit manuellem Opt-out bekommen keine ausgehenden Bot-Nachrichten.

*Rust-Ist:* ABSENT (searched: grep -rn 'is_manual_partner_opt_out' crates/ → 0 Treffer; manual_partner_opt_out wird nur in tb-analytics/partner_access.rs:178 und tb-raid/partner_setup.rs:159 als Spalte gelesen, nicht als Outbound-Chat-Opt-out-Guard).

**Verifikation:** Python: bot/chat/moderation.py:1286 `_is_manual_partner_opt_out_for_chat` (load_active_partner → manual_partner_opt_out / row[12]) is invoked as the FIRST early-`return False` in BOTH `_send_announcement` (line 1339) and `_send_chat_message` (line 1430). These two functions are the universal outbound funnel (called from 49 sites), so a manually opted-out partner receives no outbound bot messages of any kind. In Rust, the equivalent universal outbound funnel is the `ChatApi` trait (`send_message`/`send_announcement` in tb-chat/api.rs) which has NO opt-out guard. The only outbound gating I found is in the promo path: `send_promo_message`/`send_timeout_pitch` (promos.rs:784/825/788) check only `OutboundSuppressionStore::is_muted` (the `twitch_outbound_chat_suppressions` table = timeout-driven mutes, a different mechanism) and `promo_blocked_by_plan_or_flag` (which reads `streamer_plans.promo_disabled` + plan entitlements, NOT `twitch_partners.manual_partner_opt_out`). The promo channel roster gate `is_partner_channel_for_chat_tracking` resolves via `twitch_streamers_partner_state.is_partner_active` (channel_classifier.rs:117); I found no code that derives/flips `is_partner_active` from `manual_partner_opt_out`, so opt-out is not provably enforced even there. Every other `manual_partner_opt_out` read in Rust (partner_access.rs:178/191/202, internal_home.rs:610, admin_streamers.rs:89, partner_setup.rs:652) is status reporting / admin display / role configuration, never an outbound-send skip guard. The send-path opt-out guard is genuinely not ported.

**Laufzeit-Check:** Set manual_partner_opt_out=1 for a test partner in twitch_partners, trigger an outbound bot message (promo/announcement/recruitment) to that channel, and observe whether the message is still delivered; compare against the Python bot which logs "Outbound-... uebersprungen ... Partner-Opt-out".

### P2.4 [chat:moderation] Fehlgeschlagene Announcement fällt nicht auf Plain-Chat zurück (Promo-Text geht verloren)

- **Python:** `moderation.py:1372,1381,1404-1417,1423,1425`
- **Rust:** `tb-transport-twitch/src/chat.rs:238`
- **Status:** changed

**Befund:** Wenn der Announcement-Endpoint aus anderem Grund als Opt-out/Suppression fehlschlägt (fehlender moderator:manage:announcements-Scope, transienter 5xx, 403), liefert Python die Werbe-/Pitch-Nachricht weiterhin als normale Chat-Nachricht aus; Rust sendet gar nichts. Insbesondere bei dauerhaft fehlendem Announcement-Scope erhält der Kanal in Python trotzdem den Text, in Rust nie. Promo-Cooldown wird in beiden Fällen nicht verbraucht, daher Retry — aber solange der Scope fehlt, bleibt der Kanal in Rust werbefrei.

*Python-Soll:* moderation.py:1372,1381,1404-1417,1423,1425 — _send_announcement liefert bei JEDEM Nicht-2xx (inkl. 401 'missing scope', 5xx, Exception) NICHT False, sondern ruft als Fallback _send_chat_message(channel,text,source) und liefert dessen Ergebnis; False nur bei Opt-out/Suppression.

*Rust-Ist:* tb-transport-twitch/src/chat.rs:238 (send_announcement: Ok(matches!(status,200|204)) → bei Fehler Ok(false)); tb-chat/src/promos.rs:798-802 und 833-836 (sent=...unwrap_or(false); if !sent → return false, KEIN Plain-Chat-Fallback).

**Verifikation:** Python _send_announcement (bot/chat/moderation.py:1372,1381,1417,1423,1425) falls back to _send_chat_message on EVERY non-2xx/exception (incl. 401 missing-scope, 5xx, 403, transport errors), returning that result; it returns False only on Partner-opt-out (1344) or suppression (1349). The Rust counterpart has NO plain-chat fallback at any layer: the wrapper HelixChatClient::send_announcement (tb-chat/src/moderation.rs:145-176) returns Ok(false) or Err on failure, and all three promo callers (tb-chat/src/promos.rs:798-803 send_promo_message, 829-837 send_timeout_pitch, 1473-1480 targeted global preset) treat a false/failed result as 'return false' with only a debug log — none invokes self.api.send_message(...) to deliver the promo text as a normal chat message. The send_message method exists on the same ChatApi trait, so the fallback path is available but unused. tb-transport-twitch/src/chat.rs:238 confirms send_announcement yields Ok(false) on any non-200/204. Net: when the announcement endpoint fails for a reason other than opt-out/suppression (notably a permanently missing moderator:manage:announcements scope), Python still delivers the promo/pitch text to the channel via plain chat; Rust delivers nothing. Severity stays medium: the lost output is a promotional/pitch message (not a moderation/safety action); degraded promo reach when the announcement scope is absent or on transient errors, not a core-function break.</reason>
<parameter name="verify_hint">Auf einem Test-Kanal das moderator:manage:announcements-Scope des Bot-Tokens entfernen, eine Promo/Werbefrei-Pitch auslösen und beobachten: in Python erscheint der Werbetext als normale Chat-Nachricht, in Rust kommt nichts im Chat an (nur Debug-Log 'Promo-Announcement nicht gesendet').

### P2.5 [chat:moderation] Auto-Ban-Notice und Announcements umgehen Partner-Opt-out / Outbound-Suppression im generischen Sende-Pfad

- **Python:** `moderation.py:1339-1349`
- **Rust:** `tb-chat/src/moderation.rs:112-141`
- **Status:** changed

**Befund:** In Rust wird die Outbound-Suppression/das Partner-Opt-out nur im Promo-Pfad respektiert. Andere ausgehende Nachrichten — namentlich die Auto-Ban-Chat-Notice ('🛡️ Auto-Mod: … gebannt') — werden auch dann in den Kanal geschrieben, wenn der Streamer Outbound-Chat per Opt-out abgeschaltet hat oder eine aktive Suppression vorliegt. Python unterdrückt diese Notice in beiden Fällen. silent_ban deckt nur das explizite Silent-Flag ab, nicht Opt-out/Suppression.

*Python-Soll:* moderation.py:1339-1349 (_send_announcement) und 1430-1440 (_send_chat_message) prüfen GANZ OBEN _is_manual_partner_opt_out_for_chat(channel) und _get_outbound_chat_suppression(channel,source) und brechen mit return False ab — gilt für ALLE Sender, inkl. der Auto-Ban-Notice (Z.1796 ruft _send_chat_message) und des Announcement-Fallbacks.

*Rust-Ist:* tb-chat/src/moderation.rs:112-141 (HelixChatClient::send_message — kein Opt-out/Suppression-Check) und 145-176 (send_announcement — kein Check); Auto-Ban-Notice ruft engine→api.send_message direkt (moderation.rs:470). Suppression wird NUR im Promo-Pfad geprüft (promos.rs:784,825 is_muted).

**Verifikation:** Verified in current code. Python: bot/chat/moderation.py:1339-1349 (_send_announcement) and 1430-1440 (_send_chat_message) both check _is_manual_partner_opt_out_for_chat(channel) and _get_outbound_chat_suppression(channel,source) at the very top and return False — and these are the universal outbound funnel, so the auto-ban notice at line 1796 (which calls _send_chat_message) is suppressed on opt-out OR suppression; only `silent` (line 1795) is a separate gate. Rust: HelixChatClient::send_message (moderation.rs:112-141) and send_announcement (145-176) are thin Helix wrappers with NO opt-out/suppression check. The auto-ban notice is sent via self.api.send_message(broadcaster_id,&notice) at moderation.rs:470, gated only by `!silent` (pipeline.rs:723 is_silent_ban). Outbound suppression (OutboundSuppressionStore::is_muted) is consulted ONLY in the promo path (promos.rs:784,825). manual_partner_opt_out is persisted in tb-internal-api (streamer_lifecycle.rs) but never consumed by any tb-chat outbound send. The TimeoutTrackingChatApi decorator only instruments timeout-tracking, not suppression. So in Rust the auto-ban notice — and in fact all non-promo outbound (fun_responses.rs:120, commands.rs, scam_pitch.rs:1055) — bypass the partner opt-out and outbound suppression that Python enforces universally. Genuine behavioral regression. Severity medium confirmed: real and user-visible, but only fires when opt-out/suppression is actively set.

**Laufzeit-Check:** Set manual_partner_opt_out=1 (or seed an active row in twitch_outbound_chat_suppressions) for a test channel, trigger a spam auto-ban with silent_ban=0, and observe whether the "🛡️ Auto-Mod ... gebannt" notice still appears in that channel's chat (Rust sends it; Python does not).

### P2.6 [chat:irc_lurker_tracker] Timestamp columns written as DateTime<Utc> (timestamptz) but real schema columns are text -> insert/bind mismatch

- **Python:** `bot/chat/irc_lurker_tracker.py:304`
- **Rust:** `tb-monitoring/src/irc_lurker.rs:108,116-123,159-160`
- **Status:** changed

**Befund:** Against the production baseline schema the INSERT/UPDATE would fail (or coerce wrongly): Python stored ISO text strings, Rust would attempt a timestamptz bind into a text column. The crate's own test schema (irc_lurker.rs:454-455) declares these columns TIMESTAMPTZ, so unit tests pass while prod schema is text -> fixtures lie against baseline. Feature is currently never spawned, so no live breakage today, but it is a latent prod-break if ever enabled.

*Python-Soll:* bot/chat/irc_lurker_tracker.py:304 now_iso = datetime.now(UTC).isoformat(timespec='seconds'); bound into first_message_at/last_seen_at at 340,345,357,399,403 (an ISO STRING).

*Rust-Ist:* tb-monitoring/src/irc_lurker.rs:108,116-123,159-160 bind `now: DateTime<Utc>` into first_message_at/last_seen_at; real schema migrations/20260601000000_baseline_schema.sql:1405 first_message_at text NOT NULL, :1409 last_seen_at text. sqlx encodes DateTime<Utc> as timestamptz, not text.

**Verifikation:** Independently verified in current code. Rust rust/crates/tb-monitoring/src/irc_lurker.rs binds `now: DateTime<Utc>` into first_message_at/last_seen_at (INSERT_CHATTER $4 at line 104 used by upsert_chatter_seen line 123 and upsert_names_batch lines 175; UPDATE last_seen_at = $1 at line 159 bound line 162). sqlx encodes DateTime<Utc> as Postgres timestamptz. The production baseline schema (migrations/20260601000000_baseline_schema.sql) declares first_message_at text NOT NULL (1405) and last_seen_at text (1409). Postgres rejects a binary timestamptz bind into a text column (no implicit cast on INSERT/UPDATE values) -> the statement would error at runtime. The crate's own test schema (lines 454-455) declares both columns TIMESTAMPTZ, so unit tests pass against a schema that disagrees with prod baseline -> fixtures lie. Python stored ISO strings (line 304 isoformat) into the same text columns, which is correct. So the Rust port is a genuine data-type regression vs both Python behavior and the prod schema. Severity lowered from high to medium: the tracker is provably never spawned (doc lines 184-191 mirror Python experimental_irc_lurker_enabled=False); there is no runtime path to trigger it without a code change, so it is a latent break with zero current exposure, not an active prod failure.

**Laufzeit-Check:** Against the prod baseline schema, call upsert_names_batch with a live session row and observe Postgres error 42804 (column is of type text but expression is of type timestamp with time zone), or diff the column types: test schema TIMESTAMPTZ vs baseline text.

### P2.7 [chat:irc_lurker_tracker] active_session_id decoded/bound as i64 but real column is integer (int4)

- **Python:** `bot/chat/irc_lurker_tracker.py:309-318,382-390`
- **Rust:** `tb-monitoring/src/irc_lurker.rs:90`
- **Status:** changed

**Befund:** sqlx will reject decoding an int4 column into Rust i64 (mismatched types: i64 == bigint, column == int4), and the i64 bind to the int4 session_id columns is likewise a mismatch. The test schema (irc_lurker.rs:450,453) uses BIGINT so tests pass, masking the int4/bool fidelity bug class. Latent prod-break if the experimental tracker is enabled.

*Python-Soll:* bot/chat/irc_lurker_tracker.py:309-318,382-390 read active_session_id (Python int, untyped) and reuse as session_id.

*Rust-Ist:* tb-monitoring/src/irc_lurker.rs:90 `let row: Option<(Option<i64>,)>` for active_session_id, then bound as i64 to session_id (lines 120,143,163,172). Real schema: migrations/20260601000000_baseline_schema.sql:1020 active_session_id integer, :1401 session_id integer NOT NULL — both int4, not bigint.

**Verifikation:** Independently verified in the real code. Prod schema: twitch_live_state.active_session_id integer (baseline_schema.sql:1020) and twitch_session_chatters.session_id integer NOT NULL (:1401) — both int4. Rust irc_lurker.rs:90 decodes active_session_id into Option<i64> and binds i64 to session_id at lines 120/143/163/172. In sqlx-postgres i64 maps to int8/BIGINT, so decoding an int4 column into i64 AND binding i64 to int4 params both raise sqlx type-mismatch errors at runtime — the mismatch is genuine. The test harness (lines 450,453) creates these columns as BIGINT, so tests pass and never exercise the int4 path, masking the bug. HOWEVER this is latent-only: the tracker is intentionally never spawned. lib.rs has only `pub mod irc_lurker;`, no IrcLurkerTracker instantiation exists in tb-bot or any bin, and the in-code comment (lines 186-191) documents that the feature is built but deliberately not started for 1:1 parity with Python (experimental_irc_lurker_enabled=False). So it cannot break prod in its current wired state. Real bug, real fidelity-class mismatch, but dormant → severity downgraded from high to medium.

**Laufzeit-Check:** Against a freshly-migrated prod-schema DB (int4 columns), set TB_TEST_DATABASE_URL and run the tracker's upsert path (or change the test schema columns to INTEGER): sqlx returns a mismatched-types/decode error for i64 vs int4.

### P2.8 [chat:targeted_promo] MiniMax-Preset-Auswahl in Produktion nie verdrahtet — Rust wählt immer zufällig

- **Python:** `targeted_promo.py:91-145`
- **Rust:** `promos.rs:236-246`
- **Status:** missing

**Befund:** Die kontextbasierte Preset-Auswahl (passt das Preset zum Inhalt der letzten User-Nachrichten) fehlt komplett. In Produktion wird IMMER ein zufälliges Preset gesendet — die User-Snippets werden zwar aus der DB geladen (load_user_context_snippets), aber ungenutzt verworfen. Funktional sendet die Promo weiter (kein Crash), aber das beworbene 'intelligente' Targeting ist nicht aktiv. Wenn das eine bewusste Vereinfachung war, ist es ein Feature-Drop; sonst eine fehlende Verdrahtung.

*Python-Soll:* targeted_promo.py:91-145 _pick_preset_with_minimax: bei >1 Preset und vorhandenen user_snippets baut es einen Prompt (System: 'Du wählst das passendste Discord-Einladungs-Preset...'), ruft EngagementMinimaxClient.generate mit 5s-Timeout, matcht die Antwort via PRESET_MAP.get(chosen_id) + Partial-Match-Loop (p.id in chosen_id); nur bei Fehler/Timeout/unbekannter ID -> secrets.choice(presets). Das ist laut Modul-Docstring (Z.4,7) der Kernzweck des Moduls.

*Rust-Ist:* promos.rs:236-246 definiert nur das PresetPicker-Trait. Einzige Impl ist RandomPresetPicker (promos.rs:347-360), die snippets/login ignoriert und rein zufällig wählt. set_preset_picker (promos.rs:541) wird NIRGENDS aufgerufen: Produktions-Wiring chat_wiring.rs:516-524 ruft nur set_invite_resolver + set_partner_check, der PromoEngine::new-Default (promos.rs:521) bleibt RandomPresetPicker. Keine MiniMax-PresetPicker-Impl existiert im gesamten rust-Baum (grep 'impl PresetPicker' -> nur promos.rs).

**Verifikation:** Independently confirmed. Python targeted_promo.py:91-145 _pick_preset_with_minimax does context-aware preset selection via EngagementMinimaxClient (5s timeout, PRESET_MAP + partial-match, secrets.choice fallback) — the module's stated core purpose per the docstring (lines 4,7). In the rust tree the only `impl PresetPicker` is RandomPresetPicker (promos.rs:347-360), which explicitly ignores `_snippets`/`_target_login` and picks `presets.choose(rng)`. grep across the whole tree (excluding /target) shows NO MinimaxPresetPicker / no second impl. Production wiring at chat_wiring.rs:516-523 builds `PromoEngine::new(...).set_invite_resolver(...).set_partner_check(...)` and never calls set_preset_picker, so the PromoEngine::new default (promos.rs:521 Arc::new(RandomPresetPicker)) remains live. The snippets ARE still loaded and passed (promos.rs:1424 load_user_context_snippets -> 1428 pick_preset) but discarded by the random picker. So in production preset selection is always random; the intelligent targeting is genuinely absent. Severity corrected high->medium: no crash/data-loss/security; promos still send a valid curated preset, only the 'smart' content-matching is missing — a quality degradation, not a functional break.</reason>
<parameter name="verify_hint">In prod over many targeted user-promos, observe that the chosen preset shows no correlation with the target user's recent chat content and that no MiniMax preset-selection call is logged/emitted.

### P2.9 [chat:targeted_promo] Targeted-Promo-Sendepfad umgeht Outbound-Promo-Suppression (Mute-Guard) und Partner-Opt-out

- **Python:** `targeted_promo.py:259-262`
- **Rust:** `promos.rs:782-786`
- **Status:** changed

**Befund:** Auf einem Kanal, der gerade Promo-stummgeschaltet ist (z.B. Bot-Timeout/Mute-Guard aktiv oder twitch_outbound_chat_suppressions Quelle 'promo') unterdrückt Python die Targeted-Promo; Rust sendet sie trotzdem. Folge: Promo-Nachrichten/Announcements gehen in Kanäle raus, die explizit (temporär) keine Promos erhalten sollen. Zusätzlich fehlt der manual_partner_opt_out-Skip für diesen Pfad (teilweise vom Partner-Check am Aufrufer abgedeckt, aber das ist ein anderes Gate).

*Python-Soll:* targeted_promo.py:259-262 sendet via _send_announcement(...,source='promo') bzw. _send_chat_message(...,source='promo'). Beide Methoden (moderation.py:1339-1349 / 1430-1440) prüfen ZUERST _is_manual_partner_opt_out_for_chat(channel) und _get_outbound_chat_suppression(channel,'promo'); bei aktiver Promo-Suppression/Mute-Guard wird return False (nicht gesendet). Der Aufrufer (promos.py:1561) prüft selbst KEINE Suppression — die Mute-Unterdrückung der Targeted-Promo hängt ausschließlich an source='promo' in den Sendemethoden.

*Rust-Ist:* promos.rs:maybe_send_targeted_promo:1441-1446 ruft self.api.send_message(channel_id,&text) und :1473-1480 self.api.send_announcement(channel_id,&text,'purple') — ohne vorherigen self.suppression.is_muted(login)-Check. Der reguläre Pfad send_promo_message (promos.rs:782-786) prüft is_muted, der Targeted-Pfad NICHT. Der Aufrufer send_promo_if_due (promos.rs:721-728) ruft maybe_send_targeted_promo ohne Suppression-Gate (nur overall/activity/stream_start + Partner-Check :696). Transport send_announcement (tb-transport-twitch/src/chat.rs:214-239) ist roher Helix-POST ohne Suppression.

**Verifikation:** Real divergence confirmed in current code. Python targeted_promo.py:259-262 sends with source="promo" through _send_announcement/_send_chat_message (moderation.py:1339-1349/1430-1440), both of which gate FIRST on _is_manual_partner_opt_out_for_chat and _get_outbound_chat_suppression(channel,"promo") and return False when suppressed. In Rust, maybe_send_targeted_promo (promos.rs:1396-1491) calls self.api.send_message (1442) and self.api.send_announcement (1475) directly — grep over the entire function body returns NO suppression/is_muted/opt_out reference. The production ChatApi impl HelixChatClient (moderation.rs:112-176) is a raw Helix POST with no suppression, so suppression is the caller's responsibility. The regular promo path send_promo_message (promos.rs:784) DOES call self.suppression.is_muted(login); the targeted path does not — an asymmetry inside the same file. CombinedSuppression.is_muted (timeout_tracking.rs:304-310) covers exactly the claimed sources (mute-guard OR DB twitch_outbound_chat_suppressions source 'promo'). The caller send_promo_if_due (promos.rs:686-728) only gates on plan-flag, chat-tracking partner status, overall/activity-ready and stream-start-delay — no suppression gate. Net: on a promo-muted channel Rust sends the targeted promo while Python suppresses it. The manual_partner_opt_out portion is partially (but not equivalently) covered by the caller's is_partner_channel_for_chat_tracking gate, but the suppression/mute-guard gap alone is sufficient and unambiguous. Severity medium is appropriate: bounded blast radius (only the targeted-promo slot, only while a temporary mute/suppression is active), but it does push outbound messages into channels explicitly suppressed.

**Laufzeit-Check:** Set a 'promo' row in twitch_outbound_chat_suppressions (or trip the timeout mute-guard) for a live partner channel, then wait for the targeted-promo slot and observe whether an announcement/message still reaches that channel's chat.

### P2.10 [chat:connection] Stale/removed-channel local-state purge on 403 not ported

- **Python:** `bot/chat/connection.py:1498-1525`
- **Rust:** `ABSENT`
- **Status:** missing

**Befund:** Removed/untracked channels that 403 are no longer self-cleaned from local state at runtime. Leads to stale leftover state and lost self-healing cleanup; severity limited because such channels are unsubscribed/inactive anyway.

*Python-Soll:* bot/chat/connection.py:1498-1525 — when a 403 occurs for a channel that is not in twitch_streamers AND not partner-active AND has no raid-auth, join() records stale_removed_channel and calls self._purge_local_channel_state(normalized_login) to clean up local chat state, then returns False.

*Rust-Ist:* ABSENT (searched: grep -rn 'purge_local_channel_state|stale_removed_channel|purge.*channel.*state' crates/ → no hits). Rust only inserts into perm_failed (rust/crates/tb-monitoring/src/subscriptions.rs:786-789); stale channels accumulate in the in-memory perm_failed set until restart with no cleanup of local channel state.

**Verifikation:** Independently confirmed. Python connection.py:1498-1525 records a stale_removed_channel decision and calls _purge_local_channel_state() (defined at :707-732), which evicts the channel from six in-memory collections including _channel_subscription_state. In Rust, the SubscriptionManager keeps the corresponding in-memory collections perm_failed (subscriptions.rs:224) and subscription_state (:228, the explicit port of _channel_subscription_state). On 403 Rust only INSERTS into perm_failed (:786-789, :406-408) and never records a stale-removed decision or purges. Exhaustive grep shows NO .remove/.clear/.retain/discard against perm_failed or subscription_state anywhere in crates/ — they only grow until restart (rehydrate clears only `tracked`). A cleanup_stale() method exists (port of _cleanup_old_eventsub_subscriptions) but it (a) prunes only the `tracked` set, not perm_failed/subscription_state, and (b) is called solely from tests/subscriptions.rs:161, never from production. The production scout removal path (delete_monitored_streamer / part_channels) is wired to NoopScoutChatSink, so it performs no in-memory subscription-state cleanup either. The runtime self-healing purge for stale/removed channels that 403 is genuinely not ported; such entries accumulate until restart. Severity medium: the growth is bounded (one tuple per removed channel/sub-type, cleared on restart) and only affects already-inactive channels, but the self-healing cleanup semantics are truly lost.

**Laufzeit-Check:** At runtime, have a tracked channel removed/de-authorized so it returns 403 on chat subscribe, then inspect the bot's tracked_pairs / capacity used_slots and EventSub diagnostics over time — the stale channel's perm_failed entry persists and never clears without a process restart.

### P2.11 [chat:connection] join_partner_channels: Rust-Reconcile dropt raid-enabled-only Kanäle (OR→AND-Verengung der Partner-Auswahl)

- **Python:** `connection.py:2050-2051`
- **Rust:** `rust/bin/tb-bot/src/chat_wiring.rs:698-701`
- **Status:** changed

**Befund:** Kanäle mit raid_enabled=TRUE, channel:bot-Scope, aber is_partner_active != 1 erhalten in Rust KEINE channel.chat-Subscriptions, obwohl Python sie jointe (Datensammlung lief in Python laut Docstring 'für alle'). Solche Kanäle werden chat-seitig nicht mehr beobachtet. Zusätzlich neuer Filter needs_reauth=FALSE: raid-auth-Einträge mit needs_reauth=TRUE fliegen raus, was Python im Join-SELECT nicht tat. Praktischer Umfang abhängig davon, wie viele raid-aktive Nicht-Partner es gibt; bei reiner Partner-Population ist die Differenz null.

*Python-Soll:* connection.py:2050-2051 — WHERE a.raid_enabled IS TRUE OR s.is_partner_active = 1 (ODER-Logik: ein Kanal qualifiziert sich, wenn ENTWEDER raid aktiv ist ODER er aktiver Partner ist). Anschließend nur noch channel:bot-Scope-Filter (2061-2063).

*Rust-Ist:* rust/bin/tb-bot/src/chat_wiring.rs:698-701 — WHERE ps.is_partner_active = 1 AND COALESCE(ps.twitch_user_id,'')<>'' AND ra.needs_reauth = FALSE AND ra.scopes LIKE '%channel:bot%'. is_partner_active=1 ist hier PFLICHT; raid_enabled wird gar nicht als alternativer Qualifier geprüft.

**Verifikation:** Regression bestätigt im aktuellen Code. Python connection.py:2050-2051 wählt Kanäle disjunktiv: `WHERE a.raid_enabled IS TRUE OR s.is_partner_active = 1`, danach nur channel:bot-Grant- und Live-Filter — ein raid_enabled=TRUE-Kanal ohne is_partner_active=1 wird für die Datensammlung gejointet. Rust chat_wiring.rs:698-701 (reconcile_chat_subscriptions) macht is_partner_active=1 PFLICHT (AND-Kette) und prüft raid_enabled gar nicht als Alternativ-Qualifier; zusätzlich neuer Filter ra.needs_reauth=FALSE, den Pythons Join-SELECT nicht hatte. Härter gesucht: kein anderer Rust-Pfad fängt diese Kanäle chat-seitig auf — die partner_state-Query in tb-monitoring/subscriptions.rs:455 sitzt im per-Kanal-is_passive_lurker-Gate (keine Auswahl-Query), und der EngagementIrcReader nutzt eine disjunkte Menge (twitch_engagement_settings.irc_read), unabhängig von raid_enabled. raid_enabled wird unabhängig von der Partner-Aktivierung gesetzt (streamer_lifecycle.rs:694 setzt es bei Raid-Opt-in, Departnerung setzt es FALSE — getrennte Spalten), also sind raid-enabled-Nicht-Partner schema-möglich. Damit erhalten solche Kanäle (raid_enabled=TRUE, channel:bot-Scope, is_partner_active!=1) in Rust keine channel.chat.*-Subscriptions, die Python anlegte. medium korrekt: echte Regression, aber gebunden an die Existenz raid-aktiver Nicht-Partner und betrifft nur Datensammlung (Bot-Funktionen gatet Python ohnehin auf Partner); bei reiner Partner-Population ist die Differenz null.

**Laufzeit-Check:** In Prod-DB zählen: SELECT count(*) FROM twitch_streamers_partner_state ps JOIN twitch_raid_auth ra ON ra.twitch_user_id=ps.twitch_user_id WHERE ra.raid_enabled IS TRUE AND COALESCE(ps.is_partner_active,0)<>1 AND ra.scopes LIKE '%channel:bot%' — >0 belegt vom Reconcile nicht abonnierte Kanäle.

### P2.12 [chat:bot] !invite consumes 1h cooldown before the call; Python only consumes it on a successful send

- **Python:** `bot/chat/bot.py:797-801`
- **Rust:** `rust/crates/tb-chat/src/commands.rs:1263-1274`
- **Status:** changed

**Befund:** A user who runs !invite while the channel is not Deadlock-live (or when the send drops) is locked out for a full hour even though no reply was ever sent. Python would let them retry immediately once the channel is live. Degrades the user-facing !invite command on the common 'tried too early' path.

*Python-Soll:* bot/chat/bot.py:797-801 reads last cooldown; lines 844-848: ok = await self._send_chat_message(...); if ok: self._invite_cmd_cd[cd_key] = now — cooldown stamped ONLY after a successful reply.

*Rust-Ist:* rust/crates/tb-chat/src/commands.rs:1263-1274 — cooldowns.insert(key, Instant::now()) runs BEFORE invite_line is called; if invite_line returns Ok(None) (not Deadlock-live / no invite) or reply_plain fails, the 1h cooldown is already burned.

**Verifikation:** Verified real regression. Python bot.py:844-848 stamps the 1h cooldown (self._invite_cmd_cd[cd_key]=now) ONLY after ok=await self._send_chat_message(...) returns True; if no reply (line 838) or send fails, no cooldown is stamped and the user can retry immediately. Rust commands.rs:1273 does cooldowns.insert(key, Instant::now()) BEFORE calling invite_line (1276), then the Ok(_) arm (1284) silently returns when invite_line yields Ok(None)/empty, and reply_plain failures are likewise unconditional. The production impl DbInvitePort::invite_line (rust/bin/tb-bot/src/chat_wiring.rs:1138-1139) returns Ok(None) precisely on the not-Deadlock-live path, confirming the common 'tried too early' case burns the full 1h cooldown without any reply being sent. Behavioral divergence is genuine. Medium is correct: in-memory cooldown (resets on bot restart), only affects the early-try path.

**Laufzeit-Check:** On a partner channel that is NOT Deadlock-live, send !invite once, then go Deadlock-live within the hour and send !invite again — Rust stays silent (cooldown burned) whereas Python replies with the invite link.

### P2.13 [chat:bot] Successful !invite reply does not suppress the next automated promo (missing _last_promo_sent side-effect)

- **Python:** `bot/chat/bot.py:847`
- **Rust:** `ABSENT`
- **Status:** missing

**Befund:** After a bot answers !invite with a Discord link, the automated promo engine can immediately send its own Discord-promo announcement, producing back-to-back duplicate Discord advertising in chat — the exact double-advertising Python's coupling was designed to prevent.

*Python-Soll:* bot/chat/bot.py:847 — on a successful !invite reply: self._last_promo_sent[channel_login] = now, coupling the manual invite reply into the promo overall-cooldown so an automated Discord promo will not fire right after.

*Rust-Ist:* ABSENT (searched: grep -rn 'last_promo_sent|note_promo|mark_promo' rust/crates/tb-chat/src/commands.rs rust/bin/tb-bot/src/chat_wiring.rs — only PromoEngine-internal hits). CommandEngine (commands.rs:238-255) holds no PromoEngine handle, and cmd_invite (commands.rs:1276-1296) sets only invite_cooldowns. PromoEngine.overall_promo_ready_inner (promos.rs:964-968) keys off last_promo_sent, which the invite path never updates.

**Verifikation:** Verified in current code. Python bot.py:847 sets self._last_promo_sent[channel_login] = now on a successful !invite reply; this dict is the promo overall-cooldown key read by the PromoEngine (promos.py:611/815/1306/1587), so a manual invite reply suppresses the next automated Discord promo. On the Rust side, both invite paths lack this coupling: CommandEngine.cmd_invite (commands.rs:1246-1296) sets ONLY invite_cooldowns and holds no PromoEngine handle; DbInvitePort.invite_line (chat_wiring.rs:1114-1166) and the internal-api chat_command.rs handler only build/return the reply string. No invite path calls PromoEngine.mark_promo_sent or updates last_promo_sent (grep across tb-chat/tb-bot/tb-internal-api confirms mark_promo_sent is only called from within promos.rs itself). Therefore overall_promo_ready_inner (promos.rs:964) keys off a last_promo_sent that the invite path never touches, allowing an automated promo to fire immediately after a !invite reply — the back-to-back duplicate Discord advertising the Python coupling prevents. Severity medium is correct: cosmetic duplicate-ad regression, only in the narrow window where the promo cooldown is otherwise ready when !invite is used.

**Laufzeit-Check:** In a live Deadlock stream, set the promo overall-cooldown near-ready, trigger !invite, and watch chat: the automated promo fires again within seconds (Rust) instead of being suppressed for the full overall-cooldown window (Python).

### P2.14 [chat:bot] No eager partner-invite backfill at startup (_ensure_partner_invites not ported)

- **Python:** `bot/chat/bot.py:940`
- **Rust:** `ABSENT`
- **Status:** missing

**Befund:** Partners that have not yet received their first promo have no Discord invite pre-created. The end state converges (the invite is created the first time a promo fires), so steady-state behavior is the same, but: (a) there is no proactive pre-warm, so any consumer that reads twitch_streamer_invites before the first promo (e.g. the Deadlock-bot sync endpoint /streamer-invites, or the discord-invite GET handler) will see a missing invite for a freshly-active partner until that partner's first promo tick; (b) the explicit +60s retry of transient broker failures is gone (Rust recovers on the next promo cycle instead). Impact is bounded and self-healing; severity medium because cross-system invite-sync consumers can observe a temporary gap.

*Python-Soll:* bot/chat/bot.py:940 spawns self._ensure_partner_invites(); body 1172-1260 runs a SELECT (twitch_partners active without invite, lines 1184-1197), then proactively creates invites for every such partner in a loop with 0.5s pacing (1212-1226) and a 60s-deferred retry of failures (1232-1260), pre-warming _promo_invite_cache.

*Rust-Ist:* ABSENT for the eager path (searched: grep -rn 'ensure_partner|NOT EXISTS|backfill.*invite|active.*partner.*invite' bin/ crates/ — no match). Rust only creates invites LAZILY on-miss inside rust/bin/tb-bot/src/chat_wiring.rs:1267-1320 (DbInviteResolver::create_and_store_streamer_invite), triggered by resolve_invite during a promo.

**Verifikation:** Python bot/chat/bot.py:1172-1260 (_ensure_partner_invites, spawned at :940 after wait_until_ready) eagerly SELECTs all active twitch_partners with NOT EXISTS a twitch_streamer_invites row, then proactively creates an invite for each (0.5s pacing), pre-warms _promo_invite_cache, and retries transient failures after 60s. In Rust this eager path is absent. Invite creation lives only in DbInviteResolver::create_and_store_streamer_invite (rust/bin/tb-bot/src/chat_wiring.rs:1266-1335) and is reached exclusively via resolve_invite (chat_wiring.rs:1241/1256), which the PromoEngine calls only when a promo actually fires (crates/tb-chat/src/promos.rs:722, :792). No startup task, loop, or NOT-EXISTS-over-invites query spawns ahead of the first promo. The NOT EXISTS at main.rs:1274 is the EventSub subscription_maintenance_loop (cleanup of stale subscriptions), not invite backfill. Steady state converges (invite is created on the first promo tick), and the +60s explicit retry is gone (Rust recovers on the next promo cycle). Gap is bounded and self-healing: a freshly-active partner has no pre-created invite until its first promo, so a cross-system consumer reading twitch_streamer_invites in that window observes a temporary missing invite. Medium is appropriate.

**Laufzeit-Check:** In a test DB, mark a partner active with no twitch_streamer_invites row, start tb-bot, and confirm no invite row appears until that channel's first promo fires (Python created it shortly after startup).

### P2.15 [chat:bot] Chat-notification-derived subscription fallback (sub/resub/gift) is a no-op in Rust — sub telemetry dropped for broadcasters without a dedicated channel.subscribe EventSub

- **Python:** `bot/chat/bot.py:1928-1946`
- **Rust:** `rust/crates/tb-monitoring/src/dispatch.rs:481-495`
- **Status:** missing

**Befund:** For any broadcaster the chat bot is present in but which has NO dedicated channel.subscribe/channel.subscription.* EventSub subscription, sub/resub/gift events arriving via channel.chat.notification are silently discarded in Rust (no twitch_subscription_events row). Python would have captured them. This breaks the fallback-capture guarantee and can undercount subscriptions/gifts in monetization analytics for EventSub-incomplete channels. Bounded: broadcasters WITH the dedicated EventSub are unaffected (Python intentionally skips the fallback for them too, to avoid double-count).

*Python-Soll:* bot/chat/bot.py:1928-1946 forwards every channel.chat.notification sub/resub/gift notice (built at bot.py:2000-2142) to raid_bot.on_chat_subscription_notification; bot/raid/bot.py:422-453 persists it via cog._store_subscription_event, but ONLY when should_capture_chat_subscription_notice (bot/raid/bot.py:396-420) returns True — i.e. when _eventsub_has_sub(eventsub_type, broadcaster_id) is False (no dedicated EventSub subscription for that broadcaster). It is a deliberate fallback so chat-bot-only / EventSub-less channels still record subs.

*Rust-Ist:* rust/crates/tb-monitoring/src/dispatch.rs:481-495 routes the sub_kind to hooks.on_chat_subscription_notification; the trait default (dispatch.rs:211-217) is a no-op, and the live impl in bin/tb-bot/src/chat_wiring.rs:952-961 only delegates to self.inner (NoopEventSubHooks, dispatch.rs:230-233). Comment chat_wiring.rs:940-941: 'Sub-Telemetrie (B8-01) bleibt vorerst der Default-No-op des inneren Hooks.' No call to store_subscription_event (telemetry.rs:55) exists on the chat.notification path — only the native channel.subscribe/.gift/.message path (dispatch.rs:509-523) persists subs.

**Verifikation:** Independently confirmed in current code. Python bot/raid/bot.py:422-453 persists chat.notification sub/resub/gift notices via _store_subscription_event, gated by should_capture_chat_subscription_notice (396-420) to fire only when no dedicated EventSub exists for that broadcaster (fallback to avoid double-count). Rust routes the sub_kind at dispatch.rs:490-494 to on_chat_subscription_notification, but: (1) the trait default dispatch.rs:211-217 is a literal no-op; (2) the live ChatHooks impl chat_wiring.rs:952-961 only delegates to self.inner; (3) self.inner is one of RaidEventSubHooks / SubscriptionEventSubHooks / NoopEventSubHooks (main.rs:701-724), and verified via method enumeration that NONE of them override on_chat_subscription_notification — all fall through to the no-op default. grep across tb-bot + tb-monitoring shows the ONLY callers of store_subscription_event are store_telemetry on the native channel.subscribe/.gift/.message/.end path (dispatch.rs:509-526); the chat.notification path never persists. Comment chat_wiring.rs:940-941 explicitly defers it (B8-01 default no-op). So for any broadcaster the chat bot is present in but lacking a dedicated channel.subscribe/channel.subscription.* EventSub, chat-derived sub/resub/gift events are silently dropped in Rust where Python captured them. Severity corrected to medium: native EventSub is the primary capture path and works; this is a bounded fallback affecting only EventSub-incomplete channels' analytics — real data loss vs Python but no crash/user-facing break.

**Laufzeit-Check:** On a channel that has the chat bot but no dedicated subscription EventSub, trigger a (re)sub/gift and confirm no new twitch_subscription_events row appears, while the same event on a channel.subscribe-subscribed channel does insert one.

### P2.16 [chat:commands] !uban loses restart-survival: Rust reads last auto-ban only from in-memory map, never from persisted log/DB

- **Python:** `commands.py:222-229`
- **Rust:** `tb-chat/src/commands.rs:850`
- **Status:** changed

**Befund:** After every bot restart/redeploy the in-memory autoban map is empty, so !uban / !unban replies 'Kein Auto-Ban-Eintrag zum Aufheben gefunden.' even though the user was auto-banned and the record exists in tb_chat_autoban_log. Mods cannot quickly revert a wrong auto-ban post-restart — the exact scenario the Python log fallback was added to cover. Fix: have last_autoban fall back to SELECT ... FROM tb_chat_autoban_log ORDER BY banned_at DESC LIMIT 1 when the map misses.

*Python-Soll:* commands.py:222-229 — last = self._last_autoban.get(channel_key); if not last: last = self._load_last_autoban_from_log(channel_key) (reads logs/twitch_autobans.log, docstring line 23: 'überlebt Bot-Restarts'); if last: cache it. _load_last_autoban_from_log defined lines 22-50 scans the TSV log backwards for the last [BANNED] line of the channel.

*Rust-Ist:* tb-chat/src/commands.rs:850 self.autoban.last_autoban(&channel_key) -> bin/tb-bot/src/chat_wiring.rs:1202-1208 EngineAutobanStore -> tb-chat/src/moderation.rs:492-495 last_autoban() returns only self.last_autoban.lock().get(key). Persisted table tb_chat_autoban_log is INSERTed (moderation.rs:526-538) but NEVER SELECTed in production (grep 'FROM tb_chat_autoban_log' hits only test files moderation_db.rs).

**Verifikation:** Verified in current code. Python chat/commands.py:222-228 (cmd_uban) falls back from the in-memory _last_autoban map to _load_last_autoban_from_log (reads logs/twitch_autobans.log, docstring line 23 'überlebt Bot-Restarts'), so !uban works after a restart. Rust commands.rs:850 calls last_autoban() -> chat_wiring.rs:1202-1208 EngineAutobanStore -> moderation.rs:492-495, which returns ONLY self.last_autoban.lock().get(key) — the in-memory HashMap created empty at new() (moderation.rs:410) and never rehydrated. tb_chat_autoban_log is INSERTed (moderation.rs:527) but a grep across tb-chat/src AND tb-bot/src finds NO SELECT/FROM tb_chat_autoban_log in production — only test file moderation_db.rs reads it. Hence after a restart the map is empty, last_autoban() returns None, and the unban path replies 'Kein Auto-Ban-Eintrag zum Aufheben gefunden.' (commands.rs:853) even though the record exists in the table. Genuine restart-survival regression. Severity lowered to medium: mods retain Twitch's native /unban and the failure window is bans before the last restart; only the !uban/!unban convenience shortcut is degraded.

**Laufzeit-Check:** Auto-ban a test user, restart the bot, then run !uban in that channel and observe the 'Kein Auto-Ban-Eintrag' reply despite a row present in tb_chat_autoban_log.

- **Python:** `commands.py:711-716`
- **Rust:** `tb-chat/src/commands.rs:944-949`

**Befund:** User-sichtbarer Informationsverlust: der Chat sieht nicht mehr, auf welchen Kanal geraidet wurde ('Raid auf {target} gestartet' → 'Raid gestartet'). manual_raid_handler (tb-internal-api/src/handlers/raid.rs:83) liefert target_login im JSON, aber der Trait reicht es nicht durch.

*Rust-Ist:* tb-chat/src/commands.rs:944-949 — 'started' => format!("@{chatter} Raid gestartet! (Twitch-Countdown ~90s)") mit Inline-Kommentar 'UNSICHER: Für den vollständigen ... Text würde RaidCommandPort ein strukturiertes Ergebnis liefern müssen'. RaidCommandPort.manual_raid (commands.rs:108-112 + chat_wiring.rs:992-1008) gibt nur den status-String zurück, target_login wird verworfen.

**Verifikation:** Verified end-to-end. Python (bot/chat/commands.py:712-716) reads target_login from the result and replies "Raid auf {target_login} gestartet! (Twitch-Countdown ~90s)". Rust drops it: the RaidCommandPort trait returns only Result<String,String> (commands.rs:103-112), and the wiring adapter (chat_wiring.rs:991-1008) extracts solely value.get("status"), discarding target_login — even though the underlying start_manual_raid JSON does include target_login (handlers/raid.rs:18-22, mock+test at :83/:148). The chat handler (commands.rs:944-948) therefore emits a hardcoded "@{chatter} Raid gestartet! (Twitch-Countdown ~90s)" with no target channel, exactly as flagged by the inline UNSICHER comment. Real, user-visible information loss; not synthesized differently elsewhere. Severity medium is right: raid still works, only the target-channel name is missing from the success line.

**Laufzeit-Check:** Trigger !raid as an eligible partner on a live channel and read the bot's chat reply — Rust says "Raid gestartet!" without the target login, where Python said "Raid auf {target} gestartet!".

### P2.18 [chat:promos] Lurker-Tax sent as plain chat message instead of orange Helix announcement, bypassing partner opt-out and outbound suppression

- **Python:** `bot/chat/promos.py:1425-1431`
- **Rust:** `rust/crates/tb-chat/src/promos.rs:1308-1315`
- **Status:** changed

**Befund:** User-visible: Lurker-Tax appears as an ordinary chat line, not the highlighted orange announcement Python posts. More importantly, it skips the partner opt-out and outbound chat suppression checks that _send_announcement enforces, so a suppressed/opted-out channel could still receive the Lurker-Tax message. The consuming send-call is just past line 443; flagged because the in-range _build_lurker_tax_text output is delivered via a weaker path.

*Python-Soll:* bot/chat/promos.py:1425-1431 — message=_build_lurker_tax_text(...); ok = await self._send_announcement(channel, message, color='orange', source='lurker_tax'). _send_announcement (bot/chat/moderation.py:1331-1372) posts a highlighted Helix announcement (orange), and FIRST enforces partner opt-out (1339-1344) and outbound chat suppression (1346-1349), falling back to a normal chat message only when IDs/token missing.

*Rust-Ist:* rust/crates/tb-chat/src/promos.rs:1308-1315 — let text = self.build_lurker_tax_text(&selected); self.api.send_message(channel_id, &text) (plain chat send, matches against SendOutcome::Sent). No announcement color, and no partner-opt-out / outbound-suppression gate at this call site.

**Verifikation:** Confirmed in current code. Python lurker_tax (promos.py:1425-1431) delivers via _send_announcement(color="orange", source="lurker_tax"), and _send_announcement (moderation.py:1339-1349) first enforces partner opt-out (_is_manual_partner_opt_out_for_chat) and outbound chat suppression (_get_outbound_chat_suppression) before posting an orange Helix announcement, falling back to plain chat only when IDs/token are missing. _overall_promo_ready (promos.py:811-818) is a pure cooldown check and does NOT carry the suppression/opt-out gates, so on the Python side those gates live solely inside _send_announcement. The Rust counterpart maybe_send_lurker_tax_reminder (promos.rs:1191-1330) only checks overall cooldown + plan/scope/candidate guards, then sends via plain self.api.send_message(channel_id, &text) at line 1311 — no announcement color and no self.suppression.is_muted(login) / partner-opt-out check. Sibling promo paths in the same file (send_promo_message line 784, send_timeout_pitch line 825) DO call self.suppression.is_muted(login) before send_announcement, proving the gate exists and is deliberately omitted only on the lurker_tax path. Result: lurker-tax appears as an ordinary chat line instead of an orange announcement, AND a suppressed/opted-out channel can still receive it. Severity medium retained: feature is gated behind paid-plan opt-in (lurker_tax_enabled), live state, and chatters scope, so the affected, self-opted-in population is narrow.

**Laufzeit-Check:** On a live paid-plan channel with lurker_tax enabled, set an outbound suppression/partner opt-out and observe the lurker-tax reminder still posting as a plain (non-orange) chat line in Twitch chat.

### P2.19 [chat:promos] Lurker-Tax aggregation/join keyed on chatter_login only, dropping chatter_id identity key

- **Python:** `bot/chat/promos.py:469-472,491-495`
- **Rust:** `rust/crates/tb-chat/src/promos.rs:1338-1364`
- **Status:** changed

**Befund:** When a viewer's login changed between sessions but chatter_id is stable, Python still aggregates their lurk history (and matches them live) via the id key; Rust treats each login as a separate identity, fragmenting their session count / watchtime and potentially dropping them below the >=3 sessions / >=240 min thresholds. Different candidate set; some legitimate long-time lurkers won't be tax-pinged that Python would have selected (and vice-versa for login reuse).

*Python-Soll:* bot/chat/promos.py:469-472,491-495 group historical lurks by chatter_identity_key = (CASE WHEN chatter_id present THEN 'id:'||chatter_id ELSE 'login:'||lower(login)); live_candidates compute the same key (501-504) and the final JOIN is on chatter_identity_key (519-520).

*Rust-Ist:* rust/crates/tb-chat/src/promos.rs:1338-1364 groups historical purely by sc.chatter_login and JOINs live<->historical ON lc.chatter_login = hl.chatter_login.

**Verifikation:** Confirmed in current code. Python (bot/chat/promos.py:469-495,501-504,519-520) builds a chatter_identity_key = 'id:'||chatter_id (fallback 'login:'||lower(login)) and uses it for the historical GROUP BY, the live_candidates computation, and the final JOIN. Rust (rust/crates/tb-chat/src/promos.rs:1338-1364) groups historical purely by sc.chatter_login and joins live<->historical ON lc.chatter_login = hl.chatter_login — no chatter_id involved. A grep across all of crates/tb-chat/src found no chatter_identity_key construction and no other lurker-tax query; this is the sole implementation. So when a viewer keeps a stable chatter_id but changes login between sessions, Python aggregates the full lurk history under one identity while Rust fragments it per-login, altering the candidate set and able to drop a legitimate lurker below the >=3 sessions / >=240 min thresholds (and merge unrelated identities on login reuse). Behavioral divergence is genuine; medium severity is apt since it only bites on login changes/reuse, not the common case.

**Laufzeit-Check:** Find a tracked chatter whose login changed across sessions (same chatter_id), seed/inspect twitch_session_chatters, and confirm they appear as a lurker-tax candidate under Python but not under the Rust query.

### P2.20 [chat:promos] New-chatter promo gate ignores API-tracked session viewers (combined-viewers logic missing)

- **Python:** `bot/chat/promos.py:707-743`
- **Rust:** `rust/crates/tb-chat/src/promos.rs:1025-1045`
- **Status:** missing

**Befund:** The activity-promo gate requires >=2 NEW chatters since last promo (PROMO_NEW_CHATTERS_MIN=2). Python counts silent API-tracked viewers as new chatters; Rust counts only viewers who actually typed in chat. On channels with many lurkers but few typers, Rust will under-count new chatters and SUPPRESS activity promos that Python would send. update_seen_chatters (rust:947-956) is consistently chat-only, so the gap is systematic.

*Python-Soll:* bot/chat/promos.py:707-743 — _get_current_session_viewers runs an SQL query against twitch_session_chatters/twitch_live_state to fetch API-tracked viewers; _get_current_viewers_combined (726-734) unions those with the chat-activity bucket; _get_new_chatters_in_window (736-743) computes len(combined_current - seen).

*Rust-Ist:* rust/crates/tb-chat/src/promos.rs:1025-1045 get_new_chatters_in_window_inner derives 'active' solely from state.activity (chat bucket); no DB query for API viewers exists. searched grep 'get_current_session_viewers|get_current_viewers_combined|is_live = 1' in promos.rs — absent.

**Verifikation:** Independently confirmed. Python _get_new_chatters_in_window (promos.py:736-743) counts combined viewers: _get_current_viewers_combined (726-734) unions the chat-activity bucket with _get_current_session_viewers (707-724), which queries twitch_session_chatters JOIN twitch_live_state WHERE is_live=1 to include API-tracked silent viewers. Rust get_new_chatters_in_window_inner (promos.rs:1024-1045) derives 'active' ONLY from state.activity, which is populated exclusively by real chat messages (push_back at promos.rs:672 inside the per-message record path). No DB query for session viewers feeds this gate. update_seen_chatters_inner (947-956) is also chat-only. The twitch_session_chatters queries that DO exist in Rust promos.rs (line 1356) belong to the separate lurker-tax feature (get_lurker_tax_candidates), not the new-chatter promo gate. Since PROMO_NEW_CHATTERS_MIN=2 is enforced only when last_promo_sent.is_some(), Rust under-counts new chatters on lurker-heavy channels and systematically suppresses activity promos that Python would send. Severity medium is correct: real behavioral regression affecting promo cadence on a specific channel class, but self-contained, no correctness/security/data impact.

**Laufzeit-Check:** On a live lurker-heavy channel after one promo, compare Python's combined new-chatter count (chat+API session viewers) vs Rust's chat-only count by observing whether a second activity promo fires once >=2 silent API viewers join without typing.

### P2.22 [chat:promos] Lurker-Tax nutzt falsche Channelquelle — eigene Python-Query (_get_live_channels_for_lurker_tax) fehlt in Rust

- **Python:** `promos.py:1519`
- **Rust:** `ABSENT`
- **Status:** missing

**Befund:** Ein Streamer, der live ist + aktive Session hat, aber gerade NICHT Deadlock spielt (anderes Spiel/Just-Chatting), bekommt in Python die Lurker-Steuer-Erinnerung, in Rust nicht. Lurker-Tax ist ein bezahltes Plan-Feature — bezahlte Kunden verlieren das Feature außerhalb von Deadlock-Streams. Reichweite real verengt.

*Python-Soll:* promos.py:1519 lurker_tax_channels = await self._get_live_channels_for_lurker_tax(); promos.py:1646-1652 SELECT ... FROM twitch_live_state WHERE is_live=1 AND active_session_id IS NOT NULL (KEIN game-, KEIN promo_disabled-Filter), danach ∩ self._channel_ids; iteriert separat 1523-1526.

*Rust-Ist:* ABSENT (grep 'get_live_channels_for_lurker_tax' rust/crates/tb-chat/src/promos.rs -> 0 Treffer). Lurker-Tax iteriert stattdessen get_live_channels_for_promo @ rust/crates/tb-chat/src/promos.rs:705, dessen SQL @1719-1726 auf LOWER(last_game)='deadlock' AND promo_disabled=0 filtert.

**Verifikation:** Confirmed in real code. Python bot/chat/promos.py:1519 uses a dedicated source _get_live_channels_for_lurker_tax (defined 1636-1670) whose SQL is `SELECT streamer_login, twitch_user_id FROM twitch_live_state WHERE is_live=1 AND active_session_id IS NOT NULL` — NO game filter, NO promo_disabled filter — intersected only with self._channel_ids, and iterated separately at 1523-1526 (only the partner-tracking gate applies). Rust has no equivalent: grep for get_live_channels_for_lurker_tax = 0 hits. In rust/crates/tb-chat/src/promos.rs the lurker-tax reminder (maybe_send_lurker_tax_reminder, line 705) runs inside the loop over get_live_channels_for_promo() (line 678), whose SQL (1719-1726) filters `LOWER(COALESCE(l.last_game,''))='deadlock' AND COALESCE(p.promo_disabled,0)=0`, and additionally the loop `continue`s on promo_blocked_by_plan_or_flag (line 688) before reaching line 705. Net: a paid lurker-tax streamer who is live with an active session but not currently playing Deadlock (other game / Just-Chatting) gets the reminder in Python but is skipped in Rust; promo_disabled streamers also lose it in Rust. Real regression of a paid plan feature; reach genuinely narrowed.</parameter>
<parameter name="verify_hint">Set a lurker-tax-enabled streamer live with active_session_id but last_game != 'deadlock', then observe that Python sends the lurker-tax reminder while the Rust tb-chat promo loop does not.

### P2.23 [chat:promos] Lurker-Tax in Rust durch promo_blocked_by_plan_or_flag mitgeblockt — in Python nicht

- **Python:** `promos.py:1515-1518`
- **Rust:** `rust/crates/tb-chat/src/promos.rs:688-690`
- **Status:** changed

**Befund:** Ein Streamer mit chat.promos.disable-Entitlement (werbefrei) bekommt in Python weiterhin die Lurker-Steuer (separate Mechanik, eigenes Cooldown), in Rust nicht. Werbefrei sollte Bot-Werbung unterdrücken, aber Lurker-Tax ist konzeptionell eine andere Funktion; Python trennt das bewusst, Rust nicht.

*Python-Soll:* promos.py:1515-1518 filtert NUR live_channels (Aktivitäts-/Spike-Pfad) via _promo_blocked_by_plan_or_flag; lurker_tax_channels (1519) ist eine SEPARATE Liste, die diesen Block NICHT durchläuft. promos.py:1097-1130: block bei promo_disabled=1 ODER Entitlement chat.promos.disable.

*Rust-Ist:* rust/crates/tb-chat/src/promos.rs:688-690 'if self.promo_blocked_by_plan_or_flag(login).await { continue; }' steht VOR dem Lurker-Tax-Aufruf @705 in derselben Schleife — blockt Lurker-Tax mit.

**Verifikation:** Confirmed in real code. Python bot/chat/promos.py: _send_promo_if_due uses TWO separate channel lists. live_channels (1515-1518) is filtered by _promo_blocked_by_plan_or_flag, but lurker_tax_channels comes from _get_live_channels_for_lurker_tax() (1636-1670), which selects twitch_live_state WHERE is_live=1 AND active_session_id IS NOT NULL — no streamer_plans join, no promo_disabled filter. The lurker-tax loop (1523-1526) only checks is_partner_channel_for_chat_tracking. So in Python, neither promo_disabled=1 nor the chat.promos.disable entitlement blocks lurker-tax. Rust (rust/crates/tb-chat/src/promos.rs) has NO separate lurker-tax channel source; it runs lurker-tax inside the single live_channels loop (get_live_channels_for_promo). That list is SQL-filtered by COALESCE(p.promo_disabled,0)=0 (line 1726), and line 688 promo_blocked_by_plan_or_flag continues BEFORE the lurker-tax call at 705. Result: in Rust, both the promo_disabled flag AND the chat.promos.disable entitlement suppress lurker-tax, which Python deliberately keeps. The gap is actually broader than the auditor stated (the promo_disabled flag also wrongly blocks it via the SQL join, not only the entitlement). Genuine behavioral regression. Severity medium is correct: real divergence on a paid-plan feature, but narrow (only streamers with lurker_tax_enabled AND a promo-block).

**Laufzeit-Check:** Set a test streamer with lurker_tax_enabled=1 plus either promo_disabled=1 or chat.promos.disable entitlement, go live, and observe that the lurker-tax reminder fires on Python but never on Rust.

### P2.24 [chat:promos] promo_channel_allowed-Gate fehlt vor Targeted-Promo/Scam-Warnung im Periodik-Pfad

- **Python:** `promos.py:1531`

**Befund:** Ein Channel der is_partner_channel_for_chat_tracking besteht, aber promo_channel_allowed FAILT (is_partner_active=0, archiviert, oder nicht in PROMO_CHANNEL_ALLOWLIST), erhält in Rust trotzdem Targeted-Promos und Scam-Warnungen. Scam-Warnung (1552) und Targeted-Promo (1561). _promo_channel_allowed (promos.py:114-133) verlangt twitch_streamers_partner_state.is_partner_active=1 AND archived_at IS NULL (+optional PROMO_CHANNEL_ALLOWLIST).

*Rust-Ist:* rust/crates/tb-chat/src/promos.rs:686-728: vor maybe_send_targeted_promo(@726) läuft nur promo_blocked_by_plan_or_flag(@688) und partner_check.is_partner_channel_for_chat_tracking(@696) — KEIN promo_channel_allowed_db. maybe_send_targeted_promo @1398 prüft die Allowlist intern NICHT (nur Cooldown/User-Pick).

It does NOT call promo_channel_allowed_db on the targeted path. The promo_channel_allowed_db guard exists at promos.rs:746 but ONLY inside maybe_send_promo_with_stats (the activity-promo path, invoked at 731), and at 1082 (per-message) and 564 — never before the targeted-promo dispatch. maybe_send_targeted_promo (1398-1418) checks only channel cooldown + user-pick internally, never the allowlist. Python promos.py:1531 'if not self._promo_channel_allowed(login): continue' gates the WHOLE activity iteration including the targeted-promo block. The divergence is reachable: is_partner_channel_for_chat_tracking (channel_classifier.rs:117-130) reads only is_partner_active and ignores archived_at, while promo_channel_allowed_db (promos.rs:1646-1650) additionally requires archived_at IS NULL. So a channel with is_partner_active=1 AND archived_at set passes the partner-tracking gate, reaches line 726, and receives a targeted promo in Rust, whereas Python sends nothing. (Note: Rust additionally omits the PROMO_CHANNEL_ALLOWLIST env check entirely — separate weaker gate. The 'scam warning' portion of the claim is moot: the Rust periodic path has no scam-warning dispatch at all; grep for scam_warning in promos.rs is empty.
