### P2.25 [chat:token_manager] Helix /users fallback for bot id is dropped in the bot-token manager

- **Python:** `token_manager.py:182-206`
- **Rust:** `ABSENT`
- **Status:** missing

**Befund:** Twitch's /oauth2/validate effectively always returns user_id, so the fallback rarely fires; but if validate ever omits user_id, Python still recovered the bot id while Rust leaves bot_user_id empty (empty string), which would break any Helix call keyed on the bot's own id. Low real-world frequency, hence medium.

*Python-Soll:* token_manager.py:182-206 — when oauth2/validate returns 200 but no user_id, Python issues GET helix/users (Client-ID + Bearer) and sets bot_id/login from data[0].

*Rust-Ist:* ABSENT (searched: grep -rn 'helix/users' crates/tb-chat — only api.rs doc comments; the /helix/users calls in tb-transport-twitch belong to the streamer user_token flow, not BotTokenManager). token.rs validate() only reads user_id from the validate response.

**Verifikation:** Verified. Python token_manager.py:182-206: after a 200 validate that yields no user_id, it issues GET helix/users (Client-ID + Bearer) and recovers bot_id/login from data[0]. The Rust counterpart, BotTokenManager in rust/crates/tb-chat/src/token.rs, has NO such fallback: validate() (lines 347-363) only deserializes ValidateResponse (user_id has #[serde(default)] → empty string), and initialize()/refresh_with() set bot_user_id = v.user_id directly (lines 246, 387). If validate returns 200 without user_id, bot_user_id stays an empty string and is never recovered. The /helix/users calls elsewhere are unrelated: tb-transport-twitch/src/user_token.rs::fetch_token_owner is the streamer user_token OAuth flow (explicitly a port of oauth_callback.py:130), and tb-transport-twitch resolve_user_id/user_created_at are generic lookups, not the bot self-id path. An empty bot id would break consumers keyed on it (subscriptions.rs EventSub user_id/moderator_user_id at lines 385/549/634/675 — which even guard is_empty() and bail; moderation.rs sender/moderator id at 117/151/186). Genuine missing feature. Severity stays medium: Twitch /oauth2/validate reliably returns user_id for valid user tokens, so this defensive fallback almost never fires, but the blast radius if it did is real.

**Laufzeit-Check:** Point BotTokenManager.validate_url at a mock that returns HTTP 200 with login/scopes/expires_in but omits user_id, run initialize(), then assert bot_user_id() comes back empty (Rust) vs populated (Python) — and watch EventSub subscribe calls bail on the empty id.

### P2.26 [chat:twitch_api] Rust Helix GET/POST drop Python's transient + 5xx retry-with-backoff

- **Python:** `bot/api/twitch_api.py:356-436`
- **Rust:** `ABSENT`
- **Status:** missing

**Befund:** Twitch Helix 5xx blips and transient network/timeout errors are no longer retried. Under Python a single 500/502/503/504 or a TimeoutError was transparently retried up to 3x with backoff; in Rust the first failure propagates immediately. This increases user-facing failures (stream polling, user lookups, moderation actions) during Twitch upstream hiccups. Note the closed-session retry branch is correctly dropped (aiohttp-specific), but the 5xx/transient retries are real behavior loss.

*Python-Soll:* bot/api/twitch_api.py:356-436 (_get): `for attempt in range(3)` with `if r.status in {500,502,503,504} and attempt < 2:` -> sleep 0.5*(attempt+1) + continue (L387-397); and `except (TimeoutError, aiohttp.ClientError, OSError)` -> retry with backoff (L420-432). Same pattern in _post L248-353 (`attempts = max(1, min(int(max_attempts or 1),5))`, transient retry L332-345).

*Rust-Ist:* ABSENT (searched: grep -rn 'for attempt|0..3|attempts|sleep|502|503|504|tokio::time::sleep' crates/tb-transport-twitch/src/*.rs -> no output). client.rs:107-127 get()/post() build a single RequestBuilder; check_status_and_json (client.rs:230-240) returns HelixError::Status on first non-2xx with no retry.

**Verifikation:** Verified in real code. Python bot/api/twitch_api.py _get (L356-436) and _post (L248-353) retry on HTTP {500,502,503,504} and on (TimeoutError, aiohttp.ClientError, OSError) with backoff 0.5*(attempt+1), up to 3/configurable attempts. Rust tb-transport-twitch has no equivalent: every call is a single .send().await? (streams.rs:109/128/154, chat.rs send/ban/unban, eventsub.rs:84, client.rs get_users/create_clip), and check_status_and_json (client.rs:230-240) returns HelixError::Status on the first non-2xx with no retry/backoff. Grep across all transport src files and the whole crates/ tree found no retry loop, no tokio::time::sleep-based retry, no reqwest-retry/reqwest-middleware/ExponentialBackoff, and no caller-level retry wrapper around Helix calls. The 'loop' at chat.rs:447 is cursor pagination (get_chatters), not retry. The '2-Attempt-Retry' mention at chat.rs:28 is a stale doc comment with no implementing code; the test get_chatters_500_ergibt_status_fehler (chat.rs:955) confirms a 500 yields an immediate Status error. So transient 5xx blips and network/timeout errors that Python transparently retried now propagate on first failure. Real regression, but it is a resilience/robustness loss (intermittent, mostly self-correcting on next poll) rather than a correctness/security/data-loss defect, hence medium rather than high.

**Laufzeit-Check:** Point the Helix base URL at a mock that returns 503 once then 200; Python recovers transparently, the Rust client surfaces an immediate HelixError::Status (no second request observed at the mock).

### P2.27 [chat:twitch_api] Helix request timeout halved (20s -> 10s)

- **Python:** `bot/api/twitch_api.py:59`
- **Rust:** `rust/crates/tb-transport-twitch/src/client.rs:15`
- **Status:** changed

**Befund:** Helix calls that take 10-20s under Python (slow Twitch responses, large /users or /streams batches) succeeded but will now time out in Rust. Combined with the dropped retry, a slow-but-eventually-OK Twitch response becomes a hard failure. The doc comment says 'wie discord relay' suggesting the 10s was chosen deliberately, but it diverges from the Python source-of-truth value.

*Python-Soll:* bot/api/twitch_api.py:59 `timeout = aiohttp.ClientTimeout(total=20)` for the shared session.

*Rust-Ist:* rust/crates/tb-transport-twitch/src/client.rs:15 `const REQUEST_TIMEOUT: Duration = Duration::from_secs(10);` applied at client.rs:75.

**Verifikation:** Both citations verified in current code. Python bot/api/twitch_api.py:59 creates the shared session with aiohttp.ClientTimeout(total=20); the plain GET helix path (_get, line 366 `for attempt in range(3)`) uses that 20s session timeout and wraps requests in a 3-attempt retry loop that explicitly retries on TimeoutError/ClientError (line 420-432) and 5xx (line 387) with backoff. Rust crates/tb-transport-twitch/src/client.rs:15 sets REQUEST_TIMEOUT=Duration::from_secs(10), applied at line 75 via Client::builder().timeout(...), and get_users/get do a single .send() with no retry wrapper anywhere in the crate (grep found only the 2-attempt retry in chat.rs, not in the helix GET/POST path). So effective per-call timeout drops from 20s to 10s AND the retry cushion is gone for the polling GET path — a slow-but-eventually-OK Twitch response (10-20s) that succeeded under Python becomes a hard failure under Rust. Divergence is real. Severity stays medium: 10s is still a sane timeout and Helix calls rarely exceed it in practice, so impact is confined to degraded/slow Twitch conditions; the 'wie discord relay' comment shows the value was chosen deliberately but it still diverges from the Python source-of-truth.

**Laufzeit-Check:** Point the Rust HelixClient at a mock Helix server that delays its response 12s and confirm the call errors at ~10s, while the Python path tolerates it up to 20s.

### P2.28 [chat:token_error_handler] clear_failure_count omits partner technical_pause_reason='token_error' clear after successful refresh

- **Python:** `bot/api/token_error_handler.py:852-867`
- **Rust:** `rust/crates/tb-raid/src/token_blacklist.rs:110-119`
- **Status:** changed

**Befund:** A partner whose token recovered via a successful background/on-demand refresh (without going through the re-auth/store_new_auth path) keeps technical_pause_reason='token_error' set. Any dashboard/analytics/raid gate that treats 'token_error' as paused will keep the partner paused even though their token now works, until a full re-authorization clears it. The re-auth path (auth_writer::store_new_auth) does clear it, which masks the issue, but the plain refresh-recovery path does not — diverging from Python where every clear_failure_count cleared the pause.

*Python-Soll:* bot/api/token_error_handler.py:852-867 — clear_failure_count() runs UPDATE twitch_partners SET technical_pause_reason = CASE WHEN LOWER(...)='token_error' THEN NULL ... THEN DELETE FROM twitch_token_blacklist.

*Rust-Ist:* rust/crates/tb-raid/src/token_blacklist.rs:110-119 — clear_failure_count only executes DELETE FROM twitch_token_blacklist; no twitch_partners UPDATE. Caller: rust/crates/tb-raid/src/token_refresher.rs:290 invokes it after a successful refresh.

**Verifikation:** Confirmed in current code. Python clear_failure_count (bot/api/token_error_handler.py:852-867) does TWO statements: UPDATE twitch_partners clearing technical_pause_reason where it equals 'token_error', then DELETE from twitch_token_blacklist. The Rust counterpart TokenBlacklistStore::clear_failure_count (rust/crates/tb-raid/src/token_blacklist.rs:110-119) executes ONLY the DELETE; no twitch_partners UPDATE. It is invoked at token_refresher.rs:290 right after a successful background/on-demand refresh. The re-auth path masks this: auth_writer::store_new_auth (auth_writer.rs:184-194) does perform the partner UPDATE before the DELETE (mirroring Python remove_from_blacklist), so partners who fully re-authorize get cleared — but partners whose token recovers via a plain refresh do not. Impact is real and load-bearing: partner_access.rs:204-205 sets partner_status='token_error' purely on technical_pause_reason=='token_error' (independent of grace_active/error_count, which the DELETE already removes), and that gates analytics_access_allowed and landing access. So a refresh-recovered partner stays gated as token_error until they fully re-authorize. This is a genuine 1:1 divergence from Python.

**Laufzeit-Check:** For a partner with technical_pause_reason='token_error', trigger only a successful token refresh (no full re-auth), then check twitch_partners.technical_pause_reason — it stays 'token_error' in Rust but would be NULL in Python.

### P2.29 [raid:raid_tracking_runtime] Stale pending-raid cleanup (cleanup_stale_pending_raids) is never invoked in Rust production — pending raids leak until popped by arrival

- **Python:** `raid_tracking_runtime.py Z.77-120: cleanup_stale_pending_raids() calls pending_store.clean`
- **Rust:** `pending_raids.rs:452`
- **Status:** missing

**Befund:** A pending raid that never produces a channel.raid arrival (e.g. EventSub sub failed, target offline, viewer no-show) is never garbage-collected in Rust. In Python it expires after 300s with a WARNING + metric. Effect: stale entries linger in the in-memory PendingRaidStore until a matching arrival or source-unraid pops them; over a long-running process this is a slow memory/state leak and loses the operational timeout signal. Functional raid flow still works (arrivals pop their own pending), so impact is bounded, but the timeout safety-net and metric are gone.

*Python-Soll:* raid_tracking_runtime.py Z.77-120: cleanup_stale_pending_raids() calls pending_store.cleanup_stale(timeout=300s) and, per stale raid, increments raid_pending_timeout_total, logs a WARNING with age/offline->pending latency + timeout_detail, and emits a pending_timeout observability event. RaidTrackingRuntimeConfig.cleanup_timeout_seconds=300.0 (Z.59).

*Rust-Ist:* PendingRaidStore::cleanup_stale exists at pending_raids.rs Z.238-250 but has NO production caller: grep '.cleanup_stale(' across rust/ returns only the test at pending_raids.rs:452. The 300s timers in main.rs (Z.517/616/633) belong to the SubscriptionManager reconcile, not pending raids. Counter/log/event ABSENT (searched: raid_pending_timeout_total, build_pending_timeout_detail, pending_timeout — no hits).

**Verifikation:** Independently confirmed in current code. Python raid_tracking_runtime.py:77-120 implements cleanup_stale_pending_raids (pending_store.cleanup_stale, timeout 300s per RaidTrackingRuntimeConfig:59), incrementing raid_pending_timeout_total, logging a WARNING with age/offline->pending latency + timeout_detail, and emitting a pending_timeout event. Rust PendingRaidStore::cleanup_stale exists (rust/crates/tb-raid/src/pending_raids.rs:238-250) but its ONLY caller is the unit test at pending_raids.rs:452. grep across all of rust/ for '.cleanup_stale(' yields only that test plus the unrelated SubscriptionManager.cleanup_stale (main.rs:1294 / subscriptions.rs). The three 300s tokio intervals in main.rs (517 token-refresh, 616 recruitment-blacklist, 633 score-refresh) and the 5s loop (600 orphan-sweeper) do NOT sweep pending raids. No occurrences of raid_pending_timeout_total, build_pending_timeout_detail, or pending_timeout in any .rs file. PendingRaidStore is genuinely wired into production (created main.rs:544, shared into AutoRaidPipeline and RaidArrivalSinkImpl), so pending entries accumulate and are only removed by a matching channel.raid arrival (pop) or supersede_from_source. A pending raid whose arrival never fires (failed sub, target offline, no-show) is never garbage-collected, and the timeout counter/event are absent. The repo's own audit findings (findings-2026-06-15.json:3224-3228, :2755-2759) independently document the same gap. Severity medium confirmed: bounded in-memory leak (arrivals self-pop) but real loss of the timeout safety-net + observability metric.

**Laufzeit-Check:** At runtime: register an auto-raid for a target that never sends channel.raid, wait >300s, then check the bot's in-memory pending count and metrics/logs — entry persists and no raid_pending_timeout_total / pending_timeout WARNING is emitted (Rust), unlike Python.

### P2.30 [raid:raid_tracking_runtime] Orphan channel.chat.notification is never replayed when a matching pending raid registers (lost early-signal confirmation)

- **Python:** `raid_tracking_runtime.py:477-514`
- **Rust:** `ABSENT`
- **Status:** changed

**Befund:** When a channel.chat.notification raid signal arrives BEFORE the pending raid is registered (the orphan case the code is built for), Python immediately replays it to confirm/track the arrival at registration time. Rust drops that early signal: the orphan only gets logged+discarded later during arrival-confirm, and is never used as a confirmation trigger on registration. Raids whose only/earliest arrival evidence was the early chat notification can fail to be confirmed/correlated, weakening raid-arrival tracking exactly in the race-condition path this mechanism exists to handle.

*Python-Soll:* raid_tracking_runtime.py:477-514 — register_pending_raid pops a matching orphan via pop_orphan_chat_raid_notification, increments raid_orphan_chat_notification_total, emits 'pending_orphan_notification_match', then calls self._deps.orphan_chat_raid_notification_handler(payload). runtime_factories.py:510-518 wires that handler to bot.on_chat_raid_notification(...), i.e. it RE-PROCESSES the early chat-raid signal now that the pending context exists.

*Rust-Ist:* ABSENT on registration path. rust/crates/tb-raid/src/auto_raid_pipeline.rs:511-530 (register_pending) does no orphan lookup. The only orphan pop is rust/bin/tb-bot/src/raid_arrival_wiring.rs:211-225 (pop_orphan), invoked at line 671 inside confirm_pending_raid, and it merely logs + removes the orphan — it never replays it as a channel.chat.notification (no on_chat_raid_notification call; searched: grep on_chat_raid_notification / orphan.*handler / replay in raid_arrival_wiring.rs + tb-raid/src).

**Verifikation:** Verified in current code. Python register_pending_raid (bot/raid/raid_tracking_runtime.py:477-514) pops a matching orphan and replays it via orphan_chat_raid_notification_handler, wired in runtime_factories.py:510-518 to bot.on_chat_raid_notification(...). That re-feeds the early chat-raid signal through plan_chat_notification, which now finds the just-registered pending and takes the PendingMatched -> confirm_pending_raid path, correlating the early signal to the pending AT REGISTRATION TIME. The Rust counterpart register_pending (rust/crates/tb-raid/src/auto_raid_pipeline.rs:511-530, called at :277) performs NO orphan lookup/replay. The only orphan handling on the Rust side is (1) confirm_pending_raid's pop_orphan (rust/bin/tb-bot/src/raid_arrival_wiring.rs:671 -> :211-225), which only logs+removes and only fires when a SEPARATE channel.raid signal later arrives, and (2) promote_stale_orphans (raid_arrival_wiring.rs:232) which records the orphan as an INDEPENDENT arrival (correlation_status="orphan_chat_notification") after the 15s grace. So Rust genuinely lacks the registration-time replay -> the early chat-notification is never used to confirm/correlate the pending at registration. Severity corrected to medium (not high): the orphan is not lost forever — both Python and Rust have the stale-orphan promotion fallback, so in Rust the arrival is still eventually recorded as an independent arrival after grace; the real regression is loss of immediate pending-correlated confirmation in the race window, a correlation-fidelity + timing defect rather than a total arrival-tracking blackhole.

**Laufzeit-Check:** Send a channel.chat.notification raid signal for a target BEFORE the auto-raid registers its pending, then verify the raid_orphan_chat_notification_total counter increments and the arrival is logged as pending-correlated/confirmed (Python) vs only promoted as independent after ~15s grace (Rust).

### P2.31 [raid:raid_tracking_runtime] Pending-raid timeout diagnostics (build_pending_timeout_detail) not ported and timeout sweep unwired

- **Python:** `raid_tracking_runtime.py:518-553`
- **Rust:** `ABSENT`
- **Status:** missing

**Befund:** Stale pending raids are never swept in Rust (no raid_pending_timeout_total, no timeout warning, no timeout observability event), and the human-readable timeout-reason string is gone. Pending entries can accumulate in the in-memory store and operators lose the diagnostic that explains why a raid never confirmed. Behavioral, not user-facing, but a genuine dropped maintenance path.

*Python-Soll:* raid_tracking_runtime.py:518-553 build_pending_timeout_detail synthesizes a 'Timeout detail: ...' string from signal_observations (channel.raid / channel.chat.notification status+reason+detail, with channel_raid_ready/chat_notification fallback). It is consumed by the timeout sweep at lines 85-120 which increments raid_pending_timeout_total and logs a warning + emits a 'pending_timeout' observability event.

*Rust-Ist:* ABSENT (searched: grep timeout_detail / 'Timeout detail' / build_pending_timeout in tb-raid/src + tb-bot/src — no hit). The data-layer PendingRaidStore::cleanup_stale exists (rust/crates/tb-raid/src/pending_raids.rs:238) but has NO production caller (grep cleanup_stale across rust returns only SubscriptionManager/promos + its own test at pending_raids.rs:446).

**Verifikation:** Verified in actual code. Python raid_tracking_runtime.py:77-120 (cleanup_stale_pending_raids) calls build_pending_timeout_detail (518-553) to synthesize a 'Timeout detail: ...' string from signal_observations / channel_raid_ready / chat_notification fallback, increments raid_pending_timeout_total, logs a warning, and emits a 'pending_timeout' observability event. In Rust, grep across all 20 tb-* crates returns ZERO hits for build_pending_timeout / 'Timeout detail' / timeout_detail / raid_pending_timeout / pending_timeout / 'timed out after'. The only raid cleanup that exists is the pure data-layer PendingRaidStore::cleanup_stale (tb-raid/src/pending_raids.rs:238), which merely removes and returns stale entries with no counter, no warning, no event, no detail string. Its sole callers are its own unit test (pending_raids.rs:452) and the doc comment — no runtime/orchestration caller. The raid runtime files (arrival_runtime, auto_raid_pipeline, signal_correlation, arrival_confirmation) contain no stale-pending sweep or timeout handling. Confirmed: the timeout sweep is unwired and the diagnostic string is not ported.

**Laufzeit-Check:** Trigger a raid that registers a pending entry but never confirms, wait past cleanup_timeout_seconds, and check that no raid_pending_timeout_total metric, no 'Pending raid timed out after' warning, and no pending_timeout observability event ever appear in the Rust bot logs/metrics.

### P2.32 [raid:auth] linked Twitch identity for a Discord user is read from a different table/ordering than Python (twitch_streamers_partner_state vs twitch_streamer_identities)

- **Python:** `bot/raid/auth.py:504-536`
- **Rust:** `bin/tb-bot/src/raid_oauth_impl.rs:179-203`
- **Status:** changed

**Befund:** When an OAuth target is login=discord:<id> (website/Discord onboarding), the expected_twitch_login/expected_twitch_user_id baked into the OAuth state are derived from a different relation and a different tie-break order than Python. If twitch_streamers_partner_state does not contain the same rows as twitch_streamer_identities (e.g. identities present before any partner-state row exists), the Rust flow may resolve a different streamer or none, changing which account the callback expects/validates against. Impact depends on whether the view is a strict superset/equivalent of twitch_streamer_identities, which could not be confirmed from this range.

*Python-Soll:* bot/raid/auth.py:504-536 calls load_streamer_identity(conn, discord_user_id=...) which (bot/storage/partner_registry.py:455-496) selects from twitch_streamer_identities WHERE discord_user_id=... ORDER BY updated_at DESC LIMIT 1.

*Rust-Ist:* bin/tb-bot/src/raid_oauth_impl.rs:179-203: SELECT twitch_login, twitch_user_id FROM twitch_streamers_partner_state WHERE discord_user_id=$1 ORDER BY (manual_verified_at NULLS-last) DESC, (created_at NULLS-last) DESC LIMIT 1.

**Verifikation:** Real divergence, confirmed end-to-end. Python (bot/raid/auth.py:553-563 → bot/storage/partner_registry.py:455-496) resolves the linked Twitch identity for a discord:<id> OAuth target from twitch_streamer_identities directly, for ANY discord_user_id with an identity row, ORDER BY updated_at DESC. Rust (tb-raid/src/oauth_flow.rs:188-204 build_state_info → tb-bot/src/raid_oauth_impl.rs:179-203 StreamerContextResolver impl) resolves from the view twitch_streamers_partner_state, ORDER BY manual_verified_at DESC, created_at DESC. The view is NOT equivalent: per rust/migrations/20260601000000_baseline_schema.sql:1529-1555 and bot/migrations/storage_pg_v2_partner_archive_split.sql:42-118 it is twitch_partners_all_state WHERE status='active', i.e. twitch_partners p LEFT JOIN twitch_streamer_identities i ON i.twitch_user_id=p.twitch_user_id filtered to ACTIVE partners. So an identity that exists in twitch_streamer_identities but has no active twitch_partners row (the onboarding-before-partner case) yields (login,user_id) in Python but (None,None) in Rust. That makes the OAuth state's expected_twitch_login/expected_twitch_user_id empty in Rust, so the callback account-mismatch guard (raid_oauth_impl.rs:904-911) does not fire for that onboarding user, whereas Python bakes the expectation and enforces it. Tie-break ordering also differs. This is a genuine behavioral regression of a security-relevant guard, not a behavior-preserving refactor.

**Laufzeit-Check:** Insert a twitch_streamer_identities row for a discord_user_id with NO active twitch_partners row, start a discord:<id> raid OAuth, and check whether the persisted state has expected_twitch_login/user_id populated (Python) vs empty (Rust).

### P2.33 [raid:auth] User-token OAuth path lacks the 15-minute invalid_client circuit-breaker (exchange + refresh + sweep keep hammering Twitch)

- **Python:** `auth.py:864-906`
- **Rust:** `rust/crates/tb-transport-twitch/src/user_token.rs:139-169`
- **Status:** missing

**Befund:** When Twitch rejects the app credentials as invalid_client (e.g. rotated/expired secret), Python suppresses ALL user-token OAuth calls for 15 minutes and the background sweep returns 0 immediately. The Rust port has no such suppression on the OAuth user-token path: every refresh_all_due tick will iterate all raid-enabled streamers and fire a doomed refresh against Twitch, and each interactive exchange/refresh re-hits Twitch. This is wasted load / risk of Twitch rate-limiting or IP throttling during a credentials outage, and removes the diagnostic single-error-then-quiet behavior Python had.

*Python-Soll:* auth.py:864-906 exchange_code_for_token and 908-1005 refresh_token both call self._raise_if_client_auth_blocked() (256-261) at entry and on a Twitch invalid_client response call self._block_client_auth(reason) (246-254, cooldown_seconds=900.0) which sets _client_auth_blocked_until = now+900 and raises TwitchClientConfigError; success clears it (904-905, 1003-1004). refresh_all_tokens also guards: auth.py:1012-1013 `if self.is_client_auth_blocked(): return 0`.

*Rust-Ist:* rust/crates/tb-transport-twitch/src/user_token.rs:139-169 request_user_token returns UserTokenError::InvalidClient but sets/checks NO cooldown; HelixClient.is_auth_blocked (client.rs:100-101) delegates only to the APP-token AppTokenManager (token.rs), which is NOT consulted by refresh_user_token/exchange_user_code. token_refresher.rs:310-321 refresh_all_due has no is_client_auth_blocked() guard. (searched: grep 'is_client_auth_blocked|auth_blocked|InvalidClient|900|cooldown' in tb-transport-twitch/src/user_token.rs, tb-raid/src/token_refresher.rs, bin/tb-bot/src/raid_oauth_impl.rs)

**Verifikation:** Independently confirmed. Python bot/raid/auth.py implements a 15-min invalid_client circuit-breaker on the USER-token OAuth path: exchange_code_for_token (868) and refresh_token (916) call _raise_if_client_auth_blocked() at entry, both call _block_client_auth(reason, cooldown_seconds=900.0) (252) on an invalid_client response, success clears it (904-905/1003-1004), and refresh_all_tokens short-circuits with `if self.is_client_auth_blocked(): return 0` (1012-1013). The Rust user-token path has NO equivalent: rust/crates/tb-transport-twitch/src/user_token.rs request_user_token (139-169) classifies status==400 invalid_client as UserTokenError::InvalidClient (155-156) but sets/checks no cooldown; refresh_user_token/exchange_user_code never consult any block flag. The cooldown machinery (AtomicI64 + AUTH_BLOCK_COOLDOWN_SECS=900) exists ONLY in token.rs::AppTokenManager for the APP token, and HelixClient::is_auth_blocked (client.rs:100-101) delegates solely to that app manager — the user-token methods don't call it. The adapter maps InvalidClient straight through (raid_adapters.rs:125) and tb-raid/src/token_refresher.rs handles RefreshError::InvalidClient by just committing and returning Skipped per row (240-243). refresh_all_due (310-378) has no is_client_auth_blocked()/early-return guard: it queries every raid_enabled streamer and calls refresh_and_store for each due token regardless of a prior InvalidClient, so during a rotated/expired-secret outage every sweep tick re-fires doomed refreshes and each interactive exchange/refresh re-hits Twitch. Genuine regression vs Python. Severity lowered to medium: only manifests during a credentials outage (not normal operation), the cost is wasted load / rate-limit risk plus loss of Python's single-error-then-quiet diagnostic rather than a functional break — the app-token path (poller) IS protected, only the user-token OAuth/sweep path is not.

**Laufzeit-Check:** Rotate/invalidate the Twitch client secret on a test instance, watch tb-bot logs over two background sweep intervals: Rust keeps emitting a per-tick refresh attempt against Twitch for every raid-enabled streamer, whereas Python logged one invalid_client error then went quiet for 15 minutes.

### P2.34 [raid:auth] snapshot_and_flag_reauth (admin 'flag all streamers for re-auth') has no Rust port

- **Python:** `bot/raid/auth.py:1168-1189`
- **Rust:** `ABSENT`
- **Status:** missing

**Befund:** Admins lose the bulk 're-authorize everyone after a scope change' capability. Consistent with the documented B10 Discord-DM exclusion, but the underlying SQL snapshot operation (not just the DM) is gone, so even a non-DM admin endpoint cannot mass-flag for re-auth. After a future scope-profile change there is no one-shot way to force all streamers to re-authorize.

*Python-Soll:* bot/raid/auth.py:1168-1189 mass UPDATE twitch_raid_auth SET needs_reauth=TRUE, reauth_notified_at=NULL WHERE needs_reauth IS NOT TRUE AND (any token/authorized_at present); sole caller Discord admin hybrid_command reauth_all at bot/raid/commands.py:459-471.

*Rust-Ist:* ABSENT (searched: grep -rni 'snapshot_and_flag|reauth_all|needs_reauth = TRUE' rust/ --include=*.rs — only per-single-user setters token_lifecycle.rs:631 and token_blacklist.rs:202 exist; no mass flag, no reauth_all command).

**Verifikation:** Python bot/raid/auth.py:1168-1189 (snapshot_and_flag_reauth) runs a mass UPDATE twitch_raid_auth SET needs_reauth=TRUE, reauth_notified_at=NULL WHERE needs_reauth IS NOT TRUE AND (any token/authorized_at present), invoked by the admin hybrid_command reauth_all (commands.py:459-471). I searched the Rust tree harder than the auditor: every needs_reauth = TRUE writer in Rust is scoped to a single user — token_lifecycle.rs:629-633 (WHERE twitch_user_id = $2) and token_blacklist.rs:200-204 (WHERE twitch_user_id = $2). All other UPDATE twitch_raid_auth statements (streamer_lifecycle.rs, streamers_crud.rs, partner_setup.rs, auth_writer.rs, token_refresher.rs) are per-user or set needs_reauth=FALSE. Whole-tree grep for reauth_all / snapshot_and_flag / flag_all / force_reauth returns nothing. The dashboard/analytics admin handlers (admin_streamers.rs, internal_home.rs) and reauth_reminder.rs only READ/display the needs_reauth flag (compute "reauth"/"missing"/"partial"/"connected" status, send reminders) — no write-side bulk flag exists. There is genuinely no Rust mass-flag operation and no reauth_all admin command. The gap is real; severity medium stands since it is an admin-only operational capability (one-shot bulk re-auth after a scope-profile change), not user-facing or data-integrity.

**Laufzeit-Check:** In a test/staging DB seed several twitch_raid_auth rows with tokens and needs_reauth NULL/false, then confirm no Rust admin command or HTTP endpoint can flip all of them to needs_reauth=TRUE in one shot (only per-user setters exist).

Python (bot/raid/observability.py:12-179) fully implements RaidObservabilityService/RaidObservabilityEvent and it is genuinely wired into the runtime: emit_event → event_sink → insert_observability_event → twitch_observability_events (see bot/raid/runtime_factories.py:85-97, bot/raid/facades/runtime_core.py:282, bot/raid/raid_tracking_runtime.py _emit_event, bot/base.py:632/1236/1255). In Rust, tb-observability/src/lib.rs only initializes tracing and explicitly states the event-writer to twitch_observability_events 'kommt mit der Monitoring-Phase' (deferred). No ObservabilityService/ObservabilityEvent/EventSink/insert_observability_event/build_event_payload/as_storage_payload/increment_counter exists anywhere in rust/ (only a hermetic test referencing the hypertable name and the deferred-writer comment). tb-internal-api/src/handlers/telemetry_routes.rs explicitly documents that /debug/observability stays a Python proxy, not ported. The auditor's grep underreported: Rust DOES port the flow-id fragment (next_flow_id in raid_arrival_wiring.rs:65, auto_raid_pipeline.rs:237; raid_flow_id persisted in external_recruitment_store/pending_raids) — but that is only the flow-id concept, not the structured per-step event emission, in-memory counters, normalize_value/format_fields, or DB writer. The full observability pipeline is genuinely absent. The twitch_observability_events table exists in rust/migrations (baseline_schema + 20260601000100 hypertable) but no Rust code emits rows. Impact is diagnostic/telemetry-only: no raid execution path reads observability events back (no SELECT FROM twitch_observability_events in execution code) and the *_total counters are in-memory only — so medium is correct, not higher.

**Laufzeit-Check:** After running the Rust bot through a full raid flow, query the DB: SELECT count(*) FROM twitch_observability_events — it stays 0 (or empty) under Rust, whereas the Python bot populates per-step rows.

### P2.36 [raid:partner_raid_score_tracking] Missing _lookup_open_session_id fallback: confirmed raids get target_session_id=NULL when live_state.active_session_id is empty

- **Python:** `partner_raid_score_tracking.py:403-409`
- **Rust:** `bin/tb-bot/src/confirm_resolver.rs:64-91`
- **Status:** missing

**Befund:** When a partner stream's active_session_id is not yet populated in twitch_live_state at confirm time (race/gap between session open and live_state update), Python still links the tracking row to the correct open session via the streamer_login + ended_at IS NULL lookup; Rust writes target_session_id=NULL. Such rows then only resolve via the weaker NULL-session fallback path (identity + confirmed-time window + target_stream_started_at match), so some confirmed partner raids may never get linked/resolved correctly, skewing deadlock-duration stats and downstream fairness scoring. Live path: raid_arrival_wiring.rs:813-826.

*Python-Soll:* partner_raid_score_tracking.py:403-409 — active_session_id_value = _safe_int(active_session_id,0) or None; if None: active_session_id_value = _lookup_open_session_id(conn, streamer_login=streamer_login, target_stream_started_at=...). _lookup_open_session_id (138-161) queries twitch_stream_sessions WHERE LOWER(streamer_login)=LOWER(%s) AND ended_at IS NULL ORDER BY (started_at match) , started_at DESC, id DESC LIMIT 1. Python also reads streamer_login from live_state (384,400-402).

*Rust-Ist:* bin/tb-bot/src/confirm_resolver.rs:64-91 — live_state query selects only active_session_id,last_started_at,last_game (NOT streamer_login); target_session_id is derived solely from active_session_id (l.active_session_id.and_then(i32::try_from).filter(!=0)). No fallback query against twitch_stream_sessions. ABSENT (searched: grep 'ended_at IS NULL' / 'lookup_open_session' / 'twitch_stream_sessions WHERE' in confirm_resolver.rs + raid_arrival_wiring.rs — no match in confirm path).

**Verifikation:** Independently confirmed in current code. Python track_confirmed_partner_raid (partner_raid_score_tracking.py:382-409) SELECTs streamer_login from twitch_live_state and, when active_session_id is empty (active_session_id_value is None), falls back to _lookup_open_session_id (lines 138-161) which queries twitch_stream_sessions WHERE LOWER(streamer_login)=LOWER(%s) AND ended_at IS NULL, preferring the row whose started_at matches target_stream_started_at. The Rust port confirm_resolver.rs:64-91 SELECTs only active_session_id,last_started_at,last_game (NOT streamer_login) and derives target_session_id solely from active_session_id (l.active_session_id.and_then(i32::try_from).filter(!=0)); there is NO fallback to twitch_stream_sessions in the confirm path. I grepped harder than the auditor: the 'ended_at IS NULL' open-session lookup pattern DOES exist in the repo (tb-monitoring/src/telemetry.rs:237/433, scout.rs:168) but those are unrelated paths (live-state writer, confirmed_first_ever chatter flag) — none feed target_session_id during confirm resolution. The confirm path is LIVE, not dead-gated: RaidArrivalSinkImpl is used in main.rs:101, eventsub_hooks.rs:578 calls arrival.handle_channel_raid, and raid_arrival_wiring.rs:803-814 invokes confirm_resolver.resolve when should_track_confirmed_partner_raid. (The #![allow(dead_code)] header comment 'Noch nicht aus main.rs aufgerufen' is stale — the resolver IS reached.) So when active_session_id is not yet populated at confirm time, Rust writes target_session_id=NULL where Python would have linked the open session. Severity downgraded to medium: this is a narrow race window (session opened but live_state.active_session_id not yet set), and the rows still resolve via the weaker NULL-session fallback (identity + confirmed-time window + target_stream_started_at), so impact is occasional mis-linking/stat-skew rather than a hard break.

**Laufzeit-Check:** At confirm time for a partner raid whose twitch_live_state.active_session_id is empty but an open twitch_stream_sessions row (ended_at IS NULL) exists, inspect the newly written twitch_partner_raid_score_tracking row: Rust leaves target_session_id NULL whereas Python links it to the open session id.

### P2.37 [raid:partner_raid_score_tracking] Open-session fallback for target_session_id (_lookup_open_session_id) missing in Rust

- **Python:** `partner_raid_score_tracking.py:403-409`
- **Rust:** `ABSENT`
- **Status:** missing

**Befund:** When twitch_live_state.active_session_id is NULL/0 at confirm time (common right after stream start before the session id is written back), Python still attributes the tracked raid to the correct open session via streamer_login lookup; Rust stores target_session_id=NULL. *Rust-Ist:* ABSENT — bin/tb-bot/src/confirm_resolver.rs:71-91 sets target_session_id only from live_state.active_session_id (None when NULL/0). Searched: grep -rn 'lookup_open_session|ended_at IS NULL|ORDER BY.*CASE WHEN COALESCE(started_at' bin/tb-bot crates/tb-raid — no match.

Python bot/raid/partner_raid_score_tracking.py:403-409 falls back to _lookup_open_session_id (helper 138-161: newest ended_at IS NULL session for streamer_login, preferring started_at == target_stream_started_at) when active_session_id is None. Rust bin/tb-bot/src/confirm_resolver.rs:71-91 derives target_session_id ONLY from live.active_session_id (non-zero filter) and yields None otherwise — no streamer_login open-session fallback. The resolver IS live-wired (raid_arrival_wiring.rs:812-814 under should_track_confirmed_partner_raid), so this is a real path, not dead code. Gap is real but bounded: fires only when active_session_id is NULL/0 at confirm time, and downstream resolve_for_session still has a degraded confirmed_at-window fallback, so attribution is weakened, not lost — hence medium rather than high.

**Laufzeit-Check:** In a streamer DB, force twitch_live_state.active_session_id=NULL while an open twitch_stream_sessions row exists, trigger a confirmed partner-raid, then check whether the new twitch raid-tracking row got target_session_id set (Python) or left NULL (Rust).

### P2.38 [raid:partner_raid_score_tracking] _load_session_started_at decodes started_at as TIMESTAMPTZ, not TEXT-tolerant

- **Python:** `partner_raid_score_tracking.py:266-276`
- **Rust:** `score_tracking_store.rs:217-222`
- **Status:** changed

**Befund:** If twitch_stream_sessions.started_at is a TEXT column in prod (other session timestamps in this codebase are TEXT, e.g. twitch_live_state.last_started_at), the scalar decode raises, resolve_for_session_inner returns Err -> resolve_for_session returns 0 and NO rows are resolved for that session (deadlock raids stay permanently open, resolved_at NULL). Python would parse the text and proceed. Needs verification of the prod column type; the wiring test (raid_arrival_wiring.rs:1155) declares active_session_id BIGINT but does not define twitch_stream_sessions.started_at type here.

*Python-Soll:* partner_raid_score_tracking.py:266-276 SELECT started_at FROM twitch_stream_sessions; value passed through _parse_dt (19-30) which str()s and ISO-parses any text/datetime, returning None on failure.

*Rust-Ist:* score_tracking_store.rs:217-222 — sqlx::query_scalar::<Option<DateTime<Utc>>>("SELECT started_at ..."); a TEXT-typed started_at column would fail to decode and propagate sqlx::Error.

**Verifikation:** Confirmed in real code. Prod schema (rust/migrations/20260601000000_baseline_schema.sql:1465, pg_dump-derived baseline) declares twitch_stream_sessions.started_at as `text NOT NULL` (ended_at also text). But score_tracking_store.rs:217-222 binds `sqlx::query_scalar::<Option<DateTime<Utc>>>("SELECT started_at FROM twitch_stream_sessions WHERE id=$1")` — decoding a Postgres TEXT column into DateTime<Utc> raises a type-mismatch sqlx::Error. That `?` propagates out of resolve_for_session_inner, and resolve_for_session (lines 196-200) swallows the Err to return 0 (best-effort). Result: for any session, the unresolved-rows load throws before doing work, so 0 rows resolve and deadlock partner-raid tracking rows stay permanently open (resolved_at NULL). Python (_parse_dt, bot/raid/partner_raid_score_tracking.py:19-30) str()s and ISO-parses the value, tolerating TEXT and proceeding. This is a genuine TEXT->TIMESTAMPTZ migration regression. Corroborated by the project's documented 'Fixtures lügen gegen Baseline' pattern: the tb-monitoring/tb-raid test harness (tests/support/mod.rs:72) CREATEs the table with `started_at TIMESTAMPTZ NOT NULL`, so unit tests are green while prod fails. The sibling write-path crate (tb-monitoring/src/sessions/store.rs) also treats the column as DateTime<Utc>, meaning the same latent break exists there. Caveat: verified from the baseline migration (most authoritative static artifact; no later ALTER TYPE migration found), not against the live DB.

**Laufzeit-Check:** Check the live column type: in psql run \d+ twitch_stream_sessions and confirm started_at is text (not timestamptz); if text, end a deadlock-target raid session and observe partner-raid tracking rows never get resolved_at set plus a 'Resolve fehlgeschlagen' debug log.

### P2.39 [raid:integration_state] Authorization login-fallback runs on !authorized instead of only when no auth row found by user_id

- **Python:** `integration_state.py:188-192`
- **Rust:** `raid_oauth_impl.rs:431-474`
- **Status:** changed

**Befund:** When a streamer has an unauthorized twitch_raid_auth row under their primary user_id but a DIFFERENT auth row matches by login (e.g. login points to a separate/older authorized user_id), Rust can flip `authorized` to true and inject extra candidate user_ids/logins into the blacklist scans. Python would report authorized=false. Edge case (requires uid-row present+unauthorized AND a distinct authorized login-row), but it changes the authorized flag and the blocked/blacklist candidate set for that account — affects raid gating decisions.

*Python-Soll:* integration_state.py:188-192 _is_authorized: `auth_row = None; if resolved_user_id: auth_row = self._query_auth_row_by_user_id(...); if auth_row is None and twitch_login: auth_row = self._query_auth_row_by_login(...)`. The by-login query fires ONLY when the by-user_id query returned NO row. If a row exists for the user_id but it is unauthorized (raid_enabled false & authorized_at null), Python returns authorized=False from that row and never consults the login.

*Rust-Ist:* raid_oauth_impl.rs:431-474. By-uid block sets `authorized` (441-442). Then `if !authorized { ... query_auth_by_login(...) ... authorized = raid_enabled||authorized_at; candidate_user_ids.insert(...); candidate_logins.insert(...) }` (445-472). The login query runs whenever the uid-row was unauthorized, not only when it was absent.

**Verifikation:** Real behavioral divergence in the authorization fallback, exactly as cited. Python bot/raid/integration_state.py:188-192 (_is_authorized) gates the by-login query on row ABSENCE: `if auth_row is None and twitch_login:`. So when a twitch_raid_auth row exists for the user_id but is unauthorized (raid_enabled false AND authorized_at null), Python keeps that row and returns authorized=False, never querying by login. Rust rust/bin/tb-bot/src/raid_oauth_impl.rs:431-474 (resolve_integration_state, the counterpart) computes `authorized = raid_enabled || authorized_at` from the uid-row (441-442), then gates the by-login query on `if !authorized {` (445). That condition is true both when the uid-row was absent AND when it was present-but-unauthorized. In the latter case Rust runs query_auth_by_login, which can flip authorized to true (458-459) and additionally inserts a distinct login-row's twitch_user_id into candidate_user_ids (464) and its login into candidate_logins (470). query_auth_by_user_id selects the row regardless of auth status (same as Python), so nothing upstream neutralizes the gap. Net effect: in the edge case (uid-row present+unauthorized AND a separate authorized login-row mapping to a different user_id), Rust reports authorized=true and widens the blacklist candidate set where Python reports authorized=false — changing raid gating. Severity medium is correct: narrow trigger requiring two cross-referencing twitch_raid_auth rows, no crash/security escalation, but it does alter the authorized flag and candidate set.

**Laufzeit-Check:** Seed twitch_raid_auth with an unauthorized row keyed by the streamer's user_id plus a separate authorized row whose twitch_login maps to a different user_id, then call the resolve-integration-state path for that login and compare the authorized flag (and candidate user_ids) between Python and Rust.

### P2.40 [raid:partner_scores] Single-partner refresh bypasses twitch_streamers_partner_state existence/NULL gate that Python enforces

- **Python:** `partner_scores.py:296-308`
- **Rust:** `rust/bin/tb-bot/src/eventsub_hooks.rs:505-534`
- **Status:** changed

**Befund:** A go-live/EventSub event for a streamer who is in twitch_live_state but not an active partner (or whose partner row is missing/NULL) will cause Rust to write a twitch_partner_raid_scores row that Python would never create. Pollutes the score table with non-partner rows; downstream auto-raid candidate selection reads this table (auto_raid_pipeline.rs:181,394). Low blast radius if EventSub is only subscribed for partners, but the safety gate Python relied on is gone.

*Python-Soll:* partner_scores.py:296-308 refresh_partner_score -> _refresh_scores_for_ids([user_id], active_only=False) -> _load_partners (426-464) which requires the row to exist in twitch_streamers_partner_state with twitch_user_id IS NOT NULL AND twitch_login IS NOT NULL (435-437) and skips rows with empty login/id (455-456). If the user is not a known partner (or has NULL login), Python loads zero partners (349-350) and writes NO score row.

*Rust-Ist:* rust/bin/tb-bot/src/eventsub_hooks.rs:505-534: the single-refresh path takes user_id from the EventSub event and resolves login from twitch_live_state (login_for_user_id, 514) — never querying twitch_streamers_partner_state — then calls score_resolver.refresh_scores(&[(user_id, login)], ...) (522-525). compute_upserts (score_refresh.rs:206-336) trusts the passed pair and always produces an upsert row. No partner-existence gate.

**Verifikation:** Confirmed in current code. Python refresh_partner_score -> _refresh_scores_for_ids([id], active_only=False) -> _load_partners (partner_scores.py:435-437,455-456) requires a twitch_streamers_partner_state row with non-NULL login/id; for a non-partner it loads zero partners (349-350) and writes NO score row. Rust on_score_refresh (eventsub_hooks.rs:505-534) resolves login only from twitch_live_state (514) and calls score_resolver.refresh_scores(&[(user_id, login)], ...); compute_upserts (score_refresh.rs:206-336) trusts the pair and unconditionally pushes one upsert per pair — no query against twitch_streamers_partner_state. The gap is real and live: RaidEventSubHooks is wired in main.rs:701-703 and invoked by tb-monitoring handlers.rs:249/339/386 for stream.online/offline/channel.update. The monitoring roster (poller/tracked.rs:55-69) tracks monitored-only NON-partner channels too (UNION ALL of twitch_streamers that are NOT in twitch_partners), and twitch_streamers_partner_state is a VIEW derived from twitch_partners — so those channels are absent from it. An EventSub event for such a channel makes Rust write a twitch_partner_raid_scores row Python never would. The project's OWN periodic full-refresh path (main.rs:643-649) explicitly applies the WHERE is_partner_active = 1 AND twitch_user_id <> '' gate and cites partner_scores.py:439 — the single EventSub path simply omits the same guard, a clear migration regression. Caveat: the pure NULL-login case is partly covered in Rust (early return at eventsub_hooks.rs:516-519 if login unresolvable); the unguarded case is the missing/non-active partner row. Severity medium is fair: downstream auto-raid candidate selection still filters on partner activity, so the damage is score-table pollution rather than raiding non-partners.

**Laufzeit-Check:** On a test DB, insert a monitored-only channel (in twitch_streamers + twitch_live_state but absent from twitch_partners/partner_state view), fire on_score_refresh with its broadcaster_id, and check whether a twitch_partner_raid_scores row appears (Rust: yes; Python equivalent: none).

### P2.41 [raid:partner_scores] Offline-with-existing-cache branch not represented in Rust compute_scores

- **Python:** `bot/raid/partner_scores.py:738-756`
- **Rust:** `rust/crates/tb-raid/src/scoring.rs:256-296`
- **Status:** changed

**Befund:** If/when the refresh is ported, an offline partner that previously had a live-computed score would have it reset to NEUTRAL each cycle instead of retained, lowering final_score and changing selection. Currently masked because no Rust refresh runs at all (subsumed by the critical missing-pipeline finding), so impact is conditional on porting the orchestration.

*Python-Soll:* bot/raid/partner_scores.py:738-756 — when partner is offline AND a prior cache row exists, _build_score PRESERVES the cached current_started_at/current_uptime_sec/duration_score/time_pattern_score/readiness/fairness/base/final (reads them back via existing_cache) instead of overwriting with NEUTRAL/recomputed values.

*Rust-Ist:* rust/crates/tb-raid/src/scoring.rs:256-296 — compute_scores implements only a two-way input-driven path (live vs offline-no-cache, per its own doc comment L256-258); there is no existing_cache parameter or branch. grep 'existing_cache' rust/crates = empty.

**Verifikation:** Confirmed independently. Python partner_scores.py:738-756 has a three-way branch in _build_score: (1) live, (2) elif existing_cache is not None — preserves cached current_started_at/current_uptime_sec/duration/time_pattern/readiness/fairness/base/final, (3) else offline-no-cache with NEUTRAL/recomputed values. Rust compute_scores (scoring.rs:259-296) takes only ScoringInputs with no existing_cache param and unconditionally recomputes every component via the input-driven path; its own doc comment (L256-258) explicitly states only the offline-no-cache path (Python Z.757-772) is implemented. grep 'existing_cache' across rust/crates is empty; the only 'cached_*' hits in auto_raid_pipeline.rs are unrelated DE-stream fallback caching, not score-row preservation. So when partner is offline but a prior live-computed cache row exists, Rust would reset to NEUTRAL instead of retaining. Impact is genuinely conditional/dormant: no Rust refresh orchestration currently drives compute_scores to write/read the score cache (subsumed by the larger missing-pipeline finding), so the divergence only manifests once that orchestration is ported. Medium severity is correct.

**Laufzeit-Check:** Once the Rust refresh runs: bring a partner live so a non-neutral score is cached, take them offline, trigger a refresh cycle, then inspect the partner's score-cache row — Rust will show NEUTRAL/recomputed values where Python retains the prior live-computed scores.

### P2.43 [raid:raid_arrival_runtime] Secondary-confirmed-signal gate not ported: duplicate/late raid signals re-evaluated as orphan/mismatch/independent instead of being deduped as confirmation signals

- **Python:** `raid_arrival_runtime.py:432-439,`
- **Rust:** `ABSENT`
- **Status:** missing

**Befund:** When a confirmed raid is followed within the TTL by a second EventSub signal for the same source->target (e.g. channel.raid then channel.chat.notification, or an unraid), Python merges it into the existing arrival as an extra confirmation_signal (and unraid_seen flag) and stops. Rust instead re-runs the full plan: the second signal may be treated as an orphan_chat_notification (extra orphan row/sweeper promotion), a pending_mismatch warning, or — if pending was already popped — as a fresh independent arrival, potentially double-counting arrivals and missing the unraid_seen flag on the original arrival row. Multi-signal raid correlation accuracy degrades vs Python.

*Python-Soll:* raid_arrival_runtime.py:432-439, 550-557, 630-643 — each handler calls _handle_secondary_confirmed_signal(...) FIRST and returns early if True. That helper (102-166) looks up lookup_recent_raid_arrival, adds signal_type to confirmation_signals, bumps viewer_count, calls update_partner_raid_arrival, and short-circuits. The recent-arrival cache is populated on every confirm (confirm_pending_raid_arrival -> remember_recent_raid_arrival at line 310).

*Rust-Ist:* ABSENT pre-gate (searched: grep -n find_recent_arrival/handle_secondary in eventsub_hooks.rs — handle_channel_raid 71-151, handle_chat_raid_notification 159-220, handle_chat_unraid_notification 230-286 have NO recent-arrival pre-check). The only secondary logic (raid_arrival_wiring.rs:968-1023 record_secondary_signal sink) fires solely on the plan action record_secondary_signal, which signal_correlation.rs emits only when recent_arrival_present==true; all three production callers hardcode recent_arrival_present:false (eventsub_hooks.rs:132,203,277) and recent_arrival_present:true exists ONLY in tests (signal_correlation.rs:966,988,1232,1401).

**Verifikation:** Confirmed in current code. Python (raid_arrival_runtime.py:432-439, 550-557, 630-643) calls _handle_secondary_confirmed_signal (102-166) as a FIRST pre-gate in all three handlers and returns early on True, merging the duplicate/late signal into the existing arrival's confirmation_signals + unraid_seen via lookup_recent_raid_arrival/update_partner_raid_arrival. Rust has the equivalent short-circuit only inside the plan engine: signal_correlation.rs:370/516/686 reach secondary_signal_plan (emitting RecordSecondarySignal -> SecondarySignalHandled) ONLY when recent_arrival_present==true. All three production callers hardcode recent_arrival_present:false (eventsub_hooks.rs:132,203,277); the value true appears only in unit tests (966,988,1232,1401). The DB-based find_recent_arrival (arrival_tracking_store.rs:210) exists but is invoked solely inside the record_secondary_signal sink (raid_arrival_wiring.rs:968,984) — which is itself only reached via the never-emitted plan action, a circular dead path with no live entry point. So a duplicate/late same-source->target signal within TTL is re-run through the full plan (orphan / pending_mismatch / fresh independent arrival) instead of being deduped, missing the unraid_seen flag and risking double-counted arrivals. Independently corroborated by the repo's own audit (findings-2026-06-15.json:2740, coverage-2026-06-15.json:278-279). Severity lowered to medium: the live blast radius is correlation-accuracy degradation (no crash/user-facing break), and per the audit the chat.notification ingestion that produces most secondary signals is itself unwired and its Python driver is off since the Welle-B cutover, so the duplicate-channel.raid case is the main live trigger.

**Laufzeit-Check:** Send two EventSub channel.raid events for the same from->to within 600s on a live/staging bot and check twitch_raid_arrival_tracking: confirmed code keeps one row with both signals in confirmation_signals; the gap produces a second arrival row (or orphan/mismatch) instead.

There is no bot-token-first resolution, no per-user token fallback for follower lookups, no scope set to gate on, no scope-fallback warning, and no bot-path metrics. grep for resolve_bot_oauth|bot_oauth_context|scope_fallback across crates yields nothing equivalent.
