### P2.48 [raid:followers] User-token fallback for follower totals entirely dropped — totals stay unknown in practice

- **Python:** `/home/naniadm/Documents/Deadlock-Twitch-Bot/bot/raid/services/followers.py:344-375`
- **Rust:** `/home/naniadm/Documents/Deadlock-Twitch-Bot/rust/bin/tb-bot/src/wiring.rs:172-187`
- **Status:** missing

**Befund:** The Helix /channels/followers 'total' field requires a moderator-scoped token. Python escalates to the bot token (if scoped) and then to the streamer's own user token; Rust uses only the App-token, which the Rust transport comment itself says returns 401/403 → None. Result: in production the raid candidate followers_total stays at the FOLLOWERS_UNKNOWN sentinel, so the 3rd fairness tie-break level (follower count) is effectively dead and selection silently degrades to the started_at tie-break. The Rust doc comments acknowledge the intent but the wiring never supplies a moderator/user token.

*Python-Soll:* /home/naniadm/Documents/Deadlock-Twitch-Bot/bot/raid/services/followers.py:344-375 (_fetch_total: bot-token attempt, then if total is None and resolve_user_token is not None → resolve per-streamer token and retry fetch_followers_total)

*Rust-Ist:* /home/naniadm/Documents/Deadlock-Twitch-Bot/rust/bin/tb-bot/src/wiring.rs:172-187 (HelixFollowerSource.follower_total calls only helix.get_followers_total — App-token) and /home/naniadm/Documents/Deadlock-Twitch-Bot/rust/crates/tb-transport-twitch/src/streams.rs:247-273 whose own comment says App-token yields 401/403 → None. No resolve_user_token equivalent (searched: grep resolve_valid_token, resolve_user_token, moderator:read:followers in rust/).

**Verifikation:** Confirmed in current code. Python _fetch_total (bot/raid/services/followers.py:344-375) escalates: bot-token attempt, then if None and resolve_user_token is set, resolves a per-streamer user token and retries. Rust's raid follower enrichment path — HelixFollowerEnricher (rust/bin/tb-bot/src/raid_adapters.rs:65-88) → FollowerCountSource::follower_total → HelixFollowerSource (rust/bin/tb-bot/src/wiring.rs:172-187) → HelixClient::get_followers_total (rust/crates/tb-transport-twitch/src/streams.rs:250-273) — issues ONLY an App-token request via plain .get(); the transport's own comment (streams.rs:247-249) states the App-token returns 401/403 for the total field. I searched hard: get_with_user_token exists and is used by subscriptions/raids/moderation/chat/ads, and refresh_user_token exists for OAuth, but NO resolve_user_token/resolve_valid_token feeds the follower fetch, and neither follower adapter passes any token. Rust's own comments (auto_raid_pipeline.rs:71-76, raid_adapters.rs:62-64) acknowledge candidates without a resolvable count keep FOLLOWERS_UNKNOWN and sort last, so the 3rd fairness tie-break (candidate_selection.rs:245) is effectively dead in production. Severity reduced to medium: it is only the 3rd-level tie-break (reached only on a viewer_count tie) and degrades cleanly to the started_at tie-break — no crash or wrong-target raid, just reduced fairness granularity in a narrow case.

**Laufzeit-Check:** In a real raid run with the App-token configured, enable debug logs and confirm the streams.rs followers-total non-200 debug line fires and selected fallback candidates retain the FOLLOWERS_UNKNOWN sentinel instead of real counts.

### P2.49 [raid:followers] DB cache backfill from twitch_stream_sessions removed

- **Python:** `/home/naniadm/Documents/Deadlock-Twitch-Bot/bot/raid/services/followers.py:111-139`
- **Rust:** `ABSENT`
- **Status:** missing

**Befund:** Python first backfills follower totals from the most recent stored stream session per login before any Helix call — cheap, offline-resilient, and works even when no moderator token is available. Rust drops this entirely, so candidates that have a known historical follower count from a prior session are no longer pre-populated, increasing Helix calls and (combined with the user-token gap) leaving totals unknown. This is the cache layer that made the feature usable without per-streamer tokens.

*Python-Soll:* /home/naniadm/Documents/Deadlock-Twitch-Bot/bot/raid/services/followers.py:111-139 (_load_cached_totals: SELECT streamer_login, COALESCE(followers_end, followers_start) FROM twitch_stream_sessions WHERE streamer_login IN (...) AND COALESCE(...) IS NOT NULL ORDER BY COALESCE(ended_at, started_at) DESC; dedup keeping first per login) and call site L247-257

*Rust-Ist:* ABSENT (searched: grep twitch_stream_sessions, followers_end, followers_start, readonly_connection, load_cached_totals in rust/crates/tb-raid and rust/bin/tb-bot). HelixFollowerEnricher (raid_adapters.rs:69-88) goes straight to Helix with no DB pre-resolution.

**Verifikation:** Confirmed in current code. Python bot/raid/services/followers.py:111-139 (_load_cached_totals, called at L247-257 inside FollowerTotalEnricher.enrich_candidates) issues SELECT streamer_login, COALESCE(followers_end, followers_start) FROM twitch_stream_sessions WHERE streamer_login IN (...) ORDER BY COALESCE(ended_at, started_at) DESC, dedups keeping first per login, and pre-populates the per-login follower cache BEFORE any Helix call. The Rust production path has no equivalent: HelixFollowerEnricher (bin/tb-bot/src/raid_adapters.rs:69-88) delegates to HelixFollowerSource (bin/tb-bot/src/wiring.rs:172-187), which calls helix.get_followers_total(user_id) directly with no DB lookup and even discards the _login argument (the exact key Python keyed the cache on). The auto_raid path (bin/tb-bot/src/auto_raid.rs:504-511) likewise calls follower_total straight through to Helix. grep for twitch_stream_sessions in Rust returns only session-lifecycle/stats reads, none feeding raid candidate follower pre-population; no cached_totals/load_cached_totals/follower-cache equivalent exists in tb-raid or tb-bot. The cheap, offline/token-less backfill layer is genuinely dropped. Severity medium holds: it is a fallback/optimization layer (more Helix calls, no token-less pre-fill), not a crash — candidates without a Helix result fall back to the FOLLOWERS_UNKNOWN sentinel.

**Laufzeit-Check:** Trigger a raid candidate selection with a streamer that has a prior twitch_stream_sessions row but no available follower token, and observe a Helix follower call is still made (and total stays unknown) instead of being served from the stored session total.

### P2.50 [raid:followers] Bot-token + moderator:read:followers scope gating dropped

- **Python:** `/home/naniadm/Documents/Deadlock-Twitch-Bot/bot/raid/services/followers.py:271-280`
- **Rust:** `ABSENT`
- **Status:** missing

**Befund:** Python resolves the bot OAuth token and its scopes and only attempts the bot path when the moderator:read:followers scope is present, then prefers that scoped token (which can read 'total'). Rust never resolves a bot token for this and always uses the App-token. Loss of the primary working path for fetching real follower totals; reinforces the high-severity totals-unknown outcome.

*Python-Soll:* /home/naniadm/Documents/Deadlock-Twitch-Bot/bot/raid/services/followers.py:271-280 (bot_can_read_followers = bot_token and (scopes None/empty or 'moderator:read:followers' in scopes)) and L93-96 resolve_bot_oauth_context; used in _fetch_total L355

*Rust-Ist:* ABSENT (searched: grep resolve_bot_oauth_context, bot_can_read, moderator:read:followers, bot_scopes in rust/). HelixFollowerEnricher holds only a FollowerCountSource (raid_adapters.rs:65-67) with no auth context.

**Verifikation:** Confirmed in current Rust code. The Rust counterpart cited in the claim DOES exist (bin/tb-bot/src/raid_adapters.rs:65-88, HelixFollowerEnricher) — the original auditor's grep only searched rust/crates/ and missed bin/, but its conclusion is still correct. HelixFollowerEnricher::enrich calls self.followers.follower_total(uid, login) with NO auth context. That source is HelixFollowerSource (wiring.rs:173-187), whose follower_total calls helix.get_followers_total(user_id) — a single-arg App-token-only call. get_followers_total (streams.rs:250-273) sends only the App-token and its own doc-comment (L248-249, L267) states the 'total' value requires a Moderator-token, so with the App-token Twitch returns 401/403 -> None. There is NO resolve_bot_oauth_context, NO bot_scopes, NO 'moderator:read:followers' gating, and NO FollowerAuthContext/bot_can_read_followers equivalent anywhere in the non-test Rust source (verified via grep across crates/ and bin/). Python (followers.py:93-96, 271-280, 344-375) resolves the bot OAuth token + scopes, only attempts the bot path when moderator:read:followers is present (bot_can_read_followers), prefers that scoped token in _fetch_total, and falls back to the streamer token. The scoped bot-token path is genuinely dropped in Rust; it always uses the App-token path that structurally cannot return total. medium is correct for this specific sub-claim: it is the mechanism behind the separately-tracked high-severity 'follower totals permanently NULL' regression, not an independent higher-impact defect.

**Laufzeit-Check:** In Rust prod, trigger an auto-raid DE-fallback (or any session start/end) and check twitch_stream_sessions / raid candidate followers_total: they stay NULL/FOLLOWERS_UNKNOWN because the enricher only ever hits the App-token /channels/followers, which returns no 'total'.

### P2.51 [monitoring:eventsub_webhook] Broadcaster context extraction narrower: user_id and subscription.condition fallbacks dropped

- **Python:** `eventsub_webhook.py:133-150`
- **Rust:** `dispatch.rs:262-272`
- **Status:** changed

**Befund:** EventSub types whose event body carries the channel only as `user_id` (e.g. some follow/redemption shapes) or whose id is only in subscription.condition will resolve to an empty broadcaster_id in Rust. Combined with the missing empty-broadcaster_id skip (below), this can route telemetry inserts with an empty user_id or enqueue core events without a broadcaster, rather than resolving the id as Python does.

*Python-Soll:* eventsub_webhook.py:133-150 (_extract_notification_context) derives broadcaster_id from event.broadcaster_user_id OR to_broadcaster_user_id OR event.user_id OR condition.broadcaster_user_id OR condition.to_broadcaster_user_id; login likewise falls back to event.user_login.

*Rust-Ist:* dispatch.rs:262-272 (extract_context) reads ONLY event.broadcaster_user_id / to_broadcaster_user_id for id and broadcaster_user_login / to_broadcaster_user_login for login — no user_id fallback, no subscription.condition fallback.

**Verifikation:** Verified in real code. Python _extract_notification_context (bot/monitoring/eventsub_webhook.py:128-150) resolves broadcaster_id via event.broadcaster_user_id OR to_broadcaster_user_id OR event.user_id OR subscription.condition.broadcaster_user_id OR condition.to_broadcaster_user_id, and broadcaster_login additionally falls back to event.user_login (and reads subscription.condition at line 131). Rust extract_context (rust/crates/tb-monitoring/src/dispatch.rs:262-272) only checks event.broadcaster_user_id / to_broadcaster_user_id for id and broadcaster_user_login / to_broadcaster_user_login for login; it never reads event.user_id, event.user_login, or subscription.condition.*. The resulting context.broadcaster_id is consumed directly as the telemetry user_id (dispatch.rs:502+) with no downstream re-resolution. So for EventSub payloads that carry the channel only as event.user_id or only in subscription.condition, Rust yields an empty broadcaster_id where Python resolves it — a genuine narrow regression. Severity medium is correct: most subscription event bodies include broadcaster_user_id, so the dropped fallbacks affect only specific/edge event shapes.

**Laufzeit-Check:** Send (or replay) an EventSub notification whose event body carries the channel only as user_id or whose id lives only in subscription.condition, then check that the Rust telemetry insert records an empty broadcaster/user_id whereas Python resolves it.

### P2.52 [monitoring:eventsub_mixin] channel.raid not persisted through the processing inbox (no cross-restart durability / retry)

- **Python:** `bot/monitoring/eventsub_mixin.py:134-158`
- **Rust:** `crates/tb-monitoring/src/dispatch.rs:430-433`
- **Status:** changed

**Befund:** Raid-arrival processing in Rust relies on the synchronous dispatch hook plus the dispatch-level MessageId guard for idempotency. If the raid hook fails or the process dies mid-handling, there is no inbox-backed retry/dead-letter as in Python — the raid effect can be lost. This is a documented architecture choice (dispatch.rs:10) so it is a deliberate divergence, but it reduces durability of raid handling vs the Python oracle. Flagging for explicit sign-off.

*Python-Soll:* bot/monitoring/eventsub_mixin.py:134-158 — channel.raid is a first-class persisted work-type: it is pulled from the processing inbox and executed via _run_eventsub_business_effect_once(effect_name='channel_raid_arrival') i.e. exactly-once guard + inbox retry/dead-letter durability across restarts.

*Rust-Ist:* crates/tb-monitoring/src/dispatch.rs:430-433 — channel.raid is routed directly to `self.hooks.on_channel_raid(&context.event, message_id)` at dispatch time, NOT enqueued (only CORE_DELIVERY_TYPES go to the inbox, dispatch.rs:415-426). handlers.rs `handle()` has no channel.raid arm. grep for run_stream_online_followups/channel_raid_arrival in handlers/inbox = 0 hits.

**Verifikation:** Verified in current code. Python (bot/monitoring/eventsub_core_callbacks.py:87-100 + eventsub_mixin.py:1254-1297, 134-158) routes channel.raid through the durable processing inbox: the WS path registers callbacks with delivery_mode="enqueue" (eventsub_mixin.py:2112), so a raid is enqueued as work_type="channel.raid" into the persistent inbox, pulled back, and executed via _run_eventsub_business_effect_once(effect_name="channel_raid_arrival") — giving exactly-once + cross-restart retry/dead-letter. Rust (rust/crates/tb-monitoring/src/dispatch.rs:27) defines CORE_DELIVERY_TYPES = only [stream.online, stream.offline, channel.update]; channel.raid is NOT enqueued. route() (dispatch.rs:415-433) sends channel.raid synchronously to self.hooks.on_channel_raid(...) at dispatch time, and handlers.rs handle() (handlers.rs:398-406) has arms only for the 3 core types — no channel.raid arm anywhere in the inbox. The module doc (dispatch.rs:10) documents this as a deliberate Phase-6 choice. So Rust raid handling relies on the synchronous hook + dispatch-level MessageId guard for idempotency only; on hook failure or mid-handling crash there is no inbox-backed retry/dead-letter as in Python. Real durability regression. Medium is correct: raid-arrival is a best-effort engagement effect (shoutout/auto-actions), not a correctness-critical path, and redelivery idempotency is still preserved by the MessageId guard.</corrected_severity>
<parameter name="verify_hint">Trigger an incoming raid, kill the Rust monitoring process mid-handling, restart, and confirm the raid-arrival effect (e.g. shoutout/auto-action) does not re-run — versus Python where the inbox replays it after restart.

### P2.53 [monitoring:eventsub_mixin] Login-Auflösung verliert zweiten Fallback (streamer identity) und das ORDER BY last_seen_at

- **Python:** `bot/monitoring/eventsub_mixin.py:1026-1058`
- **Rust:** `tb-monitoring/src/live_state.rs:467-477`
- **Status:** changed

**Befund:** Wenn twitch_live_state mehrere Rows pro user_id hätte, wählt Rust eine beliebige statt der jüngsten (last_seen_at DESC) — in der Praxis ist twitch_user_id PRIMARY KEY (irc_lurker.rs:450), daher 1 Row, Risiko gering. Relevanter: fehlt der live_state-Eintrag ganz (neuer/seltener Kanal), liefert Python den Login noch aus der Streamer-Identity; Rust liefert leeren Login. Im stream.offline-Handler (handlers.rs:270-279) führt leerer Login dazu, dass tracker.finalize übersprungen wird (handlers.rs:319 if !login.is_empty()) → Session-Finalize per offline-Event fällt aus, bis Polling es nachholt.

*Python-Soll:* bot/monitoring/eventsub_mixin.py:1026-1058 _resolve_eventsub_broadcaster_login: Fallback 1 = SELECT streamer_login FROM twitch_live_state WHERE twitch_user_id=%s ORDER BY last_seen_at DESC LIMIT 1; Fallback 2 (1047-1057) = storage.load_streamer_identity(...).twitch_login, falls live_state leer/leerstring liefert.

*Rust-Ist:* tb-monitoring/src/live_state.rs:467-477 login_for_user_id: nur 'SELECT streamer_login FROM twitch_live_state WHERE twitch_user_id = $1' ohne ORDER BY last_seen_at DESC und ohne fetch-Begrenzung auf jüngste Row; KEIN zweiter Fallback auf streamer identity (kein load_streamer_identity-Äquivalent gefunden: grep streamer_identity → nur Kommentar).

**Verifikation:** Independently confirmed in current code. Python _resolve_eventsub_broadcaster_login (eventsub_mixin.py:1026-1058) has two DB fallbacks: (1) SELECT streamer_login FROM twitch_live_state ... ORDER BY last_seen_at DESC LIMIT 1, then (2) load_streamer_identity (partner_registry.py:455-496) reading twitch_streamer_identities.twitch_login. Rust login_for_user_id (live_state.rs:467-477) does ONLY the live_state SELECT, without ORDER BY and WITHOUT the streamer-identity fallback. grep across all tb-* crates finds no streamer_identity/load_streamer_identity equivalent wired into login resolution (twitch_streamer_identities is only joined in poller/tracked.rs, a different path). The ORDER BY omission is practically harmless (twitch_user_id is PRIMARY KEY -> max 1 row). The missing identity fallback is a real regression: when no twitch_live_state row exists, Python still returns a login, Rust returns empty. In handlers.rs:270-279 the offline handler relies on login_for_user_id, and handlers.rs:319 guards tracker.finalize with `if !login.is_empty()`, so an empty login skips session finalize on stream.offline (apply_stream_offline at :324 still runs). Polling reconciles later, so impact is bounded -> medium confirmed.

**Laufzeit-Check:** Send a stream.offline EventSub for a broadcaster that has a twitch_streamer_identities row but no twitch_live_state row, with empty event login; observe that session finalize is skipped (no offline finalize) until the next poll cycle.

### P2.54 [monitoring:eventsub_mixin] channel.raid wird im Rust-Dispatch synchron im Hook statt persistent über die Inbox verarbeitet (kein exactly-once/Retry)

- **Python:** `bot/monitoring/eventsub_mixin.py:1254-1297`
- **Rust:** `tb-monitoring/src/dispatch.rs:429-433`
- **Status:** changed

**Befund:** Raid-Events werden in Rust nicht über die persistente Processing-Inbox geführt. Schlägt die Hook-Verarbeitung fehl oder crasht der Prozess nach Annahme (204 wird erst nach erfolgreichem dispatch zurückgegeben — teilweise abgemildert), fehlt die in Python vorhandene durable Wiederaufnahme/exactly-once-Semantik. Verhaltensunterschied bei Fehler-/Retry-Pfaden der Raid-Dankesnachricht; Happy-Path identisch.

*Python-Soll:* bot/monitoring/eventsub_mixin.py:1254-1297 _enqueue_eventsub_raid_processing: raid_bot-availability-guard, to_broadcaster_id-required-Validierung, from_login-required-guard, viewer_count=int(viewers or 0), und vor allem await ...inbox.enqueue(work_type='channel.raid', message_id=..., payload={...}) — persistente, message-deduplizierte Verarbeitung.

*Rust-Ist:* tb-monitoring/src/dispatch.rs:429-433 route(): 'channel.raid' => self.hooks.on_channel_raid(&context.event, message_id).await; outcome.processed=true. Direkter Hook-Call OHNE Inbox-Enqueue; channel.raid ist nicht in CORE_DELIVERY_TYPES (nur stream.online/offline/channel.update werden enqueued, dispatch.rs:415-427).

**Verifikation:** Confirmed in real code. Python (bot/monitoring/eventsub_mixin.py:1284-1297) routes channel.raid through the durable processing inbox via inbox.enqueue(work_type="channel.raid", message_id=..., payload=...), giving message-id-deduplicated, persistent, retryable processing. Rust dispatch (rust/crates/tb-monitoring/src/dispatch.rs:415-433) excludes channel.raid from CORE_DELIVERY_TYPES (= only stream.online/offline/channel.update get enqueued, line 27) and instead calls self.hooks.on_channel_raid(&context.event, message_id) directly. The production hook (rust/bin/tb-bot/src/eventsub_hooks.rs:577-579) delegates to RaidArrivalCoordinator::handle_channel_raid (lines 71-150), which executes the full pending-lookup → plan → runtime.execute_plan(&plan) flow synchronously inline, with NO inbox enqueue anywhere. I grepped all crates: the only EventSubHooks impls are the trait default (no-op), the tests, and the bin wiring — none enqueue raids. So there is genuinely no durable/exactly-once/retry persistence for raid processing in Rust. Mitigation is partial: dispatch.rs:397-404 releases the message-id guard on an Err so the bridge can retry, but execute_plan failures that don't propagate an Err, and process crashes after the 204-equivalent, lose the durable re-attempt Python has. Happy path is identical; divergence is confined to the failure/retry path of the raid thank-you/arrival handling. Medium severity is correct.

**Laufzeit-Check:** Trigger a channel.raid notification, induce a transient failure inside raid plan execution (e.g. DB/Helix down), then confirm Rust does not re-attempt from a durable inbox after recovery whereas Python re-processes the queued channel.raid work item.

### P2.55 [monitoring:eventsub_mixin] Go-live does not clear the offline-throttle guard (stale throttle can swallow a real offline after a flap)

- **Python:** `eventsub_mixin.py:1491`
- **Rust:** `ABSENT`
- **Status:** missing

**Befund:** After a quick offline->online flap, the prior offline's 120s throttle entry (handlers.rs OFFLINE_THROTTLE_TTL_SECONDS=120) is not cleared at go-live. If the channel goes offline again within that window, the offline handler's throttle (handlers.rs:289-298) treats it as a duplicate and skips state-finalize/auto-raid/post-stream. Narrow timing window, but a legitimate offline can be suppressed.

*Python-Soll:* eventsub_mixin.py:1491 self._clear_eventsub_offline_throttle(_bid_key); def at 1082-1093 pops the in-memory dict AND releases the persistent EVENTSUB_STATE_KIND_OFFLINE_THROTTLE guard.

*Rust-Ist:* ABSENT (searched: grep 'release.*Offline|OfflineThrottle|clear.*throttle' across rust/crates/tb-monitoring/src + rust/bin/tb-bot/src). guard.release is only ever called for GuardKind::MessageId (dispatch.rs:400) and GuardKind::BusinessEffect (handlers.rs:61). on_stream_went_live (eventsub_hooks.rs:488-498) never releases GuardKind::OfflineThrottle.

**Verifikation:** Confirmed in real code. Python go-live (eventsub_mixin.py:1491→1082-1093) clears the in-memory throttle dict AND releases the persistent EVENTSUB_STATE_KIND_OFFLINE_THROTTLE guard. Rust never releases GuardKind::OfflineThrottle: guard.release() is called only for MessageId (dispatch.rs:400) and BusinessEffect (handlers.rs:61). All four on_stream_went_live impls (eventsub_hooks.rs:488, main.rs:141, wiring.rs:90, chat_wiring.rs:888) only call ensure_offline_subscription (+ reauth reminder); none touch the throttle. guard.rs confirms the mechanism: OfflineThrottle is claimed with expires_at=now+120s (handlers.rs:289-298, OFFLINE_THROTTLE_TTL_SECONDS=120) via conditional upsert. After an offline→online→offline flap within 120s, the second offline's claim() returns false (row still active) and the handler returns early at handlers.rs:304, skipping global-ban-sweep, business-effect/state-finalize, auto-raid and post-stream. Self-heals after 120s, but a legitimate offline is suppressed within that window.

**Laufzeit-Check:** In a test/staging DB, send stream.offline then stream.online then stream.offline for the same broadcaster within 120s and confirm the eventsub_guard_state row for kind=offline_throttle survives the go-live and the second offline logs "noch im 120s-Fenster, ignoriere" with no post-stream/state-finalize.

### P2.56 [monitoring:eventsub_mixin] Moderator telemetry subscriptions lost the broadcaster-token fallback (bot-token-only)

- **Python:** `eventsub_mixin.py:1814-1839`
- **Rust:** `subscriptions.rs:655-705`
- **Status:** changed

**Befund:** When the central bot is NOT a moderator in a partner channel or its token lacks the scope, Python still registered ban/unban/shoutout/follow telemetry via the broadcaster's own token; Rust does not. Those channels lose ban-analytics/shoutout/follower-funnel telemetry. Mitigated by the bot being auto-added as moderator (oauth_followups.rs:168), but not guaranteed for every channel.

*Python-Soll:* eventsub_mixin.py:1814-1839 builds auth_attempts = [bot-token, then broadcaster-token] and tries each until one succeeds (broadcaster path logs a warning, lines 1734-1740/1772-1778). Applies to channel.ban/unban/shoutout.create/shoutout.receive/follow.

*Rust-Ist:* subscriptions.rs:655-705 ensure_moderator_telemetry_subscriptions uses only the bot token (single ensure_subscription_with_condition per sub). No broadcaster fallback (searched: grep 'Broadcaster-Fallback|auth_attempts|retry.*moderator_user_id' -> none). Called from chat_wiring.rs:779-787 with bot_token only.

**Verifikation:** Verified directly. Python (eventsub_mixin.py:1814-1839) builds auth_attempts = [bot-token, then broadcaster-token] for channel.ban/unban/shoutout.create/shoutout.receive/follow (+channel.moderate) and tries each until one succeeds; the broadcaster path is a real fallback (warning logs at 1734-1740/1772-1778) used when the bot lacks the scope/isn't a moderator. Rust (subscriptions.rs:655-705 ensure_moderator_telemetry_subscriptions) only ever calls ensure_subscription_with_condition once per sub with Some(bot_token); the caller chat_wiring.rs:761-787 obtains/passes the bot token alone. Searched the whole tb-monitoring src + wiring: no broadcaster_token, no auth_attempts, no second-attempt path, and the telemetry sub types are registered nowhere else. So when the bot is not a moderator in a partner channel (403/perm_failed) Rust drops the telemetry, while Python recovered via the broadcaster's own token. Real regression; mitigated (not guaranteed) by auto-mod-add at oauth_followups.rs:168. Medium is correct: telemetry/analytics only, affected subset of channels, active mitigation — not core moderation, not money.

**Laufzeit-Check:** In a partner channel where the bot is NOT a moderator, check Twitch EventSub subscriptions (helix get-eventsub) for channel.ban/shoutout/follow on that broadcaster: present under Python, absent under Rust (logs show perm_failed / no Broadcaster-Fallback line).

### P2.57 [monitoring:eventsub_mixin] Inbound channel.ban bot-self-timeout -> TimeoutGuard.record_timeout not ported

- **Python:** `eventsub_mixin.py:2305-2324`
- **Rust:** `ABSENT`
- **Status:** missing

**Befund:** Loss of a second, independent detection channel for bot timeouts. Python catches a bot timeout even when the bot did not just attempt to send (e.g. timed out while idle); that path feeds the 2/day-5/week mute threshold and 7-day channel suppression. In Rust the TimeoutGuard only learns of a timeout when the bot's next outbound send is dropped. If the timeout expires before any send attempt, or the bot is silent, the guard never records it -> suppression/mute statistics undercount and the self-protection (back off in a channel that repeatedly times out the bot) can fail to engage. Behavioral on/off divergence for the moderation self-protection subsystem.

*Python-Soll:* eventsub_mixin.py:2305-2324 (_ban_cb): on a non-permanent channel.ban (is_permanent False = a timeout) where banned user_id == bot_id, calls get_timeout_guard().record_timeout(login_norm) and logs 'Bot-Timeout in #%s erkannt'. This is an INBOUND EventSub-driven detection of the bot being timed out in a channel.

*Rust-Ist:* ABSENT (searched: grep -rn 'is_permanent|record_timeout|bot_id_safe|Bot-Timeout' tb-monitoring/src -> none in dispatch.rs/telemetry.rs; channel.ban route dispatch.rs:549-553 calls only store_ban_event(false). Rust record_timeout is wired ONLY to the OUTBOUND send-failure path: timeout_tracking.rs:186 inside TimeoutTrackingChatApi.send, triggered by SendOutcome::Dropped bot-timeout drop codes, NOT by inbound channel.ban events.)

**Verifikation:** Verified in current code. Python eventsub_mixin.py:2305-2330 (_ban_cb): on a non-permanent channel.ban (is_permanent False = timeout) where event user_id == bot_id, it calls get_timeout_guard().record_timeout(login) and logs 'Bot-Timeout in #%s erkannt' — an inbound EventSub-driven bot-timeout detection. Rust dispatch.rs:549-553 routes channel.ban ONLY to telemetry.store_ban_event(false), which (telemetry.rs:277-310) just INSERTs into twitch_ban_events and computes ends_at; it performs NO is_permanent check, NO banned-user==bot comparison, and NO record_timeout call. Hard-searched all tb-* crates: the only record_timeout callers are tb-chat/timeout_tracking.rs:186 (inside the TimeoutTrackingChatApi.send_message decorator, triggered solely by SendOutcome::Dropped with BOT_TIMEOUT_DROP_CODES on the OUTBOUND path) plus tests. No inbound channel.ban -> TimeoutGuard bridge exists anywhere (tb-monitoring, tb-transport-twitch checked; the bot_id refs in moderation.rs are the message-pipeline self-message check, unrelated). The crate-dep direction allows it (tb-monitoring depends on tb-chat) but it is simply not wired. So the second, send-independent detection channel is genuinely absent: a bot timeout occurring while the bot is idle (or expiring before the next send) is never recorded by the guard, undercounting the 2/day-5/week mute threshold and weakening the 7-day channel self-suppression. Severity stays medium: this is a redundant/secondary detection path; the primary outbound-drop path still covers the common case, and the impact is a statistics undercount / missed backoff in a narrow idle-timeout edge, not a core break.

**Laufzeit-Check:** In a live channel, timeout the bot user via EventSub while the bot is idle (no outbound send pending), then check whether the TimeoutGuard mute/timeout counter for that channel increments and the 'Bot-Timeout erkannt' detection fires — in Rust it will not.

### P2.58 [monitoring:eventsub_mixin] Raid-arrival readiness no longer waits for subscription to reach 'enabled' — channel.raid events may be missed for early arriving raids

- **Python:** `eventsub_mixin.py:3094-3310`
- **Rust:** `bin/tb-bot/src/raid_adapters.rs:179-183`
- **Status:** changed

**Befund:** Python gated the raid start until Twitch confirmed the channel.raid subscription was actually 'enabled' (verification handshake complete), so the arrival event would be received. Rust returns ready as soon as the create POST succeeds, before Twitch verifies the webhook callback. A raid that arrives in the window before the subscription flips to 'enabled' can be silently missed (no arrival correlation / no raid-arrival side effects). Severity bounded by Twitch's typically fast verification, but for freshly-subscribed targets this is a real correlation gap.

*Python-Soll:* eventsub_mixin.py:3094-3310 ensure_raid_target_dynamic_ready: subscribes then polls _get_eventsub_webhook_subscription_status in a deadline loop (wait_timeout_seconds=8.0, poll 0.5s) and returns True only on status 'enabled' (already_enabled/enabled_after_retry/enabled); 'webhook_callback_verification_pending' keeps waiting; other statuses return False with detail.

*Rust-Ist:* bin/tb-bot/src/raid_adapters.rs:179-183 ensure_ready -> subscriptions.rs:311-320 ensure_raid_subscription -> ensure_subscription_with_condition:765-781 returns true immediately on transport.create() Ok (or is_tracked at 740-743). No status poll, no 'enabled' confirmation, no deadline loop. _get_eventsub_webhook_subscription_status has no Rust equivalent (ABSENT).

**Verifikation:** Real behavioral divergence, with one correction to the claim's framing. Python ensure_raid_target_dynamic_ready (eventsub_mixin.py:3094-3310) subscribes, then runs an up-to-8s deadline loop polling _get_eventsub_webhook_subscription_status every 0.5s and only treats the target as ready once status reaches 'enabled' ('webhook_callback_verification_pending' keeps waiting). The Rust path ManagerArrivalReadiness::ensure_ready (raid_adapters.rs:179-183) -> ensure_raid_subscription (subscriptions.rs:311-320) -> ensure_subscription_with_condition (subscriptions.rs:740-781) returns true immediately on transport.create() Ok / is_tracked, with no status poll, no 'enabled' confirmation, no deadline loop. transport.create wraps create_eventsub_webhook_subscription (eventsub.rs:61-95, via wiring.rs:37) which returns Created/AlreadyExists on HTTP 2xx/409 only and never inspects the subscription 'status' field. The only Rust read of status=='enabled' is rehydrate() (subscriptions.rs:283), a startup bestand rebuild, not the per-raid readiness path; _get_eventsub_webhook_subscription_status has no per-raid Rust equivalent (ABSENT). CORRECTION: the claim says Python 'gated the raid start until... enabled' — it does not. In raid_pipeline.py:311-321 channel_raid_ready is computed then start_raid is called unconditionally; raid_tracking_runtime.py:296-311 explicitly logs 'Proceeding best-effort' on not-ready. Both Python and Rust start the raid regardless. The genuine regression is the lost ~8s enabled-confirmation WAIT: Python deliberately blocked until the webhook verification handshake completed before starting the raid (and thus before the arrival event), shrinking the window where a freshly-subscribed target's channel.raid arrival could be missed; Rust proceeds with zero wait and no confirmation (and no compensating sleep between ensure_ready and executor.execute in auto_raid_pipeline.rs:248-265). Because the raid was never actually blocked on 'enabled' in Python, the impact is bounded to the early-arrival correlation/timing window for newly-subscribed targets — medium is correct.

**Laufzeit-Check:** Trigger an auto-raid to a target with no pre-existing chann.raid subscription and check whether the arrival event/pending-correlation fires; the Rust path will not have waited for the subscription to reach 'enabled' before starting, so a fast arrival can be missed.

### P2.59 [monitoring:monitoring] Generic storage-write retry narrowed: transient connection/admin-shutdown errors no longer retried (Rust only retries 40001/40P01)

- **Python:** `monitoring.py:103-140`
- **Rust:** `tb-monitoring/src/inbox_store/retry.rs:70-75`
- **Status:** changed

**Befund:** During Postgres restart, failover, recovery mode, or connection-pool exhaustion, Python retried the live-state/session write up to 3x with backoff; Rust treats these as non-retryable and fails the write immediately. Can drop a monitoring state write (e.g. live announcement state, session metrics) during a brief DB hiccup instead of recovering. Also default delays differ (0.5s→1s vs 0.1s→0.2s→0.4s).

*Python-Soll:* monitoring.py:103-140 _is_retryable_storage_error treats SQLSTATEs 08000/08001/08003/08004/08006 (connection), 57P01/57P02/57P03 (admin shutdown/recovery), 53300 (too many clients) AND message tokens ('shutting down','too many clients already','server closed the connection unexpectedly','timeout expired','fährt herunter', ...) as retryable; used by _run_storage_write_with_retry (monitoring.py:181-202, max_attempts=3, retry_delay=0.5 doubling) called at monitoring.py:1959/2086/2128.

*Rust-Ist:* tb-monitoring/src/inbox_store/retry.rs:70-75 is_retryable matches ONLY 40001 (serialization_failure) and 40P01 (deadlock_detected); with_write_retry (L83-107) uses base_delay 100ms / max 750ms. No equivalent for connection-loss / 'database shutting down' / 'too many clients' SQLSTATEs or message-substring detection.

**Verifikation:** Verified in current code. Python has a SEPARATE, broad retry classifier in bot/monitoring/monitoring.py:103-140 (_is_retryable_storage_error) covering connection-loss SQLSTATEs (08000/08001/08003/08004/08006), admin-shutdown/recovery (57P01/57P02/57P03), too-many-clients (53300) AND message tokens ('shutting down','too many clients already','server closed the connection unexpectedly','fährt herunter', etc.), wired via _run_storage_write_with_retry (max_attempts=3, 0.5s doubling) at lines 1959/2086/2128 (live-state, tracked-stats, category-stats writes). The Rust monitoring crate has NO equivalent: a grep across the entire rust/ tree for 08006/08000/57P01/57P02/53300/'too many clients'/'shutting down'/'server closed'/'recovery mode'/'fährt herunter' returns zero hits. Both Rust retry helpers (tb-monitoring/src/inbox_store/retry.rs:70-75 and tb-db/src/retry.rs:108-117) only match 40001/40P01 — and they explicitly state their oracle is the generic bot/storage/pg.py layer (which itself only retries {40001,40P01}, confirmed at pg.py:163,757-758), NOT monitoring.py's broader classifier. Worse than claimed: the actual Rust live-state counterpart, LiveStateStore::persist (live_state.rs:232-319), writes directly via pool.begin() and propagates every sqlx::Error with `?` — it uses neither with_write_retry nor run_transaction, so it has NO retry at all (not even for 40001/40P01). The transient-error retry behavior is genuinely absent in Rust. Severity stays medium: affected writes are monitoring/analytics (live-announcement state is re-derived each poll cycle; stats samples are periodic), so a dropped write during a brief DB hiccup is recoverable rather than permanent data loss or a crash.

**Laufzeit-Check:** On a running tb-monitoring instance, briefly restart/pause Postgres (or exhaust the connection pool) during a poll cycle and observe whether the live-state/stats write errors out immediately instead of retrying with backoff; logs will show the write failing on the first transient connection/shutdown error with no retry warning.

### P2.61 [analytics:mixin] Analytics-time bot moderator self-heal (403→auto-re-mod with 600s cooldown + partner gate + observability counters) not ported to Rust

- **Python:** `bot/analytics/mixin.py:125-169`

### P2.68 [analytics:chat_bots] Bot-Filter clause in 3 dashboard handlers drops anonymous (NULL/empty) chatters and is case-sensitive — diverges from Python build_known_chat_bot_not_in_clause

- **Python:** `bot/core/chat_bots.py:58-61`
- **Rust:** `rust/crates/tb-dashboard-api/src/handlers/lurker_analysis.rs:83,156`
- **Status:** changed

**Befund:** Two behavior changes: (1) anonymous viewers stored with NULL/empty chatter_login (identified by chatter_id) are DROPPED in Rust but COUNTED in Python — undercounts lurker totals, viewer-profile distribution, audience overlap. (2) A bot logged in with mixed case (e.g. 'Nightbot') is filtered by Python but NOT by Rust. Note: the parallel Rust path overview.rs:378-379 (chat_per_100) DOES implement the full `IS NULL OR ='' OR LOWER(...) <> ALL(...)` correctly, proving the audience handlers are the inconsistent ones. Affects all three extended-plan audience endpoints.

*Python-Soll:* bot/core/chat_bots.py:58-61 returns clause `((col) IS NULL OR (col) = '' OR LOWER(col) NOT IN (bots))` — explicitly KEEPS rows with NULL/empty login (comment line 43-44: 'so anonymous chatter_id rows are not incorrectly filtered out') and compares LOWER(col). Used in api_overview.py:1790-1793 (lurker), 2166-2173 (viewer_profiles), 2267-2278 (audience_sharing).

*Rust-Ist:* rust/crates/tb-dashboard-api/src/handlers/lurker_analysis.rs:83,156; handlers/audience.rs:215,217,232,335,360-361,418-419 all use `NOT (chatter_login = ANY($bots::text[]))`. This is case-SENSITIVE (no LOWER) and excludes NULL/empty logins because `NULL = ANY(...)` evaluates to NULL -> NOT NULL -> NULL -> row dropped by WHERE.

**Verifikation:** Independently confirmed in current code. Python build_known_chat_bot_not_in_clause (bot/core/chat_bots.py:58-61) emits `((col) IS NULL OR (col)='' OR LOWER(col) NOT IN (bots))` — keeps NULL/empty logins (explicit comment lines 43-44) and is case-insensitive. Used at the cited api_overview.py:1790/2166-2170/2267-2275 lines. The three Rust handlers use bare `NOT (chatter_login = ANY($bots::text[]))`: lurker_analysis.rs:83,156 and audience.rs:215,217,232,335,360-361,418-419. Both divergences are real: (1) no LOWER → case-sensitive, a mixed-case bot login bypasses the Rust filter; (2) NULL left operand → `NULL = ANY(...)` → NULL → NOT NULL → NULL → WHERE drops the row, so anonymous chatters (NULL/empty login, valid chatter_id) are excluded in Rust but kept in Python. Smoking gun: lurker_analysis.rs SELECTs `COALESCE(NULLIF(sc.chatter_login,''), sc.chatter_id)` to label anonymous viewers, yet its own WHERE drops exactly those rows — a self-contradiction proving the regression. The correct full clause `IS NULL OR ='' OR LOWER(...) <> ALL(...)` already exists in the same workspace (tb-analytics/watch_time.rs:125,180,193; chat_content_analysis.rs:219), confirming these handlers are inconsistent, not deliberate. Severity lowered to medium: the impact is a magnitude error in aggregate analytics (under-count of lurkers/profiles/overlap and a rare mixed-case bot leak), not a correctness/security failure; known bot logins in KNOWN_CHAT_BOTS are conventionally lowercase, so the case-sensitivity leg is largely latent, and the NULL/empty leg only diverges where anonymous chatter_id rows exist.

**Laufzeit-Check:** Run the lurker-analysis or audience endpoint for a streamer whose twitch_session_chatters/twitch_chatter_rollup has rows with NULL or empty chatter_login but a valid chatter_id, and compare the returned totals against the Python build (or against a direct row count that keeps NULL/empty); the Rust totals will be lower.

- **Python:** `api_overview.py:1973-1979`
- **Rust:** `raid_analytics.rs:232-238`
- **Status:** changed

**Befund:** On a DB/query error the Rust dashboard returns HTTP 500 with an opaque error, whereas Python returned 200 with demo-data so the frontend rendered a populated (demo) view. Frontends that branch on HTTP status (not on dataAvailable) will show a hard error instead of the intended graceful fallback. Note: the lurker handler (lurker_analysis.rs:114-120) correctly mirrors the 200/dataAvailable:false pattern, so this regression is inconsistent across the four endpoints.

*Rust-Ist:* raid_analytics.rs:232-238 and audience.rs:242-246, 375-378 all return `(StatusCode::INTERNAL_SERVER_ERROR, Json({"error":"internal_error"}))`.

**Verifikation:** Confirmed in the actual current code. Canonical Python (bot/analytics/api_overview.py) catches every Exception and returns status=200 with demo data + "Fallback: Demo-Daten wegen Fehler" for all three endpoints: raid_retention (~1973-1979), viewer_profiles (~2157-2162), audience_sharing (~2258-2263). The Rust ports in tb-dashboard-api return HTTP 500 with opaque {"error":"internal_error"} on a sqlx DB/query error: raid_analytics.rs:232-238 (raid-retention), audience.rs:242-246 (viewer-profiles dist query), audience.rs:375-378 (audience-sharing shared query). I grepped the whole tb-dashboard-api crate; no alternate graceful-fallback path exists for these three. Notably lurker_analysis.rs:114-120 is the one handler that correctly mirrors the graceful 200 pattern and even carries an explicit comment explaining Python returns 200 so the frontend (which branches on dataAvailable) does not break on 500 — demonstrating the intended pattern was known but not applied to the other three. Severity stays medium: the divergence is confined to the DB-error path; empty-result and normal paths already return 200 with dataAvailable:false. Real frontend breakage only occurs if the React client branches on HTTP status rather than dataAvailable, and only during an actual query failure.

**Laufzeit-Check:** Hit GET /twitch/api/v2/raid-retention (and viewer-profiles, audience-sharing) with a valid extended-plan session while forcing a DB/query failure (e.g. drop or rename twitch_raid_retention/twitch_chatter_rollup) and observe the Rust dashboard returns HTTP 500 whereas the Python service returned HTTP 200 with demo data.

### P2.70 [analytics:api_post_stream] Post-Stream MiniMax calls do not record token usage into the usage ledger (dropped side effect)

- **Python:** `api_post_stream.py:244`
- **Rust:** `ABSENT`
- **Status:** missing

**Befund:** Every post-stream report + word-group MiniMax completion is missing from the MiniMax usage/cost ledger. Token-spend reporting for the post-stream subsystem under-counts vs the Python baseline. Functional report output is unaffected; only accounting/observability is lost.

*Python-Soll:* api_post_stream.py:244 `_track_minimax_completion(completion, purpose="post-stream-report", model=MINIMAX_MODEL)` inside `_call_minimax`; api_ai.py:26-44 records source=twitch-bot, tokens_in/out, model, purpose into the minimax_usage ledger (best-effort).

*Rust-Ist:* ABSENT (searched: grep -rn 'record|ledger|usage|track_minimax|minimax_usage|prompt_tokens|tokens_in' crates/tb-analytics/src/post_stream.rs -> only unrelated 'recorded_at' columns; grep -rn 'post_stream' crates/tb-llm/src/*.rs -> only a doc comment). call_minimax (post_stream.rs:390-419) returns content and performs no usage accounting.

**Verifikation:** Python `_call_minimax` (bot/analytics/api_post_stream.py:242) calls `_track_minimax_completion(..., purpose="post-stream-report")`, which (api_ai.py:26-41) reads usage.prompt_tokens/completion_tokens and writes source=twitch-bot/tokens_in/out/model/purpose into the minimax_usage ledger. The Rust ledger infrastructure DOES exist (tb-llm/src/ledger.rs:149 `pub async fn record(purpose, model, tokens_in, tokens_out, success)`) and is correctly wired into other MiniMax call sites (tb-chat/src/scam_pitch.rs:1750 and tb-engagement/src/minimax_chat.rs:316). But the post-stream path in tb-analytics/src/post_stream.rs does NOT use it: `call_minimax` (lines 390-419) deserializes a `MinimaxResponse` struct that contains ONLY `choices` (no `usage` field), and never calls `tb_llm::ledger::record`. Both post-stream MiniMax call sites (the report at line 494 and the word-group call at line 1858) go through this same un-instrumented function. So every post-stream MiniMax completion is absent from the usage ledger, under-counting token spend vs the Python baseline. Functional report output is unaffected — only accounting/observability is lost — which matches medium severity.

**Laufzeit-Check:** Trigger a post-stream report for one streamer, then query the minimax_usage ledger table for rows with purpose 'post-stream-report' over that window — none will appear despite a MiniMax completion having run.

### P2.71 [analytics:api_post_stream] Twitch-OAuth admin (earlysalty) rating/AB-vote attributed to 'admin' instead of their twitch_login -> wrong UPSERT key & lost own vote/rating

- **Python:** `api_post_stream.py:1271`
- **Rust:** `stream_report.rs:324-331`
- **Status:** changed

**Befund:** For the admin who authenticates via Twitch (login 'earlysalty', the documented TWITCH_ADMIN_LOGIN), Rust stores their report rating / A/B vote under the literal key 'admin' instead of 'earlysalty'. Consequences: (1) the GET stream-report no longer embeds that admin's own rating, and GET ab-vote returns own_vote=null, so the UI shows them as not-yet-voted; (2) the ON CONFLICT key (session_id, voted_by)/(session_id, report_variant, rated_by) differs from Python, so re-votes/re-ratings land on a different row and prior Python-era 'earlysalty' votes are invisible. Discord-admin, localhost and partner cases are unaffected (they already resolve to 'admin'/'localhost'/login on both sides). Scope is narrow (single admin identity) hence medium, not high.

*Python-Soll:* api_post_stream.py:1271 rated_by = str(dashboard_session.get('twitch_login') or auth_level or 'unknown'); :1364 voted_by likewise; GET :1310 voted_by = dashboard_session.get('twitch_login') (no fallback); rating lookup rater = session_login (:1204). _get_auth_level (api_v2.py:1339-1342) promotes a Twitch-login admin (earlysalty) to auth_level='admin' WHILE dashboard_session still carries twitch_login='earlysalty'.

*Rust-Ist:* stream_report.rs:324-331 and 521-528 hardcode rated_by/voted_by='admin' for DashboardAuthLevel::Admin, ignoring AdminActor.twitch_login (auth/level.rs:196-201 stores it as actor). owner_login() stream_report.rs:55-60 returns Some only for Partner, so GET own_vote (442) and embedded rating (197) are skipped for Admin.

**Verifikation:** Confirmed in the real code. Python: for an admin who logs in via Twitch-OAuth (login 'earlysalty'), _get_auth_level (api_v2.py:1340) returns 'admin' but dashboard_session still carries twitch_login='earlysalty'. POST rate (api_post_stream.py:1271) and POST ab-vote (:1364) compute rated_by/voted_by = twitch_login OR auth_level OR 'unknown' → 'earlysalty' wins. GET ab-vote (:1310) uses twitch_login → own_vote looked up; GET embedded rating uses session_login (:1204) → rating embedded. Rust: partner_or_admin (level.rs:190-202) correctly promotes earlysalty to Admin{actor:Some(AdminActor{twitch_login:'earlysalty'})}, and the doc comment (level.rs:24-36) states the actor exists precisely for attribution. BUT stream_report.rs hardcodes the literal 'admin' for the write key (rate :326, ab-vote :523) via `Admin { .. }`, discarding actor.twitch_login; and owner_login (:55-60) returns Some only for Partner, so the embedded rating (:197) and GET ab-vote own_vote (:442) are skipped for Admin → own_vote=null / no embedded rating. Result: Rust writes under 'admin' instead of 'earlysalty', diverging both the ON CONFLICT key and the own-vote/own-rating read path. Real, narrow-scope regression; Discord-admin/localhost/partner unaffected.

**Laufzeit-Check:** Log into the dashboard as earlysalty via Twitch-OAuth, submit a report rating and an A/B vote, then GET stream-report and ab-vote: rating embeds null and own_vote=null, and the DB row is stored with rated_by/voted_by='admin' rather than 'earlysalty'.

### P2.76 [analytics:api_admin] raw_chat_health live-scope ordering inverted (ASC->DESC) can hide a lagging live streamer

- **Python:** `bot/analytics/api_admin.py:557-564`
- **Rust:** `rust/crates/tb-analytics/src/system_health.rs:121-122`
- **Status:** changed

**Befund:** The raw-chat health probe drives RAW_CHAT_LAG warnings. By selecting the freshest signal among live streamers instead of the stalest, Rust can report a healthy lag while another live streamer's ingest is actually stalled, suppressing the warning. Off-by-direction regression in an alerting code path.

*Python-Soll:* bot/analytics/api_admin.py:557-564 live query ORDER BY COALESCE(last_raw_chat_message_at, last_raw_chat_insert_ok_at, last_raw_chat_insert_error_at, updated_at) ASC NULLS LAST LIMIT 1 — picks the LIVE streamer with the OLDEST (most stale) signal to surface worst-case RAW_CHAT_LAG.

*Rust-Ist:* rust/crates/tb-analytics/src/system_health.rs:121-122 ORDER BY newest_signal_at DESC NULLS LAST LIMIT 1, with newest_signal_at = GREATEST(...) — picks the live streamer with the NEWEST (healthiest) signal.

**Verifikation:** Confirmed in current code. Python (bot/analytics/api_admin.py:557-564) selects the live streamer via ORDER BY COALESCE(message,ok,error,updated) ASC NULLS LAST LIMIT 1 — i.e. the OLDEST/stalest signal, surfacing worst-case RAW_CHAT_LAG. Rust (rust/crates/tb-analytics/src/system_health.rs:121-122) selects via ORDER BY newest_signal_at DESC NULLS LAST LIMIT 1 with newest_signal_at = GREATEST(...) — i.e. the NEWEST/freshest signal among live streamers. The lag_seconds for the chosen row is computed the same way in both (newest signal of that row), so the divergence is purely the row-selection direction: when 2+ streamers are live concurrently, Rust picks the healthiest and reports a low lag while another live streamer's ingest may be stalled, suppressing the RAW_CHAT_LAG warning. This is an off-by-direction (ASC->DESC) regression in an alerting path, exactly as claimed. The Rust doc comment lists Bugs B/C/D but never flags this ordering as intentional. Severity stays medium: the bug only manifests with multiple simultaneous live streamers; with the common single-live-streamer case both produce identical results.

**Laufzeit-Check:** Seed twitch_raw_chat_ingest_health with two live streamers (one fresh signal, one stale/lagging), call the raw_chat_health endpoint, and confirm Rust returns the fresh streamer (low lag) while Python returns the stale one (high lag).

- **Python:** `api_admin.py:1495-1579`
- **Rust:** `rust/crates/tb-dashboard-api/src/handlers/system/eventsub.rs:45-83`
- **Status:** changed

**Befund:** The admin EventSub panel always shows the websocket as 'inactive' with 0 active subscriptions and 0/0 capacity even when subscriptions are live, because the Rust handler reads only the historical capacity snapshot table and never the live capacity overview. used is set from listener_count but max/remaining stay 0 so the capacity gauge is meaningless. *Rust-Ist:* rust/crates/tb-dashboard-api/src/handlers/system/eventsub.rs:45-83: hardcodes websocket_status='inactive', active_subscription_count=0, capacity.max=0, capacity.remaining=0 (explicit code comment lines 60-64 'OFFEN (Welle D) ... Platzhalter'); response struct (lines 19-28) omits websocketSessionId/websocketConnectedAt/websocketReconnectedAt/transportMode/raw; lastKnownSubscriptions (line 67) are raw parsed items with NO snapshotAt injected

**Verifikation:** Confirmed in current code. Rust handler (rust/crates/tb-dashboard-api/src/handlers/system/eventsub.rs:45-83) hardcodes websocket_status="inactive", active_subscription_count=0, capacity.max=0, capacity.remaining=0 in BOTH branches; used comes only from listener_count. The backing query (tb-analytics/src/system_eventsub.rs) reads ONLY twitch_eventsub_capacity_snapshot — there is no live capacity-overview equivalent anywhere in the tb-* crates (grep for used_slots/total_slots/transport/capacity_overview/websocket_session_id returns nothing but this handler). Response struct omits websocketSessionId/websocketConnectedAt/websocketReconnectedAt/transportMode/raw; last_known_subscriptions are raw parsed items with no snapshotAt injection. In-code comment lines 60-64 explicitly marks this deferred (OFFEN Welle D / Platzhalter). Genuine behavioral gap. ### P2.79 [analytics:api_admin] system/health drops analytics-db-fingerprint mismatch/check warnings and fingerprint fields

- **Python:** `api_admin.py:1407-1442`
- **Rust:** `rust/crates/tb-dashboard-api/src/handlers/system/health.rs:14-37`
- **Status:** missing

**Befund:** The Rust system/health endpoint cannot signal that the dashboard and bot service point at different analytics databases (the exact mismatch class called out in project memory as a real prod hazard). Admins lose the 'analytics_db_fingerprint_mismatch' error banner and the fingerprint values entirely.

*Python-Soll:* api_admin.py:1407-1442 & 1488-1491: computes analyticsDbFingerprint / internalAnalyticsDbFingerprint / analyticsDbFingerprintMismatch and appends serviceWarnings code 'analytics_db_fingerprint_mismatch' (level error) or 'analytics_db_fingerprint_check_failed' (level warning); response includes those four fields

*Rust-Ist:* rust/crates/tb-dashboard-api/src/handlers/system/health.rs:14-37 HealthResponse has no analyticsDbFingerprint*/internalAnalyticsDbFingerprint fields and the handler (44-119) never emits the two fingerprint warnings

**Verifikation:** Verified the gap in real code. Python (api_admin.py:1407-1442,1488-1491) reads three app-keys set at dashboard-service startup (app.py:722-826, which fetches the bot's internal-API analytics fingerprint, compares it to its own analytics-DB fingerprint, and sets ANALYTICS_DB_FINGERPRINT_MISMATCH_KEY/ERROR_KEY/INTERNAL_API_...KEY), then emits the four response fields (analyticsDbFingerprint, internalAnalyticsDbFingerprint, analyticsDbFingerprintMismatch + per-warning fields) and the two serviceWarnings codes analytics_db_fingerprint_mismatch (error) / analytics_db_fingerprint_check_failed (warning). The cited Rust handler (system/health.rs) HealthResponse (lines 23-37) has none of those four fields and the handler body (44-119) emits only RAW_CHAT_LAG and RAW_CHAT_ERROR — never the two fingerprint warnings. I grepped the entire rust/crates tree harder: the ONLY analytics-DB-fingerprint code is tb-internal-api/src/handlers/healthz.rs, which merely COMPUTES and returns the bot-side fingerprint (the upstream value the dashboard would compare against). There is no mismatch-comparison logic anywhere: tb-dashboard-api contains zero upstream-fingerprint fetch, zero mismatch app-state, and no INTERNAL_API_ANALYTICS_DB_FINGERPRINT equivalent (its 'fingerprint' hits are the unrelated session/device-binding RequestFingerprint). So the dashboard's system/health cannot signal the dashboard-vs-bot analytics-DB divergence — exactly the prod hazard flagged in project memory. Severity stays medium: the fingerprint primitive does exist in Rust (healthz), so the gap is the missing comparison + surfacing wiring rather than the whole concept; it is a real diagnostic blind spot, not a functional/security break.

**Laufzeit-Check:** Point the Rust dashboard and bot internal-API at two different analytics DBs, then GET /twitch/api/admin/system/health with admin auth and confirm the response has no analyticsDbFingerprintMismatch field nor an analytics_db_fingerprint_mismatch serviceWarning.

### P2.82 [analytics:backend_extended] Rust drops the follower_delta bogus-reset guard in metrics + trend SUM (counts deltas Python deliberately excludes)

- **Python:** `backend_extended.py:140-142`
- **Rust:** `streamer_analytics_native.rs:196`
- **Status:** changed

**Befund:** Sessions where followers_end=0 while followers_start>0 (a known data-glitch where the follower count failed to fetch and reset to 0, yielding a large negative follower_delta) are EXCLUDED in Python but COUNTED in Rust. Rust's total_followers_delta, followers_per_session, followers_per_hour, followers_trend and the insight conversion_rate are skewed (often strongly negative) versus Python whenever such a glitched session exists in the window. Note: the discovery_timeline SUM has no guard in Python either, so Rust matches there; the regression is specifically in the metrics and trend aggregates.

*Python-Soll:* backend_extended.py:140-142 (and 166-168, 266-268, 283-285): SUM(CASE WHEN s.follower_delta IS NOT NULL AND NOT (s.followers_end = 0 AND s.followers_start > 0) THEN s.follower_delta ELSE 0 END) as total_followers

*Rust-Ist:* streamer_analytics_native.rs:196 SUM(COALESCE(s.follower_delta, 0))::BIGINT (metrics) and :275 SUM(COALESCE(s.follower_delta, 0))::BIGINT (trend)

**Verifikation:** Verified in the real code. Python backend_extended.py uses SUM(CASE WHEN s.follower_delta IS NOT NULL AND NOT (s.followers_end = 0 AND s.followers_start > 0) THEN s.follower_delta ELSE 0 END) in BOTH the metrics query (lines 140-142, 166-168) and the trend query (lines 266-268, 283-285), deliberately excluding glitched sessions (followers_end=0 while followers_start>0, which produce a large negative delta). The Rust counterpart at rust/crates/tb-internal-api/src/handlers/streamer_analytics_native.rs uses SUM(COALESCE(s.follower_delta, 0))::BIGINT at line 196 (fetch_metrics) and line 275 (fetch_trend). COALESCE only reproduces Python's NULL guard; the bogus-reset exclusion is absent. grep across the file confirms followers_start/followers_end appear only in the discovery_timeline row struct (515-516, 558-559) and DDL, never in any aggregate filter for metrics or trend. So glitched sessions are excluded in Python but counted in Rust, skewing total_followers_delta, followers_per_session, followers_per_hour, followers_trend and downstream insights. Note the discovery_timeline (line 371) also lacks the guard, but Python's discovery query lacks it too, so that one matches — the regression is specifically the metrics+trend aggregates as claimed. Severity corrected to medium: the divergence is real and silently skews several user-facing follower metrics, but only triggers when a specific data-glitch session exists in the window; it does not break functionality.

**Laufzeit-Check:** Insert a session with followers_start>0 and followers_end=0 (negative follower_delta) in the window, then compare the Python analytics endpoint's total_followers_delta against the Rust internal-api streamer-analytics response for the same login/window — Python excludes it, Rust includes the negative delta.

Code comment at :1666 explicitly admits 'Vereinfachung: nur eigener Login + Standard-Bots' but no KNOWN_CHAT_BOTS NOT IN clause is bound. nightbot/streamelements/moobot/fossabot/streamlabs are counted as viewers.

Rust counterpart at rust/crates/tb-dashboard-api/src/handlers/internal_home.rs:1668-1681 (cited path was wrong by a 'rust/' prefix, found via find) has community_sql with WHERE only 'AND LOWER(COALESCE(sc.chatter_login,'')) <> LOWER($1)' — own login only, no NOT IN bot list, no binds for bots. The comment at :1666 falsely claims 'nur eigener Login + Standard-Bots' but no standard-bot filter exists. grep for KNOWN_CHAT_BOTS / any bot name across all crates/*.rs returned zero hits, so the filter is genuinely absent, not implemented elsewhere. The gap inflates total_viewers (bots counted) and returning_viewers (bots are not first-time-streamers), skewing the returning ratio and thus the 20%-weighted community sub-score and overall health number vs Python. ### P2.84 [analytics:api_v2] GET /twitch/api/v2/streamers dropdown drops recent (90-day) non-partner streamers and the partner-table existence fallback

- **Python:** `bot/analytics/api_v2.py:574-623`
- **Rust:** `/home/naniadm/Documents/Deadlock-Twitch-Bot/rust/crates/tb-analytics/src/streamers.rs:13-32`
- **Status:** missing

Admin/comparison views that relied on seeing recent non-partner streamers lose those entries. Rust also adds is_live/viewer_count ordering (an improvement), but the dropped 90-day union and the isPartner flag are behavioral regressions vs Python.

*Python-Soll:* bot/analytics/api_v2.py:574-623 _load_api_v2_streamers_data: probes `SELECT 1 FROM twitch_streamers_partner_state LIMIT 1` (has_partner_table fallback); builds partner_logins from is_partner_active=1 UNION recent_logins from `SELECT DISTINCT LOWER(streamer_login) FROM twitch_stream_sessions WHERE started_at >= NOW() - INTERVAL '90 days'`; returns {login, isPartner: login in partner_logins} over sorted(partner|recent) — so non-partner streamers active in last 90 days appear with isPartner=false.

*Rust-Ist:* /home/naniadm/Documents/Deadlock-Twitch-Bot/rust/crates/tb-analytics/src/streamers.rs:13-32 active_streamers selects ONLY `FROM twitch_streamers_partner_state WHERE is_partner_active = 1` (no twitch_stream_sessions 90-day union, no table-existence probe); /home/naniadm/Documents/Deadlock-Twitch-Bot/rust/crates/tb-dashboard-api/src/handlers/streamers.rs:22-30 StreamerJson::from hardcodes `is_partner: true`.

**Verifikation:** Verified in actual code. Python _load_api_v2_streamers_data (api_v2.py:574-623) returns the UNION of partner logins (is_partner_active=1) and recent_logins (DISTINCT LOWER(streamer_login) FROM twitch_stream_sessions WHERE started_at >= NOW() - INTERVAL '90 days'), with isPartner=(login in partner_logins), plus a has_partner_table existence probe. The single Rust implementation tb-analytics/src/streamers.rs:13-32 active_streamers queries ONLY twitch_streamers_partner_state WHERE is_partner_active = 1 — no 90-day twitch_stream_sessions union, no table-existence probe. The handler tb-dashboard-api/src/handlers/streamers.rs:22-30 hardcodes is_partner: true for every row. grep across all crates/*.rs found no alternative query supplying the 90-day session union for /twitch/api/v2/streamers (route bound once at lib.rs:243-244 -> streamers_handler -> active_streamers). Rust tests even assert the narrower behavior (non-partner streamer_b excluded; isPartner always true). So recently-active non-partner streamers are dropped from the admin dropdown — a real behavioral regression. Severity lowered to medium: admin-only endpoint, result set is narrower not broken, the dropped table-existence probe is moot (table exists in migrated schema), and the is_partner=true flag is harmless given all returned rows are partners now.

**Laufzeit-Check:** Seed twitch_stream_sessions with a recent (under 90 days) non-partner login absent from twitch_streamers_partner_state, then call GET /twitch/api/v2/streamers as admin and check that login is missing from the Rust response but present in Python.

### P2.85 [analytics:api_v2] _require_v2_auth access-denied JSON payloads not reproduced for v2 JSON APIs (error contract divergence)

- **Python:** `api_v2.py:1269-1289`
- **Rust:** `tb-dashboard-api/src/handlers/spa.rs:139-150`
- **Status:** changed

**Befund:** Block is still enforced (partner_status_gate + extended_gate deny access), so no security regression, but the JSON error contract differs: a frontend that branches on error code (account_blocked vs dashboard_access_restricted) or reads redirectUrl/technicalPauseReason from the denial body will not receive those fields and may mis-route or show a generic error instead of the intended redirect.

*Python-Soll:* api_v2.py:1269-1289 + _analytics_access_denied_payload(1184-1209)/_landing_access_denied_payload(1211-1220): blocked/departnered/restricted partners hitting /twitch/api/* get HTTP 403 JSON with error='account_blocked'|'dashboard_access_restricted', plus redirectUrl, partnerStatus, technicalPauseReason, operationalState, tokenErrorGraceExpiresAt; admin-host non-priv → 403 error='admin_required'+auth_level

*Rust-Ist:* tb-dashboard-api/src/handlers/spa.rs:139-150 (SPA path: plain-text 403 / redirect, no JSON fields); tb-dashboard-api/src/auth/mod.rs:124-153 extended_gate emits only 401 loginUrl OR 403 plan_required; auth/partner_gate.rs:162-171 emits 403 error='partner_inactive'+reauth_url. No handler emits error='dashboard_access_restricted'/'account_blocked' with redirectUrl/technicalPauseReason/operationalState/tokenErrorGraceExpiresAt (searched: grep 'dashboard_access_restricted|account_blocked|redirectUrl|technicalPauseReason' crates/ → only auth_status.rs success payload)

**Verifikation:** Independently confirmed. Python _require_v2_auth (bot/analytics/api_v2.py:1269-1289) returns, for blocked/restricted partners hitting /twitch/api/*, HTTP 403 JSON via _landing_access_denied_payload (1211-1220, error='account_blocked') and _analytics_access_denied_payload (1184-1209, error='account_blocked' or 'dashboard_access_restricted'), each carrying message, partnerStatus, redirectUrl, technicalPauseReason, operationalState (+ tokenErrorGraceExpiresAt on the analytics variant). No Rust denial path reproduces this contract. Searched the whole rust/ tree: the only emitter of technicalPauseReason/operationalState/tokenErrorGraceExpiresAt/account_blocked/dashboard_access_restricted is handlers/auth_status.rs, and that is the GET auth-status SUCCESS payload, not a denial body. The actual v2-API denial paths emit different shapes: (a) spa.rs:139-150 returns a plain-text 403 'This account is blocked...' or a redirect to the legacy dashboard, with no JSON fields; (b) auth/mod.rs extended_gate (109-153) emits only 401 {error,loginUrl} or 403 plan_required {error,required_entitlements,required_plans}; (c) auth/partner_gate.rs partner_status_gate (162-171) emits 403 {error:'partner_inactive', message, reauth_url}. AccessState (tb-analytics/partner_access.rs) does carry technical_pause_reason/operational_state/token_error_grace_expires_at, but those are only surfaced in auth-status, never reconstructed into a denial body. The block is still enforced (partner_status_gate + extended_gate deny access), so no security regression — but the JSON error contract genuinely differs: a frontend branching on error=='account_blocked'/'dashboard_access_restricted' or reading redirectUrl/technicalPauseReason from a denial response on /twitch/api/* will not receive those fields and may mis-route or show a generic error. Severity medium is appropriate (contract/behavioral divergence, no security impact).

**Laufzeit-Check:** As a blocked/departnered partner (twitch_dash_session set), curl a /twitch/api/v2/* analytics endpoint and inspect the 403 body: Rust returns plain-text/partner_inactive/plan_required, Python returns JSON with error=account_blocked|dashboard_access_restricted + redirectUrl + technicalPauseReason.

- **Python:** `api_v2.py:2007-2013`
- **Rust:** `internal_home.rs:18`
- **Status:** missing

**Befund:** Both internal-home read and changelog-write endpoints lose their abuse throttle. No 429/Retry-After is ever emitted. Write endpoint is admin-gated so blast radius is limited, but the read endpoint is reachable by any authenticated session and now has no per-route cap. Behavioral drop vs Python.

*Python-Soll:* api_v2.py:2007-2013 GET calls _internal_home_rate_limit_response(max=_INTERNAL_HOME_RATE_LIMIT_MAX_REQUESTS, window=_INTERNAL_HOME_RATE_LIMIT_WINDOW_SECONDS); api_v2.py:2096-2102 changelog POST calls it with the changelog-write limits. _internal_home_rate_limit_response (1710-1772) returns 429 {error:rate_limit_exceeded} + Retry-After header when the limiter denies.

**Verifikation:** Independently confirmed the gap. Python api_v2.py:1710-1772 defines _internal_home_rate_limit_response returning HTTP 429 {error:rate_limit_exceeded} + Retry-After header when the limiter denies; it is invoked on GET (2007-2013) with the read limits and on changelog POST (2096-2102) with the changelog-write limits. In Rust, internal_home.rs:18 explicitly documents 'Rate-Limit ... NICHT portiert'; get_handler (263-427) and changelog_handler (1991-2092) contain no rate-limit call. grep across the whole tb-dashboard-api crate shows no rate_limit/Retry-After/429 path in internal_home.rs. The exported RateLimiter (auth/security.rs) is only used for dashboard AUTH rate-limiting, not these routes; route wiring in lib.rs:152-157 attaches get/post handlers directly with no governor/tower rate-limit layer (no governor dep in Cargo.toml). So neither endpoint emits 429/Retry-After — a genuine behavioral drop vs Python. Severity stays medium: it is a defense-in-depth abuse throttle, not an auth/correctness hole; the write route is admin-gated, and the read route still requires an authenticated session, only losing its per-route cap.

**Laufzeit-Check:** Hammer GET /twitch/api/v2/internal-home rapidly with one authenticated session and confirm no 429/Retry-After ever returns in Rust, whereas Python returns 429 after the configured max within the window.

### P2.87 [analytics:admin_streamer_queries] DB overview row counts switched from exact COUNT(*) to reltuples estimate

- **Python:** `admin_streamer_queries.py:827`
- **Rust:** `system_database.rs:38-51`
- **Status:** changed

**Befund:** The admin 'System / Database' overview now shows approximate row counts instead of exact ones. For freshly created or never-analyzed tables PostgreSQL reports reltuples = 0 (or -1 in PG14+, clamped to 0 by .max(0)), so a populated-but-unanalyzed table shows 0 rows. After bulk inserts/deletes the figure can be stale by a large margin until autovacuum runs. The module docstring (system_database.rs:3) acknowledges this as intentional ('Schätzung, wie Python-Seite') but the Python side actually does exact COUNT(*), so the doc's premise is wrong and this is a genuine divergence from the source of truth. Impact is display-only (admin diagnostics), not a data/security issue, hence medium. If exact counts are desired this should be COUNT(*); if estimate is an accepted improvement for large-table performance, it is a deliberate (but undocumented-as-such) trade-off the user should confirm.

*Python-Soll:* admin_streamer_queries.py:827 — row = conn.execute(f"SELECT COUNT(*) AS total FROM {table_name}").fetchone(); returns exact live row count per table.

*Rust-Ist:* system_database.rs:38-51 — SELECT reltuples::BIGINT FROM pg_class WHERE relname=$1 ...; then count.max(0) at line 62. reltuples is the planner's row ESTIMATE maintained by ANALYZE/autovacuum, not an exact count.

**Verifikation:** Independently confirmed in current code. Python _admin_database_row_count (bot/analytics/admin_streamer_queries.py:827) runs exact `SELECT COUNT(*) AS total FROM {table}`. The Rust counterpart database_stats (rust/crates/tb-analytics/src/system_database.rs:38-51) instead runs `SELECT reltuples::BIGINT FROM pg_class WHERE relname=$1 ...` and pushes count.max(0) (line 62) as row_count. reltuples is the planner's ANALYZE/autovacuum-maintained estimate, not a live count. The admin endpoint GET /twitch/api/admin/system/database (rust/crates/tb-dashboard-api/src/handlers/system/database.rs:47) is wired to this single function — no alternate exact-count path exists in the tb-* crates (grep for COUNT(*) returns only unrelated stats/test queries). Consequence: never-analyzed tables report reltuples=0 (or -1 clamped to 0), and post-bulk-insert/delete figures are stale until autovacuum. The module docstring (line 3) claims 'Schätzung, wie Python-Seite' but Python is exact, so the documented justification is factually wrong. Display-only admin diagnostics, no data/security impact — medium is correct.

**Laufzeit-Check:** On the admin System/Database overview, insert many rows into a tracked table without running ANALYZE and compare the displayed row count to a manual exact COUNT(*) — they will diverge (estimate stale or zero) until autovacuum/ANALYZE runs.

The Rust retention_curve_handler (retention_curve.rs lines 99-138) computes drop_events from the median curve and hardcodes "type": "unknown" at line 134 — there is no query against twitch_ad_break_events in this handler. ### P2.90 [analytics:api_performance] Leaderboard yourTier uses filtered avg and never returns null, unlike Python peer-group logic

- **Python:** `api_performance.py:1238-1240`
- **Rust:** `category_leaderboard.rs:185-209`
- **Status:** changed

**Befund:** When the request uses tier= or exclude_external=1 (HAVING threshold), Rust's my_avg comes from the filtered set (or session fallback) instead of the full category average, so yourTier can be a different bucket. Additionally Rust returns a yourTier where Python returns null (no qualifying peers). Affects the 'your tier' indicator shown to extended-plan streamers.

*Python-Soll:* api_performance.py:1238-1240 your_tier = _get_peer_group_stats(...)['tier']; _get_peer_group_stats (210-276) derives my_avg from an UNFILTERED all-category AVG query and RETURNS None when no same-tier peers with session metrics exist (peer_logins empty -> return None at 275-276).

*Rust-Ist:* category_leaderboard.rs:185-209: your_tier computed from your_avg_opt (taken from the tier/threshold-FILTERED leaderboard rows, set at 165-167) or a twitch_stream_sessions AVG fallback, then get_tier(avg); always Some(tier) when any avg exists — no peer-existence null path.

**Verifikation:** Confirmed in current code. Python derives yourTier via _get_peer_group_stats (api_performance.py:210-276), whose my_avg comes from an UNFILTERED all-category AVG (223-231), independent of the request's tier= / exclude_external= params, and it returns None (yourTier=null) when no same-tier peers exist (275-276). Rust (category_leaderboard.rs) sets your_avg_opt from the FILTERED leaderboard rows: rows are produced by SQL that applies HAVING AVG(viewer_count) <= 100 when exclude_external (85), and `filtered` further narrows by tier range (125-132); your_avg_opt is taken at 152 from those rows. So when exclude_external=1 or tier= is set and the streamer's true average is excluded, Rust's avg differs from Python's full category average (it falls to a twitch_stream_sessions AVG fallback at 181-194). Furthermore Rust computes your_tier = avg.map(|a| get_tier(a)) (197) — always Some(tier) when any avg exists, with no peer-existence null path. Both divergences claimed (filtered/fallback avg producing a different bucket; and Some vs Python's null) are real. Impact is limited to the yourTier indicator string for extended-plan streamers; rankings/counts unaffected, so medium is correct.

**Laufzeit-Check:** Call /twitch/api/v2/category-leaderboard with streamer=<a streamer whose true category avg exceeds 100> and exclude_external=1 (or a tier= that excludes them) against both Python and Rust endpoints and compare the yourTier field.

**Verifikation:** Confirmed in current code. The Rust handler chat_deep_minimax.rs:82 calls EngagementMinimaxClient::raw_completion(...), which (minimax_chat.rs:337-352) discards the token counts via `let (raw_text, _, _, _)` and never calls tb_llm::ledger::record. The ledger::record call exists ONLY in generate() (minimax_chat.rs:316), not in the raw_completion/messages_completion/messages_completion_uncapped paths. grep across tb-engagement/tb-dashboard-api/tb-analytics confirms no other ledger write in the handler chain. So every chat-deep-minimax request consumes paid tokens but books 0 into the usage ledger — a genuine missing side-effect vs Python. Note: this is a wider pattern affecting other raw_completion/messages_completion_uncapped callers too, but the specific claim is accurate.
