### P2.92 [analytics:api_audience] Peak-hours chat-message query drops anonymous (NULL chatter_login) rows that Python keeps

- **Python:** `api_audience.py:155-170`
- **Rust:** `audience_demographics.rs:91-98`
- **Status:** changed

**Befund:** Anonymous chat messages (chatter_login NULL) are excluded from the weighted peak-hours computation in Rust but included in Python. This lowers total_samples, per-hour counts, sessionsWithActivity and coverage, which can flip peakQualityMethod (no_data/low_coverage/real_samples) and confidence, and can change or suppress the returned peakActivityHours array. Streamers whose chat has a notable share of NULL-login (anonymous/legacy) message rows would see different — and emptier/lower-confidence — peak-hour analytics than the Python source of truth. Empty-string logins are still kept (since '' is not in the bots list), so the gap is specifically NULL logins. Note: this construct (_compute_weighted_peak_hours) lives in the audited range (lines 118-209); its Rust port is in audience_demographics.rs.

*Python-Soll:* api_audience.py:155-170 builds the message filter via build_known_chat_bot_not_in_clause(column_expr='cm.chatter_login'); that clause (bot/core/chat_bots.py:58-61) is "((cm.chatter_login) IS NULL OR (cm.chatter_login) = '' OR LOWER(cm.chatter_login) NOT IN (...))" — i.e. messages with a NULL or empty chatter_login are explicitly KEPT and counted into per_session_hours/total_samples.

*Rust-Ist:* audience_demographics.rs:91-98 — the peak-hours message query filters with "AND NOT (cm.chatter_login = ANY($3::text[]))". For a NULL chatter_login, "NULL = ANY(...)" yields NULL, so "NOT NULL" is NULL and the row is DROPPED. No "IS NULL OR = ''" branch is present (contrast watch_time.rs:125, where the equivalent backfill query correctly keeps NULL/empty logins).

**Verifikation:** Verified in current code. Python api_audience.py:155-170 uses build_known_chat_bot_not_in_clause (chat_bots.py:58-61) which yields "(chatter_login IS NULL OR chatter_login = '' OR LOWER(chatter_login) NOT IN (...))" — NULL/empty logins are explicitly KEPT (docstring chat_bots.py:43-44 confirms intent: keep anonymous rows). Rust audience_demographics.rs:91-98 filters with only "AND NOT (cm.chatter_login = ANY($3::text[]))". For NULL chatter_login, "NULL = ANY(...)" is NULL, "NOT NULL" is NULL, and a NULL WHERE predicate excludes the row, so NULL-login messages are dropped. The "IS NULL OR = ''" branch is absent from the Rust port. Empty-string logins still survive in both ('' = ANY(bots) is FALSE, not NULL), so the gap is specifically NULL logins — exactly as claimed. This lowers total_samples/per-hour counts/sessions_with_activity/coverage in Rust, which can change peakQualityMethod, confidence, and the returned peak-hours array vs the Python source of truth.

**Laufzeit-Check:** For a streamer with NULL chatter_login chat rows in the window, call the v2 audience-demographics endpoint and compare sampleCount/coverage/peakActivityHours against the Python analytics output for the same streamer/days/timezone — Rust will report fewer samples and lower coverage.

### P2.93 [analytics:api_audience] follower-funnel confidence threshold formula changed for low session counts

- **Python:** `api_audience.py:715`
- **Rust:** `follower_funnel.rs:207`
- **Status:** changed

**Befund:** Python applies max(3,...) to the THRESHOLD; Rust applies max(3,session_count) to session_count BEFORE the 0.6 factor. They diverge for session_count 1-4: e.g. session_count=1, follower_valid_samples=1 -> Python threshold 3 => 'medium', Rust threshold (max(1,3)*3/5=1) => 'high'. Streamers with very few sessions get a wrongly inflated 'high' confidence label in dataQuality.confidence. Verified across session_count 1-100; mismatch only at 1,2,3,4.

*Python-Soll:* api_audience.py:715 elif follower_valid_samples >= max(3, int(session_count * 0.6)): confidence = 'high'

*Rust-Ist:* follower_funnel.rs:207 } else if follower_valid_samples >= session_count.max(3) * 3 / 5 {

**Verifikation:** Confirmed in current code. Python (api_audience.py:715) computes the 'high' threshold as max(3, int(session_count*0.6)) — int() truncates the 0.6 product first, then max(3,...) clamps the THRESHOLD, so for session_count 1-4 the threshold is always 3. Rust (follower_funnel.rs:207) computes session_count.max(3) * 3 / 5 — it clamps session_count to >=3 BEFORE multiplying by 0.6, yielding integer thresholds of 1 (sc 1-3) and 2 (sc 4). Concrete divergence: session_count=1, follower_valid_samples=1 -> Python threshold 3 => 'medium', Rust threshold 1 => 'high'. They match for session_count>=5 (both reduce to floor(sc*0.6)>=3). Mismatch only at sc 1,2,3,4, exactly as claimed. This is a genuine, unfixed port regression: streamers with very few sessions get an inflated 'high' dataQuality.confidence. The repo's own audit notes (2026-06-13 line 870, 2026-06-14 line 515, findings-2026-06-15.json) flag the identical bug and the suggested fix has not been applied — line 207 still holds the buggy formula. Severity medium is appropriate: cosmetic confidence label only, no data corruption, narrow input range.

**Laufzeit-Check:** Hit the follower-funnel dashboard endpoint for a channel with session_count 1-4 and follower_valid_samples=1 and check dataQuality.confidence returns 'high' (Rust) vs 'medium' (Python).

### P2.97 [analytics:api_audience] Demographics handler has no top-level error catch: DB failure returns zeroed 200 instead of Python's 500

- **Python:** `api_audience.py:1065`
- **Rust:** `audience_demographics.rs has no equivalent catch: every query uses .unwrap_or_default() (e`
- **Status:** changed

**Befund:** On a backend/DB fault, Python returns 500 (client can detect failure) while Rust silently returns success with empty/zero metrics, masking outages and feeding misleading 0-values to the dashboard. Note loyalty_curve.rs:58-62 DOES map Err->500 faithfully; only the demographics handler diverges.

*Python-Soll:* api_audience.py:1065 wraps the entire body in try/except; 1579-1581 catches Exception -> log + analytics_internal_error_response() (HTTP 500). Any query/transaction failure surfaces as an error response.

*Rust-Ist:* audience_demographics.rs has no equivalent catch: every query uses .unwrap_or_default() (e.g. lines 186,208,274,388) or .fetch_optional(...).await.ok().flatten() (221,235,352,363). A DB error therefore yields empty rows -> a fully-formed 200 response with zeroed analytics.

**Verifikation:** Confirmed in real code. Python api_audience.py wraps the whole demographics body in try (line 1065) and lines 1579-1581 catch Exception -> log + analytics_internal_error_response() = HTTP 500. Rust audience_demographics_handler (audience_demographics.rs) has NO top-level error catch: every query uses .fetch_all(&pool).await.unwrap_or_default() (lines 78,98,186,208,274,388) or .fetch_optional(&pool).await.ok().flatten() (221,235,352,363), so a DB error yields empty/None, which flows through the counting logic into a fully-formed Json 200 with zeroed metrics (lines 461-505). The handler only ever returns 400 (gate/missing streamer) or 200 — no Err->500 path exists. The cited contrast holds: loyalty_curve.rs:58-62 explicitly maps Err->INTERNAL_SERVER_ERROR, so only the demographics handler diverges. On a DB fault Python signals failure (500) while Rust silently returns success with zero analytics, masking outages.

**Laufzeit-Check:** Stop/break the dashboard DB (or point at a missing table) and call GET /twitch/api/v2/audience-demographics?streamer=x; observe HTTP 200 with all-zero metrics in Rust vs HTTP 500 from the Python endpoint.

### P2.98 [analytics:api_raids] raid-analytics swallows DB query errors (empty 200) instead of Python's 500 error response

- **Python:** `api_raids.py:45-475`
- **Rust:** `raid_analytics.rs:393`
- **Status:** changed

**Befund:** A transient DB failure or schema problem during raid-analytics surfaces to the client as 'no raids / empty analytics' (success) rather than an error, masking outages and showing misleading zeroed analytics to the streamer. Python would have returned a clear 500. Inconsistent with the adjacent retention handler. Recommend mapping the three primary query Results to a 500 like raid_retention_handler does.

*Python-Soll:* api_raids.py:45-475 entire body inside try/except; api_raids.py:473-475 except Exception: log.exception(...) ; return analytics_internal_error_response() — any DB/query failure yields the standard internal-error (500) response.

*Rust-Ist:* raid_analytics.rs has NO outer error wrapper around the analytics body; the main queries use .fetch_all(&pool).await.unwrap_or_default() (raid_analytics.rs:393 retention_rows, :495 follow_rows, :569 incoming_raw) and per-raid sub-queries use .ok().flatten() (:595, :606, :651). On a DB error these return empty vectors, so the handler responds 200 with empty per_source/follow_attribution/null incoming_summary instead of an error. (Note: the sibling raid_retention_handler at :232-238 DOES map errors to 500; raid_analytics_handler does not.)

**Verifikation:** Confirmed in current code. Python _api_v2_raid_analytics wraps the entire analytics body in try/except Exception (api_raids.py:45-475): on any DB/query failure it logs and returns analytics_internal_error_response() (a 500). The Rust counterpart raid_analytics_handler (rust/crates/tb-dashboard-api/src/handlers/raid_analytics.rs:364-720 — note the file is under tb-dashboard-api/src/handlers/, not the cited path, but it is the correct port) has NO outer error wrapper. Its three primary queries use .fetch_all(&pool).await.unwrap_or_default() (retention_rows :393, follow_rows :495, incoming_raw :569) and per-raid sub-queries use .ok().flatten()/.ok() (:595, :606, :651). On a DB error these collapse to empty vectors, so the handler falls through to the final Json(...).into_response() at :706 and returns 200 with empty per_source, follow_attribution=null, incoming_summary=null — instead of Python's 500. The adjacent raid_retention_handler at :232-238 DOES match base_rows Err -> 500 INTERNAL_SERVER_ERROR, proving the inconsistency. So a transient DB/schema failure is masked as 'empty analytics' success. Severity medium is appropriate: read-only analytics endpoint, no data corruption, but it hides outages and shows misleading zeroed data to the streamer.</reason>
<parameter name="verify_hint">Hit GET /twitch/api/v2/raid-analytics for a valid extended-plan streamer while the relevant raid tables are renamed/dropped or the DB is unreachable; Rust returns HTTP 200 with empty per_source and null follow_attribution/incoming_summary, whereas Python returns HTTP 500.

### P2.99 [dashboard:partner_registry] Block does not disable twitch_raid_auth in Rust (raid stays armed on blocked streamer)

- **Python:** `bot/storage/partner_registry.py:1613-1632`
- **Rust:** `tb-analytics/src/streamers_crud.rs:628-686`
- **Status:** missing

**Befund:** Blocking a partner via the dashboard no longer disarms their stored raid OAuth. A blocked streamer can still be picked up by raid/auth flows that gate on twitch_raid_auth.raid_enabled, and needs_reauth/reauth_notified_at are not cleared. Behavioral on/off divergence vs Python; the block becomes incomplete.

*Python-Soll:* bot/storage/partner_registry.py:1613-1632 — set_streamer_block_state (called from mixin L83 via the block/unblock/toggle_block branch L66-94) runs a second UPDATE on twitch_raid_auth: SET raid_enabled = CASE WHEN <blocked> THEN FALSE ELSE raid_enabled END, needs_reauth=... ELSE FALSE, reauth_notified_at=... ELSE NULL, plus _normalize_related_tables.

*Rust-Ist:* tb-analytics/src/streamers_crud.rs:628-686 — archive_streamer Block/ToggleBlock only UPDATE twitch_partners (technical_pause_reason='blocked', manual_partner_opt_out=1, raid_bot_enabled=0). No UPDATE of twitch_raid_auth.raid_enabled and no related-table normalization. (searched: grep -n 'twitch_raid_auth' streamers_crud.rs — only in test DDL).

**Verifikation:** Confirmed in current code. Python dashboard block path: bot/dashboard/streamer_admin_mixin.py:66-94 routes block/unblock/toggle_block to storage.set_streamer_block_state (L83), which (partner_registry.py:1613-1632) runs a second UPDATE on twitch_raid_auth setting raid_enabled=FALSE and clearing needs_reauth/reauth_notified_at when blocked, plus _normalize_related_tables (L1633). Rust block path routes block exclusively through archive_streamer (tb-analytics/src/streamers_crud.rs:628-686 Block/ToggleBlock), which only UPDATEs twitch_partners (technical_pause_reason='blocked', manual_partner_opt_out=1, raid_bot_enabled=0). Both Rust call sites confirm this is the only block logic: streamer_lifecycle.rs:869-888 and dashboard-api block_handler->run_archive (admin_streamers.rs:563-578,522-536). No UPDATE of twitch_raid_auth.raid_enabled and no related-table normalization on block; raid_enabled is only disabled in the separate departner_streamer/departner_active_partner flow (streamers_crud.rs:514-526), not invoked by block. _normalize_related_tables is explicitly documented as not ported (streamer_lifecycle.rs:216-217). Severity lowered to medium: Rust does mirror raid_bot_enabled=0 on twitch_partners, so partner-status-gated raid selection is covered; only the stored OAuth row twitch_raid_auth.raid_enabled and the two reauth flags stay armed. Real exploitability depends on raid candidate selection gating on twitch_raid_auth.raid_enabled independently of partner status — plausible (token_store/partner_roster reference raid_enabled) but not a guaranteed always-on hole, so high overstates it.

**Laufzeit-Check:** Block a partner via the dashboard, then query twitch_raid_auth for that twitch_user_id and check raid_enabled is still TRUE (and needs_reauth/reauth_notified_at unchanged) under Rust, vs FALSE/cleared under Python.

### P2.100 [dashboard:streamer_admin_mixin] Erfolgs- und Fehlschlag-DM an Streamer komplett entfernt (B10-Drop) — Verifizierungsergebnis erreicht den Streamer nicht mehr

- **Python:** `streamer_admin_mixin.py:437-472`
- **Rust:** `ABSENT`
- **Status:** missing

**Befund:** User-sichtbarer Funktionsverlust: Bei manueller Verifizierung (Erfolg) bekommt der Streamer keine Bestätigungs-DM mehr; bei Fehlschlag keine Aufforderung, die Voraussetzungen zu prüfen und /streamer erneut zu starten. Laut Memory-Direktive B10 bewusst entfernt — daher Information, kein Bug. Falls die DM-Kommunikation noch erwünscht ist, dem User vorlegen.

*Python-Soll:* streamer_admin_mixin.py:437-472 (_notify_verification_success sendet VERIFICATION_SUCCESS_DM_MESSAGE) und :518-568 (failed-Pfad sendet langen Fehlertext 'Hey! Deine Deadlock-Streamer-Verifizierung konnte leider nicht abgeschlossen werden...' + 4 differenzierte Rückmeldungen je nach Zustellbarkeit)

*Rust-Ist:* ABSENT — grep -rn 'VERIFICATION_SUCCESS|notify_verification|fehlgeschlagene Verifizierung|user.send' crates/tb-internal-api crates/tb-dashboard-api liefert nichts; tb-internal-api/src/handlers/streamers.rs:552 + :671-672 dokumentieren den Drop explizit ('per B10-Direktive gedroppt'). Rust antwortet bei failed nur '{login}: Verifizierung fehlgeschlagen {role_note}' (streamers.rs:679-683).

**Verifikation:** Independently confirmed in current code. Python (bot/dashboard/streamer_admin_mixin.py — auditor's path was slightly off, file is under bot/dashboard/) actively sends DMs: success path (_notify_verification_success, lines 437-472) sends VERIFICATION_SUCCESS_DM_MESSAGE; failed path (lines 537-568) sends the detailed "Hey! Deine Deadlock-Streamer-Verifizierung konnte leider nicht abgeschlossen werden..." text with differentiated delivery feedback. The Rust port (crates/tb-internal-api/src/handlers/streamers.rs verify_handler) has NO DM capability whatsoever: the permanent/temp success branch (596-646) calls no notification function at all, and the failed branch (664-684) only departners + revokes role, returning "{login}: Verifizierung fehlgeschlagen {role_note}". A hard grep across all tb-* crates for user.send/send_dm/VERIFICATION_SUCCESS/the German DM text returns ONLY documentation comments (streamers.rs:38-39, 546-552, 671-672) that explicitly state the DMs were dropped per B10-Direktive. No counterpart exists under any other name/module. So the gap is real. Per the in-code comments and the B10 directive in memory, this is an intentional drop ("keine Discord-DMs mehr"), not an accidental regression — hence informational, but the user-visible behavior loss is genuine and confirmed. Medium severity holds: communication loss only on manual admin verification, by design.

**Laufzeit-Check:** In the Rust dashboard, run a manual streamer verify (success and failed) and confirm the linked Discord account receives no DM, only a role change and a short status message.

### P2.101 [dashboard:routes_title] Dashboard title suggestion drops Deadlock rank + live-state context (rank_display / live_state never passed to generate_title)

- **Python:** `routes_title.py:133-141,149-150`
- **Rust:** `title.rs:186-187`
- **Status:** missing

**Befund:** Titles generated from the dashboard 'Vorschlag' button never incorporate the streamer's Deadlock rank or live game state. generate_title in Rust fully supports both (title_ai.rs:404-407 builds rank_line/live_line and sanitize uses rank_display), and the chat-command path wires them — so dashboard suggestions are systematically lower quality than the equivalent !title command and than the Python dashboard. Likely root cause: steam_lookup rank/live functions take a SQLite db_path which the dashboard handler (Postgres-only) was not given, so the dependency was silently dropped.

*Python-Soll:* routes_title.py:133-141,149-150: discord_user_id = _get_discord_user_id(twitch_user_id); if discord_user_id: rank_info = await get_rank_for_discord_user(...); rank_display = ...; if include_live: live_state = await get_live_state_for_discord_user(...); then generate_title(..., rank_display=rank_display, live_state=live_state, ...)

*Rust-Ist:* title.rs:186-187: generate_title(limiter, &user_id, keywords, &prompt_history, &prompt_knowledge, None, None, "dashboard") — rank_display and live_state hardcoded None. ABSENT (searched: grep -rn get_rank_for_discord_user / get_live_state_for_discord_user / discord_user_id in tb-dashboard-api -> no hits; the resolution exists only in tb-chat/commands.rs:582-646 for the !title chat command, not the dashboard route).

**Verifikation:** Independently confirmed. Python bot/dashboard/routes_title.py:133-152 resolves discord_user_id then fetches rank_display (get_rank_for_discord_user) and live_state (get_live_state_for_discord_user when include_live) and passes both into generate_title. The Rust dashboard handler tb-dashboard-api/src/handlers/title.rs:180-189 calls generate_title(..., None, None, "dashboard") with rank_display and live_state hardcoded None. Grep across the entire tb-dashboard-api crate finds no rank/live resolution for the title route (the discord_user_id/live_state hits are all unrelated handlers: leaderboard, network, internal_home). The resolution exists only in the chat path tb-chat/src/commands.rs:632-666, which correctly populates and forwards both args. The params are load-bearing: title_ai.rs:404-407 injects rank_line/live_line into the prompt and sanitize_generated_title (title_ai.rs:213,238,282) gates rank words via rank_display. So dashboard 'Vorschlag' titles never incorporate Deadlock rank or live state, making them systematically lower quality than both the !title chat command and the Python dashboard. Root cause is consistent with the auditor's note: rank/live live in steam_lookup keyed on a SQLite steam_db_path() that the Postgres-only dashboard handler never reaches for. Severity corrected to medium: this is degradation of an opt-in enrichment, not a broken/missing core feature — titles still generate.

**Laufzeit-Check:** From the dashboard, click the title 'Vorschlag' button for a streamer who has a linked Deadlock rank, and confirm the suggested title ignores rank context while the same streamer's !title chat command reflects it.

### P2.102 [dashboard:routes_title] include_live request flag is a no-op in Rust dashboard suggest (parsed and echoed but never fetches live_state)

- **Python:** `routes_title.py:114,140-141`
- **Rust:** `title.rs:30-31`
- **Status:** changed

**Befund:** A client that sets include_live=true gets a response claiming live_context_used:true while no live context was actually used in generation. The flag has zero effect on the produced title, contradicting both the response field and the Python contract.

*Python-Soll:* routes_title.py:114,140-141: include_live = bool(body.get('include_live', False)); ... if include_live: live_state = await get_live_state_for_discord_user(discord_user_id) — the flag actually gates a live-state lookup that feeds the prompt.

*Rust-Ist:* title.rs:30-31 (#[serde(default)] pub include_live: bool) and title.rs:196: "live_context_used": body.include_live — the value is only echoed back in the response; it never triggers any live_state fetch (which is always None per the previous finding).

**Verifikation:** Confirmed in current code. In /home/naniadm/Documents/Deadlock-Twitch-Bot/rust/crates/tb-dashboard-api/src/handlers/title.rs, suggest_handler parses include_live (lines 30-31) but only ever reads it at line 196 to echo it back as "live_context_used": body.include_live. The actual generate_title call (lines 180-190) passes None for the live_state argument (line 187), and the handler never imports LiveStateStore, never resolves a discord_user_id, and never fetches live state. grep across the crate confirms include_live appears only at title.rs:31 and :196. By contrast, Python routes_title.py:140-141 fetches live_state = get_live_state_for_discord_user(...) when include_live is true and passes it into generate_title (line 150), so the flag genuinely gates prompt content. The capability exists in Rust (tb-chat title_ai.rs generate_title accepts live_state: Option<&PromptLiveState> and builds a live_line at :407; the chat --live command path in commands.rs:556/644 honors it), proving the dashboard handler simply fails to wire it. Result: a dashboard client sending include_live=true receives live_context_used:true while no live context influenced the title — a real behavior/contract regression, but scoped to one endpoint affecting prompt quality and a misleading response field, hence medium.

**Laufzeit-Check:** POST to the dashboard title suggest endpoint with include_live:true and a live streamer; the response shows live_context_used:true yet the generated title and server-side prompt contain no live game/title context (identical to an include_live:false request).

### P2.103 [dashboard:abbo_billing_routes] abbo_pay: Stripe checkout AGB/Widerrufsrecht custom_text not set in Rust

- **Python:** `abbo_billing_routes.py:98-107`
- **Rust:** `ABSENT`
- **Status:** missing

**Befund:** The legally-relevant German acknowledgment text (AGB + Verlust des Widerrufsrechts nach §356 BGB) is no longer displayed at Stripe checkout. consent_collection still forces a generic ToS checkbox, but the explicit Widerrufs-Hinweis is gone — a consumer-law / compliance divergence for a revenue path.

*Python-Soll:* abbo_billing_routes.py:98-107 — sets `session_payload["custom_text"]["terms_of_service_acceptance"]["message"]` with the German AGB link + §356 Abs. 5 BGB Widerrufsrecht acknowledgment shown on the Stripe checkout page.

*Rust-Ist:* ABSENT (searched: `grep -n 'custom_text|terms_of_service_acceptance|AGB' billing_page.rs` -> no matches). Rust session_payload (billing_page.rs:198-230) sets consent_collection.terms_of_service=required but no custom_text message.

**Verifikation:** Python (abbo_billing_routes.py:99-107) sets session_payload["custom_text"]["terms_of_service_acceptance"]["message"] with the German AGB link + §356 Abs. 5 BGB Widerrufs-acknowledgment, shown directly on the Stripe checkout page. The Rust checkout payload (tb-dashboard-api/src/handlers/billing_page.rs:198-214) builds the same session but sets only consent_collection.terms_of_service=required — no custom_text key. Grepping the whole tb-* crate set (excluding .claude worktrees) finds custom_text/terms_of_service_acceptance nowhere; the §356/Widerruf matches are all in legal.rs (the standalone /twitch/agb page), not the checkout session. StripeClient::create_checkout_session (tb-analytics/src/stripe/client.rs:148) is a pure pass-through that form-encodes exactly the params given and injects nothing. So the explicit Widerrufs-acknowledgment text at Stripe checkout is genuinely gone. Severity stays medium (not higher): the generic forced ToS checkbox remains and the linked AGB itself contains the §356 clause, so it is a consumer-law/UX divergence rather than a total absence of consent.

**Laufzeit-Check:** Trigger /twitch/abbo/bezahlen for a paid plan on the Rust dashboard and inspect the resulting Stripe-hosted checkout page (or the created session's custom_text field) for the §356/AGB acknowledgment line; it will be absent.

No market handler file exists in crates/tb-dashboard-api/src/handlers/.

In Rust, the dashboard crate tb-dashboard-api has 80+ handler files (listed) but NONE for market — grep for market_data|/twitch/market|market-share|market_research|build_market in crates/tb-dashboard-api/src returns 0 hits, and lib.rs registers 0 market routes. The only native Rust market code is in a DIFFERENT service: tb-internal-api (port 8776) at /internal/twitch/v1/market-share plus tb_analytics::market — i.e. the market-share computation IS ported, but as the worker endpoint, not as the three dashboard routes. The Python api_market_share is itself only a proxy to that worker, so its dashboard route is unported; market_research and api_market_data have no Rust equivalent at all. The fallback proxy (main.rs:50-64) is gated on TB_DASHBOARD_LEGACY_FALLBACK_URL; the None branch installs no fallback, so with the env unset the three paths 404. Nuance: the underlying market-share data already lives in Rust, but the three dashboard route-group entries the claim targets are genuinely absent. Severity lowered to medium: this is an admin/internal-only Market Research page (require_token / v2-admin / localhost gating), not a user-facing feature, and per workspace rules admin tooling ranks below user-visible parity; the core computation also already exists in Rust.

### P2.107 [dashboard:server_v2] Discord-Dashboard external validate-session fallback not ported — admin login via master_dash_session fails when session is not in local DB

- **Python:** `server_v2.py:426-430`

- **Status:** missing

**Befund:** If the Discord-Dashboard mints sessions in its own store and the Twitch dashboard never receives/persists them locally, a Discord admin whose session is not already in the Twitch dashboard DB cannot authenticate (forward_auth -> 401) where Python would have validated against the Discord API and minted the session. Depends on whether sessions are still shared via the DB at cutover; if they are, impact is nil.

*Python-Soll:* server_v2.py:426-430 + 497-553 — when _discord_admin_required and no local session, _fetch_discord_dashboard_session POSTs the session_id to {base}/internal/twitch/v1/discord/validate-session with X-Internal-Token, mints a synthetic discord_admin session, caches it (5 min) and persists it via save_discord_admin_session.

*Rust-Ist:* crates/tb-dashboard-api/src/auth/level.rs:236-243 + auth/session.rs:699-747 (load_admin_session) — only a local dashboard_sessions DB lookup for type 'discord_admin'. No HTTP call exists (searched: grep -rn 'validate-session|discord/validate|save_discord_admin_session|fetch_discord_dashboard|discord_admin_base' crates/tb-dashboard-api/src — no hits).

**Verifikation:** Python (server_v2.py:415-485 validate_admin_session + 497-553 _fetch_discord_dashboard_session) has a documented fallback: when _discord_admin_required and no local session matches the master_dash_session cookie, it POSTs the session_id to {base}/internal/twitch/v1/discord/validate-session with X-Internal-Token, and on {valid:true} mints a synthetic source='discord_dashboard' session, caches it (~5min) and persists it via save_discord_admin_session. The Rust admin path is purely local: auth/level.rs:236-243 calls state.load_admin_session, which (session.rs:699-747) only consults the in-memory admin_cache and fetch_session_payload (session.rs:1031-1051) — a single SELECT from dashboard_sessions WHERE session_type='discord_admin'. No HTTP validate-session call, no synthetic-session minting, no save_discord_admin_session counterpart exists. Hard grep across crates/ and bin/ (excluding target) for validate-session, validate_session, discord/validate, save_discord_admin_session, fetch_discord_dashboard, discord_admin_base, discord_dashboard returned only cookie-name/doc references; reqwest exists only for the strangler proxy (proxy.rs) and the broker discord_link calls — neither hits validate-session. The forward_auth.rs header even enumerates deliberately-deferred Python behavior (IP/fingerprint = ticket B3-10) but never mentions this fallback, so it was dropped, not consciously reimplemented. Gap is real. Severity stays medium because impact is conditional: if Discord-admin logins write their session row into the shared dashboard_sessions table (type discord_admin) at cutover, the local lookup succeeds and the fallback is never exercised; the gap only bites a Discord admin whose master_dash_session lives solely in the Discord-Dashboard's own store.

**Laufzeit-Check:** In prod, hold a valid Discord-Dashboard master_dash_session whose row is absent from the Twitch dashboard's dashboard_sessions table, hit GET /twitch/auth/validate with that cookie, and observe 401 (Python would 200 after validating against the Discord internal API).

### P2.108 [dashboard:server_v2] Global default security headers (X-Frame-Options/nosniff/Referrer-Policy/COOP/XSS) not applied on any Rust dashboard response

- **Python:** `server_v2.py:1076-1082`
- **Rust:** `ABSENT`
- **Status:** missing

**Befund:** Every Rust dashboard response (admin forms, billing, analytics, login pages) ships without clickjacking (X-Frame-Options: DENY), MIME-sniffing (nosniff), referrer-leak, and COOP protections that Python guaranteed in-app. Unless Caddy injects these at the proxy (not verified, and Python set them regardless), this is a defense-in-depth regression — notably X-Frame-Options: DENY is dropped on all non-demo pages, re-enabling framing/clickjacking that Python blocked.

*Python-Soll:* server_v2.py:1076-1082 — _security_headers_middleware sets on EVERY non-demo response: response.headers.setdefault('X-Frame-Options','DENY'); 'X-Content-Type-Options':'nosniff'; 'X-XSS-Protection':'1; mode=block'; 'Referrer-Policy':'strict-origin-when-cross-origin'; 'Cross-Origin-Opener-Policy':'same-origin'.

*Rust-Ist:* ABSENT (searched: grep -rn 'X-Frame-Options|X-Content-Type-Options|Cross-Origin-Opener-Policy|X-XSS-Protection|Referrer-Policy|SetResponseHeaderLayer|nosniff' over rust/crates rust/bin → no hits except demo.rs frame-ancestors CSP). build_router (lib.rs:820-837) applies only CompressionLayer globally; no response-header middleware. main.rs (bin/tb-dashboard/src/main.rs) adds only Extension layers.

**Verifikation:** Verified in actual code. Python bot/dashboard/server_v2.py:1067-1082 (_security_headers_middleware) sets on EVERY non-demo response: X-Frame-Options: DENY, X-Content-Type-Options: nosniff, X-XSS-Protection: 1; mode=block, Referrer-Policy: strict-origin-when-cross-origin, Cross-Origin-Opener-Policy: same-origin (demo paths swap DENY for a CSP frame-ancestors). The Rust counterpart build_router (rust/crates/tb-dashboard-api/src/lib.rs:820-838) merges all sub-routers and applies ONLY CompressionLayer::new() globally — no SetResponseHeaderLayer / response-header middleware. A hard grep over all of rust/ (excluding target/ build artifacts and the audit docs that already flagged the same gap) returned zero hits for X-Frame-Options, nosniff, X-Content-Type-Options, X-XSS-Protection, Referrer-Policy, Cross-Origin-Opener-Policy, or SetResponseHeaderLayer. The only Rust security-header code is handlers/demo.rs::with_demo_csp, which sets a CSP frame-ancestors value for the embed-exception path only — it does NOT emit the default DENY/nosniff/Referrer/COOP bundle for normal pages. So every native Rust dashboard response ships without these headers. Severity kept at medium (not raised): the project's own audit (rust/docs/audit/_work/findings-2026-06-15.json:2358) records that production Caddy (Caddyfile:77-89) injects X-Frame-Options: DENY + CSP frame-ancestors at the edge, so external clickjacking is mitigated for proxied access; the in-app gap is a genuine defense-in-depth regression (and nosniff/Referrer-Policy/COOP/XSS are not guaranteed by Caddy on all routes), most visible on direct port-8769 access.

**Laufzeit-Check:** curl -sI the running tb-dashboard directly on its local port (8769) for a non-demo route (e.g. /analyse) and confirm X-Frame-Options/X-Content-Type-Options/Referrer-Policy/Cross-Origin-Opener-Policy are absent from the response headers.

### P2.109 [dashboard:abbo_routes] Lurker-tax readiness signal (moderator:read:chatters scope check) dropped — user not warned the feature won't fire

- **Python:** `abbo_routes.py L86-162 _abbo_scope_state reads twitch_raid_auth.scopes AND falls back to t`
- **Rust:** `ABSENT`
- **Status:** missing

**Befund:** The Rust lurker-tax JSON API returns only the on/off flag. A streamer can enable the toggle while the bot lacks moderator:read:chatters and gets no warning that the reminder will never fire — a silent dead-toggle. The actual chat runtime still checks the scope, so it's a UX/observability regression, not a data-corruption bug.

*Python-Soll:* abbo_routes.py L86-162 _abbo_scope_state reads twitch_raid_auth.scopes AND falls back to the bot token manager's scopes; L548-552 computes has_chatters_scope; L573-579 renders a notice-warn 'Bot-Scope moderator:read:chatters fehlt … feuert die Lurker Steuer nicht' when the scope is missing.

*Rust-Ist:* ABSENT (searched: grep -rn 'moderator:read:chatters|abbo_scope|has_moderator_read_chatters' crates/tb-dashboard-api/src → no match; lurker_tax_settings.rs::get_handler L77-98 returns only {lurker_tax_enabled:bool} with no readiness field; catalog payload carries no scope readiness).

**Verifikation:** Python abbo_routes.py L548-579 surfaces a notice-warn when moderator:read:chatters is missing ("...feuert die Lurker Steuer nicht"). The Rust dashboard handler lurker_tax_settings.rs::get_handler (L77-98) returns ONLY {lurker_tax_enabled: bool} with no readiness/scope field. I searched tb-dashboard-api, tb-analytics billing/catalog.rs, and tb-engagement/lurker_signal.rs for any has_moderator_read_chatters/chatters-scope readiness exposed to the dashboard — none (the "readiness" hits are all unrelated Stripe billing-readiness). The runtime DOES still gate on the scope: tb-chat/src/promos.rs L1248-1266 reads twitch_raid_auth.scopes and returns silently (dead toggle) when moderator:read:chatters is absent. So a streamer can enable the toggle while the scope is missing and gets no warning — confirmed UX/observability regression, not data corruption. (Minor nuance: Rust checks the streamer's twitch_raid_auth.scopes only, dropping Python's bot-token-manager fallback, but the user-facing warning is genuinely gone.)

**Laufzeit-Check:** In the v2 streamer dashboard, GET /twitch/api/v2/streamer/lurker-tax-settings for a channel whose twitch_raid_auth lacks moderator:read:chatters; the JSON returns only the on/off flag and the UI shows no readiness warning, while the chat reminder never fires.

### P2.110 [dashboard:abbo_routes] Streamer self-service promo-disable toggle (POST /twitch/abbo/promo-settings) has no Rust port — feature only works via dead Python :8765 proxy

- **Python:** `abbo_routes.py:630-661`
- **Rust:** `ABSENT`
- **Status:** missing

**Befund:** Entitled paid streamers can no longer disable promos from the dashboard once Rust is live and the Python :8765 service is down (per project memory '502 = totes 8765'). The toggle silently 404s or 502s. The streamer_plans.promo_disabled column is still read by the bot/admin, so the value is honored — but partners have no way to set it.

*Python-Soll:* abbo_routes.py:630-661 — abbo_promo_settings: entitlement gate chat.promos.disable, CSRF body verify, then UPDATE streamer_plans SET promo_disabled=%s WHERE LOWER(twitch_login)=LOWER(%s), redirect /twitch/abbo?profile=saved.

*Rust-Ist:* ABSENT (searched: grep -rn 'promo-settings|promo_disabled|streamer_plans SET promo' rust/ ; lib.rs build_billing_page_router:775-814 registers other /twitch/abbo/* routes but not promo-settings; partner_gate.rs:32 is a passive-gate path constant only; admin_streamers.rs:264/393 only READ promo_disabled). Reachable only via proxy.rs Strangler fallback to Python 8765.

**Verifikation:** Independently verified the gap is real. Python POST /twitch/abbo/promo-settings (bot/dashboard/abbo_routes.py:630-661) gates on entitlement chat.promos.disable, CSRF-verifies, then UPDATE streamer_plans SET promo_disabled = %s WHERE LOWER(twitch_login)=LOWER(%s) and redirects /twitch/abbo?profile=saved. In Rust I found NO counterpart: (a) no handler file (handlers/ has lurker_tax_settings.rs, billing*.rs, admin_* but no promo_settings.rs); (b) no route registration in tb-dashboard-api/src/lib.rs for promo-settings (only /twitch/abbo redirect handlers at 779-781 and the lurker-tax toggle at 197, which was ported to a NEW path /twitch/api/v2/streamer/lurker-tax-settings); (c) grep for 'SET promo_disabled' or any 'UPDATE ... promo_disabled' write across crates/ returns nothing — promo_disabled is only READ (promos.rs:1679 bot gating, admin_streamers.rs:264/393 admin read). partner_gate.rs:32 is merely a PASSIVE_ALLOWED_EXACT_PATHS constant: it lets the path through the partner-status middleware but mounts no handler, so the route 404s in Rust and the Strangler proxy (lib.rs:13 'Fallback-Proxy → Python 8765') can only 502 since :8765 is dead. The auditor's reading was accurate. The notable nuance is that the sibling lurker-tax toggle WAS ported (proving the team migrated this family of toggles selectively) yet promo-settings was left behind — a genuine missing-feature regression. Severity corrected to medium: impact is limited to a niche self-service toggle for entitled streamers; the column is still honored by the bot once set, and the value can in principle be set out-of-band — no core billing/auth flow breaks. So it is a real but narrow regression, not high.

**Laufzeit-Check:** On the live Rust dashboard host, log in as an entitled (chat.promos.disable) streamer, toggle the promo-disable checkbox on /twitch/abbo and submit, then observe a 404/502 instead of redirect to /twitch/abbo?profile=saved and confirm streamer_plans.promo_disabled stays unchanged.

### P2.111 [dashboard:abbo_routes] Legacy abbo lurker-tax form-POST route path is gone; reimplemented at a different v2 path with the paid entitlement gate intentionally removed

- **Python:** `abbo_routes.py:664-700`
- **Rust:** `handlers/lurker_tax_settings.rs:1-158`

**Befund:** Two real behavior changes vs Python: (1) the old form-POST URL no longer resolves natively (works only via dead Python proxy) so any client/form still posting to /twitch/abbo/lurker-tax-settings breaks; (2) the lurker-tax toggle is now available to ALL partners, not only chat.lurker_tax-entitled paid plans. Per the handler doc this gate removal was a deliberate Grillme decision (so the entitlement widening is an intended product change, not an accidental regression), but the route-path change and body-CSRF drop are migration-driven and could orphan any legacy form still targeting the old path.

*Python-Soll:* abbo_routes.py:664-700 — POST /twitch/abbo/lurker-tax-settings gated by _billing_plan_has_entitlement(plan_id,'chat.lurker_tax') (line 672), body CSRF token (676-678), form checkbox, redirect outcomes ?lurker_tax=saved|error.

*Rust-Ist:* handlers/lurker_tax_settings.rs:1-158 reimplements the upsert at the NEW route /twitch/api/v2/streamer/lurker-tax-settings (lib.rs:196-197); doc lines 8-11 explicitly drop the chat.lurker_tax paid gate ('weitet ihn bewusst auf alle Partner aus'); no body CSRF token (header-CSRF model, lib.rs:485). Old /twitch/abbo/lurker-tax-settings is not natively registered (partner_gate.rs:30 is gate-list only).

**Verifikation:** Independently confirmed in current code. Python bot/dashboard/abbo_routes.py:664-700 gates POST /twitch/abbo/lurker-tax-settings behind _billing_plan_has_entitlement(plan_id,'chat.lurker_tax') (line 672) with body CSRF (676-678) and ?lurker_tax=saved|error redirects. Rust handlers/lurker_tax_settings.rs reimplements the upsert at the NEW path /twitch/api/v2/streamer/lurker-tax-settings (lib.rs:196-197), with NO paid-entitlement check (only login/admin auth in resolve_target) and header-CSRF instead of body-CSRF (lib.rs:489). Doc lines 8-11 explicitly state the chat.lurker_tax gate is intentionally dropped and widened to all partners. The old path /twitch/abbo/lurker-tax-settings exists only in partner_gate.rs:30 (passive allow-list, no native handler); the repo's own audit (findings-2026-06-15.json:2051,2057) confirms it falls through to the dead Python proxy -> 502. Both divergences are real: route-path change orphans any legacy form, and the entitlement gate is genuinely removed. The gate widening is documented as a deliberate product decision, so medium severity (behavioral divergence, not a hard regression) is correct.

**Laufzeit-Check:** Live-POST an die alte URL /twitch/abbo/lurker-tax-settings als authentifizierter Partner -> erwartet 502 (kein nativer Handler); und ein nicht-chat.lurker_tax-berechtigter Partner kann den Toggle ueber /twitch/api/v2/streamer/lurker-tax-settings dennoch setzen (200 ok).

### P2.112 [dashboard:routes_entry] POST /twitch/discord_link form route dropped — replaced only by a different GET OAuth flow

- **Python:** `routes_entry.py:52`
- **Rust:** `discord_link.rs:191-195`
- **Status:** missing

**Befund:** The legacy admin/streamer form-based Discord-profile write (with explicit member_flag override and X-Internal-Token-style _require_token gating) has no native counterpart; POST /twitch/discord_link returns 404. Callers still POSTing the old form (e.g. legacy admin UI or scripts) break. The replacement OAuth flow is a different mechanism with different inputs/gating.

*Python-Soll:* routes_entry.py:52 web.post('/twitch/discord_link', server.discord_link); body L261-303: _require_token, body-CSRF verify, parses login/discord_user_id/discord_display_name and member_flag (mark_member = member_raw in {'1','true','on','yes'}), calls server._discord_profile(...), distinguishes ValueError vs upstream-service error (is_upstream_service_error) vs generic, redirects ok/err to /twitch/stats.

*Rust-Ist:* discord_link.rs:191-195 registers GET /twitch/auth/discord/link and /twitch/auth/discord/link/complete (Discord-OAuth-broker flow). No POST /twitch/discord_link form route exists (grep '/twitch/discord_link' route() = 0). The native flow derives the member flag from Discord roles (discord_link.rs:161-165), not from a posted member_flag field, and requires a Partner session rather than _require_token.

**Verifikation:** Confirmed: the Python admin-dashboard form route POST /twitch/discord_link (routes_entry.py:252-303 → _dashboard_save_discord_profile) writes a streamer's Discord profile with explicit member_flag override + discord_user_id + discord_display_name, gated by _require_token and body-CSRF. The live admin React SPA still calls it: bot/admin_dashboard/src/api/client.ts:1120 updateStreamerDiscordProfile → submitLegacyAction('/twitch/discord_link', {member_flag:'1'|''}). Crucially, the Rust dashboard (tb-dashboard-api) NATIVELY SERVES THE SAME SPA — admin_spa.rs:45 DEFAULT_ADMIN_DIST_PATH = "bot/admin_dashboard/dist" — but does NOT register /twitch/discord_link. lib.rs:690 build_admin_legacy_forms_router registers only manual-plan/+clear; lib.rs:714-717 explicitly names B1-DISCORD-LINK as an un-built follow-up ticket. The only native counterparts are partial/different: (1) tb-dashboard-api discord-flag (admin_streamers.rs:586) toggles ONLY the boolean is_on_discord, it does NOT persist discord_user_id/discord_display_name; (2) the GET /twitch/auth/discord/link OAuth flow (discord_link.rs) is Partner self-service with member-flag derived from Discord roles (discord_link.rs:161-165), Partner session not _require_token — a different mechanism/inputs as the auditor said. Full member_flag-override parity DOES exist as discord_profile_handler at POST /internal/twitch/v1/streamers/:login/discord-profile (tb-internal-api/handlers/streamers.rs:862, alias member_flag, same isdigit/120-char/raid_auth+Helix logic), but that is the internal-token API at a different path and I found NO admin-UI caller wired to it. Unregistered dashboard routes fall to the strangler proxy → Python 8765, which the code+memory repeatedly call dead ("toten Python 8765"). Net: an admin saving a streamer's Discord profile via the live admin UI hits a 404/502, with no native admin-reachable replacement for the discord_user_id/display_name+member_flag write.

**Laufzeit-Check:** In the live admin SPA at /twitch/admin, open a streamer and submit the Discord-profile form (discord_user_id + display name + member flag); the POST /twitch/discord_link returns 404/502 instead of redirecting to /twitch/stats?ok=…, and the discord_user_id/display_name do not persist.

### P2.113 [dashboard:routes_billing] Sync: existing product/price IDs are marked 'reused' WITHOUT Stripe verification — stale/deleted checked-in IDs never recreated

- **Python:** `routes_billing.py:439-449`
- **Rust:** `billing_stripe_sync.rs:100-103`
- **Status:** changed

**Befund:** Python actively validates any existing product/price ID against Stripe and self-heals (recreates) if the object was deleted or the API errors. Rust trusts the checked-in default unconditionally. If a checked-in product/price ID is later deleted or invalidated in Stripe, Rust keeps reporting it as 'reused' and never recreates it, while Python would recover. Sync becomes a no-op that leaves billing pointing at a dead Stripe object. Counters (reused_products/reused_prices) are also inflated vs Python where a failed retrieve would NOT count as reused.

*Python-Soll:* routes_billing.py:439-449 (Product.retrieve + deleted-check, clear on exception -> recreate) and 498-504 (Price.retrieve verify, clear on exception -> lookup/create)

*Rust-Ist:* billing_stripe_sync.rs:100-103 (non-empty product default -> immediately 'reused', no retrieve) and 146-151 (non-empty price default -> immediately 'reused', no retrieve)

**Verifikation:** Code-level gap is real and matches the claim exactly. Python (routes_billing.py:439-449, 498-504) actively calls stripe.Product.retrieve / stripe.Price.retrieve on any existing ID, checks the deleted flag, and on a deleted object OR any API exception clears the ID so the downstream create/lookup path recreates it — a self-healing verify cycle. Rust (billing_stripe_sync.rs:100-103 product, 146-151 price) takes the checked-in default (product_id_default / price_id_default from the compile-time consts PRODUCT_ID_DEFAULTS / PRICE_ID_DEFAULTS in tb-analytics/src/billing/catalog.rs:228-286) and, if non-empty, immediately sets status 'reused' and bumps reused_products/reused_prices with NO Stripe retrieve call. So if a checked-in product/price ID is later deleted or invalidated in Stripe, Rust keeps reporting it 'reused' and never recreates; Python would detect and recover. Counters also diverge (Python does not count a failed retrieve as reused; Rust always does). The behavioral divergence the claim asserts is present in the actual current code. Mitigating context (documented at module lines 8-17): Rust's source of truth is the immutable compiled-in defaults, and there is no runtime write-back (persisted_to_windows_vault=false) — so even adding a retrieve+recreate would not close the loop persistently, and the IDs are real production values unlikely to be deleted. That lowers likelihood/severity but does not refute the gap. Severity corrected high->medium: serious consequence (silent billing pointing at a dead Stripe object, no auto-recovery) but low-probability trigger and only affects the manual admin sync endpoint.

**Laufzeit-Check:** In Stripe test mode delete one checked-in product/price ID, POST the sync endpoint as admin (non-dry-run), and confirm Rust still reports that plan as 'reused' instead of recreating it (Python would report created/recreated).

### P2.114 [dashboard:routes_billing] Sync response drops product_id_map, price_id_map and readiness fields

- **Python:** `routes_billing.py:578-592`
- **Rust:** `billing_stripe_sync.rs:228-241`
- **Status:** changed

**Befund:** The admin JSON API contract changed: any caller (admin dashboard tooling) reading product_id_map/price_id_map/readiness from the sync response gets undefined. The created/reused IDs computed during the run are reported only inside operations[] and are not persisted anywhere (vault drop is documented/intentional), so the resulting ID maps are effectively unrecoverable from this endpoint. The vault-persist drop itself is documented; the silent removal of the three top-level response keys is the divergence.

*Python-Soll:* routes_billing.py:578-592 — payload includes product_id_map, price_id_map, readiness (and persisted_to_windows_vault as a real bool)

*Rust-Ist:* billing_stripe_sync.rs:228-241 — payload omits product_id_map/price_id_map/readiness; hardcodes persisted_to_windows_vault=false and adds persist_skipped_reason

**Verifikation:** Verified in current code. Python routes_billing.py:579-592 returns a payload with top-level product_id_map (product_map), price_id_map (price_map), readiness, plus a computed persisted_to_windows_vault bool. Rust billing_stripe_sync.rs:228-241 emits a payload that omits product_id_map/price_id_map/readiness entirely, hardcodes persisted_to_windows_vault=false and adds persist_skipped_reason. A grep across crates/ shows product_id_map/price_id_map appear nowhere else in Rust, and the handler never builds product/price-map accumulators — created/reused IDs surface only inside operations[]. The module doc (lines 8-17) intentionally documents ONLY the vault-persist drop; the removal of the three top-level response keys is undocumented and silent, exactly as claimed. This is a real admin JSON API contract divergence. Severity stays medium: the consumer is privileged admin tooling only, the IDs remain recoverable from operations[], and readiness is reachable via its own endpoint — so impact is bounded, not high.

**Laufzeit-Check:** POST /twitch/api/billing/stripe/sync-products with dry_run and compare the Python vs Rust JSON: Rust response lacks the product_id_map, price_id_map and readiness top-level keys.

- **Python:** `bot/dashboard/pages.py:483-765`
- **Rust:** `ABSENT`
- **Status:** missing

**Befund:** The internal market-intelligence dashboard (KPIs, 24h market-volume chart, meta snapshot, sentiment, viewer overlap, question radar, live monitored channels) has no equivalent in the Rust dashboard. If the Rust dashboard has replaced/supersedes the Python one for this path, hitting /twitch/market yields 404 and the feature is lost. ### P2.119 [dashboard:live] Owner-only Discord-ID gate for chat-action dropped at cutover

- **Python:** `bot/dashboard/live/live.py:63`
- **Rust:** `ABSENT`

**Befund:** In Python only one specific Discord owner may trigger an outbound bot chat message into a streamer channel from the dashboard; any other privileged admin is denied and the attempt is audit-logged. In Rust, any privileged (internal-token/localhost) caller of /streamers/:login/chat-action can send. Narrower-than-Python authorization is lost. The only Rust chat-action is crates/tb-internal-api/src/handlers/python_stubs.rs:204-260 which gates solely on auth.is_privileged() (line 210), with no owner-specific Discord-ID restriction and no AUDIT log.

**Verifikation:** Independently confirmed. Python live.py:63 defines _DASHBOARD_OWNER_DISCORD_ID="662995601738170389" and live.py:1794-1807 (admin_partner_chat_action) rejects with the German owner-only message plus an AUDIT warning unless _get_discord_admin_user_id(request) equals that owner ID. In Rust the ONLY chat-action is crates/tb-internal-api/src/handlers/python_stubs.rs:204 (chat_action_handler), routed at crates/tb-internal-api/src/lib.rs:248-249, gated solely on auth.is_privileged() (line 210) with no Discord-ID owner check and no AUDIT-deny log. grep across all rust crates+bins for 662995601738170389 / DASHBOARD_OWNER / freigeschaltete / owner gating returns no equivalent. The Rust dashboard crates (tb-dashboard, tb-dashboard-api) have NO chat-action route at all (their 'chat' hits are unrelated AI-analysis chat sessions), and no SPA/frontend file calls chat-action. So the owner restriction is genuinely enforced nowhere in Rust: any privileged (internal-token/localhost) caller can send. The auditor's note is correct — the Rust handler ports internal_api/routes/streamers.py which never had the gate (a cutover scoping decision, not a translation bug), but the owner gate is genuinely lost. Severity stays medium: this narrows authorization within an already-privileged surface (internal-token/localhost required), not a public auth bypass.

(bot/admin_dashboard/src/api/client.ts:1120). ### P2.122 [dashboard:?] UI-schema -> template-schema converter (_to_template_config) and validate_config_dict not ported; ported dashboard_config helpers are dead code in tb-monitoring

The runtime sink (template.rs from_dict) consumes already-template-shaped config from another source, never running the UI->template mapping that the Python save endpoint performs. Any future Rust port of the save endpoint must re-implement _to_template_config faithfully or the saved UI config will not map correctly to the runtime announcement (e.g. {rolle} mention never substituted, twitch_logo icon, ping role from mentions.role_id).

*Python-Soll:* _to_template_config L101-189 ({rolle}->{mention_role} replace L135, icon_mode twitch_logo->twitch L139-143/166-170, image_mode derivation L130-132, mentions.role_id->static_ping_role_ids[int] L115-116/186, allowed_editor_role_ids digit-filter L181-185, force_stream_url True L176); _validate_config_dict L192-201

*Rust-Ist:* ABSENT for converter+validator (searched: grep -rn 'to_template_config|use_streamer_ping_role.*from|validate_config' crates bin). Helpers that WERE ported (dashboard_config.rs default_live_announcement_config/deep_merge/parse_config_json) have NO production consumer (searched: grep -rn 'parse_config_json|default_live_announcement_config|dashboard_config::' crates bin excl mod.rs/own-tests -> 0 hits)

**Verifikation:** Confirmed in current code. Python _to_template_config (live_announcement_mixin.py L101-189) maps the UI-schema (content/embed.author.name/embed.image.use_stream_thumbnail/mentions.role_id/mentions.enabled) to the template-schema with all cited transforms ({rolle}->{mention_role} L135, twitch_logo->twitch L139-143/166-170, image_mode derivation L130-132, role_id->static_ping_role_ids[int] L115-116, allowed_editor_role_ids digit-filter L181-185, force_stream_url True L176). _validate_config_dict L192-201 wraps it. grep across all tb-* crates (to_template_config|template_config|validate_config|twitch_logo|use_streamer_ping_role|static_ping_role_ids|allowed_editor_role_ids) finds NO Rust converter or validator under any name. The ported dashboard_config.rs (default_live_announcement_config/deep_merge/parse_config_json) has zero production consumers — the only reference outside the file is `pub mod dashboard_config;` in announce/mod.rs; no caller of parse_config_json/default_live_announcement_config/deep_merge exists in crates or bin. Meanwhile the Rust runtime sink (sink.rs L107/L122) SELECTs config_json and feeds it straight to AnnouncementConfig::from_json (template.rs L118-193), which reads template-schema keys (content_template, author.name_template, images.image_mode, mentions.static_ping_role_ids/use_streamer_ping_role) — keys the Python save endpoint never writes (it stores raw UI-schema cfg, L634). The only Rust INSERTs into twitch_live_announcement_configs are in test files; there is no Rust save endpoint. So both the converter and validator are genuinely unported, and the ported pure helpers are dead code. ### P2.124 [dashboard:stats] Parallel `leaderboard` (!twl-replacement) endpoint serializes Discord user IDs to any authenticated session, unlike Python's localhost-only gate

- **Python:** `bot/dashboard/core/stats.py:179-183,`
- **Rust:** `rust/crates/tb-dashboard-api/src/handlers/leaderboard.rs:71`
- **Status:** changed

**Befund:** Privacy divergence: the /twitch/api/v2/leaderboard JSON endpoint exposes Discord snowflake IDs and display names to any logged-in partner, whereas the Python page restricted that data to localhost/admin. This endpoint is outside this exact stats page (it is the !twl Discord-leaderboard port) but reuses the same partner-state Discord data; if any partner-accessible UI consumes it, it leaks Discord IDs that the legacy never showed partners. Discord ID is correctly a JSON string (not the number-serialization bug class), so the issue is purely the missing localhost gate. Recommend confirming the endpoint's audience and stripping Discord fields for non-admin callers.

*Python-Soll:* bot/dashboard/core/stats.py:179-183, 1181-1183: discord_user_id/discord_display_name are blanked and the Discord column dropped whenever not show_discord_private (i.e. for every non-localhost/partner request). Discord IDs are localhost/admin-only.

*Rust-Ist:* rust/crates/tb-dashboard-api/src/handlers/leaderboard.rs:71 gate is only auth.is_authenticated() (module doc line 20: 'Partner/Admin/Localhost'); lines 249-250 unconditionally serialize "discord_user_id": r.discord_user_id and "discord_display_name": r.discord_display_name for every category entry, with discord_user_id stored/returned as String (lines 61, 193) — so a partner session calling /twitch/api/v2/leaderboard receives other streamers' raw Discord IDs.

**Verifikation:** Verified in current code. rust/crates/tb-dashboard-api/src/handlers/leaderboard.rs:71 gates only on auth.is_authenticated(), which per auth/level.rs:70-73 is true for Localhost, Admin AND Partner. Lines 248-250 then serialize discord_user_id and discord_display_name unconditionally for every entry, with no localhost/admin branch — so a Partner session calling GET /twitch/api/v2/leaderboard receives other streamers' raw Discord snowflake IDs and display names. The codebase even has an is_privileged() helper (level.rs:66, Localhost|Admin only) that the handler does NOT use. The analogous web surface in Python (bot/dashboard/core/stats.py:23,181-183) blanks discord_user_id/discord_display_name whenever not show_discord_private (= _is_local_request), i.e. restricts that data to localhost. Caveat the claim acknowledges itself: the Rust module's cited SOURCE is bot/community/leaderboard.py (the Discord !twl command, where IDs are shown because the audience is Discord, no localhost concept), not stats.py — so there is no exact 1:1 Python web counterpart. But the port converted a Discord-only command into a partner-reachable web JSON endpoint that leaks Discord IDs to partners, a privacy gap the equivalent web page (stats.py) never had. The Discord ID is correctly serialized as a JSON String (Option<String>, line 61) so the number-serialization bug class does not apply. Severity stays medium: data is Discord IDs/display names (semi-public, not emails/PII), exposure limited to authenticated partners, and no partner-facing UI consumer is confirmed to render it yet.

**Laufzeit-Check:** With a valid twitch_dash_session (partner, non-localhost) cookie, GET /twitch/api/v2/leaderboard and check whether the returned category entries contain non-null discord_user_id/discord_display_name for streamers other than the caller.

### P2.126 [dashboard:billing_mixin] Stripe price/product ID vault-override layer dropped (STRIPE_PRICE_ID_MAP / STRIPE_PRODUCT_ID_MAP)

- **Python:** `billing_mixin.py:190-221`
- **Rust:** `ABSENT`
- **Status:** missing

**Befund:** Admins can no longer remap Stripe price/product IDs at runtime via vault (e.g. switch test->live price IDs, hot-fix a wrong price ID, or onboard new prices) without a Rust recompile+redeploy. The Python admin Stripe-provisioning route that creates products/prices and persists the map has no Rust path, so created prices cannot be wired live. Default price IDs are hardcoded in the binary.

*Python-Soll:* billing_mixin.py:190-221 _billing_price_id_map/_billing_product_id_map parse STRIPE_PRICE_ID_MAP/STRIPE_PRODUCT_ID_MAP from runtime vault and merge over defaults; _billing_set_price_id_map/_billing_set_product_id_map (202-221) write remapped IDs back via _write_keyring_secret to STRIPE_PRICE_ID_MAP + TWITCH_BILLING_STRIPE_PRICE_ID_MAP. _billing_refresh_runtime_secrets:172-179 loads these keys. Consumed at checkout via _billing_price_id_for_plan (224-234) and persisted by admin route routes_billing.py:574-575.

*Rust-Ist:* ABSENT (searched crates/bin: grep 'STRIPE_PRICE_ID_MAP','STRIPE_PRODUCT_ID_MAP','price_id_map','set_price_id_map','persisted_to_windows_vault','_write_keyring_secret' -> no hits). Rust resolves price IDs only from compile-time PRICE_ID_DEFAULTS/PRODUCT_ID_DEFAULTS (crates/tb-analytics/src/billing/catalog.rs:228-346, used billing_page.rs:187 price_id_default).

**Verifikation:** Verified in current code. Python (bot/dashboard/billing/billing_mixin.py:147-234) reads STRIPE_PRICE_ID_MAP/STRIPE_PRODUCT_ID_MAP (+ TWITCH_BILLING_* aliases) from the runtime vault in _billing_refresh_runtime_secrets, merges them OVER the defaults in _billing_price_id_map/_billing_product_id_map, consumes them at checkout via _billing_price_id_for_plan, and writes created IDs back via _billing_set_price_id_map/_billing_set_product_id_map (_write_keyring_secret). The Rust path resolves price/product IDs ONLY from compile-time const arrays: catalog.rs:228 PRICE_ID_DEFAULTS / :281 PRODUCT_ID_DEFAULTS via price_id_default/product_id_default; grep confirms NO env/vault/Infisical read and NO runtime-map merge anywhere in tb-analytics/src/billing. The sync-products endpoint IS ported (billing_stripe_sync.rs) but its own module doc and JSON response explicitly mark the persist/override layer as a deliberate, deferred deviation: persisted_to_windows_vault=false, persist_skipped_reason="runtime_vault_unavailable_using_checked_in_defaults", and the doc calls writing the runtime override a "crate-fremdes Folge-Ticket (Handoff)". So both the read-side override-merge AND the write-back are genuinely absent. The gap (no runtime remap/hot-fix/onboard-new-price without recompile) is real. Severity corrected to medium: checked-in defaults cover the live plans so the steady state works, but the runtime override capability is a genuine operational regression, not a live-breaking one.</reason>
<parameter name="verify_hint">Set STRIPE_PRICE_ID_MAP in Infisical to a remapped price for a paid plan, restart the dashboard, start a checkout for that plan, and observe the session still uses the compile-time default price ID (override ignored); also POST the sync-products endpoint and observe persisted_to_windows_vault=false in the response.

### P2.127 [dashboard:billing_mixin] Partner raid-score cache not refreshed after billing changes

**Verifikation:** Python billing_mixin.py invokes _billing_refresh_partner_raid_score_cache from 3 billing mutation paths (subscription sync line 583 = Stripe webhook; manual plan set line 1485; manual plan clear line 1534), proactively refreshing the partner raid score after a plan change. In Rust the billing counterparts do NOT trigger any score refresh: rust/crates/tb-analytics/src/stripe/webhook_apply.rs apply_event calls upsert_subscription_state + sync_plan_to_streamer_plans only (no raid-score call), and rust/crates/tb-dashboard-api/src/handlers/admin_manual_plan.rs set_manual_plan/clear_manual_plan contain zero score/refresh references. A grep for refresh_scores/refresh_partner across all crates/bin shows call sites ONLY in raid arrival wiring, eventsub hooks, monitoring poller, chat wiring, and the scheduled reconciliation loop in bin/tb-bot/src/main.rs — none in the billing/plan-mutation path. The refresh_scores subsystem (bin/tb-bot/src/score_refresh.rs) exists and works but is never wired to billing. Result: after buying/changing a raid-relevant plan (raid_boost/bundle), the new entitlement only takes effect on the next scheduled score-refresh run — a real staleness window in raid candidate ranking, matching the claim. Severity stays medium: bounded staleness, no crash/data loss.

**Laufzeit-Check:** In a test DB, set a streamer to raid_boost via the Stripe-webhook apply path (or admin manual-plan endpoint), then immediately read twitch_partner_raid_scores.last_computed_at / raid_boost_multiplier and observe it is unchanged until the scheduled reconcile interval elapses.

### P2.129 [dashboard:billing_mixin] Admin manual-plan set/clear does not refresh the partner raid score cache

- **Python:** `bot/dashboard/billing/billing_mixin.py:1485-1489`
- **Rust:** `ABSENT`
- **Status:** missing

**Befund:** After an admin sets or clears a manual plan that changes the raid tier (e.g. granting/removing raid_boost), the cached partner raid score in twitch_partner_raid_scores is not recomputed immediately. The streamer's raid eligibility/score stays stale until the next natural recompute, so an admin-granted raid_boost does not take effect in raid selection right away (and a cleared boost keeps inflating the score). Eventual-consistency divergence, not a hard error.

*Python-Soll:* bot/dashboard/billing/billing_mixin.py:1485-1489 (set) and 1534-1538 (clear) both call self._billing_refresh_partner_raid_score_cache(...) after the DB write; definition at 52-108 schedules refresh_partner_raid_score_async(twitch_user_key) which recomputes twitch_partner_raid_scores (raid_boost_multiplier is plan-tier dependent, see bot/raid/partner_raid_score_tracking.py:132,187,208).

*Rust-Ist:* ABSENT — crates/tb-dashboard-api/src/handlers/admin_manual_plan.rs set_manual_plan (172-237) and clear_manual_plan (240-268) return after the UPDATE with no score-refresh call. searched: grep -rn 'refresh_partner_raid_score|raid_score_cache|recompute.*score' crates/ — only tb-raid stores/tests, no invocation from the manual-plan handler.

**Verifikation:** Python set/clear (bot/dashboard/billing/billing_mixin.py:1485-1489 and 1534-1538) both invoke _billing_refresh_partner_raid_score_cache after the streamer_plans write, scheduling refresh_partner_raid_score_async to recompute twitch_partner_raid_scores (raid_boost_multiplier is plan-tier dependent). The Rust handler rust/crates/tb-dashboard-api/src/handlers/admin_manual_plan.rs set_manual_plan (172-237) and clear_manual_plan (240-268) perform the UPDATE, compute only effective_plan_id (resolve_plan_snapshot, read-only), and return — no score-refresh of any kind. The actual recompute engine (ScoreRefreshResolver in rust/bin/tb-bot/src/score_refresh.rs) IS live, but it is only triggered from tb-bot via eventsub hooks, the stream poller (online/offline), and raid arrival — never from the manual-plan handler (the handler lives in the dashboard-api crate, which cannot reach the tb-bot resolver). So after an admin grants/removes a raid_boost-tier manual plan, the cached partner raid score stays stale until the streamer's next natural stream event recomputes it. Eventual-consistency divergence that self-corrects, not a hard error — medium is correct.

**Laufzeit-Check:** In a test DB, as admin set a raid_boost manual plan for an offline streamer via POST /twitch/admin/manual-plan, then read twitch_partner_raid_scores.raid_boost_multiplier for that user — it stays unchanged (no immediate recompute) until a stream online/offline or raid event fires.
