### P2.131 [dashboard:state_store] Partner-Access session_type string differs: Python 'partner_access' vs Rust 'partner_token' — cross-runtime session invisibility

- **Python:** `state_store.py:18`
- **Rust:** `session.rs:234`
- **Status:** changed

**Befund:** The durable partner-access cookie session is written/read under a DIFFERENT session_type string in Rust than the Python state_store used. A partner-access row persisted by the Python service (type 'partner_access') is invisible to the Rust loader (filters session_type='partner_token') and vice versa. During/after cutover, durable partner-access logins issued by one runtime would not be honored by the other, forcing re-login. Within a pure-Rust deployment it is internally consistent, so impact is limited to mixed-runtime / migration windows. Worth confirming which string the live dashboard_sessions rows actually carry before treating Rust as authoritative.

*Python-Soll:* state_store.py:18 _PARTNER_ACCESS_SESSION_TYPE = "partner_access"; used in load_partner_access_session (291-297) and save_partner_access_session (299-313) -> sessions_db.upsert_session(..., _PARTNER_ACCESS_SESSION_TYPE, ...)

*Rust-Ist:* session.rs:234 pub const PARTNER_ACCESS_SESSION_TYPE: &str = "partner_token"; (doc comment lines 232-233 explicitly justifies it via Python session["auth_type"]="partner_token" in services.py, NOT the state_store row session_type). Used as the dashboard_sessions.session_type when persisting/loading partner-access rows.

**Verifikation:** Independently confirmed the divergence in the real code. Python's durable partner-access DB row carries session_type = "partner_access": state_store.py:18 defines _PARTNER_ACCESS_SESSION_TYPE = "partner_access", and save_partner_access_session (lines 299-313) passes it as the session_type arg to sessions_db.upsert_session (line 309); load_partner_access_session (291-297) reads the same. The "partner_token" string in Python (services.py:603/622) is only a payload field session["auth_type"], stored INSIDE the encrypted payload dict — NOT the row discriminator column. Rust uses the wrong source: rust/crates/tb-dashboard-api/src/auth/session.rs:234 sets PARTNER_ACCESS_SESSION_TYPE = "partner_token" and its doc comment (line 233) explicitly mis-justifies it via Python's auth_type rather than the state_store session_type. That constant is bound directly to the dashboard_sessions.session_type column in persist_new_session (INSERT … session_type = $2, lines 385-396) and in the fetch query (WHERE session_type = $2, line 449 pattern via fetch_session_payload, line 677/839). Therefore a partner-access row persisted by Python ("partner_access") is invisible to the Rust loader (filters "partner_token") and vice versa. Real, reviewable gap; not refuted. Within a pure-Rust deployment it is internally consistent, hence medium, but any pre-cutover Python-issued durable partner cookies (and pre-existing partner_access rows in the live DB) are silently dropped by Rust, forcing re-login.

**Laufzeit-Check:** On the live DB, list distinct session_type values in dashboard_sessions; presence of any 'partner_access' rows alongside Rust filtering on 'partner_token' confirms the orphaned-row gap.

### P2.132 [dashboard:state_store] Discord-admin and affiliate OAuth state save/consume flows have no Rust counterpart (deferred login flows)

- **Python:** `state_store.py:150-228`
- **Rust:** `ABSENT`
- **Status:** missing

**Befund:** The Discord-admin dashboard login and the affiliate OAuth/connect onboarding flows present in live Python are not yet portable in the Rust dashboard — there is no way to mint/consume those OAuth states. This is consistent with the documented migration plan (admin login = deferred ticket B3-10), so it is an expected gap rather than a silent regression, but until those tickets land the Rust dashboard cannot service Discord-admin or affiliate logins that Python currently handles. Flagged so the gap is tracked against the cutover, not lost.

*Python-Soll:* state_store.py:150-228 save/consume/load for _DISCORD_ADMIN_OAUTH_STATE_TYPE, _AFFILIATE_OAUTH_STATE_TYPE, _AFFILIATE_CONNECT_STATE_TYPE. Live callers: server_v2.py:180-181,332 (state dicts), auth_mixin.py:919 (discord_admin oauth states), affiliate_mixin.py:188-197 (affiliate_oauth_state).

*Rust-Ist:* ABSENT (searched: grep -rn 'oauth_state:discord_admin|oauth_state:affiliate|affiliate_connect|discord_admin_oauth' crates/tb-dashboard-api bin/tb-dashboard -> 0 state-flow hits). forward_auth.rs:24 documents Discord-Admin-Login as ticket B3-10 'Phase 1' (not yet built); affiliate_portal.rs is a read-only stats page with no OAuth state handling.

**Verifikation:** Verified against real code. Python bot/dashboard/auth/state_store.py:12-16 defines _DISCORD_ADMIN_OAUTH_STATE_TYPE ("oauth_state:discord_admin"), _AFFILIATE_OAUTH_STATE_TYPE ("oauth_state:affiliate"), _AFFILIATE_CONNECT_STATE_TYPE ("oauth_state:affiliate_connect"), each with save/consume/load methods (lines 150-228). The cited path "dashboard_v2/state_store.py" is wrong (actual is bot/dashboard/auth/state_store.py), but the substance holds. On the Rust side, the dashboard session/state layer (crates/tb-dashboard-api/src/auth/session.rs) implements only two OAuth-state flows: OAUTH_STATE_SESSION_TYPE="oauth_state:twitch" (save/consume_oauth_login_state) and PARTNER_LOGIN_STATE_TYPE="oauth_state:partner_login" (save/consume_partner_login_state). Grepping the whole rust/ tree for "oauth_state:discord_admin", "oauth_state:affiliate", "affiliate_connect", and save/consume_discord_admin/affiliate_oauth yields zero state-flow hits (only unrelated affiliate_pii storage). The "discord_admin" string in Rust is the post-login SESSION type (session.rs:357,366) and a cookie/auth-level discriminator, not the OAuth-state mint/consume. forward_auth.rs:21-27 explicitly documents the Discord-admin login as deferred ticket B3-10. affiliate_portal.rs and admin_affiliate.rs are read-only stats/admin views with no authorize/redirect/connect/state_token handling — affiliate access in Rust rides the existing Twitch Partner/Admin session, with no affiliate OAuth onboarding. So the gap is real and correctly classified as an expected/tracked cutover gap, not a silent regression. Medium severity is appropriate: it is a missing onboarding/login path, but flagged-and-deferred rather than a break in live Rust behavior.

**Laufzeit-Check:** In the live Rust dashboard, attempt the Discord-admin login flow and an affiliate OAuth/connect onboarding; observe there is no endpoint that mints/consumes an oauth_state:discord_admin or oauth_state:affiliate(_connect) token (only oauth_state:twitch and oauth_state:partner_login exist).

### P2.133 [dashboard:partner_auth_mixin] Rate limiting removed on both partner auth routes

- **Python:** `partner_auth_mixin.py:109-110`
- **Rust:** `lib.rs:666-673`
- **Status:** missing

**Befund:** The one-time-login issuance and token-consume endpoints have no brute-force / abuse throttle in Rust. Token-consume (login) in particular is now unthrottled, easing token-guessing/replay attempts against the 401 path.

*Python-Soll:* partner_auth_mixin.py:109-110 (link: _check_rate_limit max_requests=10, window=60s → 429) and 152-153 (login: max_requests=20, window=60s → 429).

*Rust-Ist:* lib.rs:666-673 build_partner_login_router registers both routes with NO rate-limit layer; handlers/partner_login.rs link_handler/login_handler contain no rate-limit logic. ABSENT (searched: grep -n 'rate' handlers/partner_login.rs, lib.rs partner router block).

**Verifikation:** Verified in current code. Python enforces _check_rate_limit on both partner routes (partner_auth_mixin.py:109-110 link=10/60s→429, 152-153 login=20/60s→429; method defined at auth_mixin.py:615). Rust: build_partner_login_router (lib.rs:666-673) registers /twitch/auth/partner/link and /twitch/auth/partner/login with no rate-limit (nor CSRF) layer; handlers link_handler/login_handler (partner_login.rs) contain zero rate-limit logic. A reusable RateLimiter exists (auth/security.rs, exported lib.rs:18) and is wired into other handlers (self_explainer, title) but NOT into the partner routes. The merged app (lib.rs:820-838) adds only CompressionLayer globally — no rate-limit middleware covers these routes. So the throttle is genuinely absent vs. Python = real regression. Note: impact is overstated — login is HMAC-verified + atomic single-use state consume (replay-proof, no realistic guessing vector) and link requires auth.is_privileged(), so this is defense-in-depth loss rather than an open brute-force hole. Medium is acceptable, leaning low.

**Laufzeit-Check:** Hammer POST /twitch/auth/partner/login with >20 requests in 60s from one client and confirm none return HTTP 429 (Rust) vs. Python which returns 429 after the threshold.

### P2.134 [dashboard:partner_auth_mixin] admin_session same-origin guard dropped on link issuance

- **Python:** `partner_auth_mixin.py:107-108`
- **Rust:** `handlers/partner_login.rs:82-91`
- **Status:** missing

**Befund:** For Discord-admin-session (cookie-based) callers, Python required the request to be same-origin (CSRF-style protection) before minting a partner login link. Rust grants the privileged Admin level a link without that guard, so a cross-site request riding an admin's session cookie could mint a partner login link.

*Python-Soll:* partner_auth_mixin.py:107-108 — if auth_mode == 'admin_session' and not _partner_link_has_same_origin(request): raise HTTPForbidden('same-origin partner link request required').

*Rust-Ist:* handlers/partner_login.rs:82-91 link_handler only checks auth.is_privileged(); no Origin/Referer/Host same-origin validation. ABSENT (searched: grep -n 'origin\|Origin\|Referer\|same' handlers/partner_login.rs — none).

**Verifikation:** Python (partner_auth_mixin.py:107-108) guards the partner-link issuance: when auth_mode == "admin_session" (cookie-based Discord-admin or admin auth-level, per services.py:446-486, the CSRF-vulnerable class — distinct from localhost/noauth/admin_header) it requires same-origin via _partner_link_has_same_origin (Origin/Referer must match Host). The Rust link_handler (handlers/partner_login.rs:75-91) only checks auth.is_privileged() (true for DashboardAuthLevel::Admin{..}, which IS the master_dash_session cookie path per level.rs:6-7,40-67). I grepped the whole tb-dashboard-api crate for same-origin/Origin/Referer/Sec-Fetch logic: the only Referer hit is a doc comment in forward_auth.rs; no handler validates request origin. I also verified the route is NOT behind an equivalent CSRF gate: csrf_protect is applied via .layer() on the sub-routers at lib.rs:489/553/637, but build_partner_login_router (lib.rs:666-673) carries only .with_state(pool) and is .merge()d at lib.rs:823 — axum .merge() does not propagate sibling-router layers, so the partner/link POST has neither same-origin nor CSRF-token validation. The guard is genuinely absent. Severity kept at medium rather than higher because the admin cookie master_dash_session is set SameSite=Lax (session.rs build_session_cookie), which already blocks classic cross-site POST form submissions, so the missing same-origin check is primarily a defense-in-depth/belt-and-suspenders regression rather than a wide-open hole.

**Laufzeit-Check:** From a different origin, send a POST to /twitch/auth/partner/link carrying a valid master_dash_session admin cookie with a cross-site Origin/Referer header and observe a 200 with a minted login_token instead of a 403 same-origin rejection.

### P2.135 [dashboard:partner_auth_mixin] Rust adds a partner-resolution gate (find_partner_for_login -> 403) that Python's one-time login never performed

- **Python:** `partner_auth_mixin.py:150-204`
- **Rust:** `handlers/partner_login.rs:179-188`
- **Status:** changed

**Befund:** Behavior differs: Python issues an access session for any valid token regardless of current twitch_partners state; Rust denies (403) if the bound login is not a currently non-blocked partner. This is arguably stricter/safer, but it is a real behavioral change and couples link issuance to a mandatory 'login' body field Python did not require (link_handler 400s without it).

*Python-Soll:* partner_auth_mixin.py:150-204 auth_partner_login consumes the token and creates the access session purely from next_path; it does NOT look up twitch_partners and the token/state carry no partner identity (services.py:362-368 payload has only v/sid/next/iat/exp; state payload services.py:369-373 has no login).

*Rust-Ist:* handlers/partner_login.rs:179-188 login_handler calls state.find_partner_for_login(&login,'') and returns 403 'Kein aktiver Partner' when no non-blocked partner row matches; the 'login' is bound at link time (partner_login.rs:94-102 requires body.login, session.rs:534 stores it).

**Verifikation:** Independently confirmed in current code. Python one-time partner login carries NO partner identity and performs NO twitch_partners lookup: auth_partner_link (partner_auth_mixin.py:102-148) reads only next_path; PartnerLoginTokenService.issue (services.py:345-391) builds token payload {v,sid,next,iat,exp} and state {created_at,expires_at,next_path} with no login; consume (services.py:393-444) returns only next_path/issued_at/expires_at; auth_partner_login (lines 150-204) creates the session via PartnerAccessService.create (services.py:617-640) which stores {auth_type:partner_token, request-binding, timestamps} with no twitch_login/user_id, no DB partner query, no blocked check. Rust diverges: link_handler (partner_login.rs:94-102) requires body.login (400 if missing) — a field Python never required; save_partner_login_state (session.rs:525-548) stores login; login_handler (partner_login.rs:179-188) calls find_partner_for_login(&login,'') (session.rs:603, SELECT FROM twitch_partners WHERE technical_pause_reason <> 'blocked') and returns 403 'Kein aktiver Partner' on no match. So Rust adds a mandatory partner-resolution gate and a mandatory login body field that Python's flow lacked. Real behavioral divergence; stricter/safer (not a security weakening), hence medium not high.

**Laufzeit-Check:** Issue a one-time partner login token for a login whose twitch_partners row is missing or technical_pause_reason='blocked', then POST it to /twitch/auth/partner/login: Python yields a 302 partner session, Rust returns 403; also POST /twitch/auth/partner/link without a login field — Rust 400s, Python succeeds.

### P2.136 [dashboard:partner_auth_mixin] No rate limiting and no same-origin check on partner link/login routes

- **Python:** `partner_auth_mixin.py:109-110`
- **Rust:** `handlers/partner_login.rs:75-128`
- **Status:** missing

**Befund:** Token-issuance and token-consume endpoints are unthrottled in Rust, removing brute-force/abuse protection Python had. The admin-session same-origin CSRF guard on link issuance is also gone, so an admin's browser could be induced cross-site to mint a partner login link.

*Python-Soll:* partner_auth_mixin.py:109-110 auth_partner_link enforces _check_rate_limit(max_requests=10, window=60s) and 107-108 requires same-origin (Origin/Referer == Host) when auth_mode=='admin_session'; auth_partner_login:152-153 enforces _check_rate_limit(max_requests=20, window=60s).

*Rust-Ist:* handlers/partner_login.rs:75-128 link_handler and 135-216 login_handler contain no rate-limit and no Origin/Referer check (grep 'rate_limit|same_origin|Origin|Referer' over partner_login.rs + auth/ shows the limiter lives in auth/security.rs but is not wired into these handlers).

**Verifikation:** Verified against current code. Python partner_auth_mixin.py: auth_partner_link enforces a same-origin (Origin/Referer==Host) check when auth_mode=='admin_session' (107-108) and a 10-req/60s rate limit (109-110); auth_partner_login enforces a 20-req/60s rate limit (152-153). Rust handlers/partner_login.rs link_handler (75-128) gates only on auth.is_privileged() — no Origin/Referer check and no rate limit; login_handler (135-216) does HMAC verify + atomic single-use state consume but no rate limit. A DB-backed sliding-window RateLimiter exists in auth/security.rs (its own docstring at line 27 even cites partner_auth_mixin.py:109) and is exported from lib.rs:18, but grep shows allow()/allow_login() have ZERO production call sites — referenced only by security.rs's own tests. build_partner_login_router (lib.rs:666, merged at 823) registers both POST routes with only .with_state(pool) — no rate-limit layer, no origin middleware. The self_explainer.rs RateLimiter is a separate in-memory limiter for the public ask endpoint, unrelated. So both regressions are real: the porting work built the limiter but never wired it into these handlers, and the admin-session same-origin CSRF guard on link issuance was dropped entirely. Severity stays medium: link issuance is still behind is_privileged() (admin/loopback) so the missing same-origin guard is a conditional CSRF surface, and login retains HMAC + single-use replay protection that blunts brute force — but token-issuance/consume are genuinely unthrottled vs Python.

**Laufzeit-Check:** Hit POST /twitch/auth/partner/login many times in <60s with bogus tokens from one IP and confirm no 429 ever returns; and as an authenticated admin send POST /twitch/auth/partner/link with a foreign Origin header and confirm it still returns 200 instead of 403.

### P2.137 [dashboard:auth_mixin] TWITCH_DASHBOARD_AUTH_REDIRECT_URI validation dropped (_build_oauth_redirect_uri not ported)

- **Python:** `auth_mixin.py:153-198`
- **Rust:** `crates/tb-dashboard-api/src/handlers/auth_login.rs:596`
- **Status:** missing

**Befund:** A misconfigured TWITCH_DASHBOARD_AUTH_REDIRECT_URI that points to /twitch/raid/callback, uses http on a non-localhost host, carries userinfo, or targets an unexpected path is silently accepted in Rust. Python would log a warning and disable native login; Rust builds the authorize URL with the bad redirect_uri. Worst case the value collides with the raid callback path (which Python explicitly forbids) or an attacker-influenced path, undermining the callback-path guarantee. This is the (d) 'OAuth redirect / env wiring' migration bug class.

*Python-Soll:* auth_mixin.py:153-198 _build_oauth_redirect_uri: validates scheme in {https,http}, http only for 127.0.0.1/localhost/::1, rejects userinfo, requires netloc, REJECTS path=='/twitch/raid/callback', and whitelists path to only LEGACY ('/twitch/auth/callback') or SHARED ('/callback/twitch'); returns None (disabling native login) on any violation.

*Rust-Ist:* crates/tb-dashboard-api/src/handlers/auth_login.rs:596 oauth_login_config_from_env reads `let redirect_uri = non_empty_env("TWITCH_DASHBOARD_AUTH_REDIRECT_URI")?;` with NO validation. ABSENT (searched: grep 'raid/callback|build_oauth_redirect|must use http|user info|must point to' over auth_login.rs+session.rs; only normalize_raid_success_redirect_url at auth_login.rs:465 found, which validates the raid SUCCESS redirect target, not the OAuth authorize redirect_uri).

**Verifikation:** Confirmed in current code. Python bot/dashboard/auth/auth_mixin.py:153-198 _build_oauth_redirect_uri validates the OAuth authorize redirect_uri: rejects userinfo, requires scheme in {https,http} with http only for 127.0.0.1/localhost/::1, requires netloc, explicitly rejects path=='/twitch/raid/callback', and whitelists path to LEGACY('/twitch/auth/callback') or SHARED('/callback/twitch'), returning None (disabling native login) on any violation. The Rust counterpart oauth_login_config_from_env (auth_login.rs:596) reads non_empty_env("TWITCH_DASHBOARD_AUTH_REDIRECT_URI") with no validation, and login_handler (auth_login.rs:116-137) only checks redirect_uri is non-empty before passing it raw into build_login_authorize_url. I grepped the whole rust workspace for raid/callback, 'must use http', 'must point to', userinfo handling and redirect validation; the only validator found is normalize_raid_success_redirect_url (auth_login.rs:465), which validates the raid SUCCESS redirect target (scheme/host/userinfo only — and even it lacks the path checks), NOT the OAuth authorize redirect_uri. So the validation, including the explicit raid-callback-path collision guard and the callback-path whitelist, is genuinely absent. Severity downgraded from high to medium: the value is operator-controlled env (Infisical), not attacker input, and Twitch's own registered-redirect-uri enforcement at the authorize step backstops arbitrary paths from completing OAuth; the real regression is dropped defense-in-depth/config hardening (silent acceptance + no warn log + raid-callback-collision guard), not a directly exploitable auth bypass.

**Laufzeit-Check:** Set TWITCH_DASHBOARD_AUTH_REDIRECT_URI to http://evil.example/twitch/raid/callback, restart tb-dashboard, hit /twitch/auth/login and confirm Rust 302-redirects to a Twitch authorize URL carrying that bad redirect_uri instead of logging a warning and serving the unconfigured/503 native-login-disabled response.

### P2.138 [dashboard:auth_mixin] auth_login: Rate-Limit (30 Req/60s -> 429) fehlt in Rust

- **Python:** `auth_mixin.py:1216-1217`
- **Rust:** `ABSENT`
- **Status:** missing

**Befund:** Der OAuth-Login-Startpunkt ist in Rust ungedrosselt. Python bremst Flooding (z.B. State-Spam in dashboard_sessions) mit 429; Rust nicht. Jeder Aufruf legt eine neue State-Row an -> potenzielle DB-Aufblaehung/Missbrauch.

*Python-Soll:* auth_mixin.py:1216-1217: if not self._check_rate_limit(request, max_requests=30, window_seconds=60.0): return web.Response(text='Zu viele Anfragen...', status=429)

*Rust-Ist:* ABSENT (searched: grep -rn 'rate_limit|429|max_requests|GovernorLayer|TOO_MANY' crates/tb-dashboard-api/src/handlers/auth_login.rs ; login_handler auth_login.rs:108-139 hat keinen Rate-Limit-Schritt, keine RateLimit-Layer auf der /twitch/auth/login-Route in lib.rs:650)

**Verifikation:** Python auth_mixin.py:1216-1217 (auth_login) gates the OAuth login start with _check_rate_limit(max_requests=30, window=60s) -> 429. In Rust, login_handler (auth_login.rs:108-139) goes config-check -> sanitize_next -> save_oauth_login_state -> redirect, with NO rate-limit step, and the route lib.rs:650 (.route("/twitch/auth/login", get(login_handler))) has no limiting layer. A DB-backed sliding-window RateLimiter DOES exist (security.rs, explicitly documented as the port of the Python store) but is DEAD CODE: RateLimiter::new has zero call sites outside its own module and allow_login/.allow() is never invoked anywhere in the crate; lib.rs only re-exports it and uses require_internal. The other RateLimiters found (self_explainer.rs in-memory, TitleRateLimiter) are unrelated endpoints. So the running Rust login path is genuinely ungated. Every call still INSERTs a state row in dashboard_sessions, matching the claimed DB-bloat/abuse impact. (Minor inaccuracy: claim says 30/60s; security.rs default is 10/60s and Python auth_login uses 30 — value differs but the gap is real either way.) Medium is correct: soft brute-force/abuse dampening on an unauthenticated endpoint (Python itself fail-opens on DB error), not an auth bypass.

**Laufzeit-Check:** Hammer GET /twitch/auth/login >30 times within 60s from one client against the live Rust dashboard and confirm none return 429 (Python returned 429 after 30); cross-check by counting new rate_limit:dashboard_auth rows vs no throttle.

### P2.139 [dashboard:auth_mixin] OAuth context-token cookie CSRF binding dropped in Rust callback

- **Python:** `auth_mixin.py:1335-1349`
- **Rust:** `auth_login.rs:170-328`
- **Status:** missing

**Befund:** Rust relies solely on the single-use server-side state token for CSRF defense. The extra cookie-binding layer (ties the callback to the same browser that started login) is gone, weakening login-CSRF / state-fixation resistance compared to Python. The single-use state still blocks replay, so impact is a reduced-defense-in-depth rather than an open hole — but it is a real security-behavior reduction vs the source of truth.

*Python-Soll:* auth_mixin.py:1335-1349 — callback requires a per-flow context_token: reads expected from state, presented from the oauth-context cookie, validates format and secrets.compare_digest; any mismatch/missing → 400. Set at login via _set_oauth_context_cookie (cookie name _oauth_context_cookie_name, helpers at 704-715).

*Rust-Ist:* auth_login.rs:170-328 callback_handler_inner performs no cookie/context-token check; login_handler (auth_login.rs:108-139) sets no context cookie. ABSENT (searched: grep -rni 'context_token|oauth_context|oauth_ctx|ctx_token|context.cookie' crates/tb-dashboard-api/src → no relevant hit). OAuthLoginState carries only next_path + redirect_uri (session.rs:481-494).

**Verifikation:** Confirmed regression. Python auth_callback (bot/dashboard/auth/auth_mixin.py:1335-1349) requires a per-flow context token: expected value read from the persisted OAuth state payload (set at login line 1249 + cookie at 1272 via _set_oauth_context_cookie), presented value read from the oauth-context cookie, validated for format and compared via secrets.compare_digest — any missing/mismatch returns 400 and clears the cookie. The Rust login_handler (handlers/auth_login.rs:108-139) builds OAuthLoginState { next_path, redirect_uri } only, generates no context token and sets no context cookie; callback_handler_inner (170-328) consumes the single-use state, exchanges code, gates partner and creates the session, but performs NO cookie/context-token check. OAuthLoginState decode (auth/session.rs:481-494) reads only next_path + redirect_uri — no context_token field exists at all. Independent grep over crates/tb-dashboard-api/src for context_token|oauth_context|oauth_ctx|ctx_token|context.cookie returns no non-test hit. So the browser-binding defense-in-depth layer is genuinely absent in Rust. The single-use state token still blocks replay/fixation of a consumed state, so the practical impact is reduced login-CSRF/state-fixation resistance rather than an open hole — medium is correct.

**Laufzeit-Check:** Start the native OAuth login in one browser, copy the resulting callback URL (code+state), then open it in a different browser/incognito with no cookies — Rust completes the login (302 + session cookie) whereas Python returns 400 "OAuth state ungültig oder abgelaufen".

### P2.140 [dashboard:auth_mixin] No rate-limiting on Rust OAuth callback / login / discord-link routes

- **Python:** `auth_mixin.py:1278-1279`
- **Rust:** `auth_login.rs:170`
- **Status:** missing

**Befund:** The OAuth callback and Discord-link endpoints can be hammered without the per-IP 429 throttle Python enforced, increasing brute-force / abuse surface on auth endpoints (e.g. state-token guessing, broker-call amplification). Functional login still works; this is a dropped protective side-effect.

*Python-Soll:* auth_mixin.py:1278-1279 auth_callback _check_rate_limit(30/60s)→429; 1442-1446 discord_auth_login 10/60s; 1509-1513 discord_auth_complete 20/60s; 1689-1693 discord_link_auth_login 10/60s; 1745-1749 discord_link_auth_complete 20/60s — all return 429/Retry-After.

*Rust-Ist:* callback_handler_inner (auth_login.rs:170) and login_handler (auth_login.rs:108) contain no rate-limit; discord_link.rs:64/107 contain none. RateLimiter exists (auth/security.rs:35) but is only referenced by handlers/title.rs and handlers/self_explainer.rs (grep -rln 'RateLimiter::' → those two files only); not wired in lib.rs for the auth router (lib.rs:650-653).

**Verifikation:** Independently confirmed in current code. Python enforces per-IP 429 throttles: auth_mixin.py:1216/1278 callback (30/60s), 1442 discord_login (10/60s), 1509 discord_complete (20/60s), 1689/1745 discord_link login/complete (10/60s, 20/60s) — all returning 429/Retry-After. Rust counterparts have NO rate-limit: build_auth_router() (lib.rs:648-655) registers login/callback/shared-callback/logout with zero limiter layer; auth_login.rs login_handler (108) and callback_handler_inner (170) contain none; discord_link.rs link_start_handler (64) and link_complete_handler (107) and build_discord_link_router (191) contain none. The purpose-built DB-backed auth limiter auth::security::RateLimiter (security.rs:35, whose own doc-comment cites porting DashboardAuthRateLimitStore.allow_request) is DEAD CODE: grep shows it is only re-exported in lib.rs:18 and never instantiated/called anywhere (no RateLimiter::new / allow_request / reserve_rate_limit usage of it). The RateLimiter::new hits in self_explainer.rs are a different locally-defined struct (self_explainer.rs:275); title.rs uses tb-chat's TitleRateLimiter. So the per-IP throttle Python enforced on these auth/OAuth/discord-link endpoints is genuinely absent — a dropped protective side-effect (login still works). Severity stays medium: the state token is a 24-byte CSPRNG, single-use, atomically consumed (consume_oauth_login_state DELETE...RETURNING), so state-guessing brute force is not realistic, but the discord-link broker hop and the callback code-exchange remain un-throttled (request/abuse amplification surface).

**Laufzeit-Check:** Hammer GET /twitch/auth/login (or /twitch/auth/discord/link) from one IP >10x within 60s against the live tb-dashboard and confirm no 429/Retry-After is ever returned, whereas the Python dashboard returned 429.

### P2.141 [internal_api:policy] normalize_text_field length limit uses byte count (Rust) vs character count (Python) — rejects valid umlaut/multibyte text

- **Python:** `policy.py:319`
- **Rust:** `handlers/telemetry_routes.rs:148`
- **Status:** changed

**Befund:** A discord_username or source_hint within the character limit but containing multibyte UTF-8 (e.g. German umlauts ä/ö/ü = 2 bytes each) can exceed the byte limit and be rejected with 'invalid <field>' (400) in Rust though Python accepts it. Up to ~50% fewer allowed chars for fully non-ASCII input. Matches the project's documented umlaut-fragility concern.

*Python-Soll:* policy.py:319 `if len(text) > max_length:` (len = char count); called for discord_username max=200, source_hint max=100

*Rust-Ist:* handlers/telemetry_routes.rs:148 `if text.len() > max_length` (String::len = UTF-8 byte count); call sites telemetry_routes.rs:435 (discord_username,200) and :460 (source_hint,100)

**Verifikation:** Real code confirms the divergence. Python bot/internal_api/policy.py:319 uses `len(text)` (Unicode code-point count). The Rust counterpart — actually in tb-internal-api (not tb-dashboard as cited) at rust/crates/tb-internal-api/src/handlers/telemetry_routes.rs:148 — uses `text.len()` where `text` is a Rust `String`, returning the UTF-8 BYTE length. Call sites match the claim: line 435 discord_username max=200, line 460 source_hint max=100. Both share a docstring claiming "Parität" but the semantics diverge for non-ASCII: German umlauts (ä/ö/ü) are 2 bytes, so a value within the char limit but containing multibyte UTF-8 can exceed the byte limit and be rejected with `invalid <field>` in Rust though Python accepts it (up to ~50% fewer allowed chars for fully non-ASCII). The repo's own audit (rust/docs/audit/2026-06-13-python-rust-port-audit.md:919-924) already flagged this and recommended `text.chars().count()`; the code still uses `text.len()`, so it remains unfixed. Medium stands: silent rejection of valid non-ASCII input breaking documented parity, but bounded (only near-limit multibyte input; realistic German values rarely exceed 100/200).

**Laufzeit-Check:** POST a telemetry request with a discord_username of >100 multibyte umlaut chars (so byte length >200 but char count <200) and observe Rust returns 400 invalid request body while Python accepts it.

### P2.142 [internal_api:policy] parse_bool loose-coercion semantics lost for mark_member (serde bool instead of policy.parse_bool)

- **Python:** `policy.py:268-282`
- **Rust:** `handlers/streamers.rs:225-236`
- **Status:** changed

**Befund:** If a client sends mark_member as a JSON string ('"true"'/'"false"') or number (1/0) — accepted and coerced by Python — Rust serde deserialization fails the whole request body (400/422) instead of coercing. Only absent-key falls back to default=true; an unparseable-typed value errors rather than coercing. Affects the discord-profile/member-mark route.

*Python-Soll:* policy.py:268-282 parse_bool coerces strings ('yes'/'on'/'0'/'false'...) and numbers to bool; routes/streamers.py:350 `mark_member = server._parse_bool(body.get('mark_member', body.get('member_flag')), default=True)`

*Rust-Ist:* handlers/streamers.rs:225-236 `pub mark_member: bool` with `#[serde(default = "default_mark_member")]` (default true) — raw bool deserialization, no string/number coercion

**Verifikation:** Confirmed in actual code. Python bot/internal_api/policy.py:268-282 parse_bool loose-coerces strings ('yes'/'on'/'1'/'0'/'false'...) and numbers to bool, and routes/streamers.py:350 uses server._parse_bool(body.get('mark_member', body.get('member_flag')), default=True). The Rust counterpart in rust/crates/tb-internal-api/src/handlers/streamers.rs deserializes the discord-profile body via Json<DiscordProfileRequest> (handler line 868); the struct field is `pub mark_member: bool` with `#[serde(default = "default_mark_member")]` (lines 226-231) — raw serde bool, which only accepts JSON true/false. A loose-coercion helper parse_bool_loose DOES exist in the same file (line 384) and is correctly applied to require_link (line 341), but it is NOT applied to mark_member. Therefore a client sending mark_member as a JSON string ("true"/"false") or number (1/0), which Python accepts and coerces, makes serde fail the entire request body (422). Absent key falls back to default=true in both. The gap/regression is real; the sibling parse_bool_loose used elsewhere shows it was simply not wired here.

**Laufzeit-Check:** POST to /internal/twitch/v1/streamers/:login/discord-profile with valid token and JSON body where mark_member is "true" (string) or 1 (number); Rust returns 422 while Python accepts and coerces it.

### P2.143 [internal_api:streamers] Idempotency layer entirely missing on all six streamer mutation routes (add/remove/verify/archive/discord-flag/discord-profile)

- **Python:** `streamers.py:39-52,85-91`
- **Rust:** `ABSENT`
- **Status:** missing

**Befund:** Duplicate/retried POST/DELETE requests (client retry, network retry, double-click) are NOT deduplicated on streamer routes. A retried 'add' re-runs add+backfill; retried 'verify' re-promotes; retried 'remove' re-departners. No 409 on key-reuse-with-different-body, no replay of cached responses. The shared Rust idempotency layer already exists and is wired to raid/telemetry — only streamers were left incomplete. This is the documented-but-unfinished part of the migration.

*Python-Soll:* streamers.py:39-52,85-91 (add), 95-112,127-133 (remove), 147-160,182-188 (verify), 202-215,237-243 (archive), 256-270,306-312 (discord-flag), 326-339,380-386 (discord-profile) — every handler calls server._prepare_idempotency(...) → handles replay (200 + X-Idempotency-Replayed:1), in-flight dedup (await _wait_idempotency_result, 30s timeout → 503), 409 idempotency_conflict on key reuse with different fingerprint, 400 on key >128 chars, and _release_idempotency_owner in finally to cache the result. Impl: app.py:587-681.

*Rust-Ist:* ABSENT (searched: `grep -rni 'idempoten' rust/crates/tb-internal-api/src/handlers/streamers.rs` → only doc comments at lines 52/403/450, no code; `grep -n 'IdempotencyState' src/handlers/streamers.rs` → none; lib.rs:296 adds Extension(IdempotencyState::new()) but only raid_oauth.rs:700 and telemetry_routes.rs:37 extract/use it — none of streamers::{add,remove,verify,archive,discord_flag,discord_profile}_handler take the Extension or call prepare()). Module doc streamers.rs:52 explicitly: 'Kein Idempotency-Caching (kommt mit dem geteilten Idempotenz-Layer)'.

**Verifikation:** Independently confirmed the gap in current code. The shared idempotency layer fully exists in Rust (rust/crates/tb-internal-api/src/idempotency.rs: IdempotencyState, prepare(), 400 on key>128, 409 idempotency_conflict on fingerprint mismatch, replay with X-Idempotency-Replayed:1, in-flight wait→503/timeout) and is wired into the router (lib.rs:296 layers Extension(IdempotencyState::new())). It IS consumed by raid_oauth.rs:700 and telemetry. But none of the six streamer mutation handlers consume it: I read the signatures of add_handler (streamers.rs:285-290), remove_handler (458-463) and discord_flag_handler (788-793) and grepped all handlers — none extract Extension<IdempotencyState> nor call prepare()/Prepared. The module doc at streamers.rs:52 explicitly states the streamer routes have no idempotency caching ("kommt mit dem geteilten Idempotenz-Layer"). So Python's per-route idempotency (replay/dedup/409/400) is genuinely absent on add/remove/verify/archive/discord-flag/discord-profile in Rust — a real, documented migration gap. Downgraded from high to medium: the layer protects only requests that send an Idempotency-Key header (opt-in; missing key → skipped), so it is not blanket dedup of all double-clicks/retries; the underlying writes are also largely self-correcting (re-add/re-verify converge to same partner state) and the layer already exists, leaving only handler wiring outstanding.

**Laufzeit-Check:** Send two identical POST /internal/twitch/v1/streamers requests with the same Idempotency-Key header; Rust runs the add twice with no X-Idempotency-Replayed:1 on the second, whereas Python returns 200 + X-Idempotency-Replayed:1.

### P2.144 [internal_api:discord_log] Idempotency-Key divergiert für Non-ASCII-Payloads (ensure_ascii=True nicht reproduziert)

- **Python:** `discord_log.py:63`
- **Rust:** `self_explainer_log.rs:129-151`
- **Status:** changed

**Befund:** Bewiesen via Python-Vergleich: payload mit embed-title 'Frägé' → ensure_ascii=True hash48=06f47d4f41b38644fc496207a1d12950bf56faf2f6b81799 vs ensure_ascii=False=f610fa8c0310f2482477fded7c8060755a6a278be76d0583. Damit weicht der Rust-X-Idempotency-Key bei jedem Embed/Content mit deutschen Umlauten (ä/ö/ü) oder Emoji vom Python-Key ab. Folgen: (1) Dedup-Kontinuität gegenüber der Python-Implementierung gebrochen (gemischter Betrieb / Replays über den Cutover); (2) der ASCII-Längenvorteil von ensure_ascii (≤128-Zeichen-Garantie des Broker-Keys wird hier zwar durch [:48] ohnehin gehalten). Request selbst geht durch (Broker akzeptiert), aber die Dedup-Semantik des 'gewollten Dedup desselben Besuchers mit identischer Frage+Antwort' (docstring L60-61) divergiert genau bei den deutschsprachigen Inhalten, die dieser Bot typischerweise sendet. Fix: kanonisches JSON mit ASCII-Escaping (z.B. eigene Serialisierung die Non-ASCII zu \uXXXX escaped) statt serde_json::to_string.

*Python-Soll:* discord_log.py:63 — json.dumps(payload, ensure_ascii=True, sort_keys=True, separators=(',',':')); L64-65 sha256 hexdigest[:48] → key 'self-explainer:<hex>'. ensure_ascii=True escaped Non-ASCII zu \uXXXX.

*Rust-Ist:* self_explainer_log.rs:129-151 (canonical_json) ruft serde_json::to_string(&sort_value(value)); Kommentar L149-150 behauptet 'serde_json escaped Non-ASCII per \uXXXX, daher byte-identisch' — das ist falsch. serde_json::to_string gibt rohes UTF-8 aus (escaped nur 0x00-0x1F, '"', '\\'), KEIN \uXXXX für 0x80+.

**Verifikation:** Bestätigt im echten Code. Das Rust-Gegenstück liegt nicht in tb-analytics/self_explainer_log.rs (das ist nur das DB-Log), sondern in tb-internal-api/src/handlers/self_explainer_log.rs. Dort baut idempotency_key() (L111-123) den Key via canonical_json() (L129-152), das mit serde_json::to_string(&sort_value(value)) serialisiert (L151). Die Python-Seite (bot/internal_api/routes/discord_log.py:63) nutzt json.dumps(..., ensure_ascii=True, sort_keys=True, separators=(',',':')), L64-65 sha256().hexdigest()[:48] → 'self-explainer:<hex>'. Der Kommentar im Rust-Code (L149-150) behauptet, serde_json escape Non-ASCII per \uXXXX und sei daher byte-identisch — das ist nachweislich falsch: serde_json::to_string gibt rohes UTF-8 aus (escaped nur 0x00-0x1F, '\"', '\\'), kein \uXXXX für Codepoints ≥0x80. Es gibt keinen Custom-Serializer/Formatter (grep bestätigt: nur stock serde_json, workspace-dep). Empirisch reproduziert: Payload {'embed':{'title':'Frägé'}} → ensure_ascii=True serialisiert '{\"embed\":{\"title\":\"Fr\\u00e4g\\u00e9\"}}' (hash48 ed2a5db5...), ensure_ascii=False = raw UTF-8 '{\"embed\":{\"title\":\"Frägé\"}}' (hash48 a11cfba3...) — verschiedene Digests. serde_json produziert exakt die ensure_ascii=False-Form, weicht also bei jedem Embed/Content mit ä/ö/ü oder Emoji vom Python-Key ab. Folge: Dedup-Kontinuität gegenüber Python (gemischter Betrieb / Replays über den Cutover) bricht genau bei den deutschsprachigen Inhalten, die dieser Bot typischerweise sendet. Request selbst geht durch (Broker akzeptiert Key, ≤128 Zeichen durch [:48] gewahrt), aber die Cross-Impl-Dedup-Semantik divergiert. Severity medium bestätigt: real und häufig getroffen (dt. Content = Norm), aber auf die Cutover-/Mixed-Phase begrenzt; Rust-intern bleibt der Key deterministisch.

**Laufzeit-Check:** Laufzeit: gleichen self-explainer-Payload mit Umlaut im embed-title (z.B. 'Frägé') durch Python-Route und Rust-Handler schicken und den erzeugten X-Idempotency-Key vergleichen — sie unterscheiden sich.

The limit (1..200) bounded view of the processing inbox is unavailable natively. Registered at telemetry.py:329 web.get(f'{base}/debug/eventsub-processing', ...).

The Rust route table (lib.rs:73-282) registers /debug/observability, /debug/chatters/:login, and /eventsub/processing/requeue, but NOT /debug/eventsub-processing. A grep for the route string 'eventsub-processing' across ALL rust/crates/**/*.rs returns zero hits. Unmatched requests fall through to .fallback(legacy_fallback_handler) (lib.rs:283), which returns StatusCode::NOT_FOUND (legacy_proxy.rs:74-80) when no Python proxy is configured — which is the cutover state (Python dead). The limit-parametrized, {ok,eventsubProcessing}-shaped diagnostic view of the processing inbox is therefore unavailable natively; the 'eventsubProcessing' block in python_stubs.rs::observability_handler serves a different route (/debug/observability) and is hard-capped at 20, so it does not satisfy this endpoint's contract. The tb-monitoring inbox_store has list_pending/list_dead_letters that exist but are not wired to any HTTP handler. Severity medium is correct: operator/support-only diagnostic, no known internal consumer, data partially exists elsewhere — but the dedicated endpoint and its contract are genuinely dropped.

**Laufzeit-Check:** curl with the internal token against GET /internal/twitch/v1/debug/eventsub-processing?limit=50 and observe HTTP 404 {"error":"not_found"} instead of a {ok,eventsubProcessing} JSON body.

### P3.1 [chat:connection] Chat-join observability events (twitch_observability_events) not written in Rust

- **Python:** `connection.py:203-211`
- **Rust:** `ABSENT`
- **Status:** missing

**Befund:** The per-channel chat-join decision audit trail (flow_id, remaining_missing scopes, subscription_state, decision/detail, mod_retry_cooldown, exception) that Python persisted to twitch_observability_events and exposed as _last_chat_join_diagnostic is not produced by Rust. Any admin dashboard / diagnostics reading twitch_observability_events for flow_type='chat_join' shows nothing for the Rust path. Pure observability loss; live join behavior itself still works via the webhook reconcile.

*Python-Soll:* connection.py:203-211 _log_chat_join_decision calls insert_observability_event(flow_type='chat_join', flow_id=..., entity_login, entity_id, step='terminal_decision', decision=..., details=payload); plus L197 stores self._last_chat_join_diagnostic.

*Rust-Ist:* ABSENT (searched: grep -rn 'insert_observability_event|flow_type|chat_join|terminal_decision|join_decision' crates -> tb-observability/src/lib.rs only init_tracing; lib.rs:4 comment: 'Observability-Event-Writer (mpsc -> twitch_observability_events) kommt mit der Monitoring-Phase'). No code writes twitch_observability_events.

**Verifikation:** Independently confirmed. Python bot/chat/connection.py:203-211 (_log_chat_join_decision) calls insert_observability_event(flow_type='chat_join', step='terminal_decision', decision=..., details=payload) and stores _last_chat_join_diagnostic (L197). On the Rust side, tb-observability/src/lib.rs contains ONLY init_tracing (fmt+EnvFilter) plus an explicit comment that the event-writer "kommt mit der Monitoring-Phase" (not yet built). A hard grep across all crates/ and bin/ (excluding docs/target) for INSERT INTO twitch_observability_events, insert_observability_event, emit_event, flow_type, terminal_decision, chat_join found NO writer anywhere — the only chat-join-related Rust code (tb-monitoring scout.rs/subscriptions.rs) does streamer-table reconcile via sqlx and tracing:: log macros, never persisting structured observability events. The table exists in migrations (baseline_schema.sql + observability_hypertable.sql) but stays empty for the Rust path. So the per-channel chat-join audit trail Python wrote is genuinely absent in Rust. Severity downgraded to low: this is pure observability/diagnostics loss with no user-visible impact — live join behavior works via webhook reconcile (claim concedes this), only the admin diagnostic trail/dashboard query is empty. The project's own audit (findings-2026-06-15.json, opus-vollaudit) already flags it MITTEL/missing, calls the severity "überzogen", and notes it is likely a deliberate simplification for the webhook model.

**Laufzeit-Check:** After running the Rust bot through several chat-join reconcile cycles, query twitch_observability_events WHERE flow_type='chat_join' — it returns zero rows, confirming no Rust event persistence.

### P3.2 [chat:connection] bot-banned detection narrowed: Rust dropped Python's unconditional 'banned' substring match

- **Python:** `bot/chat/connection.py:837-843`
- **Rust:** `rust/crates/tb-chat/src/timeout_tracking.rs:73-79`
- **Status:** changed

**Befund:** Python treats any error body containing the word 'banned' as a bot ban regardless of HTTP status; Rust requires either the exact phrase 'user is banned'/'sender is banned' or a 4xx status with substring 'ban'. A 200/5xx response or a 429 body that says only 'banned' (without 'user is'/'sender is' and not 4xx) is no longer flagged. This can let a real bot-channel-ban slip through detection, so the bot keeps attempting to post into a channel where it is banned instead of recording the banned state. Rust also widened to status 401 and 'sender is banned' (improvements), but the unconditional-'banned' drop is a genuine narrowing.

*Python-Soll:* bot/chat/connection.py:837-843 — `_looks_like_bot_banned_error`: returns True if 'user is banned' in lowered, OR if 'banned' in lowered (unconditional, any status), OR if status in {400,403} and 'ban' in lowered.

*Rust-Ist:* rust/crates/tb-chat/src/timeout_tracking.rs:73-79 — returns True only for 'user is banned'/'sender is banned', OR matches!(status,400|401|403) && contains('ban'). The unconditional `'banned' in lowered` branch is gone.

**Verifikation:** Independently confirmed in current code. Python bot/chat/connection.py:837-843 (_looks_like_bot_banned_error) has an unconditional `if "banned" in lowered: return True` (lines 839-840) that fires for ANY status. Rust rust/crates/tb-chat/src/timeout_tracking.rs:73-79 (looks_like_bot_banned_error) only returns true for the exact phrases "user is banned"/"sender is banned", OR for status in {400,401,403} with substring "ban". The unconditional-"banned" branch is genuinely absent. I grepped the whole rust/crates tree for alternative banned-detection (sender_banned drop-code path, conversation_scam, global_ban) — none compensates: bot_banned_reason only calls looks_like_bot_banned_error inside the SendOutcome::HttpError arm, so an HTTP body that says only "banned" without a 4xx-in-{400,401,403} status and without the exact phrases is no longer flagged as a bot channel ban. Concrete divergence: a 429/5xx body (or any status outside {400,401,403}) containing the bare word "banned" triggers Python's blacklist-for-bot-ban but not Rust's, so a real ban could go unrecorded and the bot keeps trying to post. Severity lowered to low: the realistic trigger window is narrow because actual Twitch ban responses for the send/mod paths return 4xx with the exact "user is banned"/"sender is banned" phrasing, both of which Rust still catches; Rust also added 401 and "sender is banned" coverage. The claim's technical assertion (the unconditional branch was dropped) is accurate.

**Laufzeit-Check:** Force a mock chat/mod-send response with a non-{400,401,403} status (e.g. 429 or 500) whose body contains only the word "banned" (not "user is banned"/"sender is banned") and observe that the channel is not recorded as bot-banned in Rust while Python's path would blacklist it.

### P3.3 [chat:commands] !raid: spezifischer OAuth-fehlt-Gate (has_enabled_auth) entfällt

- **Python:** `commands.py:640-646`
- **Rust:** `tb-chat/src/commands.rs:915-933`
- **Status:** missing

**Befund:** Streamer ohne aktiviertes OAuth bekommen in Python die klare Meldung 'OAuth fehlt – Twitch-Bot autorisieren'. In Rust läuft der Fall in den manual_raid-Pfad (vermutlich generischer 'unavailable'/Fehlertext), ohne die Autorisierungs-Aufforderung. Der !raid_enable-Verweis ist bewusst entfernt, aber der OAuth-Status-Hinweis selbst geht verloren.

*Python-Soll:* commands.py:640-646 — if not self._raid_bot or not self._raid_bot.auth_manager.has_enabled_auth(twitch_user_id): ctx.send('OAuth fehlt – Anforderung: Twitch-Bot autorisieren mit !raid_enable.'); return

*Rust-Ist:* tb-chat/src/commands.rs:915-933 (cmd_raid) prüft nur is_fully_authed, keinen has_enabled_auth-Gate. ABSENT (searched: grep -rn 'has_enabled_auth' tb-chat tb-transport-twitch tb-crypto → keine Treffer; grep in bin/tb-bot chat_wiring RaidCommandAdapter → kein OAuth-Enable-Check vor manual_raid).

**Verifikation:** Confirmed gap. Python !raid (bot/chat/commands.py:640-646) has TWO distinct gates: has_enabled_auth(twitch_user_id) → bool(row and row[0]) checks twitch_raid_auth.raid_enabled (auth.py:1779-1789), emitting "OAuth fehlt – Twitch-Bot autorisieren mit !raid_enable"; then _is_fully_authed checks needs_reauth (line 649-656). Rust cmd_raid (tb-chat/src/commands.rs:915-933) keeps only is_fully_authed, which queries needs_reauth ALONE (commands.rs:491-502) and never reads raid_enabled. get_partner (commands.rs:441-455) gates on twitch_streamers_partner_state.is_partner_active — a SEPARATE table from twitch_raid_auth.raid_enabled. So an active partner with raid_enabled=0 AND needs_reauth=0 is a reachable state (e.g. departner at streamer_lifecycle.rs:299 sets raid_enabled=FALSE without touching needs_reauth). In that state Python short-circuits with the clear authorize-message; Rust passes both gates, runs the full pipeline, and only fails downstream when RaidExecutor.execute (raid_executor.rs:73-81) calls get_valid_token (raid_enabled-gated, token_provider.rs:40) → None → RaidOutcome::Failed("No valid token for X"), surfacing in chat via the generic `other =>` branch (commands.rs:960) as "Raid fehlgeschlagen: No valid token for X" instead of the OAuth-status hint. The has_enabled_auth gate and its distinct message are genuinely ABSENT (grep across tb-chat/tb-raid/tb-bot confirms no equivalent pre-check). Severity lowered to low: the trigger state is narrow and the user still receives a (generic) failure message rather than silence; the dropped !raid_enable wording itself is an intentional Grillme-Block-8 decision, only the OAuth-status hint is the real loss.

**Laufzeit-Check:** Set an active partner's twitch_raid_auth.raid_enabled=0 with needs_reauth=0, run !raid in that channel, and observe whether the reply is the clear "authorize the bot" hint (Python) or a generic "Raid fehlgeschlagen: No valid token" (Rust).

### P3.4 [chat:engagement_commands] Permission-denied feedback dropped: !engagement_on/off silently ignore unauthorized users instead of replying

- **Python:** `engagement_commands.py:104-108`
- **Rust:** `rust/crates/tb-chat/src/commands.rs:1316-1318`
- **Status:** changed

**Befund:** An unauthorized viewer typing !engagement_on/!engagement_off gets a visible rejection message in Python but total silence in Rust. User-observable behavior change: the command appears to do nothing, no feedback that it was a permissions issue. Not a security hole (the action is still blocked) but a UX/parity regression vs the Python source of truth.

*Python-Soll:* engagement_commands.py:104-108 (cmd_engagement_on) and 130-134 (cmd_engagement_off): `if not await self._engagement_can_toggle(ctx): await ctx.send(f"@{ctx.author.name} Nur Broadcaster, Mods oder Super-Mod dürfen das."); return`

*Rust-Ist:* rust/crates/tb-chat/src/commands.rs:1316-1318 (cmd_engagement_on) and 1357-1359 (cmd_engagement_off): `if !self.is_engagement_admin(event).await { return; }` — bare return, no self.reply call on the denial path

**Verifikation:** Verified in real code. Python cmd_engagement_on (engagement_commands.py:104-108) and cmd_engagement_off (130-134) reply 'Nur Broadcaster, Mods oder Super-Mod dürfen das.' on the denial path before returning. Rust cmd_engagement_on (commands.rs:1316-1318) and cmd_engagement_off (1357-1359) do a bare `return` with no self.reply on the !is_engagement_admin branch — reply only fires on DB Ok/Err. Unauthorized users get silence in Rust vs a visible rejection in Python. Action still blocked, so no security impact; purely a dropped UX feedback string. Lowered severity to low: this is a single cosmetic feedback message, not functional/state divergence.

**Laufzeit-Check:** In a test channel, have a non-mod/non-broadcaster viewer type !engagement_on and observe whether the bot posts the 'Nur Broadcaster, Mods oder Super-Mod dürfen das' rejection (Python) or stays silent (Rust).

### P3.6 [raid:bot] Chat-derived subscription telemetry fallback not implemented (should_capture + on_chat_subscription_notification)

- **Python:** `bot/raid/bot.py:396-453`
- **Rust:** `crates/tb-monitoring/src/dispatch.rs:211`
- **Status:** missing

**Befund:** Sub/resub/gift events arriving only via channel.chat.notification (e.g. on channels where the dedicated channel.subscribe/channel.subscription.* EventSub is missing or failed to subscribe) are NOT persisted in Rust. Python recovered these as a fallback; Rust silently drops them, undercounting subscription telemetry for affected channels. The dedup gate (skip when dedicated sub active) is also absent, but since the store path is a no-op there is no double-count today.

*Python-Soll:* bot/raid/bot.py:396-453 — should_capture_chat_subscription_notice() checks cog._eventsub_has_sub(eventsub_type, broadcaster_id) and only returns True (=capture) when NO dedicated EventSub sub exists; on_chat_subscription_notification() then calls cog._store_subscription_event(broadcaster_id, event, event_type) to persist the sub/resub/gift as a fallback telemetry record.

*Rust-Ist:* crates/tb-monitoring/src/dispatch.rs:211 on_chat_subscription_notification is a trait DEFAULT no-op (doc: 'Default no-op bis B8-01 die Telemetrie-Persistenz verdrahtet'); bin/tb-bot/src/chat_wiring.rs:952-961 only forwards to inner: Arc<dyn EventSubHooks> which is the no-op default. No should_capture/has_sub dedup gate exists (searched: grep -rn 'should_capture|eventsub_has_sub|has_sub' across rust -> 0 impls). route_chat_notification (dispatch.rs:490) calls the hook unconditionally for sub kinds.

**Verifikation:** Confirmed in current code. Python (bot/raid/bot.py:396-453) implements a chat-derived subscription telemetry fallback: should_capture_chat_subscription_notice() gates on cog._eventsub_has_sub(eventsub_type, broadcaster_id) (capture only when no dedicated EventSub exists), and on_chat_subscription_notification() persists via _store_subscription_event. In Rust, on_chat_subscription_notification is a trait DEFAULT no-op (crates/tb-monitoring/src/dispatch.rs:211, doc 'Default no-op bis B8-01 die Telemetrie-Persistenz verdrahtet'). route_chat_notification (dispatch.rs:490-494) forwards sub-kind notice_types to this hook unconditionally. The only override is bin/tb-bot/src/chat_wiring.rs:952-961 (ChatHooks), which merely forwards to inner: Arc<dyn EventSubHooks>; the inline comment at lines 939-941 explicitly states 'Sub-Telemetrie (B8-01) bleibt vorerst der Default-No-op des inneren Hooks.' A grep of all non-test impls of fn on_chat_subscription_notification returns only the trait default + the forwarding ChatHooks, so the chain terminates at the no-op. No should_capture/eventsub_has_sub/has_sub dedup gate exists anywhere in the Rust tree. So chat.notification sub/resub/gift events are not persisted in Rust. Caveat: the auditor's broader implication that ALL subscription telemetry is absent is wrong — the DEDICATED EventSub path (channel.subscribe/.gift/.message) IS fully wired via store_telemetry (dispatch.rs:501-528) -> TelemetryStore::store_subscription_event (crates/tb-monitoring/src/telemetry.rs:55). The gap is strictly the chat-derived FALLBACK for channels whose dedicated EventSub subs are missing/failed. Severity lowered to low: no crash, no double-count (store path is no-op), no user-facing breakage — only telemetry undercount on edge-case channels with broken EventSub provisioning.

**Laufzeit-Check:** On a channel where the dedicated channel.subscribe/subscription.* EventSub subscription is absent, trigger a sub/resub/gift and confirm no new row lands in twitch_subscription_events while the chat.notification was received.

### P3.7 [raid:commands] Discord admin bulk re-auth command 'reauth_all' (+ snapshot_and_flag_reauth) not ported to Rust

- **Python:** `/home/naniadm/Documents/Deadlock-Twitch-Bot/bot/raid/commands.py:459-572`

The bulk snapshot_and_flag_reauth operation has no Rust equivalent. This is an admin-operational command, not user-facing runtime behavior; the Discord cog remains Python so this may be intentional architecture split. If Python cog is retired, this capability is lost.

*Python-Soll:* /home/naniadm/Documents/Deadlock-Twitch-Bot/bot/raid/commands.py:459-572 — cmd_reauth_all: calls auth_manager.snapshot_and_flag_reauth() (line 471) to snapshot tokens + set needs_reauth=1 for ALL streamers, fetches WHERE needs_reauth IS TRUE (line 480), DMs each flagged streamer a re-auth embed+button, writes reauth_notified_at=CURRENT_TIMESTAMP (line 534).

*Rust-Ist:* ABSENT (searched: grep -rin 'reauth_all|snapshot_and_flag' crates/ → 0 hits; grep -rln 'serenity|poise' --include=Cargo.toml → 0 hits; internal-api needs_reauth refs in diagnose.rs/streamer_lifecycle.rs are read-only diagnostics, no bulk-flag trigger). auth_writer.rs:171 only RESETS needs_reauth=FALSE on individual re-auth; no bulk snapshot-and-flag-all primitive exists.

Python bot/raid/auth.py:1168-1189 snapshot_and_flag_reauth runs one bulk UPDATE twitch_raid_auth SET needs_reauth=TRUE, reauth_notified_at=NULL across ALL rows holding token/authorization material; bot/raid/commands.py:459-572 cmd_reauth_all calls it (line 471), then DMs each flagged streamer and sets reauth_notified_at. No Rust equivalent exists: grep for reauth_all/snapshot_and_flag finds only Python code + audit docs (the repo's own findings-2026-06-15.json and coverage audit already flag exactly this gap). All Rust needs_reauth=TRUE writes are single-row and error-driven (token_blacklist.rs:202, token_lifecycle.rs:631) — none is a bulk snapshot-and-flag-all primitive. auth_writer.rs:171 only RESETS needs_reauth=FALSE on individual re-auth. internal-api needs_reauth references (diagnose.rs, streamer_lifecycle.rs) are read-only diagnostics / lifecycle reactivation, not a bulk-flag trigger. No serenity/poise dependency exists, so the Discord DM path has no native home. reauth_reminder.rs only reads the flag passively. Severity downgraded medium→low: this is an admin-only operational command (force mass re-auth after a scope change), not user-facing runtime behavior; the Discord cog remains Python and the repo's grillme decision was to rebuild it into the admin dashboard later — a planned migration, not a live hot-path regression. Capability is only lost if/when the Python cog is retired.

**Laufzeit-Check:** In a staging Twitch-Bot DB, count rows with needs_reauth=TRUE before/after; run the Rust admin/internal-api surface and confirm no endpoint flips all token-bearing rows to needs_reauth=TRUE in one shot (only Python /reauth_all does).

### P3.8 [raid:raid_pipeline] Observability metrics counter facility (incl. raid_flow_started_total) entirely missing in Rust port

- **Python:** `bot/raid/raid_pipeline.py:485-492`
- **Rust:** `ABSENT`
- **Status:** missing

**Befund:** No raid-flow metrics are produced by the Rust pipeline. Dashboards/alerts relying on counters such as raid_flow_started_total see zero data after cutover. This is operational/telemetry loss only — it does not change whether raids fire or who is raided. Likely intentional simplification (Rust standardised on tracing logs), but it is a genuine behavioral drop vs Python, so flagged.

*Python-Soll:* bot/raid/raid_pipeline.py:485-492 (_increment_counter via injectable self._deps.increment_raid_observability_counter); invoked at line 283 `self._increment_counter("raid_flow_started_total")`. Declared default-None hook at lines 78.

*Rust-Ist:* ABSENT (searched: `grep -rn 'increment_raid_observability_counter|increment_counter|raid_flow_started_total|counter|metric' crates/tb-raid/src` -> no hits; `grep -n 'pub fn|counter|increment' crates/tb-observability/src/lib.rs` -> only `init_tracing()` at lib.rs:11). auto_raid_pipeline.rs does not import tb-observability and emits no metric.)

**Verifikation:** Independently confirmed the gap. Python: `increment_raid_observability_counter` is a real, default-None injectable hook (raid_pipeline.py:78,485-492) backed by an actual counter_store dict (bot/raid/facades/runtime_core.py:254 with `_raid_observability_counter_store`), invoked for `raid_flow_started_total` at raid_pipeline.py:283 and across ~12 sites in raid_tracking_runtime.py. Rust: NO equivalent. tb-observability/src/lib.rs contains only `init_tracing()`; its own doc comment states the observability-event/counter writer "kommt mit der Monitoring-Phase" (deferred). grep across rust/crates + rust/bin for `observability_counter|raid_flow_started|flow_started_total|raid_observability` returns zero functional hits, and NO crate depends on tb-observability (only its own Cargo.toml). auto_raid_pipeline.rs imports no metrics facility and emits no counters. NOTE: a different counter (DB strike-tracking `increment_raid_disabled_strikes`) IS ported (strikes_store.rs), but that is not the observability counter the claim targets. Partial mitigation: auto_raid_pipeline.rs emits `tracing::info!` at the equivalent flow points (incl. a flow_id at line 237-238), so flow-started events can be derived from logs — but the named counter facility / `raid_flow_started_total` metric genuinely does not exist. Severity corrected to low: behavior of raids (firing/targeting) is unchanged; this is operational telemetry loss only, with structured logs providing a partial substitute signal.

**Laufzeit-Check:** After a Rust raid fires, query the counters/metrics surface (or the twitch_observability_events table) for `raid_flow_started_total` — it will be absent/zero, while Python populated it.

### P3.9 [raid:runtime_support] User-scope legacy-token fallback WARNING (warn/clear_user_scope_fallback_once) dropped — operator signal lost

- **Python:** `bot/raid/runtime_support.py:97-124`
- **Rust:** `ABSENT`
- **Status:** missing

**Befund:** Rust never emits the diagnostic that the bot is falling back to the legacy broadcaster token instead of the bot token for a given area/subject. This is exactly the bot-token-migration signal the operator relies on (cf. MEMORY: Bot-Token-Write-Back); its absence hides degraded-auth conditions.

*Python-Soll:* bot/raid/runtime_support.py:97-124 — warn_user_scope_fallback_once logs a once-only WARNING 'RaidBot: nutze Legacy-Broadcaster-Token fuer <area> (<subject>). Der Bot-Token sollte diesen Pfad uebernehmen.' deduped via bot._user_scope_fallback_warned set; clear_user_scope_fallback_warning re-arms it after recovery. Wired via runtime_factories.py:348-349.

*Rust-Ist:* ABSENT (searched: grep -rn 'user_scope_fallback|Legacy-Broadcaster|Bot-Token sollte|fallback_warned' crates/ -> 0 hits across whole rust tree).

**Verifikation:** Independently confirmed. Python emits a once-only WARNING (runtime_support.py:97-124, re-armed via clear_user_scope_fallback_warning) wired into the follower-total lookup paths: recruitment_messaging.py:402 (recruitment follower lookup) and followers.py:153 (raid candidate follower lookup). It fires exactly when the bot token cannot resolve /channels/followers (missing moderator:read:followers or bot path fails) and the code falls back to the streamer/user token. On the Rust side this is genuinely absent: grep across rust/crates and rust/bin finds zero equivalent (no 'user_scope_fallback', 'Legacy-Broadcaster', 'Bot-Token sollte', no counterpart under a renamed module). The reason is structural — Rust resolves follower totals only via the App-token path (wiring.rs:179 HelixFollowerSource -> get_followers_total, App token, returns None when scope missing; raid_adapters.rs:62-67 HelixFollowerEnricher 'Best-effort über den App-Token') and recruitment passes followers_total=None outright (raid_arrival_wiring.rs:529, recruitment_messaging.rs). Rust has no bot-token->streamer-token fallback branch, so the diagnostic that signals it never exists. The audit's own findings file already catalogs this (findings-2026-06-15.json:2910), explicitly listing 'die _warn_user_scope_fallback_once/_clear_-Warnungen' among the lost recruitment-follower observability. So the claim is accurate: the operator diagnostic is missing. Severity corrected from medium to low: it is a single once-only WARN log line, pure operator telemetry with no user-visible text, message, or persisted-state effect (the audit confirms invite_variant/followers_total=None changes no output or stored state). It is one diagnostic in a broader, already-known observability gap, not a functional regression.

**Laufzeit-Check:** In a Rust tb-bot live session, trigger a recruitment send / raid candidate selection for a target whose follower total the bot token cannot read (no moderator:read:followers) and confirm no 'Legacy-Broadcaster-Token'-style WARNING ever appears in the bot logs.

### P3.10 [raid:runtime_support] Structured followers-total legacy fallback (get_followers_total_result_with_legacy_fallback) not ported

- **Python:** `bot/raid/runtime_support.py:127-143`
- **Rust:** `ABSENT`

**Befund:** The Rust followers resolution loses the structured success/failure surface (ok/http_status/error_code/request_attempted) that the analytics decision (above) records. Combined with the missing log_analytics_followers_decision, the entire followers-resolution diagnostic chain is gone, and there is no raid-side caller of get_followers_total at all yet.

*Python-Soll:* bot/raid/runtime_support.py:127-143 — prefers api.get_followers_total_result, else wraps legacy api.get_followers_total into a structured dict {ok, data, http_status:200|None, error_code:'legacy_none_result'|None, request_attempted:True}. Wired via runtime_factories.py:338 and 648.

*Rust-Ist:* ABSENT (searched: grep -rn 'get_followers_total_result|legacy_none_result|legacy_fallback' crates/ -> 0). Rust tb-transport-twitch/src/streams.rs:250 get_followers_total returns Option<i64> directly (None on non-200), with no result-dict, no http_status synthesis, no 'legacy_none_result' code, and no result-vs-legacy compat branch. The raid crate never calls it outside a test (streams.rs:556).

Python's get_followers_total_result_with_legacy_fallback (runtime_support.py:127-143) is a compat shim that prefers api.get_followers_total_result — which returns a structured dict {ok, data, http_status, error_code, request_attempted} (twitch_api.py:728-829) — and only wraps legacy get_followers_total when the structured getter is absent (mock/old API). That structured surface feeds the analytics decision logging in sessions_mixin.py. In Rust, get_followers_total (tb-transport-twitch/src/streams.rs:250-273) returns Result<Option<i64>, HelixError>, flattening every non-200 to Ok(None) with no http_status/error_code/request_attempted and no legacy_none_result code. Its sole caller HelixFollowerSource::follower_total (tb-bot/src/wiring.rs:179-186) further collapses to Option<i32> (None on error/non-200). grep across rust/ for get_followers_total_result/legacy_none_result/request_attempted in followers context returns nothing — the structured success/failure surface genuinely does not exist. Two corrections to the auditor: (1) there IS a non-test caller of get_followers_total (wiring.rs:179), so 'no raid-side caller at all' is wrong; (2) the Python helper is backward-compat plumbing around an already-structured method, not the primary logic. The real loss is diagnostic/observability only — Rust still resolves the follower count correctly — so severity is low rather than medium.

**Laufzeit-Check:** Trigger a follower-total fetch with an App-token (no moderator scope) and inspect Rust logs: only a debug line appears, no structured ok/http_status/error_code/request_attempted fields are recorded anywhere.

### P3.11 [monitoring:eventsub_webhook] extract_context drops Python broadcaster_id/login fallbacks (user_id, user_login, condition.*)

- **Python:** `eventsub_webhook.py:133-150`
- **Rust:** `dispatch.rs:262-272`
- **Status:** changed

**Befund:** For event payloads that only carry user_id/user_login (some Twitch event shapes) or where the id lives in subscription.condition, Rust derives an empty broadcaster_id/login. Telemetry inserts keyed on user_id (store_follow_event, store_first_message_event, etc. dispatch.rs:569-578 use context.broadcaster_id) could be written with an empty key or mis-attributed. Affects follow / first-message / condition-only events. Verify the actual Twitch payload shapes for the registered sub types to confirm which ones lack broadcaster_user_id.

*Python-Soll:* eventsub_webhook.py:133-150 broadcaster_id falls back through event.broadcaster_user_id → to_broadcaster_user_id → event.user_id → condition.broadcaster_user_id → condition.to_broadcaster_user_id; broadcaster_login falls back broadcaster_user_login → to_broadcaster_user_login → user_login.

*Rust-Ist:* dispatch.rs:262-272 extract_context reads only ['broadcaster_user_id','to_broadcaster_user_id'] for id and ['broadcaster_user_login','to_broadcaster_user_login'] for login — no event.user_id, no event.user_login, no condition.* fallback.

**Verifikation:** Verified both sides in current code. Python bot/monitoring/eventsub_webhook.py:133-150 derives broadcaster_id via event.broadcaster_user_id -> event.to_broadcaster_user_id -> event.user_id -> condition.broadcaster_user_id -> condition.to_broadcaster_user_id, and broadcaster_login via broadcaster_user_login -> to_broadcaster_user_login -> user_login. Rust rust/crates/tb-monitoring/src/dispatch.rs:262-272 extract_context only reads event["broadcaster_user_id"|"to_broadcaster_user_id"] for the id and event["broadcaster_user_login"|"to_broadcaster_user_login"] for the login. It never consults event.user_id, event.user_login, or subscription.condition.*. So three Python fallbacks are genuinely absent in Rust — the divergence is real, not refuted. store_telemetry (dispatch.rs:501-578) keys every insert on context.broadcaster_id, so an empty id would mis-key/empty-key the insert. Severity downgraded to low because for the actually registered handler sub types (channel.follow, channel.subscribe, channel.cheer, channel.chat.user_first_message, channel.ban, etc.) Twitch's standard event payload always includes broadcaster_user_id in the event object, so the primary lookup succeeds and the missing fallbacks (defensive paths for verification challenges / non-standard payload shapes) do not normally fire. Practical exposure is narrow but the code gap is confirmed present.

**Laufzeit-Check:** Feed the native EventSub receiver a notification whose event omits broadcaster_user_id but carries only user_id (or whose id sits in subscription.condition) and confirm the resulting telemetry row is written with an empty/blank broadcaster key.

### P3.12 [monitoring:eventsub_mixin] Unknown EventSub work_type silently dropped instead of dead-lettered

- **Python:** `bot/monitoring/eventsub_mixin.py:159`
- **Rust:** `crates/tb-monitoring/src/handlers.rs:404-410`
- **Status:** changed

**Befund:** If a producer ever enqueues a work-type the handler doesn't know (e.g. the channel.raid or stream.online.followups types that DO exist in the Python schema, or a future/renamed type), Python surfaces it loudly (dead-letter + critical log) while Rust swallows it as a single warn and drops the work permanently. Loss is silent and unrecoverable (record acked). Low frequency but a real fidelity regression in the error-handling contract.

*Python-Soll:* bot/monitoring/eventsub_mixin.py:159 — `raise RuntimeError(f"unknown eventsub processing work_type: {normalized_work_type}")` at the end of _process_eventsub_processing_record. An unknown/typo'd work-type raises, which the inbox treats as a processing failure → retry then dead-letter (visible via _internal_eventsub_processing_debug, alertable via _handle_eventsub_processing_dead_letter log.critical).

*Rust-Ist:* crates/tb-monitoring/src/handlers.rs:404-410 — `other => { tracing::warn!(work_type = other, "Inbox: unbekannter Work-Type — verworfen"); Ok(()) }`. Returning Ok(()) marks the inbox record as successfully processed, so it is removed/acked and never retried or dead-lettered.

**Verifikation:** Verified in real code. Python (bot/monitoring/eventsub_mixin.py:159) ends _process_eventsub_processing_record with `raise RuntimeError(f"unknown eventsub processing work_type: ...")`, which the inbox treats as a processing failure → retry → dead-letter + critical-log. Rust (crates/tb-monitoring/src/handlers.rs:404-410) has the catch-all `other => { tracing::warn!(...); Ok(()) }`. I confirmed via inbox_runtime.rs:162-183 that `Ok(())` → `mark_delivered` (acked/removed, no retry), while `Err` → `handle_failure` → retry with backoff and dead-letter+hook+error-log after INBOX_MAX_ATTEMPTS (5). So an unknown work-type IS silently acked and permanently dropped in Rust vs loudly dead-lettered in Python — the error-handling contract divergence is real and exactly as described.

However the auditor's impact framing is partly wrong: in Rust only CORE_DELIVERY_TYPES = ["stream.online","stream.offline","channel.update"] are ever enqueued into the processing inbox (dispatch.rs:415-426), and the handler matches exactly those 3. channel.raid is NOT inbox-routed — it goes to a direct hook (dispatch.rs:430-431), and there is no stream.online.followups enqueue path. So the catch-all arm is unreachable under current normal traffic; it can only fire if a future developer adds a new type to CORE_DELIVERY_TYPES/the enqueuer but forgets the matching handler arm. That makes it a latent dev-time fidelity gap, not a live data-loss path — hence low rather than medium.

**Laufzeit-Check:** Enqueue a record into twitch_eventsub_processing_inbox with an unrecognized work_type and observe it gets marked delivered (no dead-letter row, single warn log) instead of landing in the _dead_letter table with a critical/error log.

### P3.13 [monitoring:eventsub_mixin] Blacklist-Raid streamer whisper not ported — streamer no longer told why their raid was cancelled

- **Python:** `eventsub_mixin.py:2829-2834`
- **Rust:** `bin/tb-bot/src/eventsub_hooks.rs:380-393`
- **Status:** missing

**Befund:** When a streamer manually raids a blacklisted channel, Python cancels AND whispers them an explanation (cancelled vs. too-late wording). Rust cancels but sends nothing user-facing — the streamer gets no feedback about why their raid vanished. Documented as an intentional deferral pending the chat-token cutover (single-refresher constraint), so it is a known gap rather than an accidental regression, but it is still a missing user-visible behavior versus the Python baseline.

*Python-Soll:* eventsub_mixin.py:2829-2834 calls _send_blacklist_raid_whisper; 2836-2917 builds two distinct messages (cancelled vs. ran-through), gates on user:manage:whispers scope (2856), skips self bot_id==streamer_id (2862), POSTs helix/whispers (2887-2912).

*Rust-Ist:* bin/tb-bot/src/eventsub_hooks.rs:380-393 cancels + only tracing::warn! logs; doc 323-326 explicitly defers the whisper to the Chat-Cutover. ABSENT (searched: grep -rn 'whisper|manage:whispers|helix/whispers|Heads-up|Raid-Blacklist' --include=*.rs — no Twitch whisper sender exists).

**Verifikation:** Confirmed in real code. Python bot/monitoring/eventsub_mixin.py:2829-2917 (claim cited cogs/ but actual path is bot/monitoring/) calls _send_blacklist_raid_whisper, which builds two distinct messages (cancelled vs. ran-through "Heads-up" wording at 2865-2875), gates on user:manage:whispers scope (2856), skips self when bot_id==streamer_id (2862), and POSTs helix/whispers (2887-2912). Rust BlacklistRaidGuard.handle in bin/tb-bot/src/eventsub_hooks.rs:346-394 cancels the raid but only emits tracing::warn! logs; the doc at lines 323-326 explicitly defers the streamer whisper to the Chat-Cutover (single-refresher token constraint). Grepped harder than the auditor across tb-transport-twitch, tb-helix, bin/: no helix/whispers, no user:manage:whispers, no whisper sender exists. The helix client (tb-transport-twitch/src/chat.rs) has only send_chat_message and send_announcement. The only 'whisper' hits are OpenAI audio-transcription (STT in tb-engagement), unrelated to Twitch whispers. So the user-facing feedback is genuinely absent. Severity lowered to low: this fires only on the rare manual-raid-onto-blacklisted-channel path, is explicitly best-effort, and Twitch silently drops whispers to non-followers (Python returns 204 without delivery), so the Python baseline was already unreliable — and it is a documented deliberate deferral, not an accidental regression.

**Laufzeit-Check:** As a test streamer manually raid a blacklisted channel; Python whispers a "Heads-up" explanation, Rust only writes a warn log and the streamer receives nothing.
