### P3.15 [analytics:api_public] Network endpoint: Rust does not lowercase/strip login nor skip empty logins (Python normalizes + filters)

- **Python:** `api_public.py:219-221`
- **Rust:** `crates/tb-dashboard-api/src/handlers/network.rs:20-29`
- **Status:** changed

**Befund:** If twitch_streamers_partner_state yields a mixed-case or blank twitch_login, the Rust /public/network output preserves case and emits a row with login="" instead of dropping it. Python guaranteed lowercase, non-empty logins to the website. Visual/dedupe drift on the public partner network graph; low risk only because the partner view normally holds clean lowercased logins.

*Python-Soll:* api_public.py:219-221 — login = str(row[0] or '').strip().lower(); if not login: continue

*Rust-Ist:* crates/tb-dashboard-api/src/handlers/network.rs:20-29 From<NetworkStreamerRow> sets login: r.twitch_login (no .strip()/.lower(), no empty-row skip)

**Verifikation:** Confirmed in real code (paths under rust/). Python bot/analytics/api_public.py:219-221 does login = str(row[0] or "").strip().lower() then skips empty logins. Rust handler crates/tb-dashboard-api/src/handlers/network.rs:20-29 From<NetworkStreamerRow> sets login: r.twitch_login with no trim/lowercase and no empty-skip. The query crates/tb-analytics/src/network.rs:24 projects SELECT sp.twitch_login verbatim; the LOWER() calls are only in the JOIN condition and ORDER BY, not the selected column. The view chain (twitch_streamers_partner_state -> twitch_partners_all_state -> twitch_partners.twitch_login) passes login through unchanged, and twitch_login has no lowercase CHECK constraint — the unique index idx_twitch_partners_active_login_lower is on LOWER(twitch_login), which permits mixed-case storage. twitch_login is NOT NULL but an empty string '' is not excluded. So a mixed-case or blank stored login would be emitted raw (or as login="") by Rust, whereas Python normalizes and drops it. Notably other Rust read handlers (raid_analytics.rs, chat_social_graph.rs) DO apply defensive .to_lowercase()/unwrap_or_default on output, so the pattern exists but was not applied to the network handler. Severity lowered to low: Rust write paths into twitch_partners normalize via .to_lowercase() (streamer_lifecycle.rs) and the shared DB history was Python-normalized, so non-normalized/blank rows are a near-impossible production state — exactly the auditor's stated low-risk caveat. The behavioral gap itself is genuine and present.

**Laufzeit-Check:** Insert a partner row with a mixed-case or empty twitch_login directly into twitch_partners, then GET /twitch/api/v2/public/network and compare the login field against the Python endpoint's output.

### P3.16 [analytics:demo_data] Demo multi-profile system collapsed to a single hardcoded profile

- **Python:** `demo_data.py:2905-2909`
- **Rust:** `crates/tb-dashboard-api/src/handlers/demo.rs:33`
- **Status:** missing

**Befund:** Python let the demo switch between three streamer personas with distinct scaled metrics; Rust exposes only midcore_live and a 2-entry streamer list that is internally inconsistent (lists smallquest_tv but allowedDemoProfiles excludes it, and drops megaarena_gg). Lower impact because the runtime advertises only one allowed profile, so the SPA likely never requests others — but the streamers endpoint still leaks an unsupported login.

*Python-Soll:* demo_data.py:2905-2909 (_resolve_demo_profile honors ?streamer), 2938-3185 per-profile scaling (viewer_factor/retention_shift/monetization_factor/...), 3425-3428 three profiles smallquest_tv/midcore_live/megaarena_gg; build_demo_streamers (2912-2913) returns all ALLOWED_DEMO_PROFILES.

*Rust-Ist:* crates/tb-dashboard-api/src/handlers/demo.rs:33 DEMO_LOGIN="midcore_live"; demo.rs:38 allowedDemoProfiles:["midcore_live"]; demo.rs:130-136 demo_streamers lists exactly [smallquest_tv, midcore_live] (megaarena_gg absent).

**Verifikation:** Independently confirmed in the real code (rust/crates/tb-dashboard-api/src/handlers/demo.rs). Python (bot/analytics/demo_data.py:2755-2913, 3425-3427) defines three profiles smallquest_tv/midcore_live/megaarena_gg, resolves ?streamer via _resolve_demo_profile (2904-2909), applies per-profile scaling (_profile_rank_scale 2938-2967), and build_demo_streamers (2912-2913) returns ALL allowed profiles. Rust collapses this: DEMO_LOGIN is hardcoded "midcore_live" (line 33); allowedDemoProfiles is ["midcore_live"] only (line 38); demo_streamers returns [smallquest_tv, midcore_live] but megaarena_gg is dropped (lines 130-136); there is no ?streamer query handling anywhere — every payload (auth-status/overview/ai) is fixed to midcore_live. The internal inconsistency is real: streamers lists smallquest_tv though allowedDemoProfiles excludes it and no code path serves per-streamer data. This is an explicit documented "lean" design choice (module doc lines 8-12, Grillme Block 6 — don't port the 3589-LOC demo verbatim), but the claimed behavioral regression genuinely exists. Severity lowered to low: it is an auth-free marketing demo with no real/DB data; the runtime advertises only one profile so the SPA switcher is effectively single-entry, and the only observable defect is the cosmetic smallquest_tv leak in the streamers list.

**Laufzeit-Check:** Request /twitch/demo/api/v2/streamers and compare its login list against allowedDemoProfiles in the demo page HTML; note smallquest_tv appears in streamers but not in allowedDemoProfiles, and megaarena_gg is absent from both.

### P3.17 [analytics:api_admin] Raw-chat lag warning threshold lowered from 900s to 120s

- **Python:** `api_admin.py:1446-1461`
- **Rust:** `rust/crates/tb-dashboard-api/src/handlers/system/health.rs:71-81`
- **Status:** changed

**Befund:** Rust raises the 'raw_chat_lag' service warning at 2 minutes of lag whereas Python only warns at 15 minutes. Admins will see far more (earlier) lag warnings on the system/health panel than the legacy behavior, and the threshold semantics changed from >= to > (boundary value 900 itself now compared differently). Different alerting behavior for the same underlying data.

*Python-Soll:* api_admin.py:1446-1461: warning emitted only when raw_chat_lag_seconds >= _RAW_CHAT_LAG_WARNING_SECONDS (=900, defined at api_admin.py:71) for a live-scope channel

*Rust-Ist:* rust/crates/tb-dashboard-api/src/handlers/system/health.rs:71-81: warning pushed when h.is_live_scope && lag > 120

**Verifikation:** Confirmed in current code. Python bot/analytics/api_admin.py:71 defines _RAW_CHAT_LAG_WARNING_SECONDS = 900 and lines 1446-1447 emit the raw_chat_lag_high warning only when is_live_scope and raw_chat_lag_seconds >= 900. Rust rust/crates/tb-dashboard-api/src/handlers/system/health.rs:71-80 pushes the RAW_CHAT_LAG warning when h.is_live_scope and lag > 120. So Rust warns at 2 minutes of lag vs Python's 15 minutes (7.5x lower threshold), and the operator changed from >= to > (boundary value handled differently). The divergence is real and independently verified. Severity downgraded to low: impact is confined to an admin-only system/health panel showing earlier/more frequent informational warnings — no functional, data, or security consequence. The surrounding Rust block is even annotated '// Bug D', suggesting a known fixture, but the 120s literal is a concrete deviation from legacy 900s.

**Laufzeit-Check:** On the admin system/health panel, induce ~2-15 min of raw-chat ingestion lag on a live-scope channel and observe that the Rust endpoint surfaces the RAW_CHAT_LAG warning far earlier than the legacy Python panel did.

### P3.18 [analytics:api_v2] X-Admin-Token header authentication path dropped in Rust auth-level cascade

- **Python:** `api_v2.py:1353-1362`
- **Rust:** `ABSENT`
- **Status:** missing

**Befund:** Any caller that authenticated to v2 dashboard APIs via the X-Admin-Token header (e.g. internal/admin tooling, scripts) is treated as auth_level='none' in Rust and rejected (401). Cookie/Discord-admin and localhost still work, so impact is limited to header-token consumers; if none exist in prod this is inert, but it is a genuine behavioral drop versus Python.

*Python-Soll:* api_v2.py:1353-1362 (_get_auth_level): admin_token=getattr(self,'_token',None); admin_header=request.headers.get('X-Admin-Token'); if admin_token and admin_header and secrets.compare_digest(...) → return 'admin'

*Rust-Ist:* ABSENT (searched: grep -rn 'X-Admin-Token|x-admin-token|compare_digest' crates/ → tb-dashboard-api/src/auth/level.rs:214-277 has only localhost + master_dash_session cookie + twitch_dash_session + partner-access cookie; auth_status.rs:450-453 explicit comment 'Token-basierter Admin-Zugang (X-Admin-Token) ist NICHT in DashboardAuthLevel implementiert ... Python prüft zusätzlich X-Admin-Token')

**Verifikation:** Independently confirmed in current code. Python bot/analytics/api_v2.py:1353-1362 checks request header X-Admin-Token against self._token via secrets.compare_digest and returns 'admin'. The Rust DashboardAuthLevel extractor in rust/crates/tb-dashboard-api/src/auth/level.rs:214-277 implements only four branches: localhost (is_local_request), master_dash_session cookie (admin), twitch_dash_session cookie (partner/admin-promotion), and PARTNER_ACCESS_COOKIE (twitch_dash_session_partner). There is NO X-Admin-Token header branch, so a caller authenticating via that header on the cookie-gated v2 dashboard routes (auth-status, /analyse) falls through to DashboardAuthLevel::None and is rejected. The omission is explicit and acknowledged in rust/crates/tb-dashboard-api/src/handlers/auth_status.rs:450-453 ('Python prüft zusätzlich X-Admin-Token, aber dieses Feature ist für auth-status nicht gebraucht'). This is a genuine behavioral drop versus Python. Severity downgraded to low: it only affects the DashboardAuthLevel cookie-gated routes (browser-oriented); the separate token-based AuthLevel/ExpectedToken path still covers the token-authenticated analytics routes, and there is no known internal consumer hitting these specific routes via X-Admin-Token — matching the project's own audit which rated it NIEDRIG. Minor inaccuracy in the claim: the cited comment's first line now reads 'X-Internal-Token' though line 452 still references 'X-Admin-Token'; substance of the gap is unchanged.

**Laufzeit-Check:** Send a request to a v2 dashboard route (e.g. /twitch/api/v2/auth-status) from a non-loopback host with only the X-Admin-Token header set to the configured token and no session cookie; Rust returns 401/none while Python returns admin.

### P3.19 [analytics:api_v2] /twitch/api/v2/billing/catalog returns a completely different response shape in Rust (wrong endpoint ported)

- **Python:** `api_v2.py:2519-2560`
- **Rust:** `rust/crates/tb-dashboard-api/src/lib.rs:800-803`
- **Status:** changed

**Befund:** Any client that hit the Python v2 endpoint expecting the simple {plans:[{price_monthly,features,entitlements,is_current}]} shape gets a structurally different JSON. The dashboard_v2 bundle calls '/billing/catalog' with a cycle param and reads .plans (matches the richer handler), so the live SPA is likely unaffected; but the v2 endpoint contract itself diverged and the simple handler was effectively dropped. is_current bundle-aliasing for analysis_dashboard is lost. Verify no other consumer (mobile/legacy) depends on the old v2 shape.

*Python-Soll:* api_v2.py:2519-2560 _api_v2_billing_catalog wired at api_overview.py:128 to '/twitch/api/v2/billing/catalog'; returns {plans:[{id,name,tier,price_monthly,features,entitlements,is_current}]} for a fixed 4-plan list (raid_free, raid_boost, analysis_dashboard, bundle_analysis_raid_boost), no 'cycle' param, with is_current bundle-aliasing (analysis_dashboard true when current==bundle_analysis_raid_boost).

*Rust-Ist:* rust/crates/tb-dashboard-api/src/lib.rs:800-803 routes '/twitch/api/v2/billing/catalog' to billing_page::catalog_handler (billing_page.rs:352-446), which is the port of routes_billing.py:api_billing_catalog — a richer endpoint taking ?cycle and returning plans + current_subscription + billing_profile + payment + stripe_price_id/checkout_available, WITHOUT Python's per-plan price_monthly/features/entitlements/is_current(+bundle alias) shape.

**Verifikation:** Verified in current code. Python `_api_v2_billing_catalog` (bot/analytics/api_v2.py:2519-2560, wired at api_overview.py:128) returns `{plans:[{id,name,tier,price_monthly,features,entitlements,is_current}]}` for a fixed 4-plan list, with is_current bundle-aliasing (analysis_dashboard true when current==bundle_analysis_raid_boost). Rust routes BOTH `/twitch/api/billing/catalog` and `/twitch/api/v2/billing/catalog` to the same `billing_page::catalog_handler` (lib.rs:796-803), whose docstring (billing_page.rs:350) self-identifies as the port of `routes_billing.py:api_billing_catalog`. That handler takes `?cycle`, emits all 8 BILLING_PLANS (catalog.rs:417-461) with per-plan `monthly_net_cents`+`price{}` (NO float `price_monthly`), `entitlements`+`features` present, and `is_current` computed as strict `pid == current_plan_id` (billing_page.rs:389) — NO bundle-alias — plus extra `current_subscription`/`billing_profile`/`payment`/`stripe_price_id`/`checkout_available`. So the v2 endpoint contract genuinely diverged and the simple v2 handler was effectively dropped; bundle-aliasing for analysis_dashboard is lost. However the only LIVE consumer of the v2 base path (bot/dashboard_v2/src/api/billing.ts, API_BASE=/twitch/api/v2) is purpose-built for the RICH shape — it passes `cycle` and derives price_monthly from `price.effective_monthly_net_cents ?? monthly_net_cents`. The only code expecting the simple `{plans:[{price_monthly}]}` shape (bot/dashboard_preview/src/api/billing.ts) is not wired/served anywhere on the server. Hence real divergence in the verifiable code, but no live consumer depends on the dropped shape → severity reduced to low.

**Laufzeit-Check:** Hit GET /twitch/api/v2/billing/catalog on the running Rust dashboard with a partner session and confirm the JSON has top-level current_subscription/billing_profile/payment and per-plan monthly_net_cents/price{} (rich shape) instead of the flat price_monthly/is_current-with-bundle-alias shape.

### P3.20 [analytics:admin_streamer_queries] Detail settings object drops manualPartnerOptOut

- **Python:** `admin_streamer_queries.py:498`
- **Rust:** `rust/crates/tb-dashboard-api/src/handlers/admin_streamers.rs:138-162`
- **Status:** missing

**Befund:** Frontend StreamerInfo type (bot/admin_dashboard/src/api/types.ts:41 manualPartnerOptOut?) reads it from settings; in detail view the opt-out flag is always undefined. Top-level partnerStatus still reflects it, so display partly compensates, but any settings-level toggle/indicator bound to settings.manualPartnerOptOut is dead.

*Python-Soll:* admin_streamer_queries.py:498 settings.manualPartnerOptOut = bool(manual_partner_opt_out)

*Rust-Ist:* rust/crates/tb-dashboard-api/src/handlers/admin_streamers.rs:138-162 StreamerSettings struct has no manual_partner_opt_out field; value only used at :311 to compute partner_status — ABSENT from settings payload

**Verifikation:** Code gap is real and confirmed. Python admin_streamer_queries.py:498 emits settings.manualPartnerOptOut inside the detail payload's settings object. The Rust counterpart StreamerSettings struct (admin_streamers.rs:138-162) has no manual_partner_opt_out field, and the detail_handler settings construction (387-411) omits it; the value is only consumed at :311 to compute partner_status. So the detail response's settings object lacks the key — a genuine fidelity divergence from the Python oracle.

However the auditor's stated impact is wrong. The frontend type manualPartnerOptOut? (types.ts:41) and its reader (client.ts:623) belong to the LIST endpoint's StreamerRow, and Rust DOES emit manual_partner_opt_out at the TOP LEVEL of the list item (admin_streamers.rs:60/243), so the list view is unaffected. For the detail view, StreamerDetail.settings is a generic Record<string,unknown> (types.ts:86) and NO component reads settings.manualPartnerOptOut — grep across all .tsx/.ts (excluding client/types) finds zero consumers. StreamerDetail.tsx reads other settings keys (grantedScopes, oauthStatus, isOnDiscord, manualPlanId...) but never the opt-out flag. The only place it surfaces is the raw JSON debug dump (StreamerDetail.tsx:660 JSON.stringify(settings)). Top-level partnerStatus reflects the opt-out correctly in both views. So there is no dead toggle/indicator; the practical effect is one missing key in a debug JSON dump. Real omission, negligible user impact -> severity lowered to low.

**Laufzeit-Check:** GET /twitch/api/admin/streamers/{login} against the Rust API for a streamer with manual_partner_opt_out=1 and inspect the JSON: settings object will lack manualPartnerOptOut while top-level partnerStatus still shows the departnered/opt-out state.

### P3.21 [analytics:admin_streamer_queries] List 'archived' flag returns true for empty-string archived_at (list vs detail inconsistency)

- **Python:** `admin_streamer_queries.py:385`
- **Rust:** `rust/crates/tb-dashboard-api/src/handlers/admin_streamers.rs:238`
- **Status:** changed

**Befund:** If prod twitch_partners_all_state.archived_at stores '' (empty TEXT) for non-archived streamers rather than NULL, the list endpoint marks them archived=true while the detail endpoint marks them archived=false — inconsistent badge/filtering in the streamer list. Impact is nil if prod always uses NULL; high if '' is used.

*Python-Soll:* admin_streamer_queries.py:385 'archived': bool(archived_at) — empty string archived_at is falsy → archived=false

*Rust-Ist:* rust/crates/tb-dashboard-api/src/handlers/admin_streamers.rs:238 archived: r.archived_at.is_some() — Some("") → true; contrast detail handler :342-345 which correctly uses is_some_and(|s| !s.trim().is_empty())

**Verifikation:** The divergence is real in the current code. List handler admin_streamers.rs:238 sets `archived: r.archived_at.is_some()`, so `Some("")` → archived=true. The detail handler (admin_streamers.rs:342-345) uses `is_some_and(|s| !s.trim().is_empty())`, and the list row's own partner_status helper (tb-analytics/src/admin_streamers.rs:97-98) also trims empty strings — so the list `archived` boolean is internally inconsistent with both its own partner_status and the detail endpoint. Python (_admin_streamer_list_row at admin_streamer_queries.py:385, `bool(archived_at)`) treats both NULL and '' as not-archived, so line 238 is a genuine regression vs Python. HOWEVER the impact is downgraded to low because the data source cannot produce `Some("")`: archived_at comes from the view twitch_partners_all_state, defined (baseline_schema.sql:1211-1215) as `COALESCE(p.admin_archived_at, CASE WHEN status='archived' THEN p.departnered_at ELSE NULL END)`, which yields a real timestamp or NULL — never ''. All lifecycle/CRUD code sets admin_archived_at to NULL or NOW(), never ''. So the buggy branch is latent/unreachable under the real schema; the inconsistency exists in code but does not manifest in prod.

**Laufzeit-Check:** Query the twitch_partners_all_state view for any row where archived_at = '' (empty, not NULL); if none exist, the list/detail badge inconsistency cannot trigger and only a hypothetical '' value would diverge.

### P3.22 [analytics:api_viewers] Runtime chat/raid bot logins are not excluded in Rust viewer analytics (only static KNOWN_CHAT_BOTS + streamer-self)

- **Python:** `api_viewers.py:32-52`
- **Rust:** `viewers.rs:34-41`
- **Status:** missing

**Befund:** If a streamer's configured chat or raid bot login is not in the 10-entry static KNOWN_CHAT_BOTS list, that bot is counted as a viewer in segments/directory/detail and is reachable via viewer-detail. Inflates viewer counts and pollutes segmentation. Documented limitation, but a behavioral gap vs Python.

*Rust-Ist:* viewers.rs:34-41 viewer_exclusion_logins returns KNOWN_CHAT_BOTS + streamer-self ONLY; comment lines 28-31 explicitly state dynamic bot accounts are not reachable in this crate. Detail gate viewers.rs:634 checks KNOWN_CHAT_BOTS||login==streamer (no runtime bots).

**Verifikation:** Confirmed in real code. Python api_viewers.py:32-52 dynamically gathers bot logins from owner._bot_token_manager.bot_login, _twitch_chat_bot.nick (+ its _token_manager.bot_login), and _raid_bot.auth_manager.token_manager.bot_login, feeding all NOT IN clauses (line 60) and the detail 404 gate. The Rust counterpart at tb-dashboard-api/src/handlers/viewers.rs:34-41 (viewer_exclusion_logins) returns only the 10 static KNOWN_CHAT_BOTS + streamer-self; the doc comment lines 28-31 openly states the dynamic chat/raid bot logins are not reachable from this crate. Detail gate viewers.rs:634 only checks KNOWN_CHAT_BOTS || login==streamer. I grepped tb-dashboard-api, tb-db schema, and tb-chat/chatter_tracking for any DB-persisted runtime bot login that this handler could query — none exists, so the value is genuinely unreachable here. This is a real behavioral gap, not a differently-built equivalent. Severity corrected to low: it only triggers when a streamer's configured chat/raid bot login is absent from the 10-entry static list (most common bots are covered), and the worst case is bounded cosmetic inflation of one login in counts/segments plus that login being reachable via viewer-detail — no security or data-integrity impact.

**Laufzeit-Check:** Configure a streamer whose chat/raid bot login is NOT one of the 10 KNOWN_CHAT_BOTS, let that bot post in chat, then call the viewer-directory/segments and viewer-detail endpoints and confirm the bot login appears as a counted viewer (Python excludes it, Rust does not).

The readiness DATA survives as JSON at /twitch/api/billing/readiness (readiness_handler billing_page.rs:454, readiness_payload :470). Net: a real but minor presentation/admin-UX gap with no live dead link and no data loss — already tracked as a pre-existing audit finding at severity low (findings-2026-06-15.json:1586, changelogStatus open). Per the claim's own downgrade condition ('if it was also dropped, downgrade to low'), severity is low.

**Laufzeit-Check:** On the live Rust dashboard request GET /twitch/abbo/stripe-settings as a local/Discord-admin and observe a 404 (no handler), while GET /twitch/abbo returns a 301 to /twitch/pricing (no page that links to stripe-settings) and GET /twitch/api/billing/readiness returns the readiness JSON.

### P3.25 [dashboard:routes_entry] POST /twitch/reload (reload_cog) admin endpoint fully dropped — no Rust equivalent

- **Python:** `routes_entry.py:53`
- **Rust:** `ABSENT`
- **Status:** missing

**Befund:** Admin reload tooling endpoint returns 404 (no native route; legacy proxy points at dead Python 8765, and is off by default when TB_DASHBOARD_LEGACY_FALLBACK_URL unset). The X-Admin-Token auth path, audit logging, and cog-reload trigger are gone. Low blast radius (operator convenience) but a real dropped admin capability.

*Python-Soll:* routes_entry.py:53 registers web.post('/twitch/reload', server.reload_cog); body L306-336: auth = _is_local_request OR _is_discord_admin_request OR _check_admin_token(X-Admin-Token); audit-log unauthorized/triggered with peer; invokes server._reload_cb() and returns its message, else 401 'Unauthorized' / 501 'Kein Reload-Handler definiert'.

*Rust-Ist:* ABSENT (searched: grep -rn 'reload_cog|reload_cb|/twitch/reload|Kein Reload-Handler' crates/ bin/ → 0 matches; route not in any build_*_router in lib.rs:646-838).

**Verifikation:** Verified independently. Python: bot/dashboard/routes_entry.py:53 registers web.post('/twitch/reload', server.reload_cog); the handler body (routes_entry.py:306-336) does three-way auth (_is_local_request OR _is_discord_admin_request OR _check_admin_token via X-Admin-Token header), audit-logs unauthorized/triggered with sanitized peer host, then invokes server._reload_cb() (-> mixin.py:781 _reload_twitch_cog, an unload->load hot reload) returning its message, else 401 'Unauthorized' / 501 'Kein Reload-Handler definiert'. Rust: exhaustive grep over rust/crates and rust/bin found ZERO matches for /twitch/reload, reload_cog, _reload_cb, or 'Reload-Handler'. No build_*/Router::new() registration carries the route. Existing 'reload' hits are unrelated (spam_filter pattern reload, engagement_settings db_error label, highlight state). Notably crates/tb-dashboard-api/src/handlers/auth_status.rs:447-453 explicitly documents that the Python X-Admin-Token path is NOT implemented in Rust. So the endpoint, its X-Admin-Token auth, audit logging and cog-reload trigger are genuinely dropped. Severity corrected to low: the capability is a discord.py-specific runtime extension hot-reload (bot.reload_extension) that has no architectural counterpart in the compiled Rust binary (which is updated via systemctl restart, not in-process cog reload), and the impact is operator convenience only with low blast radius. The auditor's relative-path search (crates/ bin/) missed nothing here because the real tree is under rust/, but my search of the correct tree confirms the same conclusion: absent.

**Laufzeit-Check:** On the live host, POST to http://127.0.0.1:<tb-dashboard-port>/twitch/reload and confirm it returns 404 (no native route) rather than a 200/401/501 reload response.

### P3.26 [dashboard:live_announcement_mixin] 'Test per DM' feature for live-announcement builder absent in Rust

- **Python:** `live_announcement_mixin.py:973-1091`
- **Rust:** `ABSENT`
- **Status:** missing

**Befund:** The 'Test per DM' dashboard button (live_announcement_mixin.py:1328) and its /twitch/api/live-announcement/test endpoint produce no behavior in Rust; partners cannot preview their announcement as a real Discord DM before going live.

*Python-Soll:* live_announcement_mixin.py:973-1091 _la_send_test_dm builds a Discord embed (title[:256], description[:4096], color default 0x9146FF, author/fields[:1024]/thumbnail/image/footer with '| TEST-Preview' suffix), an optional link Button (label[:80], default 'Auf Twitch ansehen'), and DMs the resolved user with allowed_mentions=none; _la_dm_target_user_id (926-945) resolves the recipient.

*Rust-Ist:* ABSENT. grep -rin 'TEST-Preview|send_test_dm|test-dm' --include=*.rs over rust/ -> no hits. load_active_partner exists (tb-internal-api/src/streamer_lifecycle.rs:126) but is not wired to any DM-target/test-DM path for this builder.

**Verifikation:** The Python 'Test per DM' feature is present and matches the citation: bot/dashboard/live/live_announcement_mixin.py defines _la_send_test_dm (973-1091, builds embed title[:256]/description[:4096]/color 0x9146FF/author/fields[:1024]/thumbnail/image/footer '| TEST-Preview', optional link Button label[:80] default 'Auf Twitch ansehen', user.send with AllowedMentions.none()), _la_dm_target_user_id (926-945, resolves via session user_id or load_active_partner), the api_live_announcement_test_send handler (358), the 'Test per DM' button (1328) and the /twitch/api/live-announcement/test endpoint (2018). I grepped hard across rust/ (tb-dashboard-api, tb-internal-api, tb-monitoring): there is NO /twitch/api/live-announcement/test route, NO test-DM handler, and NO DM-send path for this builder. The Rust live-announcement port (tb-monitoring/src/announce/*) covers only config parsing, templates, and the broker sink; the Rust 'latest_preview' is an unrelated VOD-link preview. So the behavior genuinely produces nothing in Rust — gap confirmed. HOWEVER this is a deliberate scope exclusion, not an overlooked regression: numerous Rust source comments document the 'B10-Direktive (keine Discord-DMs mehr) — von Nani ausgeschlossen' (streamers.rs:38/547/552/671, report_dispatcher.rs:7), and project MEMORY records 'B10-DMs ausgeschlossen'. The test-DM depends entirely on the Discord-DM channel the user intentionally removed across the whole cutover. Hence I downgrade medium->low: the feature is correctly absent by design, not a broken port.

**Laufzeit-Check:** Open the Rust live-announcement dashboard in prod, click 'Test per DM' (if the button is even rendered) and confirm no Discord DM arrives / the /twitch/api/live-announcement/test request 404s — consistent with the B10 no-DM exclusion.

### P3.27 [dashboard:partner_auth_mixin] Existing-session short-circuit dropped: Rust always creates a new session on /partner/login

- **Python:** `partner_auth_mixin.py:189-197`
- **Rust:** `handlers/partner_login.rs:189-215`
- **Status:** missing

**Befund:** An already-logged-in admin/partner who re-hits the login link gets a brand-new session minted (extra DB rows, cookie churn, and for an admin it could downgrade them to a partner session). Python deliberately reused the existing session and only redirected.

*Python-Soll:* partner_auth_mixin.py:189-197 — if _get_dashboard_auth_session OR _get_discord_admin_session OR _get_partner_access_session is present, raise 303 redirect WITHOUT creating a new session.

*Rust-Ist:* handlers/partner_login.rs:189-215 — login_handler unconditionally calls create_partner_session and sets a fresh cookie; no check for an already-authenticated session. ABSENT (searched: grep _get_dashboard_auth_session / _get_discord_admin_session / load_admin_session within partner_login.rs — none).

**Verifikation:** Confirmed in current code. Python auth_partner_login (bot/dashboard/auth/partner_auth_mixin.py:189-197) checks _get_dashboard_auth_session OR _get_discord_admin_session OR _get_partner_access_session and, if any exists, raises a 303 redirect WITHOUT creating a new session. The Rust counterpart login_handler (rust/crates/tb-dashboard-api/src/handlers/partner_login.rs:135-216) takes only state/config/pool/body — no DashboardAuthLevel extractor, no reading of existing session cookies — and unconditionally calls create_partner_session (line 191) + sets a fresh PARTNER_COOKIE_NAME cookie (lines 202-214) before redirecting. No session-existence short-circuit exists anywhere in this handler or crate path; grep for load_admin_session/load_partner_session shows usages only in level.rs/partner_gate.rs/auth_login.rs gating, never in partner_login.rs. The claim's cited line range (189-215) is slightly off (real handler is 135-216) but the substance holds. Severity lowered to low: the partner login token is single-use (consume_partner_login_state is atomic), so a literal re-hit of the same link 401s before any session is minted; the realistic trigger is a still-authenticated admin/partner consuming a freshly-issued link for themselves, yielding an extra partner session row/cookie. The new cookie is under PARTNER_COOKIE_NAME, so the 'admin downgrade' claim is only partially accurate (cookies likely coexist rather than overwrite, but auth resolution may then prefer the partner cookie). Net effect: minor extra DB rows + cookie churn, not a security or correctness break.

**Laufzeit-Check:** While already logged in as admin/partner (valid cookie present), issue a fresh partner link for yourself and POST /twitch/auth/partner/login with it; observe a new partner Set-Cookie and a new session row instead of a plain redirect.

### P3.28 [internal_api:runner] Port-bind retry/backoff loop on EADDRINUSE dropped — transient port-busy on restart now hard-fails the bot

- **Python:** `runner.py:144-190`
- **Rust:** `rust/bin/tb-bot/src/main.rs:1197-1202`
- **Status:** missing

**Befund:** On a fast restart/redeploy where the old process still holds 127.0.0.1:8776 in TIME_WAIT or is mid-shutdown, Python would retry for ~7.5s and recover; Rust exits(1) immediately, requiring an external supervisor (systemd) restart loop to recover. The systemd user-service likely restarts it, so the net effect is a noisier crash-restart instead of an in-process retry — degraded resilience, not a permanent outage.

*Python-Soll:* runner.py:144-190 — max_retries=5, retry_delay=0.5; for attempt in range(5): on OSError where exc.errno in (10048, EADDRINUSE=98) and attempt<4 -> log.warning('port busy ... retrying in %.1fs'), await asyncio.sleep(retry_delay), retry_delay*=2 (0.5->1->2->4s), continue.

*Rust-Ist:* rust/bin/tb-bot/src/main.rs:1197-1202 — `tokio::net::TcpListener::bind(addr).await.unwrap_or_else(|e| { tracing::error!(...); std::process::exit(1); })`. No AddrInUse check, no retry, no backoff (greps: AddrInUse|EADDRINUSE|max_retries|retry|attempt|busy|retrying over main.rs -> no matches).

**Verifikation:** Python runner.py:144-193 has the exact loop the claim describes: max_retries=5, retry_delay=0.5, OSError where errno in (10048, EADDRINUSE=98) and attempt<4 -> warn "port busy ... retrying in %.1fs", asyncio.sleep, retry_delay*=2, continue (0.5->1->2->4s ~= 7.5s total). The only TCP socket bind in Rust is main.rs:1197-1202: tokio::net::TcpListener::bind(addr).await.unwrap_or_else(|e| { tracing::error!("Bind-Fehler ..."); std::process::exit(1); }) — no ErrorKind::AddrInUse check, no retry, no backoff, no SO_REUSEADDR handling. Grepping AddrInUse|EADDRINUSE|retry|backoff|reuse across both tb-bot bin and the tb-internal-api crate found only unrelated hits (SQL query .bind, RPC/raid retry jobs, the enforce_internal_api_runtime hardening which is a separate concern). So the transient-port-busy in-process recovery genuinely does not exist in Rust — the gap is real. Severity downgraded to low: the bot runs under a systemd user-service that auto-restarts on crash, so a transient TIME_WAIT/mid-shutdown bind collision yields a noisy crash-restart instead of a permanent outage. It is degraded resilience, not data loss or a hard outage, and a fresh process on a cleared port recovers automatically.

**Laufzeit-Check:** Start a dummy listener on 127.0.0.1:8776, then launch tb-bot and observe it logs "Bind-Fehler" and exits immediately (exit 1) instead of logging "port busy ... retrying"; check systemd journal for the resulting restart loop.

### P3.29 [internal_api:pg] Rust query drops the s.discord_user_id linkage filter — already-linked streamers (linked via twitch_streamers.discord_user_id) wrongly returned as link candidates

- **Python:** `/home/naniadm/Documents/Deadlock-Twitch-Bot/bot/storage/pg.py:4162`
- **Rust:** `/home/naniadm/Documents/Deadlock-Twitch-Bot/rust/crates/tb-analytics/src/streamer_link.rs:45`
- **Status:** changed

**Befund:** A streamer whose Discord link is recorded on twitch_streamers.discord_user_id (not on twitch_streamer_identities) is excluded by Python but INCLUDED by Rust. The Discord-bot matcher would re-attempt to AI-match and re-link an already-linked partner, risking duplicate/incorrect linkage and wasted matcher work. Magnitude depends on how often discord_user_id lives on twitch_streamers vs identities; the column exists and is part of the original exclusion contract.

*Python-Soll:* /home/naniadm/Documents/Deadlock-Twitch-Bot/bot/storage/pg.py:4162 — WHERE includes `(s.discord_user_id IS NULL OR s.discord_user_id = '')` alongside the identities filter. twitch_streamers has a real discord_user_id column (pg.py:2215).

*Rust-Ist:* /home/naniadm/Documents/Deadlock-Twitch-Bot/rust/crates/tb-analytics/src/streamer_link.rs:45 — WHERE only filters `(i.discord_user_id IS NULL OR i.discord_user_id = '')`; the s.discord_user_id predicate is absent.

**Verifikation:** The gap is real in code. Python (bot/storage/pg.py:4160-4161) filters on BOTH (s.discord_user_id IS NULL OR ='') AND (i.discord_user_id IS NULL OR =''). Rust (rust/crates/tb-analytics/src/streamer_link.rs:45) only filters i.discord_user_id; the s.discord_user_id predicate is absent. The column twitch_streamers.discord_user_id is real (pg.py:2216) and is a valid linkage location. A sync trigger (pg.py:2398-2445, trg_twitch_streamers_sync_identity) mirrors s.discord_user_id -> i.discord_user_id, which masks the gap in the COMMON case — BUT the trigger only fires when COALESCE(NEW.twitch_user_id,'') <> '', and the query's LEFT JOIN i.twitch_user_id = s.twitch_user_id only surfaces i.discord_user_id when twitch_user_id is non-empty. So a streamer with s.discord_user_id set but s.twitch_user_id NULL/empty (or whose identity row is missing/predates the trigger) is EXCLUDED by Python yet INCLUDED by Rust — the matcher would re-attempt to AI-link an already-linked partner. The Rust unit tests cannot catch this: the test DDL (streamer_link.rs:103-108) omits the discord_user_id column on twitch_streamers entirely and creates no sync trigger, so the s.* path is untested. Severity lowered to low because the trigger backstops the dominant case; the residual gap is a narrow edge state (twitch_user_id empty while discord_user_id set), not the medium-frequency case the claim implies.

**Laufzeit-Check:** In prod DB, set twitch_streamers.discord_user_id for an active partner whose twitch_user_id is empty/NULL, then call list_unlinked and confirm the row still appears (Rust) while Python's list_unlinked_streamers omits it.

## Rust-Verbesserungen (kein Bug — bewusst besser gebaut)  (635)

_Konstrukte, die der zeilengenaue Vergleich als „in Rust sauberer/sicherer, gleiches oder besseres Verhalten" eingestuft hat. Bewusst NICHT als Befund gewertet._

- **[sonstige:partner_roster]** Python dict/tuple-Row-Adapter; Rust nutzt typisierte FromRow-Structs — gleiche Defaults (None/leer).  —  `sqlx::FromRow (partner_roster.rs RosterRow, live_state.rs OfflineSourceState)`
- **[sonstige:moderation]** Rust lowercases keys internally; Python does not. All Python callers (eventsub_mixin login_norm/_login_norm, moderation _normalize_channel_login_safe) already pass lowered logins, so Rust's extra lowercasing is defensive and cannot diverge. Not a finding.  —  `moderation.rs:628/649/659/678/688 login.to_lowercase()`
- **[sonstige:backend]** Rust deserialize_id_as_string accepts both JSON String and Number for the Discord snowflake -> stringified. Correctly guards against the known broker-snowflake-as-number bug class. Empty handled in sink.rs:323 (filter non-empty).  —  `rust/crates/tb-transport-discord/src/backend.rs:55-79 (SendResultInner.message_id, deserialize_id_as_string)`
- **[sonstige:?]** Python's getattr name-probing list is a duck-typing artifact; a typed Rust hook (on_score_refresh) supersedes it. Not a finding.  —  `N/A (dynamic-introspection dispatch not needed in Rust)`
- **[sonstige:?]** Candidate-object probing (partner_raid_score_service / _raid_bot / host) is dynamic dispatch replaced by explicit hook wiring.  —  `N/A`
- **[sonstige:?]** inspect.signature kwarg-mapping is duck-typing glue, replaced by typed hook args (twitch_user_id, login, trigger).  —  `N/A`
- **[sonstige:chat_hype_timeline]** time_bucket('1 minute') replaced with date_trunc('minute') — documented equivalent, removes TimescaleDB dependency, same 1-min buckets. Same WHERE incl chatter_login IS NOT NULL AND bot clause. Improvement, not a finding.  —  `chat_hype_timeline.rs:135-145`
- **[sonstige:client]** Python tried the stripe Python SDK first, fell back to REST on exception. Rust has no Python SDK; the native StripeClient IS the REST path. The fallback branch is intentionally collapsed — same/greater behavior (one robust REST path). NOT a finding.  —  `client.rs:148 create_checkout_session (REST-only native client)`
- **[sonstige:client]** Python offloaded the blocking SDK/urllib to a thread. Rust is natively async (reqwest .await) — the thread-offload wrapper is unnecessary. Same/greater behavior.  —  `client.rs post_form async (reqwest)`
- **[sonstige:session]** Rust nutzt freie Cookie-Builder + Aufrufer-uebergebenes secure-Flag statt Owner-Mixin; gleiche Wirkung.  —  `session.rs:112-161 (freie Funktionen build_session_cookie/clear_session_cookie statt Owner-Klasse)`
- **[sonstige:session]** Python: stiller Erfolg trotz DB-Fehler (Session nur im Cache). Rust: fail-closed (Login schlaegt fehl). Bewusste Haertung (session.rs:304-307 Doku) — sicherer, kein Regress.  —  `session.rs:334-337 persist_new_session bubbelt Err -> create_partner_session gibt Err`
- **[chat:spam_filter]** Built once via OnceLock; applied char-by-char in normalize_spam_text. Same effect.  —  `crates/tb-chat/src/spam_filter.rs:195-223 (HOMOGLYPH_TABLE OnceLock + normalize_spam_text)`
- **[chat:types]** EventSub-native: typed source_broadcaster_user_id fallback to broadcaster, source fields cleared after promotion. Same precedence as Python (source_broadcaster or broadcaster).  —  `crates/tb-chat/src/types.rs:107-124 (with_effective_channel)`
- **[chat:moderation]** regex crate lacks lookbehind; uses (?:^|[^\w])@(...)\b + group(1) — equivalent exclusion of word@host.  —  `rust/crates/tb-chat/src/mention_scoring.rs:75-92`
- **[chat:moderation]** Auth header assembly relocated into transport client; user-token + Client-Id supplied per Helix call. Same effective behavior, no separate context helper needed.  —  `rust/crates/tb-transport-twitch/src/chat.rs:241-360 (ban_user/delete_chat_message with Client-Id + user token)`

- **[chat:spam_ai_review]** Rust drops runtime CREATE TABLE (prod schema migrated). Same on/off behavior — tables exist via migrations. GOOD, not a finding.  —  `scam_pitch.rs:1795-1811, 1834-1849 (no CREATE TABLE)`
- **[chat:spam_ai_review]** Rust-only convenience entry not wired into pipeline (pipeline.rs:691 uses maybe_review_with_reasons). No behavioral impact.  —  `scam_pitch.rs:1468-1570`
- **[chat:irc_lurker_tracker]** Python adapts to executemany-or-cursor; Rust issues per-row async queries in a loop. Same net effect (batch insert/update). - **[chat:irc_lurker_tracker]** Three separate asyncio tasks collapsed into one run() reconnect loop + serve() tokio::select! that multiplexes read, NAMES-poll interval, and JOIN/PART commands. - **[chat:irc_lurker_tracker]** Same 10s read timeout (CONNECT_TIMEOUT), same :tmi.twitch.tv 001 success and PING handling. Rust ADDS an n==0 (EOF) -> return None break (:325-327). Return value differs (() vs PendingRaid) but caller already owns the raid, so no information loss. Insert-under-normalized-key semantics identical. None-payload short-circuit is handled caller-side in Rust (no raid built -> no store call).  —  `pending_raids.rs:173-176 (store(raid: PendingRaid): insert under raid.key())`

- **[dashboard:__init__]** Defines the public re-export surface of the package. In Rust this role is filled statically by `pub use` statements in lib.rs (e.g. lib.rs:16 pub use auth::csrf::csrf_protect, lines 16-27). The specific DashboardRuntime* contract types originate from bot/runtime/dashboard_runtime (a different module outside this unit); this __init__ merely forwards them. Equivalent re-export behavior achieved at compile time.  —  `/home/naniadm/Documents/Deadlock-Twitch-Bot/rust/crates/tb-dashboard-api/src/lib.rs:16-27 (pub use re-exports)`
- **[dashboard:__init__]** PEP 562 lazy attribute access: on access of a name in __all__, imports .runtime, fetches the attr, caches it in globals(), returns it; raises AttributeError for unknown names. This is a Python import-deferral optimization, not application logic. Rust resolves symbols eagerly at compile time via pub use, which is strictly equivalent or better (no lazy-load failure mode, no runtime AttributeError path). Improvement, not a finding.  —  `/home/naniadm/Documents/Deadlock-Twitch-Bot/rust/crates/tb-dashboard-api/src/lib.rs (module system / pub use)`
- **[dashboard:route_deps]** Pure import/boilerplate for the dataclass DI-container pattern. Rust has no analog because it does not use runtime dependency-container dataclasses; it uses Axum State/Extension. No behavior.  —  `N/A (Python DI plumbing)`

- **[dashboard:app]** Python on_cleanup stops the eventsub bridge and closes the aiohttp internal-API client. - **[dashboard:app]** Python on_startup starts the eventsub bridge. Rust starts its native EventSub receiver in tb-monitoring/tb-bot composition root, not the dashboard. No behavior lost.  —  `crates/tb-monitoring (native receiver startup)`

- **[dashboard:app]** Python standalone runner: resolves host/noauth/port (TWITCH_DASHBOARD_HOST/NOAUTH/PORT, default 127.0.0.1:8765), noauth loopback guard, runtime PID lock, builds app, sets up RotatingFileHandler access log (5MB x3) at logs/twitch_dashboard_access.log, AppRunner+TCPSite, runs until cancelled. - **[dashboard:eventsub_bridge]** Python: Twitch->Python-Dashboard(8765)->HTTP-hop->bot(8776). Rust deliberately eliminates the dashboard HTTP-forwarding bridge in favor of a native in-process webhook receiver in tb-bot (Twitch->Caddy->WebhookReceiver->EventSubDispatcher->InboxRuntime). webhook_receiver.rs:6-11 explicitly documents this as a fix for a Python bug where channel.chat.message was silently dropped by the bridge. The retry/backoff/dead-letter state machine is preserved (relocated) in InboxRuntime. No dashboard-side bridge runtime exists in tb-dashboard/tb-dashboard-api (grep for InboxRuntime/dispatch_eventsub_notification/bridge in those paths = empty). GOOD.  —  `ARCHITECTURALLY REPLACED: tb-monitoring webhook_receiver.rs:1-22 (docstring) + dispatch.rs (EventSubDispatcher) + inbox_runtime.rs (InboxRuntime); wired in tb-bot/src/main.rs:786-825`
- **[dashboard:eventsub_bridge]** Same fields conceptually: injectable clock (with_clock), wakeup (Notify), stop (watch channel), task (JoinHandle). Client/HTTP-dispatch field is gone by design (in-process dispatch via EventSubDispatcher). Faithful structural equivalent.  —  `inbox_runtime.rs:75-94 (InboxRuntime::new/with_clock) + start():97-113 (Notify wakeup, watch stop channel, JoinHandle task)`
- **[dashboard:eventsub_bridge]** Rust models lifecycle via owned handle rather than an active() flag; dispatch path no longer branches on active (see dispatch_or_enqueue note). No behavioral loss.  —  `inbox_runtime.rs: start consumes self -> InboxRuntimeHandle; liveness via JoinHandle/watch`
- **[dashboard:eventsub_bridge]** Spawns the same poll loop. Idempotency-by-active-check is replaced by ownership (start consumes self), so double-start is impossible by construction.  —  `inbox_runtime.rs:97-113 InboxRuntime::start -> spawns run() via tokio::spawn, returns handle`
- **[dashboard:eventsub_bridge]** Faithful: signals stop, wakes worker, awaits task completion.  —  `inbox_runtime.rs:284-288 InboxRuntimeHandle::shutdown (stop.send(true), wakeup.notify_one, task.await)`
- **[dashboard:eventsub_bridge]** message_id normalization preserved (dispatch.rs:367 trim+filter non-empty). The 'requires message_id' RuntimeError becomes the receiver rejecting missing header. No data loss.  —  `dispatch.rs:352-405 EventSubDispatcher::dispatch (message_id trimmed/filtered empty) + webhook_receiver.rs (message_id from header)`
- **[dashboard:eventsub_bridge]** Python branches synchronous-dispatch-when-inactive vs enqueue-when-active. Rust always dispatches in-process; the dispatcher itself decides enqueue (CORE_DELIVERY_TYPES -> inbox) vs direct hook processing. enqueue wakes the worker (notify_one). Behaviorally equivalent or superior (no lost-notification window).  —  `webhook_receiver.rs:209-231 (ensure_dispatch_ready then dispatch) + dispatch.rs route():422 enqueuer.enqueue + inbox_runtime.rs:269 wakeup.notify_one`
- **[dashboard:eventsub_bridge]** Python's startup-pending deferral (bot EventSub not yet ready) is replaced by returning 503 so Twitch's native webhook retry redelivers. The exact trigger string 'eventsub notification dispatch inactive' is preserved (dispatch.rs:84). _OUTBOX_STARTUP_WAIT_SECONDS (fixed 5s) and mark_deferred are intentionally gone because retry responsibility moved to Twitch. Sound for the new in-process architecture.  —  `webhook_receiver.rs:207-216 + dispatch.rs:77-90 DispatchNotReady::DispatchInactive -> HTTP 503 (Twitch retries). No store-side mark_deferred analog (grep mark_deferred in rust = ABSENT).`
- **[dashboard:eventsub_bridge]** The HTTP call self._client.dispatch_eventsub_notification is replaced by direct in-process dispatch/handler invocation. Eliminates the HTTP hop that silently dropped notifications in Python. GOOD.  —  `dispatch.rs:352-405 EventSubDispatcher::dispatch (in-process) + handler.handle in inbox_runtime.rs:165`
- **[dashboard:eventsub_bridge]** Upstream-HTTP-error classification is unnecessary in-process. The startup-pending signal is preserved as DispatchInactive->503. Other errors route to the same retry/dead-letter machine via HandlerError.  —  `dispatch.rs:80-90 DispatchNotReady (DispatchInactive / CallbackNotRegistered) -> 503; handler errors -> HandlerError -> retry/dead-letter`
- **[dashboard:eventsub_bridge]** DispatchOutcome carries ok/duplicate/queued/processed natively; no JSON envelope re-parsing. The ok==False startup-pending path maps to DispatchInactive->503. No behavioral loss.  —  `dispatch.rs:92-100 DispatchOutcome{ok,...} returned directly in-process; readiness via ensure_dispatch_ready (webhook_receiver.rs:209)`
- **[dashboard:eventsub_bridge]** Rust uses a typed error variant instead of isinstance+string-matching. Cleaner, same intent.  —  `dispatch.rs:80-90 (DispatchNotReady enum is the typed equivalent; matched directly, no string sniffing)`
- **[dashboard:eventsub_bridge]** Exact same magic string preserved as a typed variant. Rust matches the enum variant rather than normalizing+comparing the string. Faithful semantics, safer implementation.  —  `dispatch.rs:84 #[error("eventsub notification dispatch inactive")] DispatchInactive`
- **[dashboard:client]** Typed error subclass. In Rust the EventSub flow is in-process so there is no client-side upstream-error type; errors surface via ApiError at the receiving handler. Architecturally absorbed, no behavioral loss.  —  `rust/crates/tb-http-core/src/lib.rs (ApiError); rust/crates/tb-monitoring/src/dispatch.rs (DispatchNotReady etc.)`
- **[dashboard:client]** Python's standalone dashboard_service process POSTs EventSub over HTTP to the bot via this client. The wrapper itself is obsolete by design.  —  `ABSENT as a standalone dashboard HTTP client; receiving contract in rust/crates/tb-internal-api/src/handlers/eventsub.rs:28; in-process dispatcher in rust/crates/tb-monitoring/src/dispatch.rs:352`
- **[dashboard:client]** Error-passthrough mapping is meaningful only for an HTTP client receiving an upstream status/payload. With the in-process dispatcher there is no HTTP response to map; the dispatcher returns Rust errors directly. - **[dashboard:client]** Python's client inspects the HTTP envelope's 'ok' field and converts ok:false to a 503 upstream_unavailable. Same Twitch-facing outcome (503 -> retry) reached more directly. Not a regression.  —  `rust/crates/tb-internal-api/src/handlers/eventsub.rs:59-65 (dispatcher.dispatch Err -> ApiError::unavailable() i.e. 503); dispatch.rs returns ok/duplicate/queued/processed outcomes`

- **[dashboard:auth_mixin]** Rust prunt Admin-Cache lazy beim Lookup; In-Memory-LRU-Prune-by-created/expires durch DB-getriebenes Modell ersetzt. Gleiche Netto-Wirkung (abgelaufene Sessions ungueltig).  —  `crates/tb-dashboard-api/src/auth/session.rs:699-747 (load_admin_session pruned cache + payload_expired delete)`
- **[dashboard:auth_mixin]** In-Memory-State-Cleanup durch DB-TTL-State (single-use consume) ersetzt; abgelaufene States werden serverseitig invalidiert.  —  `crates/tb-dashboard-api/src/auth/session.rs (consume_oauth_login_state/TTL-basierte States)`
- **[dashboard:auth_mixin]** Lazy-cached repo accessor; Rust holds DashboardAuthState as Extension with pool+caches. Equivalent caching, idiomatic.  —  `crates/tb-dashboard-api/src/auth/session.rs DashboardAuthState (cached state w/ pool)`
- **[dashboard:auth_mixin]** In-memory session caches present in DashboardAuthState; structural equivalent.  —  `session.rs (admin_cache/partner_cache fields)`
- **[dashboard:__init__]** Import of the lazy-compat helper. This exists only to support Python's legacy import-path indirection. Rust has no equivalent need: module re-exports are declared natively. No behavior lost.  —  `Rust native module system (mod/pub use)`
- **[dashboard:__init__]** Registers a lazy __getattr__ that resolves the legacy name `_DashboardAffiliateMixin` to the module `.affiliate_mixin` on first access (see _compat.export_name_map). This is a Python import-system shim for backwards-compatible attribute access; it does not itself implement affiliate logic. The actual affiliate dashboard feature is present on the Rust side (affiliate_portal.rs / admin_affiliate.rs exist). Rust exposes such modules via native `mod`/`pub use`, so the lazy-export indirection has no counterpart and needs none. The real behavioral parity lives in affiliate_mixin.py, which is outside this 1-5 line range.  —  `/home/naniadm/Documents/Deadlock-Twitch-Bot/rust/crates/tb-dashboard-api/src/handlers/affiliate_portal.rs (+ admin_affiliate.rs, partner_gate.rs)`
- **[dashboard:auth_mixin]** Python sweeps expired in-memory caches; Rust expires states via SQL TTL filter on every consume. Same net effect, no in-proc cache.  —  `consume_oauth_login_state DELETE…RETURNING + expires_at filter (session.rs:440-495)`
- **[dashboard:gutschrift]** Rust inlines label formatting with a safe range guard returning '' for out-of-range, vs Python raising ValueError on invalid year/month. Same output for valid inputs; Rust is safer.  —  `admin_affiliate.rs:250-254 (inline in gutschrift_base_value)`
- **[dashboard:affiliate_mixin]** Rust uses a first-class StripeClient (HTTP) instead of a dynamic SDK import; same capability (transfers), no behavioral loss. Not a finding.  —  `crates/tb-analytics/src/stripe/client.rs (StripeClient)`
- **[dashboard:affiliate_mixin]** Rust uses pg_advisory_xact_lock (auto-released at commit/rollback) instead of session lock + explicit unlock. Same lock key/namespace -> Python and Rust serialize against each other. Safer (no leaked lock on panic). Not a finding.  —  `crates/tb-analytics/src/affiliate_commission.rs:154-162 (pg_advisory_xact_lock inside process_commission tx)`
- **[dashboard:affiliate_mixin]** Rust always operates on a real PgPool transaction; the Python guard against non-transactional connections is structurally unnecessary in Rust (type system guarantees it). Not a finding.  —  `crates/tb-analytics/src/affiliate_commission.rs:154 (pool.begin())`
- **[dashboard:affiliate_mixin]** Faithful port: 30% floor, _MAX_PENDING_COMMISSION_CENTS=5000, advisory lock crc32 namespace 1103151689, duplicate detection, pending/skipped status. Schema-fidelity fix: customer lookup uses customer_reference instead of twitch_login (documented; column twitch_login never existed). int4 binds correctly handled.  —  `crates/tb-analytics/src/affiliate_commission.rs:105-237 process_commission`
- **[dashboard:affiliate_mixin]** Schema applied via migrations not per-request runtime DDL — cleaner. No finding.  —  `rust/migrations/*.sql (sqlx migrations) instead of runtime split-on-semicolon execution`
- **[dashboard:affiliate_mixin]** Per-request ensure-tables replaced by migrations; PII plaintext->encrypted migration handled in pii module. Cleaner, no per-call DDL. No finding.  —  `migrations + crates/tb-analytics/src/affiliate_pii.rs (cipher-based PII)`
- **[internal_api:app]** Python aiohttp app wiring; Rust uses axum modular handlers/router. Architectural equivalent — same set of routes/callbacks materialized as handler modules + Extension ports.  —  `/home/naniadm/Documents/Deadlock-Twitch-Bot/rust/crates/tb-internal-api/src/lib.rs:12-36`
- **[internal_api:app]** Python normalizes a configurable base_path. Rust hardcodes INTERNAL_API_BASE_PATH constant ('/internal/twitch/v1') for all routes. Both deployments use the same fixed path; the normalization branches are config-time defensiveness that cannot fire in the real deployment. No observable behavioral change.  —  `/home/naniadm/Documents/Deadlock-Twitch-Bot/rust/crates/tb-internal-api/src/lib.rs:71`
- **[internal_api:app]** Python returns 200 with ok:false on missing dispatcher; Rust returns 503 (ApiError::unavailable) so the EventSub bridge buffers+retries durably. Documented intentional hardening (lib.rs:42); strictly safer.  —  `/home/naniadm/Documents/Deadlock-Twitch-Bot/rust/crates/tb-internal-api/src/handlers/eventsub.rs:51,63`
- **[internal_api:app]** Rust chat_action_handler returns 503 with explicit error when ChatActionPort absent (TB_CHAT_ENABLED off) instead of placeholder 200. Documented improvement.  —  `/home/naniadm/Documents/Deadlock-Twitch-Bot/rust/crates/tb-internal-api/src/handlers/python_stubs.rs:257`
- **[internal_api:app]** Raid-blacklist CRUD fully native (lib.rs:111-126); stubs unreachable. Native handlers replace the RuntimeError-raising stubs entirely.  —  `/home/naniadm/Documents/Deadlock-Twitch-Bot/rust/crates/tb-internal-api/src/handlers/raid_blacklist.rs`
- **[internal_api:app]** Python binds module-level _*_impl helpers as staticmethods on the class. Rust implements the underlying helpers as free functions in security.rs (host_without_port, is_loopback_host, is_loopback_origin, compare_internal_token, datetime/uuid/decimal/set json). The aliasing layer itself is a Python-dispatch concern; behavior of each helper is ported. Several normalize helpers (_parse_allowlist_ids, _coerce_optional_positive_int, _normalize_login, _normalize_raid_auth_target, _normalize_tracking_token, _normalize_text_field, _normalize_live_announcement_item, _normalize_raid_state_payload, _safe_bad_request_detail) live in their respective handler modules (e.g. raid_oauth.rs normalize_discord_user_id:345) — outside this exact range's core but reachable.  —  `rust/crates/tb-internal-api/src/security.rs:45 (host_without_port), :90 (is_loopback_host), and policy helpers`
- **[internal_api:app]** Python uses inspect.signature reflection to decide which of discord_user_id/scope_profile kwargs the dynamically-injected _raid_auth_url callable accepts, then str(...).strip(). This defensive dynamic-dispatch exists only because Python injects an arbitrary callable. Rust has a single fixed trait signature auth_url(login, Option<discord_user_id>, Option<scope_profile>) -> Result<String>. Net behavior identical (login + optional hints passed through); the reflection machinery is correctly collapsed away. Not a finding.  —  `rust/crates/tb-internal-api/src/handlers/raid_oauth.rs:153-158 (RaidOAuthPort::auth_url)`
- **[internal_api:app]** Python re-parses response.text JSON back into a value for caching/replay; on failure -> {}. Not a finding.  —  `rust/crates/tb-internal-api/src/idempotency.rs:315 (complete takes &Value payload directly)`
- **[internal_api:app]** Python json default serializer split into typed helpers in Rust (datetime->isoformat +00:00, Decimal->number, UUID->str, set->list). Same serialization contract.  —  `security.rs:232 datetime_to_iso / decimal_to_json / uuid_to_json / set_to_json`
- **[internal_api:app]** Rust uses axum (status, Json(..)).into_response(); same JSON body + status semantics.  —  `idempotency.rs:111 json_error + axum Json responses across handlers`
- **[internal_api:app]** Intentionally proxied to legacy Python (sends real Discord DM); documented as deliberate non-native. Behavior preserved via proxy.  —  `lib.rs:252-254 POST /raid/requirements -> python_stubs::raid_requirements_handler (legacy proxy)`
- **[internal_api:app]** Python's 6 attach_* registrars replaced by explicit axum path routing. - **[internal_api:app]** Server state assembled via axum state/extensions instead of constructor kwargs.  —  `lib.rs:284-298 .with_state(pool) + .layer(Extension(..)) stack`
- **[internal_api:runner]** Rust has no separate runner object; the internal API is served inline as the tb-bot process main listener. Simpler composition; behavior covered by individual items below.  —  `rust/bin/tb-bot/src/main.rs:1166-1209`
- **[internal_api:runner]** No is_running query needed: serve runs for process lifetime. State-tracking field eliminated.  —  `rust/bin/tb-bot/src/main.rs:1204 (axum::serve owns lifetime)`
- **[internal_api:runner]** Rust logs error + process::exit(1) on fatal start error instead of stashing a string for later polling. No external poller exists in Rust path.  —  `rust/bin/tb-bot/src/main.rs:1199-1202,1190-1193`
- **[internal_api:runner]** Single inline serve call, started once at boot — re-entrancy guard unnecessary.  —  `rust/bin/tb-bot/src/main.rs:1204`
- **[internal_api:runner]** Cancellation handled by tokio runtime/signal layer, not an inline catch. No behavioral regression in the serve path.  —  `rust/bin/tb-bot/src/main.rs:1204 (tokio runtime owns cancellation)`
- **[internal_api:runner]** Bind errors handled with explicit exit(1)+log; serve() errors .unwrap (panic). Generic catch-all parity is loose but only matters for transient bind errors, captured by the missing-retry finding.  —  `rust/bin/tb-bot/src/main.rs:1197-1202,1208 (.unwrap on serve)`
- **[internal_api:runner]** Internal API is the process's primary listener; teardown happens via process exit / tokio shutdown rather than an explicit cleanup method. No separate consumer calls stop() in the Rust path.  —  `rust/bin/tb-bot/src/main.rs:1204 (no explicit stop; process exit)`
- **[internal_api:_helpers]** Python `bind` is an aiohttp-specific adapter: it closes over `server` and returns an async `_handler(request)` that calls `handler(server, request)`, converting the 2-arg internal handler form into aiohttp's 1-arg handler signature. In the axum port this adapter is structurally obsolete: handlers are registered directly via get()/post() (lib.rs:73+) and receive shared server state through framework-injected extractors (Extension/State/Json, e.g. healthz.rs). Same capability (handlers gain access to shared state), achieved idiomatically without a manual closure. No behavioral loss; not a finding.  —  `/home/naniadm/Documents/Deadlock-Twitch-Bot/rust/crates/tb-internal-api/src/lib.rs:73-106 (route wiring) + src/handlers/healthz.rs:11-13 (extractor-based handler signatures)`
- **[internal_api:_helpers]** The forwarding closure body; subsumed by axum's extractor mechanism that supplies state to each handler. Faithful capability, cleaner mechanism.  —  `axum handler functions with extractor parameters (e.g. /home/naniadm/Documents/Deadlock-Twitch-Bot/rust/crates/tb-internal-api/src/handlers/healthz.rs healthz_handler)`
- **[internal_api:policy]** Python single dispatcher (datetime->isoformat, Decimal->float, UUID->str, set->list, else TypeError). Rust splits into typed helpers; datetime_to_iso replicates isoformat (+00:00, micros only if !=0). Non-finite Decimal -> Null instead of float (safer). Native serde handles the rest. Same observable output.  —  `security.rs:232-266 (datetime_to_iso/decimal_to_json/uuid_to_json/set_to_json)`
- **[internal_api:policy]** Architectural improvement, same external contract. Python normalizes pre-built dicts coming back from _live_active_announcements (validates each field, may raise ValueError -> 500). Rust builds items directly from DB rows (db::list_active_announcements) and constructs referral_url server-side via build_referral_url, so the data is trusted-by-construction. Equivalent or stricter behavior (invalid rows are skipped via `continue`, not raised).  —  `/home/naniadm/Documents/Deadlock-Twitch-Bot/rust/crates/tb-internal-api/src/handlers/telemetry_routes.rs:59-67 (AnnouncementItem), 300-358 (live_active_announcements_handler)`
- **[internal_api:policy]** Python validates an INCOMING referral_url (scheme in {http,https}, non-empty netloc, len<=2000). Rust GENERATES the url (https://www.twitch.tv/<login>?ref=... or https://www.twitch.tv/) so it is always a valid https URL with netloc -> the validation is structurally unnecessary. Greater-or-equal safety, contract-compatible.  —  `telemetry_routes.rs:235-246 (build_referral_url), 344`
- **[internal_api:streamers]** Rust never throws 500 here; transport failures are typed (Failed->200 ok:false) and missing-port->503. Same-or-better behavior; not a finding.  —  `python_stubs.rs:290-301 Failed variant -> 200 ok:false; no-port -> 503`
- **[internal_api:streamers]** Rust does full departner lifecycle (status→departnered, raid-auth-disable, role revoke, live-state clear) — documented migration-bug fix vs old 1:1 path. Greater behavior. Not a finding.  —  `streamers.rs:474-530`
- **[internal_api:streamer_link]** Python route handler wraps in try/except -> 500. BUT Python's pg.list_unlinked_streamers ALSO try/excepts and returns [] (pg.py:4167-4169), so in practice a DB error already yields []->200 in Python, never reaching the route's 500. Rust mirrors the storage-level resilience: list_unlinked error -> Vec::new() -> 200 {ok:true, entries:[]}. Same observable behavior as Python's effective path; the route-level 500 was effectively dead code. Not a finding.  —  `streamer_link.rs:47-53`
- **[internal_api:global_ban]** Python: kein Auth-Check im Handler (Middleware/Token-Header). Rust ergaenzt zusaetzlich per-Handler is_privileged()->401 PLUS loopback_guard-Layer. Strenger/gleichwertig, keine Regression.  —  `rust/crates/tb-internal-api/src/handlers/global_ban.rs:115-117,154-156,178-180,201-203; lib.rs:303-307`
